>
>
> **RN-845 to RN-850 (2026-08-03): NOTHING DREW A SECOND BODY, AND NOW IT DOES.** The previous lane in this seat hit a hard stop and named it exactly: of 207 exports none returned a body position, `kCinderOrbitRadiusM` lived in `sim_world.h`, which is not in the wasm build, and **it refused to transcribe the constant**. Physics did not copy the number, it MOVED it into `orbital.h` and published `of_body_state` / `of_body_facts` (ABI stays 22, both symbol-detected). This lane is the consumer. **Cinder from Forge measures 1.9099477 degrees at 1.2000000e7 m, and Forge from Cinder 5.7319680 degrees**, matching the predecessor's own sizing arithmetic to four figures, which is the acceptance criterion it wrote down before anything could be drawn. `docs/screenshots/RN845_cinder_in_forge_sky.png` is the in-game view, `RN845_forge_from_cinder.png` is a blue-and-tan half Forge in a black sky over lit regolith, and `RN845_cinder_approach.png` is Cinder at **23.06 degrees from 1,000 km** with craters at several scales and a terminator full of rim shadows. All three are lit and textured; none is a form-only or untextured pass.
>
> **THE MOON IN THE SKY IS MADE OF THE MOON YOU LAND ON, and that was the one decision that could not be taken back later.** The disc's height and albedo are baked from the body's OWN `SurfaceOracle` through a second `PlanetBody` handle, which is what `createBodyHandle` exists for (D-006). A procedural crater texture would have produced an identical first screenshot and would have been a second authority on the shape of a world. The albedo is **`TerrainShader`'s own `terrainAlbedo` rule** rather than the raw biome palette, and that is not decoration: a hard ten-value classifier at 2.4 km per texel made Forge's coastlines read as dithered speckle, because there is no value between Ocean and Plains for a bilinear filter to interpolate through. `terrainAlbedo` is continuous in the height field, so it antialiases the classifier for free and makes the disc read as terrain rather than as a biome map. It costs no extra oracle calls.
>
> **A LAMBERT SPHERE DOES NOT LOOK LIKE A MOON, AND THAT IS PHYSICS RATHER THAN TASTE.** Lambert falls off as cos(incidence) and drives the limb of a full moon to black; the real Moon is very nearly uniform across its disc, because regolith backscatters. An airless body therefore gets **LOMMEL-SEELIGER**, renormalised so its sub-solar point matches Lambert's, so the choice of law is not also a brightness change. A body with air keeps Lambert plus a limb and a twilight arc. **Which law runs is `AtmosphereProfile::present()`**, the same authority RN-840 used, so a third body authored in `atmosphere.h` gets the right disc with no render-side edit. Under ART-DIRECTION.md a grey disc is a failure, and the falloff was the defect: no amount of texture would have fixed a Lambert ball.
>
> **THE TWILIGHT ARC WAS WRONG THREE TIMES AND EACH FRAME NAMED THE NEXT FAULT.** See RN-847; the third fault is the one worth carrying, which is that the term had no SHAPE and no amount of retuning its brightness was going to give it one. Both of its numbers are the body's own air and neither is written down: its width is /core's atmosphere ceiling and its falloff is the scale height MEASURED from `_of_atmo_density` at 5600 m. /core's profile turns out to be a clean exponential (rel 0.4095 at 5 km and 0.16768 at 10 km both give 5599 m), so a two-point fit is a fit to the right shape rather than a number that merely looks measured.
>
> **PLANETSHINE HAS AN HONEST SOURCE NOW, I MEASURED IT IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, AND I DID NOT WIRE IT.** Full Forge delivers **1.837e-4 of the sun's irradiance** to Cinder's ground and new Forge 7.455e-6, a 24.6x swing across the phase, and the colour is BLUE (r 1.67e-4 against b 2.08e-4) because Forge's measured mean reflectance is 0.110 and mostly ocean. Sanity by size: earthshine on our own Moon is about 1e-4, and Forge subtends three times the angle Earth does from the Moon. **At the shipped exposure that is worth about 0.9 of an 8-bit count, and Cinder's night ground already sits at p50 15.93.** RN-846 has the four controls and the four-point sun sweep that say the 15.93 is a CONSTANT and is none of this domain's four ambient mechanisms.
>
> **THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS BYTE-IDENTICAL ACROSS TWO BINARIES, and getting there needed the harness measured first.** Two isolated trees from the SAME commit (62289f1) differing only by my five files and fifteen lines of `Systems.ts`, the SAME wasm forced into both by sha (they had drifted apart, because another lane rebuilt the wasm twice while this one ran, and comparing across that would have been a false result), and the same camera. **The harness is only deterministic about one run in three:** three repeats of one binary at one configuration gave two hashes, and three repeats of the other gave three. So byte-identity is not an available standard per run, and the honest control is the pixel delta against the RUN-TO-RUN NOISE. Noise floor 3.39 to 3.60 per cent of pixels at max delta 48 to 54; **my build under `?skybodies=0` against a build containing no Celestial code at all: 3.31 to 3.68 per cent at max delta 50 to 55, i.e. inside the floor**; and in the runs where the harness was deterministic the two are **BYTE-IDENTICAL across the two binaries** (`cmp` clean). The feature itself, for scale, moves 6.54 per cent of pixels at max delta 220, four times the noise's worst.
>
> **R17 IS NO LONGER BLOCKED ON AN INSTRUMENT, AND THE ANSWER IS STILL "DO NOT CHANGE THE DEFAULT" - FOR A DIFFERENT AND BETTER REASON THAN LAST TIME.** RN-843 refused a new `RELIEF_GRAD_UV` because its metric counted CONTRAST and could not tell "less artefact" from "less signal". A subagent built the anisotropy instrument that was owed, validated it (isotropic mottle 0.066, pure corduroy 0.9993, a factor of 15), priced the foreshortening trap that would otherwise have handed it three quarters of its own answer, and swept three sites. **The null holds and the mechanism is now split in two.** On Cinder the support really does stamp its own scale and the artefact would yield to it. On Forge's Beach it will not yield at any value, because at a ONE-TEXEL support, which is as close to the field's own gradient as the knob reaches, the term still reads 0.94 directional: **the corduroy is in `of_ground_relief` under biome 1's channel weights, not in the shader constant.** See RN-850. That escalates Beach out of this file entirely.
>
> **A FINDING THAT BELONGS TO A LOOK-DEVELOPMENT LANE AND MAY BE THE MOST USEFUL THING HERE.** Cinder's night ground is a flat p05 12.93 / p50 15.93 at sun elevations of -0.35, -0.60 and -0.85 alike, i.e. it does not care how far below the horizon the sun is. It survives `?lamp=0`, `?iblground=0` and `?bouncelit=0` unchanged and `?horizonocc=0` moves it 0.71 of a count. It is not a tone-curve lift: with the camera actually pointed at the sky, the airless night sky is **exactly 0.00 across 144,000 pixels, p05 to max**. So there is a constant ambient on the terrain that no mechanism this domain has built accounts for, it is seventeen times the size of the planetshine that is supposed to be the physical reason a lunar night is not black, and until it is dealt with **no night-lighting change on Cinder can be measured at all**.
>
> **RN-840 to RN-844 (2026-08-03): THE MOON WAS RENDERED WITH EARTH'S AIR, AND EVERY AVAILABLE FIX FOR THAT WAS A DELETION.** World-gen landed Cinder with a nine-rung crater ladder reaching 1.8 m and the renderer erased all of it: `Boot.ts` called `forgeAtmosphere(body.radiusM)` unconditionally, so the ONLY body-derived input to the sky was the RADIUS. Cinder got Earth's Rayleigh coefficients over a 200 km ball, a blue sky, and an aerial perspective that veiled every crater in a white sheet (`RN840_A_before_atmoson.png` is a total white-out at 12 km, worse than the pair that raised this). The only remedy on offer was `?atmos=0`, which does not model a vacuum, it DELETES THE SKY BOX, and the box carries RN-64's ground shell, which is the entire ambient of every stock material. So the moon had exactly two states, fog bank and black lithograph, and no way through the middle. **The split that fixes it is one boolean becoming two:** `atmosphere` (does the box exist) and `scattering` (does the integral run). An airless body keeps the box, paints black above the horizon and lit regolith below it, and `?atmos=0` stays byte-for-byte what it always was. **Both halves are driven off /core's own `AtmosphereProfile::present()`**, read through `_of_atmo_density` and `_of_atmo_space_altitude`, the same two exports the map already draws its air line from, so a third body authored in `atmosphere.h` renders correctly with no render-side edit at all. **NO WASM REBUILD WAS NEEDED OR DONE**: both exports were already shipping.
>
> **The shadow fill was TWO defects, and neither one alone does anything, which is why the isolation table matters more than the result.** `skyView = 0.5 + 0.5*dot(n, up)` is the sky-view factor of a facet on an INFINITE TANGENT PLANE, so a 21 degree slope is told it sees 96.7 per cent sky and 3.3 per cent ground. On a body with air that error is invisible, because `skyAmb` is large and the two channels are interchangeable in magnitude; **in a vacuum `skyAmb` is exactly zero and the whole ambient rides on the channel the flat-plane assumption had already set to nothing.** Separately, the bounce term's RADIANCE carried the fragment's own `shadow`, so a shaded fragment was told its entire neighbourhood was shadowed too. Measured at Cinder's spawn, 16 degree sun, geometric mask, four arms: shadow p50 **4.00** (neither) then **4.00** (occlusion only) then **5.21** (bounce only) then **8.43** (both). A **2.1x** that neither term reaches alone, because one sets the WEIGHT of the ground channel and the other its RADIANCE, and a weight times zero is zero. Sunlit p50 moved 85.91 to 89.97, **+4.7 per cent**, so the fill is a fill and not a brightening. Both controls are exact and both were measured red.
>
> **R17 AND REID'S "CONTOUR LINES SCRIBBLED ON THE GROUND" ARE ONE BUG, CONFIRMED, AND IT IS NOT THE AMPLITUDE.** `?groundreliefamp=0` removes 95.5 per cent of the high-pass tail on Cinder's crater floor and 99.2 per cent on Forge's forest floor. The specular lobe is innocent (37 per cent against relief's 95.5) and there is no sign flip: both bodies show a two-sided perturbation, and Cinder only READS bright because dark regolith under a low sun leaves the positive lobe the only half with anywhere to go. The cause is `RELIEF_GRAD_UV = 0.0311`, derived from `RELIEF_FINE_M = 0.45 m` on the stated claim that 0.45 m is "the finest authored wavelength". **It is not**: `of_ground_relief.png` carries gradient energy down to the texel (3.5 mm) and its channels decorrelate at 0.12 to 0.39 m. At the shipped support the finite difference is **86 to 96 per cent uncorrelated with the field's own gradient**, and on Beach it goes outright NEGATIVE one octave further out. The support is stamping its own 113 mm length scale onto the lighting, which is the corduroy in `RN843_beach_t32.png`. **I promoted the constant to a swept uniform and then REFUSED to ship a new default.** See RN-843 for why.
>
> **Two findings that belong to other lanes and may be the most valuable things here.** (1) **Forge's height field is EXACTLY CONSTANT below about a 32 m sample spacing** at the spawn: 100 per cent of centred differences read exactly 0.000 at 1 m and 2 m baselines, with sampled heights identical to the millimetre (4667.789 three times running), against Cinder which varies at every scale down to 1 m. `RN843_beach_t0.png` shows what that looks like with the relief bump off: a featureless plane with rocks lying on it. **The relief bump is the only surface detail Forge's ground has**, which is why its defects read so loudly, and why "turn it down" was never an available fix. (2) **`--sundot` is NOT dead**, contrary to the report I was handed; it is solved ONCE at boot against the SPAWN's local up, and a probe that teleports keeps the phase and loses the elevation. See RN-844.

> **RN-761 to RN-772 (2026-08-02): THE SPACE STATION, AND THE THREE NUMBERS IT WAS ALMOST BUILT AGAINST WERE ALL WRONG.** There was no space station in this project: no asset, no build script, no client loader. This is the first one and it is the largest asset in the game by every measure. Blender only, no browser, no build, no dev server; Reid's 4200 untouched. **(1) RN-761, DERELICT, AND IT IS AN ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A MOOD.** An OCCUPIED station implies crew, working hatches, powered lights, a faction and a reason they tolerate you, which are four systems nobody has built; a wreck is COMPLETE AS SHAPE, which is the only thing an art lane can deliver end to end. It is also the subject ART-DIRECTION.md asks for: on a derelict, wear and damage and asymmetry have a CAUSE, so they are authored as consequences instead of as decoration. The one concession is that some light strips still work, because a sealed hull in orbit has no ambient inside it and a corridor a player cannot see is not one they can be asked to walk down. **(2) RN-762, THE SECTION, AND ALL THREE OF ITS NUMBERS MOVED AFTER THE FIRST DRAFT WAS WRITTEN.** The brief said 2.50 m wide with 2.50 m headroom and an eye at 1.66. The physics lane measured at 400 km and **all three were wrong: gravity is 3.49886 m/s2 against 9.659126 at the surface, so the jump apex is 2.319915 m and the walker's topmost collision sample sits 1.65 m above the feet, and a player in a 2.50 m corridor jumps into the ceiling with 1.47 m to spare.** Headroom is now **4.00 m** everywhere a player stands. **Width is essentially free**, because the walker is a LINE to structures (three point samples at 0.15 / 0.9 / 1.65 m, no radius) and nothing binds it laterally, so width is now chosen for PROPORTION: 2.50 under a 4.00 ceiling is 1:1.6 and reads as a shaft, and **3.00** restores a corridor. Eye is **1.62** (`CAPSULE.eyeHeightM`), which is what the wall furniture and the interior camera are placed against. **A LOOK-DEV NUMBER AND A PHYSICS NUMBER CAN DISAGREE AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS NEGOTIABLE**; this is the first asset in the project whose form was set by a gameplay measurement rather than the other way round. **(3) RN-763, THE SHAPE, AND WHY THE INTERIOR IS FLAT WHEN THE HULL IS ROUND.** 66.97 x 61.69 x 23.67 m: a 6.40 m spine 66 m long, three branch modules (crew, hold, and the reactor that failed), a 16.20 m hall with a gallery at 5.10 and a dome at 12.00, a docking collar forward, a torn rim aft, two solar wings, two radiator banks, two truss runs and two dishes. The interior is FLAT WALLED inside a ROUND hull and the gap between them is the service void, which is what a real pressurised module is and which buys three things at once: a flat wall and a flat ceiling make down unambiguous where a barrel vault does not; the tube-to-tube saddle at every junction is a shape nobody can author without a boolean and the interior never has to describe it, because it describes a DOORWAY; and a missing void panel becomes a first-class detail with real hull ribs behind it. **NO ROTATING HABITAT RING IS WALKABLE, and that is a physics constraint rather than a preference**: down is the planet radial, `deckUnder` solves where a line THROUGH THE PLANET CENTRE exits a box so the parameter it returns IS a radius, and a non-radial up breaks it along with `climbGate`, `slopeGate`, `escapeRock` and the voxel collider. The station holds nadir attitude and gets real inverse-square gravity for nothing. **(4) RN-764, TWO RENDER STEMS, AND IT IS A CULLING FACT RATHER THAN TIDINESS.** `Station_*` is the hulls and appendages, `StationInt_*` is the fitout. The hull is visible from a kilometre and the fitout only from inside it, so a client drawing the station at range drops the interior with no hole in the silhouette; `hull_tube` builds both skins as ONE closed manifold so that split is possible at all. `check_shadow_lod` groups ladders by stem (RN-567), so the two are measured independently, which is the honest way round. **(5) RN-765, THE LADDER IS SHADOW-SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THAT IS THE ONE THING THIS ASSET DOES THAT NO OTHER LADDER IN THE PROJECT DOES.** Every greeble is placed by `Shell.part` or `Panel.part`, both of which take their proud height from `machine_form.LAYER` and never from the call site, so **a tier's worst-case deviation is exactly the shallowest layer it keeps and it is decided by ONE number before a triangle exists.** LOD1 drops everything at or under `hinge` (52 mm) and LOD2 at or under `gauge` (163 mm), the latter KEEPING `duct` on purpose because `scorch_ribs` is on that layer and it is the structure standing inside the breach, where the hull it would otherwise be measured against is the part that is missing. One build function emits all three tiers, so re-authoring LOD0 re-authors LOD1 in the same edit. **MEASURED, AND THE TWO STEMS LANDED DIFFERENTLY: `StationInt_LOD1` reads 52.80 mm against cascade 1's 56.25, earns c1 and c2, and puts the interior on a marginal 2.0x, which is the target. `Station_LOD1` reads 139.87 mm and `Station_LOD2` 278.37, so the hull earns cascade 2 only and sits at 3.0x.** The layer arithmetic predicts 52 and the hull measures 140, so something on the hull is still dropped without a host near it; four such causes were found and fixed and a fifth is open. It is stated here rather than rounded off, because 3.0x against 2.0x on the largest asset in the game is worth one more attribution pass, and the bins are already taken: **blender x[-32,-24] y[-8,0] at 139.87 and x[8,16] y[0,8] at 133.04**, which are the hull placards and doublers whose SPAN or ARC crosses a window boundary their CENTRE clears. It is the same both-extents defect as RN-766 at the two call sites that still guard only their midpoint, and it is one predicate away. **`StationInt` has no LOD2 deliberately: a coarse interior tier's only available saving is deleting the interior, which is the brief's own caveat (a tier whose whole saving IS an omission can never be admitted to a cascade), and the honest name for that saving is not drawing it.** **(6) RN-766, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART: 'BLOCK IN EVERY FEATURE OVER 56 mm' IS NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT.** The deviation metric is the distance from an LOD0 vertex to the tier's SURFACE, so a DROPPED part is cheap only when something the tier KEEPS is near it. Four times running, a part was dropped correctly by layer and still measured metres, and every one was the same defect wearing a different hat: **a greeble with no host under it.** 3,976.57 mm was a doubler plate emitted inside the hall's own hull window, where the spine does not exist at all, because the predicate guarding it was written for a BRANCH junction (which leaves two arcs behind) and the hall leaves nothing. 1,976.71 mm was a 5.68 m strake skipped by a MIDPOINT test while overhanging a window by 2.61 m. 940.86 mm was `scorch_ribs` inside the breach. 315.00 mm was that same doubler again, hosted along its length and running 13 degrees off the end of the arc ACROSS it. **A midpoint test is not an overlap test, and a part has two extents.** All four are art bugs FIRST (every one was floating in space where nobody could ever see it) and shadow bugs second, which is the useful half: a gate meant for shadow cost found invisible geometry no render would have shown. **(7) RN-767, A CLAIM IN MY OWN DOCSTRING THAT WAS FALSE.** `Shell.part` justified flat boxes on a cylinder for greebles 40 mm to 800 mm wide, which is true and is not the whole story: a chord of width w on radius r stands off by about w^2 / 8r, so a **2.10 m livery stripe on a 3.20 m hull floats 172 mm** and a **3.80 m cargo door on a 5.00 m hull floats 349 mm**, both visibly and both as deviation when dropped. Wide parts are now emitted CURVED as `hull_tube` arcs, and the threshold is derived rather than chosen: 12 mm of stand-off, comfortably inside cascade 0's own 15.47 mm texel, so a curved part and a flat one are indistinguishable to every consumer of the file. **(8) RN-768, THE COST OF THE TWO-STEM SPLIT, PRICED WHERE IT LANDS.** The hall's consoles and placards are bolted to the hull's inner skin, which lives in the OTHER stem, so dropping a 24 mm placard measured 839.96 mm: **a greeble mounted on a surface its own node does not contain has nothing to be measured against.** The hall wall fitout is therefore tier invariant, and the real fix is NAMED and not taken: a lined hall (panels the way the corridors have them) would put a host in the right node, let the fitout tier like everything else, and let the hull carry real window apertures behind it. That is a form change and it belongs in the pass Reid steers. **(9) RN-769, COLLISION IS THE FEATURE AND IT IS 57 PROXIES.** Floors, walls and ceilings as separate convex boxes, because one enclosing box cannot describe an interior and would make the station a solid lump. Three physics facts govern the set and the third is the one nobody would have guessed: the walker can stand on a proxy with NO new code (`KinematicBody` already takes the higher of the terrain floor and `StructureBodies.deckUnder`, measured 264 of 264 ticks grounded at 400 km with feet spread 0.000000 m); floors get an exact slab solve; and **R48, THERE IS NO CEILING AUTHORITY IN THE WALKER**, only `free()` with three point samples 0.75 m apart, so a horizontal proxy thinner than 0.75 m fits between two samples and the player passes clean through it (measured with a thickness control: at 0.3 m the player jumps through the ceiling and stands on the roof, at 0.8 m the rise stops dead at 0.849904 m). **Every overhead proxy here is 0.80 m against a 0.12 m visible ceiling panel**, which is a proxy being coarser than the thing it stands for, exactly as a machine's box proxy is coarser than its hull. The whole set comes out of ONE function, `collision_boxes()`, with a `PROXY_SET` constant carrying all three answers the physics question could have had, so the philosophy can change without a rendered triangle moving. Hall walls are TWELVE chords and not eight: the sagitta at r 8.10 over 30 degrees is 0.276 m of slack against 0.616 at eight, and that number is a decision rather than a default. **(10) RN-770, THE BUDGETS, ARGUED FROM WHAT THIS IS.** Every precedent in `contracts.json` was priced against MULTIPLICITY (the smelter's raise cost 22 x 4, the spider's 8 rigs x 4). **This asset's multiplicity is ONE, and the frame it is drawn in has no terrain LOD0 ring, no 3.2 M detail cards per km2 and no factory, because it is in orbit.** LOD0 16,904 against a 17,600 cap and 69,684 of 72,000 across five render nodes; 57 box proxies at 684 of 700; 11 materials of 12. At the full 4.0x the exterior plus the interior would be 130,600 triangles, 13 to 24 per cent of RN-71's measured 555k to 1.0 M surface frame that this asset never shares. **(11) RN-771, `pivot_mode: none`, WHICH IS HONEST AND NOT A FREE PASS.** The useful origin for a free-flying body is the spine axis at the hall centre, which is the artificial-gravity datum; `centre` would demand the AABB centre land there to 5 mm, and the AABB is set by the wing tips, the docking collar and the torn rim, so honouring it would force symmetric extents on an asset whose brief is asymmetry. `validate_glb` prints `none (view model)` for this branch, which is a stale message rather than a wrong check; a `free` mode is flagged to Admin and deliberately not taken mid-flight. **(12) RN-772, `render_station.py`, AND IT EARNED ITS KEEP THREE TIMES IN ONE SESSION.** Exterior, INTERIOR at standing eye height on the client's own 60 degree vertical FOV with the lens derived through `lens_for` and `sensor_fit` pinned VERTICAL, and a scale mode with the 1.80 m player body standing in the corridor and again in the hall. **Three shots came back as white slabs and each one was the instrument working rather than failing**: the hold eye stood 0.6 m from a SHUT bulkhead, the corridor scale camera 0.4 m behind another one, and the hall scale eye at r 8.51 inside a wall at r 8.10. That is render_launch_pad's 'a camera inside a box renders its inside' three times in one afternoon, and it is exactly why the mode exists: **an interior judged from outside is an interior judged by its silhouette, and a corridor with a beam across it at 1.85 m looks perfect in one.** **INVARIANTS: `validate_glb --all` 52/52 with the new asset passing all 17 applicable checks; `check_coplanar` 52 assets 0 over allowance, with space_station at 0 same across all five render nodes and absent from the allowance table; `check-proxies` OK at 52 assets, 141 proxies and 190 sockets, declared sets matching shipped sets both ways; space_station.glb rebuilds byte-identical; NO SHARED FILE WAS OPENED AT ALL, not `of_lib.py`, not `texgen.py`, not `surfaces.json`, not `Surfaces.ts`, so every other asset's bytes are untouched by construction rather than by a rebuild control; `contracts.json` edited as literal text and never through a `json.load`/`json.dump` round trip and never with `git checkout --`; zero em dashes verified in Python per file.** **NOT DONE, NAMED: THIS IS A FORM PASS AND EVERY VALUE ON IT IS A PLACEHOLDER.** It uses only roles that already exist (`Steel`, `SteelDark`, `SteelLight`, `Accent`, `Hazard`, `Iron`, `Coal`, `Plate`, `Rubber`, `Glass`, `EmissiveState`); there is no station surface family, no hull or deckplate tiling albedo and no per-vertex material channel bound, so the torn insulation is currently `Hazard` and reads as caution tape rather than as gold MLI, and the whole asset is one value. **The wreck also UNDER-READS from the two hero bearings**: the breach is one module and side-on to a single bearing, so `x34` and `xside` show a tidy station and only `xbreach` shows a wreck. **The hall's viewports are exterior-only** (nothing here cuts a hole, so the glass stands proud of the hull and a player inside sees plain skin), which is the same problem the lining fixes. The corridors carry no damage at all. Client side: nothing loads this file, `StructureView` pins every structure to `MachineBatch`'s single `panel` material, and per-part response needs `bakePartMat` plus `injectPartMat` from whatever hook the loader ends up using. Screenshots `RN761_form_{x34,xside,xaft,xbreach}`, `RN761_form_{spine_fwd,spine_aft,hub,hold,hab}`, `RN761_form_{scale,scale_hub}`.
>
> **RN-741 to RN-742 (2026-08-02): THE ETCHED SQUIGGLES WERE A DISCONTINUOUS DERIVATIVE, AND THE HOST ROCK LEAVES `coarse` BECAUSE NO TEXEL IN `coarse` IS STEEPER THAN 27 DEGREES.** **(1) RN-741, THE SQUIGGLES, AND THE IRONY IS THE FINDING.** The relief bump took `dFdx` OF THE SAMPLED HEIGHT. That field is authored with SHARP CRESTS on purpose, because RN-78's symmetric attempt photographed as choppy water and `groundtex.py` asserts per-channel asymmetry to prevent it. A sharp crest is a SLOPE DISCONTINUITY: its screen derivative jumps, the normal flips hard along every crest line, and that prints a hairline exactly there. **So the asymmetry that earns this texture its place is the same asymmetry that breaks its derivative**, and the fix therefore must not touch the field. It also explains the sweep nobody could make work: RELIEF_DEFAULT came down 0.30 to 0.12 to 0.08 chasing this, and **scaling a discontinuity does not remove one**. THE FIX SMOOTHS THE GRADIENT, NOT THE FIELD: the slope is differenced over a fixed TILE-space support (`RELIEF_GRAD_UV` 0.0311, a quarter of the finest authored wavelength, 0.112 m at depth 14) instead of over one pixel, while the SMOOTH tile-UV-to-screen mapping keeps `dFdx` because uv and position are linear across a triangle and their screen derivatives are exact. **Nothing rough is differentiated in screen space any more and nothing smooth is sampled twice.** Two extra fetches of a texture already bound and already sampled at that coordinate, inside a branch whose condition is a bare uniform. **MEASURED on a settled-frame framehash pair, restore bit-exact at both sites: Hills the change moves 10,073 of 14,400 tiles and the EXTREME EXCURSION HALVES, peak 59.21 to 29.75, while the mean magnitude falls only 22 per cent, 3.912 to 3.048; Beach peak 102.51 to 68.48 with the mean down 11 per cent. The spikes were the defect and the body was the relief.** THE NAMED FAILURE MODE WAS THE REAL RISK: band-limiting a gradient makes a term weaker as well as smoother, so 'the squiggles are gone' and 'the relief is gone' produce the SAME happy screenshot. `probes/reliefgrad.js` therefore measures THREE states and publishes a survival ratio: **0.779 at Hills and 0.888 at Beach**. Commit `d19be6d`. **(2) RN-742, THE `stone` FAMILY, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT.** `coarse` has a mean normal tilt of 7.69 degrees with a **MAXIMUM of 27.12**, and **0.0 per cent** of its ORM green below 0.60. No texel in it is steeper than 27 degrees and no part of it is ever smooth, so host rock wearing it can neither glint nor catch a raking sun, and that surface is most of every boulder, the whole spire and all the scree. `stone` measures **17.18 / 74.31 with 29.3 per cent under 0.60**, an effective band of 0.58 against 0.22. Split out for bark's reason exactly. **THE FREQUENCY SPLIT IS THE HAZARD THIS SUBJECT INVITES AND IT WAS PROVED RATHER THAN ASSERTED**: normal carries fracture facets 2 to 8 cm, albedo carries ONLY pigmentation at 10 to 20 cm off different seeds, ORM has its own band keyed on crest against crevice. Pearson r between the albedo's luma and the heightfield is **+0.0665**, against the shipped `panel` albedo's +0.2892, the ORM's own AO at +0.5556 and an identity albedo at +1.0000. **It is structurally not cobblestone rather than tuned not to be: `_stone_albedo` never reads `height` or `aux` at all**, so dust in the low ground is its own 10 cm field, and the AO already darkens what the normal dents. Drawing it a third time IS cobblestone. **(3) THE PALETTE PAIR, AND IT COSTS NO GEOMETRY.** `rock_form` has painted shear and fracture planes with the DARK role since RN-242, so `Rock` 0.90 to **0.94** (weathered, dustier, and the level can rise because the family now supplies the variation) against `RockDark` 0.92 to **0.80** (a fresh fracture face is the one part of a rock not dulled by weather, so it is the part that can still catch a raking sun) makes the pair say weathered against freshly broken: **a 0.14 authored separation where there was 0.02**, on faces that were already painted and were simply drawing the same material. Hex untouched; colour is look-dev's and Admin approved a RESPONSE move. **(4) INVARIANTS BOTH WAYS.** 23 of 51 assets moved and **every one carries a host-rock role**; 28 identical and **none does**. 3 new PNGs, **0 changed**, 22 of 22 pre-existing byte-identical, so a new family and a role move perturbed nothing else. The role move ITSELF costs zero asset bytes, because binding is resolved at RUNTIME from the manifest; the .glb rewrite is the palette travelling with it. validate_glb 51/51, check_coplanar 0 over allowance, texgen selftest 42, check_maps 125, tsc clean, and `verifyAgainstManifest` reports **no mismatches** in the running client with the batch bucketing as `nodes:stone:matte`. **(5) THE THIRD LAUNDERING CLASS CAUGHT A REAL ONE, AND IT WAS MINE.** Admin's rule is to ask what a build script READS, not what you stage. Rebuilt all 23 from a `git archive HEAD` tree carrying only my two Blender sources and hash-compared before staging: **51 of 51 .glb and 25 of 25 texgen PNGs reproduce exactly**, after the check found that **my smelter rebuild had been REVERTED under me** by another lane's correction of the same file, so one of the 23 was silently not rewritten and I would have committed an incomplete rewrite. The hashes are the joke: the machines lane committed `9226b56f` by accident when my palette was unreleased and corrected it to `0848bc10`; I then deliberately rebuilt `9226b56f`. **The same bytes were wrong then and right now, because the input was theirs to exclude and is mine to release.** **(6) THE INSTRUMENT FIX MOVED TWO OF MY FIFTEEN ROWS, so checking rather than assuming was right.** Re-measured with the stem-grouping `check_shadow_lod.py`: thirteen host-rock rows agree with the client ledger to the digit, but `boulder_iron` Full and Half moved 94.53 to **90.70** and 96.40 to **72.01**, because it is the asset with the largest ore sub-mesh relative to its host. The four cascade-1 near misses are unchanged (`boulder_stone|Low` 57.74, 1.49 mm over) and iron Half JOINS them at 15.76 mm over. **AND THE TWO INSTRUMENTS DISAGREE BY CONSTRUCTION ON MULTI-MATERIAL ASSETS: the offline tool groups by STEM and the client draws per (file, variant, FAMILY), because `NodeBatch` merges per family. For a boulder the offline tool measures a unit the renderer never draws as one. The CLIENT ledger is the operative one.** Nothing was spent on the near misses: Admin's later correction is that the marginal multiplier prices GROWTH and is not a recovery, and `floor` proved it by being degenerate, so what `Low` OMITS must be checked before anything is added back. Commit `4f53663`, with the module header's stale family list corrected in the same pass and proved comment-only at 0 output bytes. Screenshots `RN741_squiggle_{hills,beach}_{before,after}`, `RN742_{BoulderStone,RockSpire}_after`.
>
> **RN-563 to RN-568 (2026-08-02): THE SWEEP, AND THE BELT AND THE FLOOR ARE 4.53 mm FROM 1.0x.** Measured on a clean tree pinned at `d8bfcd9`, so every number is attributable and none of it read another lane's in-flight bytes. **(1) RN-567, THE TABLE.** Marginal multiplier is `1 + (cascades still drawing tier 0)`, which is what an asset lane actually banks. **At the full 4.0x: `assembler`, `generator`, `miner`, and `launch_pad`'s LaunchClamp ladder. At 3.0x: `smelter`, `box`, `inserter`, `power_pole`, `primitive_furnace`, `survival_smelter`, `foundation`, `launch_pad`'s pad ladder. At 2.0x: all four belt tiles, `floor`, `door`. At 1.0x: `wall` alone.** **(2) THE FINDING, AND IT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE TABLE: `belt_segment` LOD1 and `floor` LOD1 both measure EXACTLY 20.00 mm against cascade 0's 15.47 mm. They miss by 4.53 mm.** Both would take their asset from 2.0x to **1.0x**, which is the floor, and these are the two most numerous objects in a base by a wide margin: portcost's reference base is 57 belts against 22 smelters, and floors tile. **The whole gap is one feature edge in each** (belt at x 0.380 / height 0.293, floor at x 1.860 / height 0.400), both a suspiciously round 0.020, so both are one omitted trim rather than a shape that has to be re-thought. `belt_end_cap` is 30.00 (14.53 over) and both belt curves are 50.00 (34.53 over), so the whole belt family is within one small edit of the floor multiplier. **(3) THREE TOOL DEFECTS FOUND WHILE BUILDING IT, AND THE FIRST WOULD HAVE PUBLISHED FICTION.** `lod_nodes` is NOT one ladder: `launch_pad` declares `LaunchPad_LOD0/1/2` AND `LaunchClamp_LOD0/2` AND `LaunchClamp_Arm` in one list, and read as a single six-tier ladder the tool measured a pad against a clamp 14 m away and reported **14,090 mm**. Grouped by stem now, and `launch_pad` correctly reports TWO ladders at 3.0x and 4.0x. **A table published from the first version would have been worse than no table: every number would have looked precise and five would have been fiction.** Second, the uniform-grid broad phase was pathological on exactly the meshes it is for: a 48-triangle LOD2 leaves nearly every cell empty, so the shell search ran to its 24-shell cap at ~117,000 dict lookups PER VERTEX, about 590 million for `launch_pad` alone, and the first whole-set sweep never finished. Replaced with a bounding-sphere prune over an honest O(V x T), bounded and tiny at these sizes; `launch_pad` went from not finishing to 8 seconds and the smelter reads 127.60 mm across the swap, which is the control. **(4) RN-563 to RN-565, A COPLANAR REGRESSION I SHIPPED AND THE TAIL THAT HID IT.** 7 pairs, all in `Smelter_LOD1`, Accent against Steel on the footprint plane. `_mouth` (LOD0) stops its Steel at 1.94 and lets the painted band own 1.94 to 2.00; `_mouth_block` (LOD1) ran Steel to HALF, breaking that rule since it was written, and was harmless until RN-561 gave the tier the skirt it needed for the anchor bolts' contact shadow. The fix is not to move the skirt but to make the coarse tier obey the rule the fine one already obeys. **HOW I MISSED IT: I read `check_coplanar.py | tail -2`. On SUCCESS the last line IS the verdict; on FAILURE the verdict is followed by a six-line explanatory footer, so the same tail shows prose and hides `FAIL` six lines above.** A truncation safe on the happy path and lossy on the failure path fails in exactly the direction nobody checks. Every gate is now read by grepping its verdict token. **(5) RN-566, AND IT IS A THIRD KIND OF LAUNDERING.** `535ca7c` staged four paths by name and deliberately did NOT stage `of_lib.py`, which was dirty with the rocks lane's palette rows. The staging was right; **the artifact was not, because `build_smelter.py` READS of_lib at build time, so the committed `smelter.glb` carried their unreleased palette.** RN-151 and RN-556 are about the files you stage and NUMBERS.md's rule is about the generator's own source; this is a third thing: **a filtered commit protects the files you name, not the inputs your build read.** Explicit-path staging gives no protection at all, because the contamination is inside the bytes before staging begins and every gate passes on it. The only check that catches it is a `git archive HEAD` rebuild and a hash compare, which found 0848bc10 against my committed 9226b56f. **INVARIANTS: `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance on all 51 with the smelter back to 0 same; `validate_glb --all` 51/51; smelter deviation unmoved at 127.60 mm, marginal 3.0x; the four held files (`assets/textures/dist`, `texgen.py`, `surfaces.json`, `of_lib.py`) untouched and verified clean before every stage.** **NEXT, AND IT IS NOT MORE AUTHORING OF LOD0: close the 4.53 mm on `belt_segment` and `floor`.** Commits `535ca7c`, `d8bfcd9`.

> **RN-560 to RN-562 (2026-08-02): I PRICED A RECOVERY THAT CANNOT HAPPEN, AND THE REAL LEVER IS IN MY OWN FILES.** **(1) RN-561, THE RETRACTION, AND IT IS THE ENTRY.** RN-554 argued the smelter's +22.4% would become +0.65% once the cascades drew the tiers that already ship: `2276 + 192 + 2x48 = 2564`, a factor of 34. **The +22.4% was reproduced independently and is right. THE +0.65% IS WRONG AND IS RETRACTED.** It requires cascade 0 to draw `_LOD1`, and cascade 0 is **15.47 mm per texel while `_LOD1` deviates 325 mm from `_LOD0`**. The real figure is `2276 x 4`, and the factor of 34 is **a factor of 1.00**. **THE CAUSE IS OLDER THAN MY RAISE AND BELONGS TO NOBODY'S PASS: the pre-raise 592-triangle smelter's `_LOD1` already measured 264.8 mm.** The ladder was authored for SCREEN DISTANCE, where a cruder tier is admitted by how small it looks; a cascade admits one by how far its SURFACE moved, and nothing had ever asked a cascade to draw a cruder tier, so the number that decides it had never been taken on any asset in the game. **The error I actually made is worth naming precisely: I priced against a recovery instead of measuring it, in a report whose whole argument was that the art must be priced rather than asserted.** **(2) RN-562, THE INSTRUMENT, AND IT IS CALIBRATED AGAINST THE CLIENT.** `tools/blender/check_shadow_lod.py` reads the shipped bytes with no Blender and reports every tier's deviation beside every cascade's texel size. It reproduces the client's `__ofShadowLod.report()` figure of **325.00 mm to the penny**, which is the only reason to trust it. It exists because `report()` is the authority and the wrong place to AUTHOR against: an asset lane edits a build script and runs Blender, and should not have to boot a browser to learn whether the edit bought anything. **The definition is the client's and is deliberately not symmetric: LOD0's VERTICES to the tier's SURFACE**, because a vertex-set comparison scores a base ring that LOD1 lifted 30 mm at zero. **(3) THE LEVER, AND IT IS THE MARGINAL MULTIPLIER RATHER THAN ANY TOTAL.** What an asset lane banks is `1 + (cascades still drawing tier 0)`: 4.0x at 0,0,0 and 2.0x at 0,1,1. **At 4.0x the affordable LOD0 raise over 22 instances at a 3% ceiling is 225 triangles, which reproduces RN-554's own figure from the other direction; halving the multiplier doubles it.** So authoring a shadow-safe tier buys more frame than trimming any `_LOD0` ever will, and it is asset work in my files rather than client work I have to wait for. **(4) `_LOD1` RE-AUTHORED AS A SHADOW PROXY, 192 to 524 TRIANGLES, 325.00 mm to 127.60 mm, MULTIPLIER 4.0x to 3.0x.** The rule is not "reproduce the greebles" but "block in every LOD0 feature standing over about 56 mm proud, one box at its envelope", because 56.25 mm is cascade 1's texel and below that a cascade cannot resolve the feature anyway. **The measurement named every cause and I would have guessed none of them:** the roof JUNCTION BOX over a bare pan (325), the hopper VIBRATOR over a bare rear face (290), the ladder's bracketed LANDING which is WIDER THAN THE LADDER so a block sized to the obvious feature still missed by 160, the roof PIPE RUN (150), and the painted SKIRT this tier never had, which left the anchor bolts 141 mm over a plinth (and that one is the CONTACT shadow, the class `ShadowLod.ts` calls the most legible of the three). **STILL SHORT AND SAID PLAINLY: 127.60 mm is under cascade 2 and over cascade 1, so this is one multiplier step of the two available.** The loop is convergent and each turn of it is a measurement, not a guess. **(5) RN-560, THE FALSE COMMENT, WHICH WAS SHIPPING WRONG.** `Surfaces.ts` carried "NO ALBEDO MAP ON THE MACHINE PATH, EVER", closing with the structural guarantee that MachineBatch is pinned to `panel`, `panel` carries no albedo, and an albedo therefore could only reach a family the machine path never asks for. **RN-553 gave `panel` an albedo, which made the last sentence false while every word above it still read as current.** Retired and replaced with what is actually true ROLE BY ROLE, because the hook writes `diffuseColor` inside a role test rather than wholesale: **role 0 (every plinth, body, plate, greeble and brick, which is nearly all the surface area) the hook does not run at all and the albedo survives intact; roles 2 to 4 mix cargo over the deck, which is correct because cargo covers what it sits on; role 1 hard-writes the status chip, which is also correct because an indicator's colour is entity state and must not be modulated by paint wear.** The mean-neutral divide holds throughout because it lives in `material.color` and three composes that before the hook. **Marked NOT MEASURED in the file: this is read off the shader source and three's composition order, not off a frame, and the in-game look is owed.** **(6) INVARIANTS: `validate_glb --all` 51/51, `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, contracts.json verified row-wise against the pinned base with only the smelter row differing.** **NOT DONE, NAMED:** the other fifteen assets' `devMM` are UNMEASURED and Admin's instruction is to measure all of them before authoring anything else, because the rocks lane found four rows missing cascade 1 by under 11 mm and a near-miss is a full multiplier step for almost no triangles; `attachSurface(m,'panel')` is ROUTED as its own panel/coarse pass and deliberately not forced inside an asset commit; and this lane now PAUSES for the rocks lane's 23-asset rewrite, which includes my smelter.

> **RN-734 (2026-08-02): THE SHADOW-LOD TIER BUDGET, MEASURED ON THE ROCKS RATHER THAN GUESSED, AND THE CHEAPEST FRAME IN THE PASS IS A CONSTANT SOMEBODY ELSE OWNS.** Admin's correction: the machine ladders were authored for SCREEN DISTANCE and were never shadow-safe, so price against the MARGINAL multiplier `1 + (cascades still drawing tier 0)`, and measure `devMM` before authoring. Measured, at Mountains, `__ofShadowLod.report()`. **EVERY HOST-ROCK `_LOD1` IN THE GAME MISSES CASCADE 1's 56.25 mm BUDGET, AND FOUR OF THE FIFTEEN MISS IT BY UNDER 11 mm.** `boulder_stone|Low` deviates **57.74 mm and misses by 1.49 mm**; `boulder_coal|Low` by 3.24 mm; `boulder_copper|Low` by 10.70 mm; `boulder_iron|Low` by 10.99 mm. The worst is `boulder_coal|Full` at 143.75 mm. So every host-rock row sits at a **3.0x** marginal multiplier (cascades 0 and 1 both drawing tier 0), not the 4.0x worst case. **The ORE sub-meshes are already better** and are also the cheap ones: 42 to 49 triangles, and `boulder_iron|Full|ore` deviates only 31.89 mm, so it already earns cascade 1 at 2.0x. The mass is the host rock and the host rock is the part that fails. **THE FINDING THAT OUTRANKS RE-AUTHORING: the near-misses mean the constant is worth more than the geometry.** `SHADOW_LOD_TEXELS` ships at k=1, and the ledger already publishes `tierPerCascadeK2`. At k=2, **17 of 24 rock rows gain a full multiplier step** and every host-rock row gains except `boulder_coal` Full and Half. Measured on one build one flag apart: **savedPerFrame 12,274 to 27,791 triangles (+15,517, a 2.26x improvement in the shadow LOD's own saving) and drawn triangles 480,369 to 468,122, i.e. -12,247 or -2.55 per cent** at the Mountains camera. For scale, the whole shadow-LOD pass landed -15,157 across the factory; the node pool alone gains more than that again from one constant. **THE VISUAL COST, MEASURED AGAINST THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN FLOOR RATHER THAN AGAINST ZERO, WHICH IS THE ONLY HONEST FORM OF THIS CLAIM.** `shadowlodk` is a LOAD-TIME flag, so a pair costs two page loads and cannot be bit-exact. So the floor was taken explicitly: two loads at the SAME k=1 move **4.65 per cent** of pixels with a peak of -106.5 counts. k=1 against k=2 moves **4.66 per cent** with a peak of -101.6. **Signal over floor is 1.00x**, at sun dot 0.217 where shadows are longest and a silhouette error is most exposed, with the same node index 70 at the same 30.95 m on both sides. **What that licenses is a BOUND and not a zero: the change is smaller than 4.65 per cent of pixels, and this instrument cannot say anything finer.** One camera, one site; a stronger claim needs a shadow-region-restricted metric at several sites. **NOT MINE TO CHANGE:** k belongs to the shadow-LOD lane and the rocks are only the evidence. Escalated with the numbers. Also visible in the same report and NOT mine: `launchpads:pad_body` deviates **14,090 mm**, i.e. its `_LOD1` is fourteen metres off its `_LOD0`, which is a broken ladder rather than a coarse one. Screenshots `RN734_shadowk_{a,b,c}` (a and b are the floor pair, c is k=2).

> **RN-721 to RN-733 (2026-08-01): THE ROCK SKIN PASS PART 1. THREE FINDINGS THAT OUTRANK THE ART, AND THE MECHANISM WITHOUT WHICH NO AMOUNT OF AUTHORING REACHES A PIXEL.** Brief: skin the four ore boulders and the stone boulder, the spire and scree, and the terrain's own material response. **(1) RN-721, THE FINDING, AND IT REFRAMES THE WHOLE BRIEF.** `NodeBatch.makeBatch` builds ONE `MeshStandardMaterial` per family bucket with `metalness: ore ? 0.25 : metal ? 1.0 : 0.0` and `roughness: ore ? 0.72 : metal ? 0.38 : 0.88` as LITERALS, and `normalize()` bakes only COLOUR into a vertex attribute. So `OF_IronOre`, `OF_CopperOre` and `OF_CoalSeam` all drew at exactly 0.72 / 0.25 and every host-rock role at 0.88 / 0.0: **four minerals had TWO material responses between them and both were constants in a ternary.** Authoring per-mineral response in `of_lib.PALETTE` was writing into a void, which is why RN-156 left those rows at look-dev's defaults. The surfaces did not say what the forms say because the surfaces had no way to. **(2) RN-722, THE SECOND FINDING, AND IT INVALIDATES EVERY ROCK FRAME EVER TAKEN HERE.** `render_rocks.py` never called `surface_preview.apply_all()` and never touched `scn.view_settings`. So every rock render this project has produced showed FLAT PALETTE CONSTANTS with no normal, no ORM and no family binding, judged under Blender's default AgX rather than the shipped ACES. The `ore` family authored at RN-156 had never been photographed on a rock. Fixed: `setup_view_transform` (a knowing FOURTH copy, with the other three named as a debt rather than refactored, because those files are owned by in-flight lanes), `apply_maps()` with a `--nomaps` control so the pair is one flag apart, and `--diag <stops>` ported so a colour can be DERIVED rather than tuned against a studio. **A third defect fell out of it: `surface_preview` derives the role from `mat.name[3:]` and Blender renames on collision, so in a multi-asset frame only the FIRST boulder was ever mapped and the other three kept flat constants** (`OF_Rock.001`, `.002`, `.003`). `merge_duplicate_materials()` takes skipped from 6 to 0, guarded on base colour, metallic and roughness matching. `render_check.py` is safe only because it is single-asset; any other multi-import studio has this latent. **(3) RN-723, THE CHANNEL, INHERITED AND NOT REBUILT.** `RockShader.ts` is ONE new `onBeforeCompile` whose entire body is `injectPartMat(shader)`, splicing RN-491's `PartMaterial` into the node path; `NodeBatch.normalize` gains `bakePartMat` beside the colour bake. **DW-10 BOTH WAYS: 4 ShaderMaterial + 3 hooks becomes 4 + 4 with the flag on and 4 + 3 under `?rockmat=0`, against DW-10's cap of 5 on ShaderMaterials, which does not move.** Unavoidable, and the argument is stated rather than waved at: `PartMaterial.ts` installs no hook by design, and the node path had none to splice into (`applyWind` is on `leaf:`/`grass:` only, and a material holds exactly ONE `onBeforeCompile`, so reusing it would delete the wind rather than add a channel). The spider paid nothing only because `FurShader` already existed. **THREE THINGS THE BRIEF GOT WRONG ABOUT THE CODE, all found by the subagent and all load-bearing:** (a) `?partmat=0` is a THIRD state, where the hook installs but injects nothing, so nothing divides the base back out and a 0.02 base would leak as real metalness on every rock; the gate is therefore `rockMatEnabled() && partMatEnabled()`. (b) `assertPartMatBase` early-returns on the `partmat` flag only, so calling it for every hooked batch would THROW AT BOOT under `?rockmat=0`. (c) a single flag read still bakes for `leaf:`/`grass:`/`flat:`, which no hook reads, i.e. RN-498's dead-buffer failure again; the bake gate is the same `mineralFamily` predicate the hook uses. `flat:` is excluded by name because it holds Water and Oil and a pool surface getting its authored response is a change nobody asked for. **(4) RN-724, THE PALETTE, AND THE BLAST RADIUS DECIDED WHICH ROWS MOVED.** `of_lib` writes metallic and roughness into the exported glTF material, so a palette edit rewrites the bytes of every asset using that role. Measured before editing: the three ore-in-rock roles appear in exactly ONE asset each, `Rock` in **23** assets and `RockDark` in **15**, including a sibling lane's in-flight smelter. So only the three ore rows moved and the host-rock rows are ESCALATED, not skipped: that is RN-151's laundering hazard in .glb form, avoided by arithmetic rather than by care. IronOre 0.25/0.55 to **0.42/0.44** (dense, tight metallic glint on fresh fracture, still under the 0.5 batching split which is load-bearing); CopperOre 0.30/0.50 to **0.16/0.62** (oxide bloom, and the metalness goes DOWN because an oxide crust is a DIELECTRIC over the metal, not exposed metal); CoalSeam 0.10/0.30 to **0.04/0.34** (both halves of Reid's note are true of real coal and are not in conflict: the LIGHT-DRINKING is the albedo at luma 27, the darkest row in the palette, and the seam roles are painted on FRESH fracture, which in coal is conchoidal and vitreous; 0.34 only lifts p05 off section 2.1's 0.15 floor, since 0.30 x 0.486 was 0.146). **Effective bands, measured off the shipped `ore` ORM green (p05 0.486, p50 0.984, p95 1.000, 12.8 per cent of texels under 0.60): iron 0.21 to 0.44, copper 0.30 to 0.62, coal 0.17 to 0.34, all clearing section 2.1's 0.15 width, against ONE band of 0.35 to 0.72 shared by all three before.** The three ore boulders rebuilt and moved; **boulder_stone rebuilt BYTE-IDENTICAL, which is the negative control built into the change itself** because it wears no ore role. **(5) RN-731, THE TERRAIN SPECULAR LOBE, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT REACHES EVERY FRAME.** Until this pass the terrain's whole lighting model was `lit = albedo * irradiance`: pure Lambert, NO specular term and NO roughness input anywhere in the material. Ground that cannot glint is not a tuning problem, there was no term to tune. **THE ROUGHNESS NEEDS NO MAP, NO UNIFORM AND NO VARYING**, which is what makes it arguable at all: every signal was already computed by the fragment. `vMatW` is the per-biome material weight vector whose channels are literally grass clump / rock grain / granular / clod; `coverSel` is the same slope gate the albedo, the grain and the relief already share, so roughness cannot disagree with them about where a cliff starts; `snow` was already there; and `wet` is RN-57's band, which until now only DARKENED the albedo. `ofArtWetness` is split out of `ofArtWet` unchanged to the character so both halves read the identical scalar. **DW-10 COSTS NOTHING: it is arithmetic inside an existing ShaderMaterial's existing fragment program, no new program, no new variant, no new define, and it is compiled out of the scaled scene entirely.** Against DW-5 and DW-35 this is the cheapest rung on the stated ladder: renderer and material work that touches ZERO assets, zero triangles and zero VRAM and makes every existing asset respond to light, which is exactly what DW-35 says to exhaust before DW-5 is even discussed. **(6) RN-732, THE SKY HALF, AND THE CORRECTION THAT SAVED IT.** Schlick's Fresnel goes to 1 at grazing and a walking camera sees ground at about 8 degrees of grazing by 12 m, so with a linear `(1 - rough)` weight dry ground at roughness 0.86 would have returned **14 per cent of the sky**, against section 2.1's masked sky p50 191 and masked ground 33 to 55: a quarter of the ground's own value applied to the whole middle distance. That is named failure mode 1 arriving through the ambient instead of through the sun. Squared, dry ground returns 2.0 per cent and wet ground 81 per cent. **MEASURED, the sky half moves ZERO tiles above 0.5 counts at both sites and every rung, peak 0.15 to 0.52 counts: the failure mode is excluded by measurement rather than by hope, and the honest converse is that the sky half currently buys nothing demonstrable and its justification is the wet band, which is not yet photographed.** **(7) THE INSTRUMENT, AND ITS FIRST DRAFT WAS WRONG IN THE WAY lookdev.js ALREADY WARNS ABOUT.** `probes/terrainspec.js` v1 paired `of.screenshot()` captures and read **59.56 per cent of pixels moved with a maxDelta of MINUS 149.8 counts**, which is impossible for a purely additive term, with the restore not bit-exact at any rung. `of.screenshot()` resolves from inside the rAF drain, i.e. it WAITS FOR A FRAME, WHICH RUNS THE SIM; that number was the day clock, the wind and the creatures. Rebuilt on `of.framehash`, which renders synchronously and advances no ticks. **Hills: 6848 / 6313 / 4525 / 372 tiles of 14400 moved at sun dot 0.589 / 0.35 / 0.121 / 0.034, peak 16.70 / 7.92 / 6.24 / 3.02 counts. Beach: 8386 / 8297 / 5547 / 2710, peak 18.74 / 8.51 / 4.76 / 3.03. Restore bit-exact (peak 0.00) at every rung at both sites, ZERO tiles darkened anywhere, and section 2.1's groundNear luma moves +1.5 / +0.8 / +0.3 / 0.0 at Hills and +2.9 / +2.2 / +0.5 / +0.3 at Beach, all inside the few-counts bar.** 16 of 16 checks pass at both sites. Beach moves most, which is the right sign: it has the highest granular weight in `MAT_W` at 0.26. **(8) WHAT IS NOT DONE AND IS NAMED.** The host-rock roles `Rock` and `RockDark` are the flat ones and the measurement says so: **`coarse` has a mean normal tilt of 7.69 degrees and a MAXIMUM of 27.12 degrees, i.e. no texel in it is steeper than 27 degrees, and 0.0 per cent of its ORM green falls below 0.60**, against `ore`'s 18.45 degrees mean and 71.08 maximum. The host rock cannot glint and cannot catch a raking light, and it is most of every boulder, the whole spire and all the scree. A `stone` family split out of `coarse` is the fix and costs ZERO asset bytes because role-to-family binding is runtime, but it is a ROLE MOVE, and Admin's RN-559 note says `--only` does not refresh `roles`, so it needs a clean-tree full build. Also owed: the `ore` tiling albedo (copper's oxide bloom is a two-hue story a mean-neutral shared map cannot tell); the coal crumb ring, which currently sheds ROCK-coloured fragments from a coal boulder and wants the matte `Coal` role beside the vitreous `CoalSeam` (a contract `max_materials` raise, 3 to 4); the spire and scree, untouched. **THE `?rockmat=0` CONTROL IS LOAD-TIME, NOT RUNTIME, so its in-game evidence is a PICTURE and not a bit-exact pair**: two page loads sit at a 5 to 8 per cent moved-pixel floor, which is the same floor the decode instrument publishes, so no in-game delta is claimed for the channel. What IS proved is offline: 8 of 8 anchors hit against three 0.185.1's shipped `meshphysical` source, vertex +70 chars and fragment +685, `?rockmat=0` a total no-op, the shared-hook identity `a.onBeforeCompile === b.onBeforeCompile` true across materials, and a negative control that fires by name. **INVARIANTS.** All seven rock assets rebuilt BYTE-IDENTICAL before any edit, which is what makes 'before' a measurement rather than a file date; validate_glb 51/51 and then 4/4 on the rebuilt boulders; check_coplanar 0 over allowance, and the four boulders are at zero same-facing pairs; texgen selftest 39 checks 0 failures; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean with negative controls both ways in both lanes. `npm run check:limits` fails on 26 files including `NodeBatch.ts`, and that is PRE-EXISTING: it was 459 lines against a 400 cap at HEAD before this pass. Screenshots `RN731_{BoulderIron,BoulderCoal,BoulderCopper,BoulderStone}_{before,after}` (same node index, same range, aimCos 1.00000 on both sides, the fixture published beside every frame) and `RN731_mtn_{before,after}`. Commits `6dcfcb0`, `77b91ab`. **THE COMMIT PROTOCOL EARNED ITS KEEP TWICE IN ONE PASS, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** HEAD moved under this lane TWICE while committing. The first time, the working-tree `run.mjs` and `NodeBatch.ts` held the shadow-LOD lane's then-UNCOMMITTED rows, so a straight commit would have LAUNDERED their work in. The second time, that lane committed mid-pass and the same two files inverted: my copies now held an OLDER wording of two of their comment lines and a `ladder()` docstring block that had not existed when my copy was made, so the same straight commit would have REVERTED them. **Same file, same technique, opposite direction, within one hour**, which is Admin's point exactly: a filtered blob is only ever as good as the freshness of the base it is filtered against. The three-argument compare-and-swap refused at the right moment both times, and `docs/controllers/rendering.md` went stale a THIRD time and would have deleted the RN-681 to RN-690 entry.

> **RN-511 to RN-518 (2026-08-01): DUSK WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM. THE PALE CYAN WASH IS A BUILD GHOST COVERING THE VIEWPORT, IT IS THE SAME SIZE AT NOON, AND THE FUR'S WIND AND LAG ARE NOW MEASURED.** **(1) RN-511, THE ATTRIBUTION, AND IT IS RN-146'S SHAPE ARRIVING A SECOND TIME.** The brief's four suspects were the in-scatter term, the new exposure, RN-152's starlight floor and the prop-ambient sky half. **All four are wrong, and one measurement retires the whole class.** At ONE site (lat 2.0363, lon 144.0562, Mountains, 4,779 m of relief), ONE heading (yaw 150, pitch -6), ONE binary and ONE sun (`--sundot=0.30`, elevationDot 0.303 against 0.327, sunT 0.2018 against 0.2053, surfaceHeightM agreeing to 1e-4 m), a plain teleport-and-look reads **world mean 115.2, warm -38.2, sky RGB 42/80/133**, and `probes/spiderskin.js`'s own run reads **196.8, -46.2, sky RGB 161/197/197**. Same camera, same light, same terrain, two completely different frames, so the frame is not a function of the sun angle and every hypothesis of the form "dusk is lit wrong" is dead before any flag is toggled. **RN-459's evidence frame reproduces on HEAD to within a count** (world mean 196.81 against 195.80, warm -46.19 against -47.21, RGB 165/210/212 against 164/209/211), which is what made the hunt possible at all. **(2) THE ELIMINATION, ONE BINARY, ONE FLAG EACH, ON THE REPRODUCED FRAME.** `?levelring=0` 185.5/-56.2, `?post=0` 174.9/-63.9, `?props=0` 171.9/-62.2, `?water=0` 195.5/-47.4, `?underwater=0` 196.8/-46.2. **Every one still washed.** `?post=0` surviving is the decisive rung on its own: that flag restores the pre-stack path exactly, so the exposure, the contrast, the saturation, the split tone and the whole grade are eliminated together, and what is left has to be something DRAWN. **(3) RN-512, WHAT IT IS, AND THE POSITIVE CONTROL IS ONE LINE OF PROBE.** `probes/duskghost.js` selects a hotbar slot through the player's own number-key path (`of.build(n)`) and changes NOTHING else. Against a two-capture floor of **0.03 counts of mean and 0.006 of warm**, the world band moves: slot 1 reads 147.85/+159.45, slot 5 reads 116.54/-23.41, **slot 3 reads 203.64/-49.33**, slot 6 reads 118.66/-25.34, and slot 1 again reads **147.85/+159.449**. Peak swing **55.79 of mean and 208.78 of warm**, i.e. 1,860x and 34,800x the floor, and **the restore lands back on its own start to 0.000 and 0.001**. Slot 3's frame is RN-459's frame. Draw calls are 56 in every row and programs 39 in every row, so the ghost is small geometry drawn NEAR THE CAMERA rather than a large object: `docs/screenshots/RN511_wash_ghost_{on,off}.png` is that pair, one selection apart on one build under one light, and the ghost's own panel edges are visible down the left of the washed one. **(4) RN-513, AND THIS IS THE SENTENCE THAT KILLS THE ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS.** The same sweep at the same site at **local noon, 69.23 degrees**, moves the frame by **55.30 of mean and 202.41 of warm**, within three per cent of the dusk figures. **An effect that is the same size with the sun straight overhead is not a property of low sun.** **THE GENERAL FORM, WORTH MORE THAN THE INSTANCE: when a WHOLE FRAME changes colour, suspect something DRAWN before suspecting how things are LIT.** A lighting term acts through albedo and normal and therefore leaves the frame's structure intact; an object in front of the camera does not, and `?post=0` makes the difference between the two visible in one run. **NOT MINE TO FIX AND FLAGGED UP: the ghost is `FactoryGhost` / `BuildMode` and belongs to gameplay.** `of.build(0)` disarms it, which is the workaround every screenshot probe now needs and which `probes/furmotion.js` asserts rather than assumes. **(5) RN-515, DUSK REFERENCE LUMINANCES, WHICH SECTION 2.1 HAD NONE OF AND WHICH IS WHY THIS SHIPPED.** New `probes/dusk.js`, four sites, five rungs each, `groundNear` in the same 140 px box the noon table uses, and a `warm` axis on every band because a luma-only row cannot tell a warm dusk from a cold one and the complaint was about HUE. Measured: **`groundNear.warm` stays POSITIVE at every site down to about one degree** (Beach +68.7 to +10.2, Plains +29.4 to +3.3, Forest +13.4 to +1.3, Hills +14.8 to -0.2), `groundNear` falls monotonically at every site, and **`skyLow.warm` is between -29 and -56 everywhere**. There is no cold wash anywhere in the band at any site. That first bullet is now section 2.1's assertion and it is the one that would have caught a real one. **(6) TWO INSTRUMENT DEFECTS FOUND ON THE WAY, ONE OF THEM IN lookdev.js.** lookdev.js centres `groundFar` at `hz + 14` with a half-height of 26, so **twelve of its fifty-two rows are SKY**; at Hills at dusk that box read 152 while the ground under it read 7. And the sky mask's rule is CHROMATIC, so **the detected horizon row climbs a hundred pixels between noon and 1.5 degrees with the geometry perfectly still**, which means no box placed relative to it can carry an elevation trend. Both are recorded in 2.1 rather than quietly worked around. **(7) RN-514, THE FUR INSTRUMENT, AND IT WAS BROKEN AT BOTH ENDS BEFORE IT WAS USED.** RN-465 recorded that `furStats()` was published so the hook and the `?fur=0` boot default could each be asserted. **`SpiderFlock.furStats` is called by no file in the repository and no debug surface exposes the flock**, so nothing could read one field of it; and **`fur` was not in run.mjs's forwarded-parameter list**, so `--fur=0` was DROPPED by the runner and the URL arrived without it. Neither would ever have failed a check because no check could reach them. Fixed: `window.__ofFur` is registered in FurShader's module scope on PropWind's own precedent (state, freeze, thaw, setLag; no program, no draw call, no uniform), and `fur` and `partmat` are added to the whitelist. **(8) RN-516, THE WIND, MEASURED.** `probes/furmotion.js` pairs TWO `framehash` RENDERS with a clock write between them and NO screenshots, because `framehash` renders synchronously and advances no ticks while `of.screenshot()` resolves inside the rAF drain and runs the sim: between two screenshots eight creatures walk metres, and the sway is 18 mm. **The floor is exactly 0 tiles and 0 peak.** Separations 0.4, 1.1, 3.7 and 11.3 s move **106, 233, 185 and 206 tiles of 14,400, peaks 8.35, 17.80, 23.03 and 23.75**, and every pair restores BIT-EXACTLY. **(9) RN-517, THE LAG, AND IT IS A PROPERTY ASSERTION NOT A MAGNITUDE ONE.** Driven through the uniform so it is a question about the shader rather than about whether a creature happened to be running: floor 0/0, half lag **185 tiles peak 20.58**, full lag **262 peak 36.00**, and a lag of equal length on a different axis **227 peak 19.25**. More lag displaces more, which is what "responds to velocity" means, and the claim survives anyone tuning `LAG_M`. **THE SHARED-LAG LIMIT IS CONFIRMED AS A MEASUREMENT AND NOT TAKEN ON TRUST: one hooked material, one lag vector, EIGHT claimed rigs.** Eight creatures running eight ways stream their fur one way, exactly as RN-465 wrote down before anyone measured. **(10) THE NEGATIVE CONTROL, AND IT IS TWO-SIDED AND EXACT.** With `?fur=0`: `enabled` false, `flagPresent` true, **`hooked` empty**, and **every wind and lag pair moves exactly ZERO tiles at every separation and every vector**, while EIGHT rigs are still claimed and drawn so the control is not vacuous. With no `fur` parameter at all: **`enabled` true and `flagPresent` false, which is the shipped BOOT DEFAULT asserted in its own right** (RN-150; two features have already shipped dark because every probe passed an explicit flag). Draw invariants across the pair: geometries 55 to 55, textures 99 to 99, **programs 43 to 43**, so the hook costs no program; draw calls 91 against 85 and triangles 687,985 against 650,183 are creature-count variation between runs and are not attributable. `bakePartMat`'s gate was verified rather than assumed: `SpiderFlock.ts:178` reads `furState().enabled` and line 197 bakes only when it is true, so `?fur=0` writes no dead buffer. **(11) RN-518, THE IN-GAME CREATURE SHOT THAT FAILED TWICE, TAKEN.** `docs/screenshots/RN518_ingame_flock.png` (a column of eight marching down a dune) and `RN518_ingame_close.png`. **Both of RN-459's two failure causes are fixed rather than worked around. The aiming sweep now keeps the heading whose FUR PAIR moves most**, and a fur pair is two framehashes with the wind clock frozen and no ticks between them, so the 11,465 foliage instances that broke spiderwalk's "keep the heading that moves most" contribute EXACTLY ZERO by construction: the sweep reads 0, 0, 0, 40, 247, 196, 229, 32, 0, 0, 0, 0 across twelve headings, and **the zeroes are the point**, because a sweep that can be silent is a sweep that found the creature rather than the weather. **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT SWEEP REPORTED THE IDENTICAL 334 TILES AND 31.97 PEAK AT ALL TWELVE HEADINGS**, because `of.look` does not reach the camera until a frame runs and `framehash` renders from the camera the last frame left: twelve confident identical meaningless rows, now guarded by a check that the headings disagree. The wash is gone from the shot because `of.build(0)` disarms the ghost first. **(12) HYGIENE.** One binary per comparison, frozen by `git archive HEAD web` into the scratchpad with a `node_modules` junction so NOTHING was written into the shared checkout; served wasm byte-identical to HEAD's, bundle "client expects 21" against `OF_ABI_VERSION = 21`, `spider.glb` byte-identical to HEAD's so five live Blender lanes could not leak into the measurement. HEAD moved four times mid-pass (`4a7c133` to `b45bdd1` to `23e2461` to `d0393bf`) and `web/src` is byte-identical between the first two, which is why the dusk numbers taken on `4a7c133` stand for `b45bdd1`. Port 4200 untouched; my own port was started only after `curl` returned 000 on it. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean with a negative control red BY NAME (`TS2552` at FurShader.ts:259) and silent after a revert verified against the pinned base. **`run.mjs` and `rendering.md` both carried ANOTHER lane's uncommitted work** (RN-681's `shadowlod` whitelist entries, and a machine-pass entry), so both were committed as FILTERED BLOBS built from a PINNED base sha rather than from the moving `HEAD:` ref. **(13) NOT DONE, NAMED.** The flat families, which is the next block: `coarse` at 7.7 degrees of mean tilt with the most screen area, `panel`'s 0.032 roughness band, and the terrain's absent specular lobe. The Plains dusk foreground at luma 7.9 against a world band of 37.5, whose mechanism is written down in 2.1. Per-rig fur lag. And a `dusk.js` sky mask that does not move with the sun.
