> **RN-641 to RN-648 (2026-08-01): THE PLAYER PASS PART 1. THE HANDS WERE SIZED AGAINST A FIELD OF VIEW THE CLIENT DOES NOT USE, THE PLAYER HAD NEVER WORN A SURFACE MAP AT ALL, AND THREE OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS PASS BUILT WERE KILLED BY LOOKING AT THEM.** **(1) RN-641, THE FOV, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART IN THIS PASS.** `ASSET-SPECS 4.2` says the client runs "roughly 70 degrees" vertical; `render_check.py`'s `eye` view and `rig_common.py`'s entire hand-distance argument are both derived from it. **`CameraRig.ts` constructs every camera INCLUDING `vmCam` at `fovDeg = 60`, and `setFov` has no caller anywhere in `web/src`.** At the authored 0.62 m the visible frame height is `2 * 0.62 * tan(30) = 0.716 m`, not the 0.868 m the 2026-07-27 sizing comment computed, so **the hands are 21 per cent larger in frame than the arithmetic that placed them believed**, on the one asset whose only recorded complaint was ever that it was too big. The in-game frames make it visible: the hands sit atan(0.30/0.62) = 25.8 degrees below the eye axis against a 30 degree half-angle, so at a LEVEL view they are 86 per cent of the way to the bottom edge and `RN648_ingame_fp` shows two slivers. **`tools/blender/render_player.py` IS NOW THE REFERENCE STUDIO RIG AND THE NEXT LANE SHOULD COPY IT RATHER THAN `render_check.py`**, for two reasons that are both traps: its lens is DERIVED from a vertical FOV with `sensor_fit` pinned to `VERTICAL` (Blender's `AUTO` fit follows the LARGER output dimension while `three.PerspectiveCamera.fov` is always vertical, so an AUTO lens silently reframes when the resolution changes), and it PRINTS its view transform every run, because **`render_check.py` sets none and therefore renders every asset in this project under Blender 5.0's AgX.** **(2) RN-642, THE TEN COPLANAR PAIRS, BOTH CAUSES, TO ZERO, ROW REMOVED.** Attributed from the shipped bytes: `OF_EmissiveState`/`OF_Plate` at glTF Z = 0.2000 x2 and `OF_Plate`/`OF_Suit` at Y = 1.8000 x8. The chest state light and the pack it sits on both had their front face at y = -0.2000 exactly, and the loser of that coin toss is the state light. The comm fin's top face was authored onto the helmet crown at z = 1.800 with a comment explaining that this keeps the declared 1.80 m height crown-driven: **that reasoning was correct about the envelope and never required the two surfaces to TOUCH.** `dims_xyz_m` unchanged to the digit. `ALLOWED["player/player_body"]` deleted; held at 0. **(3) RN-643/644, TWO FAMILIES, MEASURED.** `Suit`, `SuitDark` and `Plate` are used by the two player builders and `build_armour_set.py` and by nothing else in the repo, grep-checked before anything moved, which is what makes re-pointing them a player-only act; `SuitAccent` stays on `panel` because `rocket_common.py` and the lander share it. `suitfab` (512 px, 0.5 m tile) is a plain weave from two crossed `_hair_layer`s, because a weave is two anisotropies at right angles and `_hair_layer` is the only anisotropic primitive in the file; the over-under checker is what makes a highlight run ALONG a yarn instead of sitting on a lattice of dots. `suitplate` (384 px, 0.4 m tile) is brushed grain, pitting, dings and eleven authored scratches with ONE wear mask driving albedo, roughness and metalness together. **Effective roughness `panel x Suit 0.046` and `panel x Plate 0.030` to `suitfab x Suit 0.224`, `suitfab x SuitDark 0.242`, `suitplate x Plate 0.268`; effective metalness `suitplate x Plate 0.456`**, which answers section 2.1's "the only varying metalness in the game is 8.9 per cent of one map". Normal mean tilt 21.7 and 16.8 deg against panel 10.5, bark 22.8. **(4) THE THREE TRAPS THIS PASS PAID FOR, ALL REUSABLE.** *Summing independent noise fields NARROWS the distribution* (central limit theorem): suitplate v1 summed four fields and measured an effective metalness band of **0.074, WORSE than the 0.406 it replaced**, a straight regression on the one number the family exists to fix. **Paint does not thin, it CHIPS**, so the mask is a `_smoothstep` with its knee centred on the MEASURED raw median. *Amplitude and pitch are not independent*: suitfab v1 at a 5 mm thread pitch and 0.62 of the height **rendered as knitted wool**, because **anything a player can count reads as hand-knitting**. And *a part authored to explain another part is only worth its triangles if it is actually hidden*: the knuckle guard's four plates were first authored near-ROUND with their long axis forward and rendered as **claws**, then given a dark carrier strip authored WIDER than the plates meant to hide it, which rendered as a dark triangle down the glove. **"Mostly hidden" is a claim that has to be rendered, not assumed.** All three are added to INSTRUMENTS.md. **(5) RN-645/646, THE FORM.** The cuff was a 150 mm `OF_SuitAccent` tube and SuitAccent is 2E7DBE, a saturated primary that under the corrected framing is the largest block of colour in the first-person frame; it could not be re-surfaced out of the problem because the rocket lane shares the role. **A pressure glove attaches with a metal locking ring**, so it is one now (OF_Plate, two courses, three bayonet lugs at 40/160/280 rather than four on the axes), and the accent survives as a 6 mm index stripe: same silhouette, one hundredth of the saturated area. FP arms **1,740 to 2,284 triangles against an UNCHANGED 2,600 budget, no raise requested**. **(6) RN-648, THE PLAYER HAD NEVER WORN A MAP, AND NOW DOES IN GAME.** `surfaces.json` has mapped `Suit` to a family since DW-35 and `surface_preview.py` honours it, so **every studio render of this asset since then showed maps the client did not apply**: `PlayerRig.ts` never imported Surfaces. The four consumers were `PropLibrary`, `MachineBatch`, `NodeBatch` and `SpiderFlock`, every BATCHED path and no per-object one, and **the player is the only rendered asset in the game that is neither batched nor merged**, so it fell through the single gap. Fixed with `familyForMaterial` rather than a local role table (the manifest is the authority), for **zero DW-10 slots**. **`probes/playerskin.js` caught two things on its first run:** `Surfaces.ts` keeps the client's own COPY of ROLE_FAMILY and RN-100's rule is that it moves in the SAME commit as texgen's, which I forgot and `verifyAgainstManifest` turned into a failed smoke run exactly as designed; and it reported six materials "registered TWICE" that were **two rigs**, since the body and FP arms share every role name by design, so the label now carries which rig. **The probe's own assumption being wrong is worth as much as the defect it found.** IN GAME, own port, killed after, 4200 untouched: **smoke PASS, `fparms:OF_Suit=suitfab`, `fparms:OF_SuitDark=suitfab`, `fparms:OF_Plate=suitplate`**, four slots bound, repeat 2.0 and 2.5 (tiled, not a stretched card), NoColorSpace, aoMap channel 0, `OF_Skin` and `OF_Glass` asserted STILL FLAT, 20 materials, 61 draw calls of 150. **ALSO RECORDED: a probe that grabs the canvas with `toDataURL` after an `await` writes a BLANK WHITE PNG**, because the drawing buffer is not preserved and the read lands in a later task than the draw. **(7) THE PER-PART CHANNEL: INHERITED, AND NOT NEEDED.** RN-491 to RN-498 landed mid-pass. `PlayerRig` does not merge, so the body already draws as 8 real materials and the arms as 6, each with its own authored roughness and metalness: the named use case is satisfied by construction. It matters only if the body is ever merged to cut 8 draws to 1. **(8) THE PRICE.** The FP arms are the cheapest triangles in the game and it is worth writing down why: `castShadow: false`, their own scene drawn by `vmCam` in its own pass, and `ShadowRig` lights only `scenes.near`, so they appear in **zero** cascades. The body pays the opposite: 8 colour draws plus 8 per cascade with `frustumCulled = false`, so **a triangle on the body costs 4x the same triangle on the arms**. VRAM +6.55 MB. **(9) INVARIANTS.** 44 and 27 bones untouched; all 14 and 12 clip names and every published impact frame untouched (DW-34); weights 1.0000 to 1.0000; `rest_pose` 2.69e-07 and 3.36e-07. `validate_glb --all` **51/51** both ends. `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance, both player assets at 0. Both .glb rebuild byte-identical. **All 13 pre-existing family PNGs regenerate byte-identical to HEAD, compared by sha256 against the LFS POINTER OID** rather than by piping `git show` (which returns the 131-byte pointer and reports all 13 as changed: a false alarm this pass raised and cleared). `texgen check` 107 of 107, `selftest` 39 of 39. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean with a two-sided negative control red BY NAME and byte-identical after revert. Zero em dashes verified in Python per file. **(10) THE PROCESS INCIDENT, AND IT IS MINE.** **My commit `8994acb` reverted ten files and fourteen screenshots of the machines lane's `2e79a18`.** The filtered blob was built as "HEAD plus only my hunks" against the HEAD THAT EXISTED WHEN MY PASS STARTED, so it faithfully preserved every row present then and silently deleted every row that arrived afterwards. They restored it in `b9cd970`; both lanes are whole. **A filtered blob built from a stale base is a REVERT, not an omission** (Admin, `23e2461`), and the compare-and-swap on `update-ref` cannot catch it because **the CAS protects the REF and not the TREE**. The rules now: pin the base SHA once, build every blob from that exact SHA, ASSERT the tree with `diff-tree` against it before writing, re-check HEAD against the pinned SHA in the same breath as the commit, and use `write-tree` plus `commit-tree` because `git commit` refreshes the index and can substitute a working-tree blob. This entry was itself re-spliced onto a pinned HEAD after the working-tree copy was found to revert two other lanes' headers, which is the same defect caught BEFORE the commit rather than after. **(11) NOT DONE, NAMED.** The body's own form pass (straps, harness hardware, tool loops, asymmetry, boot tread) is untouched. `Suit` at D8D3C6 is a pale bone white that ART-DIRECTION.md's high-value clause names; the proposed replacement is **B9B2A0** (luma 232 to 180, still clearly the lightest thing on the suit and still separated from section 2.1's groundNear 35 to 55, but no longer paper), raised for Admin because three lanes read that hex. A webbing family is approved and needs new additive `of_lib.PALETTE` roles. `render_player.py`'s pose-sheet mode is overexposed and mis-framed. `texgen.py --only <family>` now exists (machines lane, RN-559) but does NOT refresh `roles`/`flat_roles`, so a role move still needs a clean-tree full build. Commits `7f07eeb`, `8994acb`, `d0393bf`. Screenshots `RN641_fp_before_fp_idle_f0_{fp,fpcrop}`, `RN646_fp_after_fp_idle_f0_fp`, `RN646_body_after_rest_f0_{p34,pchest}`, `RN647_fp_sheet`, `RN648_ingame_fp{,_pitched}`.

> **RN-681 to RN-690 (2026-08-01): THE SHADOW-CASCADE LOD. THE 4x IS REAL, THE RULE IS DERIVED FROM EACH CASCADE'S OWN TEXEL RATHER THAN PICKED, AND IT BUYS 1.55% AND NOT 87%, BECAUSE THE BIG MACHINES' AUTHORED `_LOD1` IS TOO CRUDE TO BE ADMITTED TO ANY CASCADE.** The brief arrived with an arithmetic to verify rather than inherit (`2276 + 192 + 2x48 = 2564` against 2368, "+0.65% instead of +22.4%, a factor of 34"). It does not survive measurement, and the reason it does not is the whole value of this pass. **(1) RN-681, THE RULE, MEASURED AT BOTH ENDS.** A cascade may draw the CRUDEST tier `L` for which `dev(G,L) <= 1 texel`. `texel(c)` is published by `ShadowRig.update` out of the same `(2 * r) / mapSize` it already computes for texel snapping, so there is exactly one copy of it: **15.47 / 56.25 / 210.94 mm** on the high tier, re-derived rather than remembered (a probe asserts cascade 0 is 15.47 to 0.05 mm, so a split or map-size change that moved it fails loudly instead of silently retiering every asset). `dev(G,L)` is the **measured** one-sided maximum distance from LOD0's surface to tier `L`'s, computed at build time in `render/ShadowLodMeasure.ts` from the merged, world-baked geometries the batch is about to hand the GPU, not from the source primitives. One texel, not two, because section 2.1 records the PCF kernel as one texel and the map already quantises every silhouette to that grid, so the error introduced is bounded by quantisation that is there anyway; two texels is the Nyquist alternative and `?shadowlodk=2` measures it rather than arguing it. **This is why a picked constant would have shipped a defect:** "cascade 0 draws LOD1" is exactly what the brief proposed, and cascade 0 is 15.47 mm while the smelter's `_LOD1` is **325 mm**. Whole-measurement boot cost 73 to 102 ms, published as `measure.ms` so it is not a hidden regression. **(2) RN-687, THE MACHINE NUMBER, which five asset lanes are blocked on and which is the opposite of the estimate.** Per-asset, maximum surface deviation of the shipped tiers, against the three cascade texels: smelter `2276/192/48` dev **325 / 396 mm**, assembler `2976/276/36` dev **640 / 400**, generator `532/140/36` dev **559 / 559**, miner `488/180/36` dev **280 / 566**, box `564/192/36` dev **126 / 300**, belt `148/72/12` dev **20 / 70**, wall `108/72/12` dev **0 / 45**. **Every one of the five big machines is refused at all three cascades and keeps the full 4.00x multiplier.** It is not one greeble: **36.6% of the smelter's LOD0 surface** sits further from `_LOD1` than cascade 1's 56.25 mm texel (p50 35 mm, p95 204 mm), and 45.8% of the assembler's. **The raise did not break this** and that matters for who owns the fix: the pre-raise 592-triangle smelter's `_LOD1` already measured **264.8 mm**, so the ladder was never shadow-safe, it was authored for screen DISTANCE and this is the first thing that ever asked it for a world-space error bound. **The lever is now numeric and it belongs to the asset lanes:** author `_LOD2` under **210.94 mm** to earn cascade 2 (smelter 9,104 to 6,876, 3.02x); author `_LOD1` under **56.25 mm** to earn cascades 1 and 2 (to 4,792, **2.11x**); cascade 0 is unreachable for a 192-triangle stand-in, because 15.47 mm on a 3.2 m machine is 0.5% of its size, so **2.11x is the floor for a three-rung ladder** and a fourth rung at roughly 600 to 800 triangles hitting 15.47 mm would reach about 1.41x. `__ofShadowLod.report()` publishes every asset's `devMM` beside every cascade's `texelMM`, so an author can read their own margin without a rendering lane in the loop. **(3) RN-685, THE DEFECT THAT MADE THE FIRST VERSION A COUNTER WITH NO IMAGE, and it is the reason the invariant table was taken before the claim was written.** `BatchedMesh.onBeforeRender` opens with `if ( ! this._visibilityChanged && ! this.perObjectFrustumCulled && ! this.sortObjects ) return;` and `setGeometryIdAt` (BatchedMesh.js:1200) does not set that flag. `MachineBatch` turns both other conditions off, so the first version swapped every machine's geometry id three times a frame and three re-read none of them: **262,958 swaps against a frame delta of +63 triangles**, where the belts alone should have paid 13,780. **The restore half would have been worse than a missing saving:** `onBeforeRender` clears the flag as it rebuilds, so after the last cascade the live ranges are the CRUDEST tier's, and the eye pass would have taken the same early-out and drawn cascade 2's mesh to the player. Both halves now invalidate explicitly, in one place, with the citation, and only when a swap actually happened so an untouched batch keeps three's early-out and stays bit-exact. **(4) RN-686, THE INVARIANT TABLE, four scenes, both ways, ONE BINARY per pair, `?shadowlod=0` as the control.** `drawCalls / programs / triangles / geometries / textures / vramMB`: **rn15** 66/43/**1,070,337**/53/104/104.2 against 66/43/**1,070,807**/53/104/104.2 (**-470, -0.044%**); **Forest** 45/55/**591,673**/53/106/104.2 against the identical row (**0, 0.000%**); **Plains** 70/44/**2,594,794**/53/106/104.2 against 70/44/**2,596,674** (**-1,880, -0.072%**); **built factory (78 buildings, 13 smelters, 65 belts)** 71/45/**961,060**/54/103/104.2 against 71/45/**976,217**/54/103/104.2 (**-15,157, -1.553%**). **Five of the six invariants do not move at any scene.** Draw calls cannot move because the tiers live inside the same `BatchedMesh`, and `info.geometries` cannot see them for the same reason: a `BatchedMesh` is one geometry however many sub-geometries it holds, so the extra rungs are about 0.3 MB of vertex and index buffer that no published counter resolves. The factory saving is **entirely belts and nodes**: 65 belts at 212 triangles each is 13,780, the nodes are about 1,400, and the 13 smelters contribute **zero**. **(5) RN-690, THE COUNTER IS AN UPPER BOUND AND THE FRAME IS THE AUTHORITY, and Forest is the demonstration.** `savedPerFrame` counts instances whose id it swapped and cannot see `perObjectFrustumCulled`, which runs after the swap. It is therefore exact for `MachineBatch` (culling off, a factory is always near) and an over-count for `NodeBatch` (culling on, a 620 m ring is mostly outside any one cascade): the built factory agrees to **5.7%**, and **Forest reports 12,274 against a frame delta of exactly zero** because every node whose tier the rule would have changed is culled out of the cascade that would have drawn it. A counter that cannot see the cull will report a saving no pixel receives, which is the same failure as (3) wearing different clothes. **(6) RN-684, `NodeBatch` gets the shadow saving ON TOP of its distance LOD**, taking the coarser of the two so a node already at LOD2 for distance is never promoted back by a near cascade. It pays little and the trees are why: `tree_conifer` Full deviates **925 mm** at LOD1 and 3,126 mm at LOD2, `tree_broadleaf`'s leaf row 1,070 mm, so the crowns are refused everywhere and only boulders and spires (58 to 110 mm at LOD1) are ever admitted, in cascade 2 only. **(7) RN-682, THE LAUNCH PAD HAS BEEN DRAWING A FIFTH CLAMP COLUMN SINCE IT SHIPPED, standing at the launch mount where the rocket goes, which is the exact thing its own comment forbids.** The test was `c === clampCol` over `root.children`; `launch_pad.glb` has ONE root node (`LaunchPad`) with everything beneath it, so `root.children` is a list of one and the identity test could never match, while `getObjectByName` searched the whole subtree and found a grandchild. Found by triangle count, not by looking: the body template gathered **2,672** where `LaunchPad_LOD0` is **2,564**, and the 108 difference is `LaunchClamp_LOD0` exactly. Now excluded by name over the whole subtree. A second bug of the same shape was introduced and caught in the same pass: an unanchored `/^LaunchClamp_LOD\d/` matches GLTFLoader's per-primitive children (`_LOD0_0`, `_1`) as well as their Group, and gathered every clamp triangle **twice** (216 where 108 is right); the pattern is anchored. **(8) RN-683, the pad's 704 triangles of dead LOD payload are now loaded and are STILL dead, and the measurement says why.** `LaunchPad_LOD1` and `_LOD2` both deviate **14,090 mm** from LOD0, because they delete the tower (`socket_status` sits at y = 27.55 m). Against a 210.94 mm cascade that is a factor of 67. **The payload is not usable as authored**, and this is failure mode 3 (a thin feature vanishing from its own shadow) at 14 metres. `LaunchClamp_LOD2` at 210 mm IS admitted to cascade 2. **(9) RN-688, THE SHADOW-QUALITY PAIRS AT GRAZING SUN.** The three ways this goes silently wrong are named in `ShadowLod.ts` BEFORE any pair was taken: a shadow edge that moves, a contact shadow that detaches so the object reads as floating, and a thin feature whose shadow disappears. All three are bounded by `dev` in world units, which is exactly what the rule caps at one texel. Pairs at Forest and Mountains, sun elevation dot **0.104** and **0.099** matched to four decimals across each pair. First round found no visible difference at either site and flagged that the intended sky control box sits under the debug overlay and was measuring live frame-timing text (mean delta 46 to 52 counts against 15 to 19 in the subject box, which is the tell); the control moved to (1100,40)-(1500,200) and a third pair added at the built factory, where the belts are the only machine whose cruder tier is actually admitted. **(10) RN-689, `attachSurface(m, 'panel')` ON THE WHOLE BATCH: PRICED AND ROUTED, NOT FORCED, which is what the brief permits when the fix costs more than one texture per batch.** Measured share of LOD0 triangles: machines **90.3% panel / 7.3% coarse / 2.3% flat**, structures 95.5/4.5/0, pad 91.5/6.7/1.8. **But the aggregate hides the real defect, which is per ASSET:** `primitive_furnace` is **96.3% coarse and 0% panel** (Rock 144 + RockDark 132 + Soil 40 of 328) and draws entirely with plate seams, and it is the first machine a player builds in survival; belt curves are 30.2% coarse (the Rubber deck) and `foundation` 33.3%. The structural cost is exact rather than estimated: three draws one call per `BatchedMesh` per pass and there are `1 + cascades` passes, so splitting a pool into `F` families costs `(F - 1) x 4` draws, i.e. **+8 per pool for panel/coarse/flat or +4 for panel/coarse**, up to +24 across the four pools on a measured 66 to 71 against a 150 target. The second cost is the one that decides it: `MachineBatch`'s slot model assumes ONE mesh, so a split has to fan a logical slot across N meshes in lockstep and touches `acquire`, `place`, `release`, `setGeometry` and the FS-40 read-backs `drawnKeyAt` and `matrixAt`. **Recommendation to Admin: option (c), split `panel` from `coarse` only and leave `flat` in `panel`** (the status chip's fragment shader overwrites `diffuseColor` and adds emissive, so a wrong normal map on 2.3% is nearly invisible), **as its own pass with its own probe**, because forcing it into a pass whose whole purpose is recovering triangles would spend the recovery on draw calls. Option (b) from the file's own comment (select per family off `aRole`) needs two normal maps, two ORMs and a second metre-scaled UV varying bound to one material, plus replacing three's `<normal_fragment_maps>` chunk; that is the ">1 texture per batch" case the brief says to route rather than force.
>
> **RN-691 to RN-695 (2026-08-01): THE NUMBER THE ASSET LANES ACTUALLY PRICE AGAINST IS THE MARGINAL MULTIPLIER, AND IT IS 4.0x TODAY FOR EVERY BIG MACHINE, 2.0x IF `_LOD1` IS RE-AUTHORED, AND 1.0x WITH A FOURTH RUNG.** Follows RN-681 to RN-690. Admin asked for the recovered budget "the way they price", and totals are the wrong shape for that question: what an author needs is **what one more triangle on LOD0 costs the frame**, which is `1 + (the number of cascades that still draw tier 0)`. **(1) RN-691, THE MARGINAL MULTIPLIER.** Raising LOD0 grows the eye pass and grows every cascade still drawing tier 0; a cascade drawing a fixed `_LOD1` or `_LOD2` does not grow at all. So: **smelter, assembler, generator, miner today = 4.0x** (tiers `0,0,0`); **belt, floor, foundation = 2.0x** (tiers `0,1,2`); **wall = 1.0x** (tiers `1,2,2`, no cascade draws LOD0 at all). In the machines lane's own units, 22 instances in a 660,385-triangle frame at a 3.0% ceiling gives 900 triangles of frame cost per instance, so the affordable LOD0 raise is **225 triangles at 4.0x** (which reproduces their number exactly, and confirms it), **450 at 2.0x**, **900 at 1.0x**. **(2) RN-692, THE ESTIMATE IS CORRECTED, AND ONE HALF OF IT IS CONFIRMED.** `2276 + 192 + 2x48 = 2564` requires cascade 0 to draw `_LOD1`; cascade 0 is 15.47 mm and that `_LOD1` is 325 mm, so it is refused, and the real figure is `2276 x 4 = 9104`, **3.55x the estimate**. The predicted "+0.65%, a factor of 34" is **+22.4%, a factor of 1.00**: the raise costs exactly what it cost before this pass, because the smelter earns nothing from it. **The +22.4% half of their arithmetic is confirmed independently and was right.** What moved is not the smelter's price, it is that the price is now a published per-asset property with three numeric targets against it. **(3) RN-693, `?shadowlodk=2` MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED, and it is a LOOK decision for Admin rather than a perf one.** At one texel the built-factory saving is **-15,157 (-1.553%)**; at two texels, the Nyquist floor, it is **-50,260 (-5.148%)**, a factor of 3.3. Almost all of the difference is one admission: the smelter's `_LOD2` at 396 mm fits cascade 2's doubled 421.9 mm budget, taking that machine from 4.0x to 3.0x marginal and its affordable raise from 225 to 300 triangles. **It ships at one texel** because one texel is the quantisation the shadow map already has and two is a claim about what the eye will accept at 80 to 300 m, which is not a claim a perf measurement can settle. The flag exists so the pair that would settle it can be taken. **(4) RN-694, THE GRAZING-SUN PAIRS, and the control box had to be moved twice.** Three pairs on one binary, sun elevation dot matched to three decimals across each (`0.104`, `0.099`, `0.096`), the factory pair matched on scene as well (`{built:78, smelters:13, belts:65}` both sides). **No visible difference at any site:** no moved shadow edge, no detached contact shadow, no thin feature losing its shadow, including at 10x and 12x amplified luminance diffs of the belt ground-contact seam, which is the only machine whose cruder tier is actually admitted to a cascade. Subject box moved 7.94% / 1.16% / 1.95% (forest / rocks / factory), all reading as silhouette-edge noise of the same character as foliage alpha dither. **The controls:** the first box sat under the debug overlay and was measuring live frame-timing text (mean delta 46 to 52 counts against 15 to 19 in the subject, which is the tell that it was glyph edges); the second sat under the "GETTING STARTED" quest panel for 22% of its area. At the third box, `(1100,200)-(1500,360)`, **rocks reads 0 pixels moved with a maximum delta of 0.7 counts, well under the 2.7-count micro-motion floor, and factory reads 0.11%.** **Forest has no valid control at any box and its subject number is discarded with its reason: at that camera every region of the frame contains foliage, which sways and alpha-dithers, so a Forest frame contains nothing static to control against.** The claim rests on the two pairs that have clean controls, and the targeted case is one of them. **(5) RN-695, what a lane should read.** `__ofShadowLod.report()` publishes `cascades[].texelMM` beside every ladder's `devMM`, so an author can read their own margin without a rendering lane in the loop, and `probes/shadowlod.js` prints the six invariants both ways. The three targets are **15.47 mm to earn cascade 0, 56.25 mm to earn cascade 1, 210.94 mm to earn cascade 2**, as maximum surface deviation from LOD0, and the ladder that hits them is not the distance ladder: `NODE_LOD1_M` / `NODE_LOD2_M` is authored for screen size, and this is authored for a world-space error bound.

> **RN-701 to RN-705 (2026-08-02): THE k QUESTION AS THREE PICTURES, because a mean cannot tell an improvement from a degradation and this one is a Reid call.** Admin's instruction after RN-696 to RN-700: turn the number into something answerable in one look. Three matched pairs, `docs/screenshots/RN700_PAIR_{beach,factory,plains}.png`, each a single file with both sides at the same scale and the question burned into the image (`tools/smoke/pairshot.mjs`, RN-701: two files is two looks, and a viewer flicking between them compares their memory of one against their view of the other, which is the worst possible way to judge a small difference). **(1) RN-702, THE FIRST TWO FRAMINGS COULD NOT HAVE ANSWERED ANYTHING AND THAT IS THE FINDING UNDER THE FINDING.** Admin asked for grazing sun at Forest and Plains, the two sites where the ratio disagreed most. Taken at sun dot 0.08 and 0.22, **both frames are essentially black below the horizon**: this project's own calibration (section 2.1) puts Forest groundNear at luma **35** and Plains at **55** AT NOON, so at 13 degrees there is no lit ground for a shadow to fall on. A pair in which neither side shows a shadow proves nothing, and four captures were thrown away discovering it. **This also explains the numbers it was supposed to illustrate.** `saturated / floor` was only 1.34 to 1.39 at those sites, i.e. the whole parameter driven to its extreme barely cleared the noise: a shadow change has almost no luminance range to move in on a ground that dark. **The site was part of the measurement and nobody had said so.** **(2) RN-703, THE FRAME THAT CAN ADJUDICATE IS BEACH**, whose groundNear luma is **167**, four to five times Forest's, with boulders at every distance throwing long legible shadows across bright sand. Sun dot 0.452 matched to three decimals across the pair, `--wind=0`, `--debug=0`, one binary, policy verified `uniform` against `perCascade` on the two sides. B costs **4,289 fewer triangles** in that frame. **(3) RN-704, THE NEAR-FIELD PAIR, AND MY OWN PROBE WAS POINTING THE WRONG WAY.** `probes/shadowlod.js` ended its build with `of.look(yaw + 180, -8)`, but the row is laid while the player BACKS AWAY from it, so it grows towards them and ends up in FRONT on the original heading. The half turn framed the empty ground the player had just retreated over. **It cost nothing in any triangle number** (the batch draws either way, `frustumCulled` is false) and silently produced a comparison screenshot with no factory in it: **a framing bug is invisible in an aggregate and fatal in a picture.** Fixed to `of.look(yaw, ...)`; the pair now shows 78 buildings, 13 smelters and 65 belts identical on both sides, with the belt line running into the frame and its ground contact visible. B costs **12,667 fewer triangles** there. This is the pair that matters most for the decision, because **within 22 m B is STRICTER than A** (11.3 mm allowed against 15.5 mm), so it is the one place the policy can visibly IMPROVE something, and no aggregate can show that: tightening removes substitutions rather than adding them, and the metric only measures magnitude. **(4) RN-705, PLAINS IS PUBLISHED ANYWAY, CAPTIONED AS WHAT IT IS.** It is where the number read worst for B (1.47x the floor) and hiding it would be choosing the flattering frame. But the caption says what the picture actually contains: almost no boulders, and the understorey grass that fills it **is not in the shadow-LOD path at all** (RN-15 took the understorey out of the shadow pass entirely), so the subject that 1.47x was measured on is barely present. **My own reading of all three, offered as a reading and not as the decision:** I cannot separate A from B at any of the three sites. That is consistent with `saturated / floor` never exceeding 1.39, and it is the reason the recommendation is now a look and not a table. **The disposition is unchanged and stays unchanged until a human says otherwise: the shipped default is uniform one texel, and the derived policy remains behind `?shadowlodpx=4`.**

> **RN-551 to RN-558 (2026-08-01): THE SMELTER END TO END, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT OUTRANKS THE ART: AT TWENTY-TWO INSTANCES TIMES FOUR PASSES, TODAY'S RENDERER CAN AFFORD 225 TRIANGLES OF DETAIL ON THIS MACHINE.** Reid: "I also want you to start skinning the other structures." Blender only, no browser, no build, no dev server; Reid's 4200 untouched. **(1) RN-551, THE INSTRUMENT, WRONG IN THREE WAYS BEFORE A SINGLE COLOUR COULD BE JUDGED.** `render_machines.py` was built for RN-372's form pass and its own note says the lighting exists only so two GEOMETRIES can be compared. The values freeze is lifted for this set, so it now has to judge a SURFACE, and it (a) rendered under **Blender's AgX** while the game is ACES at exposure 1.2 and contrast 1.45, which is RN-456's finding ported rather than rediscovered (`setup_view_transform` prints its transform every run); (b) lit with a **flat 0.20/0.23/0.27 world at strength 1**, half the light in the frame arriving from every direction, which fills every crease a normal map spends its budget darkening: an ambient that bright IS a matte look; (c) stood the machine on a **0.20 neutral floor** when section 2.1 puts groundNear at luma 35 to 55 in soil at HSV saturation 0.25 to 0.35, and a machine bounces its floor. `--maps` / `--merged` now wire the shipped families through `surface_preview`, so a pair is ONE FLAG apart on ONE build under ONE light. **(2) RN-552, THE SMELTER FORM, 592 to 2,276 AT LOD0.** A shaft furnace is lined with refractory that grows when hot, so it is held in COMPRESSION by **tie rods** running the full height of every corner with a bearing plate and a nut at each end: that one detail is what makes a box read as a FURNACE, nothing else in the machine set has it, and it costs three boxes per corner. Brick posts laid in **courses** instead of extruded. The firebox stops being a television: a **1.376 m2 emissive slab** flush in a flat wall became a hinged, dogged, bolted casting with a **0.083 m2 peep hole and a sight strip**, seventeen times smaller and reading hotter for it. A launder with **cheeks and frozen slag on its lip**, a charging hood leaning the opposite way to the launder so intake and outlet differ by SHAPE from across the base, a hopper vibrator, a ladder to a bracketed landing, a louvre bank, a hatch, gauges, plate courses, kick plates with one dented, anchor pads, and a stack with a cleanout and a spark arrestor. **THE 0.30 m CLEARANCE IS THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT AND THE GATE ENFORCED IT TWICE:** all three 4 m machines share BODY 3.40, so a body face has 0.30 m where the assembler had 0.40, and `machine_form.junction` stacks a `plate` on a `housing` to reach **0.305**, so A JUNCTION BOX DOES NOT FIT ON A 4 m MACHINE'S WALL and lives on the roof; separately `assert_inside` refused the first blower with **"Y high by 0.06000"**, which `finned_drum`'s own docstring predicts (a drum reaches out by its radius and its fins by half their span, so the two solve together against the hard edge, and the number that moves is the span). **(3) RN-553, `panel` GETS A REAL ROUGHNESS BAND AND THE GAME'S SECOND TILING ALBEDO.** Section 2.1 item 4 measured this family at **0.032** and called it "the plastic read on every machine, plate and suit"; the suit has since moved to `suitfab`/`suitplate`, so **panel is now the industrial family and nothing else**, which is why it is RE-AUTHORED rather than duplicated under a new name: a `paint` family beside it would have been the same surface twice, and the brief's instruction to name new families distinctly was written before that move landed. **The 0.032 was arithmetic, not an accident:** the old mask was `1.0 - 0.28 * proud + (mottle - 0.5) * 0.22` and `proud` is non-zero only on a rivet top, so every flat plate in the game was 1.0 +/- 0.11. **THE THRESHOLDS ARE WHERE A PERCENTILE BAND LIVES, NOT THE COEFFICIENTS, and the first tuning got that backwards:** an `_fbm` clusters about its middle, so a 0.34 coefficient over a `(x - 0.34) / 0.52` knee still measured p05 0.686 for a Steel band of **0.136**, under the floor; moving the knee to 0.28 changes how MANY texels are wiped rather than how far. Measured effective, with a new scratch instrument because **nothing checked in measures the rule that governs whether a family is acceptable** (`check_maps` asserts a 16-count spread over the full range, which is not a percentile band and never multiplies by the palette constant): **Steel 0.032 to 0.184, SteelDark 0.224, Accent 0.204, Hazard 0.245, SuitAccent 0.224. SteelLight is 0.143 and still UNDER, and that is a PALETTE fact not a map fact:** its constant is 0.35 and an ORM channel can only multiply downward, so clearing it needs a palette edit that would re-surface the rocket parts and the tools inside a machine commit. Left, named. The albedo carries **pigmentation at 4 to 38 cm and oxide hue in one direction only** (rust is not a colour that has an opposite), never reads `height` because RN-454's split gives the heightfield the seams and the normal, and **is centred at 0.55 rather than 1.0 because the first build measured mean 0.9659 with a per-channel range of 194..255, i.e. the variance it exists to carry was CLIPPING against the byte ceiling**; `check_maps` refuses a tiling albedo outside 0.15..0.85 and the refusal is correct. **(4) RN-554, THE COST, AND IT IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE ART.** `MachineBatch.ts:195` selects `_LOD0` only, never switches by distance, and `:211` casts into three CSM cascades, so every machine triangle is drawn FOUR times. **The tree lane's 81.9% does NOT transfer, and the brief's hope that it would is corrected here:** `NodeBatch` paid that because 2,226 trees sat in a 620 m ring, whereas `MachineBatch`'s own comment says the factory is always within tens of metres, and portcost measures the whole factory at **71,184 of a 660,385-triangle frame with terrain at 87%**. **But the multiplicity transfers the other way, and it is worse:** portcost's 79-building reference base is **22 smelters and no assembler at all**, so this raise is `22 x 1684 x 4 = 148,192 triangles, +22.4% of that frame` and 5.5% of StatsProbe's 2.7M ALERT, against accepted precedents of 1.4% (assembler), 1.7 to 3.0% (rocks) and 1.9 to 3.4% (spider). **At the rock lane's 3.0% ceiling the entire affordable raise for this asset is 225 TRIANGLES, which is one greeble: under LOD0-everywhere the machine set cannot be detailed at all, and no amount of trimming changes that conclusion.** The prerequisite is named and priced: LOD1 (192) and LOD2 (48) already ship and nothing selects them, exactly as RN-243 found for the harvest nodes, and **a cascade at section 2.1's own measured 15.47 mm per shadow texel cannot resolve a 30 mm bolt head, so casting LOD0 into three of them is drawing detail that is unresolvable by construction.** With the cascades drawing the LODs already in the file the same raise costs `2276 + 192 + 2x48 = 2564` per smelter against today's 2368, **+4,312 over the reference base and +0.65% of the frame: the fix is worth 34x on this one asset.** Client-side, escalated to Admin, deliberately not taken here. **(5) RN-555, THE COPLANAR GATE EARNED ITS KEEP AND THE SECOND FIX RE-COMMITTED THE CAUSE.** 12 new same-facing pairs, attributed exactly and summing: **8 tie rods whose foot was written `TIE_Z0 = PLINTH_H`** on the reasonable-sounding grounds that a rod stands on a foundation, which is where the painted skirt's underside also is; **4 slag blocks starting at the same 0.30**. Both are RN-411's catalogued cause verbatim, a part whose extent lands exactly on its host's boundary plane. Fixed by burial, DERIVED from the plinth rather than typed; the rebuild then read **2**, because burying the slag in Z said nothing about Y and its front face still landed on the launder floor's own -1.98. **Back to 0 across 1,684 added triangles**, held there by absence from the allowance table. RN-443's lesson restated: the person who most recently proved they understand a defect is not immune to it. **(6) RN-556, TWO LANES LAUNDERED EACH OTHER'S WORK THROUGH ONE ALL-OR-NOTHING GENERATOR, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, ON ONE AFTERNOON.** `texgen.generate()` loops every entry in `FAMILIES` and rewrites the whole manifest, and the working tree held **two uncommitted families belonging to the player lane** (`suitfab`, `suitplate`, 525 lines, absent from HEAD). Regenerating in place would have written their in-flight work into the shipped bytes: RN-151's exact defect. I generated to a scratch directory instead and added **`OF_TEX_DIR`** to `surface_preview`, which previews an unshipped set and PRINTS on every run that it is doing so. **Then it happened to me from the other side:** `assets/textures/dist/of_panel_orm.png` and a NEW `of_panel_a.png` appeared in the working tree **byte-identical to my scratch build**, written by the sibling lane's own regeneration picking up my uncommitted `_panel_masks`. `of_panel_n.png` is untouched, which is the control confirming my edit was confined to ORM and the new albedo. **Neither lane can now commit `assets/textures/dist` without laundering the other's rows.** The structural fix is a per-family WRITE path (`--only <family>`, updating only that family's manifest rows); `OF_TEX_DIR` addresses only the READ side. Flagged to Admin, not built here. **(7) WHAT THE CLIENT ACTUALLY DRAWS, AND THE STUDIO PREVIEW IS MORE GENEROUS THAN IT.** `MachineBatch.makeMaterial` pins **`attachSurface(m, 'panel', ...)` on the whole batch**, so the smelter's `Rock` brick and every belt's `Rubber` deck draw with plate seams and rivets regardless of `ROLE_FAMILY`. That is ASSET-SPECS 2.9 option (a), recorded and unchanged; `surface_preview` wires per role, so my studio pair shows the brick as stone and the game will show it as plate. Stated here rather than discovered later. Also **`Rubber` is on `coarse`**, which is rock fracture on a conveyor belt, and it is used by the belt scripts and nothing else, so splitting it off is a safe exclusive move exactly as the player lane's `suitfab` was. **(8) INVARIANTS: `validate_glb --all` 51/51, with all 18 machine and structure assets rebuilt BYTE-IDENTICAL before any edit, which is what makes "before" a measurement rather than a file date; `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance on all 51 with the smelter at 0 same; `texgen check` 111 of 111 including four NEW panel tiling-albedo checks (varies 64 counts, mean 0.5386 equal to the shipped bytes, level in range); `contracts.json` verified row-wise against `git show HEAD:` with ONLY the smelter row differing, edited as literal text and never through a `json.load`/`json.dump` round trip, and never with `git checkout --`; real index verified empty before and after; zero em dashes verified in Python per file.** **NOT DONE, NAMED:** everything except the smelter, which is the miner, box, four belt tiles, generator, power pole, inserter, the five structures, `survival_smelter` and `primitive_furnace`; **`electric_smelter` IS NOT AN ASSET and never was**, because `FactoryKinds` maps `esmelter` to TypeId 0x12 and it reuses the smelter's art, so skinning the smelter skinned it and the brief's list was one item long in error; the television defect is **SYSTEMIC** and the box, miner and generator each still carry a large white emissive slab, visible in the line shot; the in-game look is unverified and owed; and **`panel`'s AO floor of 0.42 darkens flat plate to a p50 of 0.710**, which puts a machine near section 2.1's own groundNear luma, measured here and deliberately not changed. Screenshots `RN551_before_smelter_{a,b,face,det}` against `RN552_after_smelter_*` (form, one build apart under one light) and `RN553_skin_smelter_*` (skin, one flag apart), plus `RN553_line_{before,after}_line`.

> **RN-641 to RN-647 (2026-08-01): THE PLAYER PASS PART 1. THE HANDS WERE SIZED AGAINST A FIELD OF VIEW THE CLIENT DOES NOT USE, THE PLAYER HAS NEVER WORN A SURFACE MAP AT ALL, AND TWO OF THE THREE GEOMETRY VERSIONS WERE KILLED BY LOOKING AT THEM.** **(1) RN-641, THE FOV, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART IN THIS PASS.** `ASSET-SPECS 4.2` says the client runs "roughly 70 degrees" vertical; `render_check.py`'s `eye` view and `rig_common.py`'s entire hand-distance argument are both derived from it. **`CameraRig.ts` constructs every camera INCLUDING `vmCam` at `fovDeg = 60`, and `setFov` has no caller anywhere in `web/src`.** At the authored 0.62 m hand distance the visible frame height is `2 * 0.62 * tan(30) = 0.716 m`, not the 0.868 m the 2026-07-27 sizing comment computed, so **the hands are 21 per cent larger in frame than the arithmetic that placed them believed**, on the one asset whose only recorded complaint was ever that it was too big. `render_player.py` is a new rig that cannot repeat it: the lens is **DERIVED** from a vertical FOV with `sensor_fit` pinned to `VERTICAL`, because Blender's `AUTO` fit follows the LARGER output dimension while `three.PerspectiveCamera.fov` is always vertical, so an `AUTO` lens silently reframes when the resolution changes. It prints its transform every run (RN-456's rule), which matters because **`render_check.py` sets no view transform at all and therefore renders every asset in this project under Blender 5.0's AgX.** **(2) RN-642, THE TEN COPLANAR PAIRS, BOTH CAUSES, TO ZERO, AND THE ROW REMOVED.** Attributed by reading the shipped bytes, not the source: `OF_EmissiveState`/`OF_Plate` at glTF Z = 0.2000 x2, and `OF_Plate`/`OF_Suit` at Y = 1.8000 x8. **Cause one:** the chest state light is 0.020 deep centred at y -0.190 and the pack it sits on is 0.095 deep centred at -0.1525, so both front faces are at -0.2000 exactly; two front faces on one plane, both surviving the cull, and the one that loses the coin toss is the state light, which is the part of a chest pack a player actually reads. Centred at -0.196 it is proud by 6 mm and still 14 mm buried at the back. **Cause two is the interesting one:** the comm fin's top face was authored to land exactly on the helmet crown at z = 1.800, with a comment explaining that this is what keeps the declared 1.80 m height bone-and-crown driven. **That reasoning was correct about the envelope and simply never required the two surfaces to TOUCH.** Dropped 3 mm; `dims_xyz_m` is unchanged to the digit at both ends. `ALLOWED["player/player_body"] = 10` is deleted and the asset is now held at 0 alongside the four clean machines and the three rocket assets. **(3) RN-643/644, TWO FAMILIES, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF EACH FAILED ITS OWN GATE.** `Suit`, `SuitDark` and `Plate` are used by `build_player_body.py`, `build_player_fp_arms.py` and `build_armour_set.py` **and by nothing else in the repo**, checked by grep before anything moved, which is what makes re-pointing them a player-only act. They were on `panel`, and panel encodes MANUFACTURE OUT OF PLATE (seams, rivet rows, a weld bead), which is the wrong FACT about a woven garment in exactly the way `coarse` was the wrong fact about bark. **`suitfab`** (512 px, 0.5 m tile, 1024 px/m) is a plain weave built from two crossed `_hair_layer`s, because a weave is two anisotropies at right angles and `_hair_layer` is the only anisotropic primitive in the file; the over-under checker at the thread pitch is what makes a highlight run ALONG a yarn instead of sitting on a lattice of dots. **`suitplate`** (384 px, 0.4 m tile, 960 px/m) is brushed grain, micro-pitting, shallow dings and eleven authored scratch segments, with ONE wear mask driving albedo, roughness and metalness together. **THE MEASUREMENTS, all against section 2.1 item 4's 0.15 requirement and panel's measured band on the same roles:** effective roughness `panel x Suit 0.046` and `panel x Plate 0.030` **to `suitfab x Suit 0.224`, `suitfab x SuitDark 0.242`, `suitplate x Plate 0.268`**; effective **metalness `suitplate x Plate 0.456`**, which is the answer to section 2.1's complaint that "the only varying metalness in the game is 8.9 per cent of one map". Normal mean tilt `suitfab 21.7 deg`, `suitplate 16.8 deg`, against panel 10.5 and bark 22.8. **(4) THE TWO GENERATOR MISTAKES, BOTH WORTH THE SPACE.** *Summing independent noise fields NARROWS the distribution*, which is the central limit theorem doing exactly what it says. suitplate v1 summed four fields into its wear mask and measured an effective metalness band of **0.074, WORSE than the 0.406 of the panel family it was replacing** and a straight regression on the single number the family was built to fix. Paint does not thin, it CHIPS: the physically right shape is bimodal, so the mask is now a `_smoothstep` whose knee is **centred on the raw median**, measured rather than guessed (a knee at 0.20..0.46 against a raw median of 0.25 left 95 per cent of the plate fully coated and the roughness band at 0.115). Second: *amplitude and pitch are not independent*. suitfab v1 ran a 5 mm thread pitch at 0.62 of the height and **rendered as knitted wool**, because at 5 mm a player counts twenty-four threads across the back of a glove and anything countable reads as hand-knitting. 3.3 mm at 0.34 of the height, with the normal strength down 17 to 2.4, is a weave you sense without resolving. **(5) RN-645/646, THE FORM, AND THE RENDER KILLED TWO VERSIONS.** The cuff was a 150 mm `OF_SuitAccent` tube, and SuitAccent is 2E7DBE, a saturated primary that under the corrected framing is the largest block of colour in the first-person frame; it could NOT be re-surfaced out of the problem because SuitAccent is shared with `rocket_common.py` and the lander. **A pressure glove attaches with a metal locking ring**, so it is one now (OF_Plate, two courses, three bayonet lugs at 40/160/280 degrees rather than four on the axes), and the accent survives as a 6 mm index stripe: same silhouette, one hundredth of the saturated area. The knuckle guard was one unbroken 9 cm slab, which has one silhouette and one specular and is why it read as a chrome tile glued to a mitten. **Version one of the replacement authored four plates at 12.5 mm half-width against 10.5 mm half-thickness with the long axis running forward: near-round forward-pointing wedges, which from a camera sitting behind the hand rendered as CLAWS and were worse than what they replaced.** **Version two added a dark carrier strip for them to be riveted to, authored WIDER than the plates meant to hide it and tapering to a point, which rendered as a dark triangle down the back of the glove.** Version three is four flat pads at 3.6:1 aspect on `seg=8` (an octagon puts one broad facet parallel to the back of the hand where a hexagon puts two at a slant, so the plate catches light instead of reading as a dark chip) and no carrier at all. **A part authored to explain another part is only worth its triangles if it is actually hidden, and "mostly hidden" is a claim that has to be rendered rather than assumed.** **(6) THE FINDING THAT CHANGES THE SLICE: THE PLAYER HAS NEVER WORN A MAP.** `PlayerRig.ts` does not import `Surfaces.js` and there is **no `attachSurface` call for the player anywhere in the client**; the four consumers are `PropLibrary`, `MachineBatch`, `NodeBatch` and `SpiderFlock`. `surfaces.json` has said `Suit -> panel` since DW-35 and `surface_preview.py` honours it, so every studio render of this asset since then has shown maps **the game does not apply**. The client half is therefore owed and is the first item of part 2. Priced at **zero DW-10 slots** (stock `MeshStandardMaterial`, shared textures; the ledger stands at 4 ShaderMaterial + 3 hooks against a cap of 5). **(7) THE PER-PART CHANNEL: INHERITED, AND NOT NEEDED HERE.** RN-491 to RN-498 landed while this pass ran, so `PartMaterial.ts` is committed. **The player does not need it.** That mechanism exists to recover per-part roughness, metalness and a `bare` flag through a MERGE, and `PlayerRig` does not merge: it adds `gltf.scene` verbatim, so the body already draws as 8 real materials and the arms as 6, each with its own authored roughness and metalness. The named use case (fabric against a glass visor against metal fittings) is already satisfied by construction. It becomes relevant only if the body is ever merged to cut 8 draws to 1, which is a separate trade. **(8) THE PRICE, MEASURED.** FP arms **1,740 to 2,284 triangles against an UNCHANGED 2,600 budget: no raise requested**. The arms are the cheapest triangles in the game and it is worth writing down why: `Avatar.ts` creates them with `castShadow: false`, they live in `scenes.viewModel` drawn by `vmCam` in its own pass, and `ShadowRig` adds shadow-casting lights to `scenes.near` only, so they appear in **zero** of the 1 or 3 cascades. 6 draws, one pass. The body pays the opposite: 8 colour draws plus 8 per cascade, and `frustumCulled = false` means it is never culled out of any of them, so **a triangle on the body costs 4x what the same triangle costs on the arms**. Body LOD0 unchanged at 2,320. VRAM: suitfab 4.19 MB + suitplate 2.36 MB = **+6.55 MB**. **(9) INVARIANTS.** 44 bones and 27 bones untouched; all 14 and all 12 clip names untouched and every published impact frame untouched (DW-34); skin weights **1.0000 to 1.0000** on both; `rest_pose` 2.69e-07 and 3.36e-07; `anim_t0` green on 14 and 12 clips. **`validate_glb --all` 51/51 before and after.** `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance with **both player assets at 0 same**. **Both .glb rebuild byte-identical**, and all 13 pre-existing family PNGs rebuild byte-identical under the texgen additions, which is what proves the regeneration launders nothing. Clip binding proved by picture: `RN647_fp_sheet` is six frames of FP_Swing_Pickaxe as baked copies, all six distinct, none in rest and none collapsed to origin. Zero em dashes verified in Python per file. Commit `7f07eeb`. **(10) NOT DONE, NAMED, AND ONE OF THEM IS A CROSS-LANE BLOCK.** **`texgen.py` CANNOT BE COMMITTED BY THIS LANE**: it carries the industrial lane's uncommitted `_panel_albedo` (RN-553) alongside my two families, texgen has **no per-family build** and rewrites `surfaces.json` and every PNG wholesale, and their family currently fails texgen's own level check at mean 0.9659 against the 0.15..0.85 rule. The two suit families are measured, working and sitting in the working tree unstaged, and Admin has to sequence the two lanes. **The verification gap that let it happen is worth recording: I checked that `of_lib.py` was safe at the START of the pass and did not re-check `texgen.py` immediately BEFORE the write, and the other lane landed in between.** The check has to be adjacent to the write, not to the start of the pass. **Also open:** the client `attachSurface` for the player (item 6); the body's own form pass (straps, harness hardware, tool loops, asymmetry, boot tread) which is untouched; `Suit` at D8D3C6 is a pale bone white that ART-DIRECTION.md's "pastel or high-value palettes" clause arguably names, but the fix is an `of_lib.PALETTE` hex and that file is shared; a webbing family needs NEW roles and therefore an of_lib edit, raised for Admin rather than taken; `render_player.py`'s pose-sheet mode is overexposed and mis-framed (the subject is judgeable, the lighting is not); and no in-game frame has been taken, so the LOOK is verified in Blender only. Screenshots `RN641_fp_before_fp_idle_f0_{fp,fpcrop}`, `RN646_fp_after_fp_idle_f0_fp`, `RN646_body_after_rest_f0_{p34,pchest}`, `RN647_fp_sheet`.

> **RN-601 to RN-621 (2026-08-01): THE ANIMATION PASS. THE BITE THAT HAS BEEN OWED SINCE THE CREATURE WAS BUILT, AN IMPACT FRAME PUBLISHED AS AN OFFER BECAUSE NOTHING EXISTS TO READ IT, AND A WALK WHOSE PLANTED FEET WERE BEING DRAGGED BY THE BODY'S OWN SWAY.** Reid: "work on the spider animations." **(1) RN-601 to RN-612, `Spider_Bite`, 0.7000 s, 43 authored frames, IMPACT AUTHORED FRAME 22 = RUNTIME TICK 21 = 0.3500 s.** Published in the vocabulary `web/src/player/AnimGraph.ts` already uses for the player (`SWING_CLIPS[kind].impactTicks`), and printed by `build_spider.py` on every build so it lands in a build log rather than only in a doc that can go stale. **THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS: IT IS PUBLISHED, NOT READ.** `EnemySwarm.step` charges `damagePerSecond * dt` on every one of the 60 ticks a second that `Creature.biting` is non-empty; there is no windup, no telegraph, no per-attack cooldown and no discrete bite event anywhere in `enemies.h` or the client, so **there is nothing in the sim for an impact frame to gate today** and the number is an offer in the shape combat would need, not a reading of a requirement. The clip's shape follows `build_player_body._swing` deliberately (wind at `impact - 10`, settle at `impact + (n - impact)//3`): a player's pickaxe and a spider's fangs are the same event seen from the two ends, and a second authored shape for it would be a second authority on what a strike looks like. **THE AMPLITUDE IS 15 DEGREES OF HEAD AGAINST 7 OF THORAX, and that ratio is structural rather than taste**: all 24 leg bones are children of `Thorax`, so a degree of thorax pitch drags eight coxa heads through an arc about the thorax pivot and **no rotation downstream can undo a parent's TRANSLATION**, while `Head` is the only bone on the rig with no leg children and is therefore free. The brace pairs cancel the thorax pitch by negating the very list that authored it, which is `clip_idle`'s mechanism and means the two cannot drift. **TWO CORRECTIONS THE RENDERS CAUGHT, NEITHER OF WHICH A REST POSE COULD SHOW:** the first strike drove the head 34 degrees down, which rotates the chelicerae UNDER the carapace, so the fangs were plainly visible on the wind-up and hidden at the one frame that has to read as contact, and it was wrong about the target as well, since a spider bites a player or a building and both stand at roughly its own height; and the first version put the front pair back on the ground at the impact frame, and **eight legs on the floor is the pose of a creature doing nothing**, so they now reach furthest forward exactly when the fangs land, because a spider pins with the front pair and bites what it is holding. **THE FANGS DO NOT OPEN**, stated as a rig limit rather than left to be discovered: the chelicerae are geometry weighted to `Head` and there is no bone to spread them with. An L/R chelicera pair is the one place two more bones would buy something a clip cannot, and it is priced and not taken. **(2) RN-613 to RN-618, THE WALK, AND THE DEFECT WAS REAL.** `Spider_Walk` authors its stance as `rig_common.ramp`, a straight line, because a planted foot must travel backward at CONSTANT speed: that docstring calls a sine "the skate" and the whole clip was built around avoiding it. **The shipped bytes did not do that. Instantaneous stance speed swung 1.7936 to 3.2200 m/s about a mean of 2.5096, a worst deviation of 0.7222 m/s or 28.89% of the declared 2.5, INSIDE a window that is supposed to be constant.** **WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT: the MEAN was right the whole time, to 0.4%.** Every check that existed divides one arc by one duration, and **no arithmetic of that shape can see a ripple inside the window it is averaging over.** This is DW-34's shape again, an authoring defect that no render shows and only the exported curve reveals. **THE MECHANISM, ATTRIBUTED RATHER THAN CORRELATED:** `clip_walk` yaws the Thorax +/-2 degrees for body sway, and because every leg is a child of Thorax that sway was added to all eight feet whether or not they were standing on the ground. The Thorax yaws a measured +/-1.9979 degrees once per cycle, a peak rate of 0.27386 rad/s, and the four feet stand 2.1917, 2.3240, 2.5009 and 2.6171 m from the thorax head, predicting peak drags of 0.6002, 0.6365, 0.6849 and 0.7167 m/s against **measured 0.6076, 0.6446, 0.6924 and 0.7222: agreement within 1.2% across four different lever arms.** A perturbation proportional to distance from a pivot IS a rotation about that pivot, so the mechanism is identified rather than correlated, and it is the same sentence DW-34 already had on file: the foot path was right and the hip was moving underneath it. `clip_idle` already solved this exact problem on this exact rig by cancelling the thorax sway on every coxa; `clip_walk` never did. The fix is that cancellation, through ONE function both readers call, because two independent restatements of one body motion is how the drag arrived. **RESULT: worst intrinsic slip 28.89% to 5.73%, i.e. 0.7222 to 0.1434 m/s. Per leg: 24.31 to 1.14, 25.79 to 2.64, 27.70 to 4.52, 28.89 to 5.73.** Everything the gait is supposed to be is unchanged: **alternating tetrapod holds by the strict definition** (two clusters exactly 0.5000 apart, observed groups `{L1,L3,R2,R4}` and `{L2,L4,R1,R3}`), duty 0.5333, **feet on the ground never fewer than 4 of 8**, mean stance speed 2.4952 to 2.5059 against a declared 2.5. The 5.73% residual is named and left standing rather than tuned away. **A HYPOTHESIS TESTED AND REFUTED, recorded because the next reader will have it too:** that `pose_clip`'s default BEZIER handles were bowing the straight ramp. Rebuilding the walk with `interpolation="LINEAR"` produced a **byte-identical .glb** and identical measurements to four decimals, and the change was reverted rather than left standing as a no-op wearing a comment that claims a fix. **(3) RN-619, THE INSTRUMENT, `tools/blender/gait_check.py`, NEW, AND IT WAS WRONG FIRST.** It opens the shipped .glb and reports duty factor, stance speed, slip, gait clustering, feet-on-ground, inter-leg clearance and loop closure, with **no tuned threshold anywhere that turns a measurement into a verdict**, and the two places a judgement is unavoidable print the observed sets beside the literal definition being applied. Version one read `pose_bone.tail` for the Tibia and reported the foot at **z = 1.04 m when the authored tip is at 0.02**: glTF has no bone tails, it stores joints as nodes, and **Blender's importer SYNTHESISES a tail for every leaf joint.** Four numbers said so at once (duty 0.5583 against an authored 0.5000, stance phases at 0.4667 rather than 0.5000, implied speed 1.4880 against a declared 2.5, and **a stance direction that REVERSED mid-stance, which a planted foot cannot do**), and 1.4880/2.5 * 2.10 puts the tracked point at radius 1.55 m, between the knee and the foot, which is exactly a mid-bone invented tail. It now picks the foot vertex out of the DEFORMED MESH, per leg, by greatest horizontal radius among the vertices that leg's Tibia dominates, and **prints the chosen index and its rest position so the choice is auditable**. It deliberately does NOT copy `FOOT_R`, `FOOT_Z` or the azimuth table out of `build_spider.py`, because a control whose geometry is a copy of the thing it watches is this project's catalogued failure and it fails in the direction that looks like a real defect. Rest-tip check after the fix: radius 2.4061 against an authored 2.40, z 0.0219 against an authored 0.02. **(4) RN-620 to RN-621, THREE FINDINGS FOR OTHER LANES.** **(a) The recorded "31% foot slip" is NOT this file's defect and no Blender edit repairs it.** It is `SpiderFlock.ts` clamping `timeScale` at `WALK_TIMESCALE_MAX` 2.2 against a Skitterer that needs `6.0 / (2.5 * 0.75)` = 3.2, and 2.2/3.2 is exactly the 31.25% recorded. **It is the ONLY type that slips**: Ravager 2.000, Lancer 1.676, Sunderer 1.344 and Colossus 1.295 all sit under the clamp, so the whole trade falls on the early-game swarm type, which is the creature a player sees most and in the largest numbers. **A faster clip cannot help**, because the client already multiplies cycle rate by `timeScale`, so a clip that is only "the walk at half the duration" produces a bit-identical pose sequence at the same wall rate. Only a longer STRIDE buys ground speed at a readable leg rate, and that is bounded by adjacent same-side legs crossing at the frozen 35 degree azimuth spacing. A `Spider_Run` must therefore land in the same commit as the client line that selects it, or it is dead payload in the .glb. **(b) PER-INSTANCE CLIP PHASE OFFSET DOES NOT EXIST.** `SpiderFlock.pose` calls `want.reset()`, which sets `action.time = 0`, and nothing offsets it; there is no `mixer.setTime` anywhere in the repo. Eight rigs claimed on one tick play in exact lockstep, and because the mixers tick on a fixed dt that lockstep is deterministic rather than dithered by frame timing. The sim already staggers itself (`scanIn: made % SCAN_TICKS`); the render side has no analogue. This is the cheap half of idle variation and it is four lines in a file this pass could not take. **(c) THE BRIEF'S PREMISE ABOUT `animgate.js` IS BACKWARDS, AND THE CORRECTION IS LOAD-BEARING.** That probe proves the mixer using the **IDLE** clip's own 2.0 s cycle, not the walk, and its header argues the point carefully. **A t/t+period identity test is STRUCTURALLY UNAVAILABLE for a walk cycle on screen**: `EnemySwarm.march` has no arrival condition, so a 6 m/s Skitterer crosses 2.2 m in one walk period and the frame shares no tiles with itself, and no threshold makes it available. `probes/spidergait.js` therefore asserts the cycle property on a BITING creature, which is stationary and plays the idle, and leaves walk-cycle closure to `gait_check.py`, which reads it off a bone position exactly. **NOT DONE, NAMED: `probes/spidergait.js` is syntax-checked and has NEVER BEEN RUN against a client**, because wiring the bite into `SpiderFlock.ts` is a cross-lane change this pass did not take: that file carried 38 uncommitted lines of the fang lane's work when the pass began. **THE CLIENT HALF IS OWED AND IS ROUGHLY FOUR LINES**: a `BITE_CLIP = 'Spider_Bite'` constant beside the other two, a `bite` action on the `Rig` interface, `pose()` selecting it on `c.biting !== ''` instead of falling through to the idle, and a per-instance phase offset applied after `reset()`. Note that `probes/spiderwalk.js` asserts a stopped creature plays `Spider_Idle` and **will go red the moment the bite is wired**, which is correct and is that probe doing its job. **ALSO OPEN:** a second idle and a death or hit reaction were not reached, and both are blocked behind the same client selection; the far swarm in `EnemyArt.ts` still shares only five numbers with this asset, so **none of this animation work reaches any creature beyond the nearest eight**, which remains a client-lane follow-up nobody has taken. **INVARIANTS: 28 bones, none renamed, added or removed; no clip renamed and no clip retimed (`Spider_Walk` keeps its name and its 0.8000 s exactly, and only its CONTENT moved, which is not a contract change because the walk publishes no index); `validate_glb.py` PASS on all 21 checks including clips set-equality against `contracts.json`, `anim_t0` (all three clips start at t = 0), `bones` 28, `skin_weights` every vertex at sum 1.0000, `rest_pose` 6.46e-07 and `frame1_identity`; geometry untouched at lod0 4,346 of 4,400, total 8,038 of 8,200, materials 6 of 6, envelope [4.5233, 1.5975, 3.9470] to the digit; two builds byte-identical (b18a60ca); `of_lib.py` and `rig_common.py` were never opened, so no other asset can have moved.** **(5) RN-622 to RN-625, FOUR MORE THINGS THE INSTRUMENT FOUND, one of them mine and fixed.** **(a) MY OWN BITE PUT ITS BRACED FEET THROUGH THE FLOOR.** The first version dropped the Root 45 mm at the impact frame to sell the body's weight landing, and **in a forward-kinematic clip the root carries the FEET with it and no leg rotation downstream can hold them up**: the rear pair measured 46 to 53 mm below z = 0, about 6 cm at the drawn surface once the claw's 10 mm tube is counted, on the one clip a player watches from two metres away. The second half was the brace legs' own compression, authored as a NEGATIVE lift to read as taking the load, which in FK drives the tip down rather than settling the body on to the leg. **Fixed: the lunge is horizontal, the sense of drop is carried by thorax and head pitch (which pivot rather than translate), and femur and tibia are now opposed so the knee takes the flex and the tip stays put. Worst penetration 0.0533 to 0.0106 m at the axis, i.e. grazing at the surface; leg 3 goes from -0.0460 to +0.0003.** The braced claim now holds in both axes: rear feet travel 0.0769 and 0.0863 m against the striking pair's 0.3804. **(b) THE SUSPECTED KNEE INTERPENETRATION IS NOT THERE, and the suspicion was mine.** Adjacent same-side knees close to **0.2814 m** at the sweep extremes against a contact distance of 0.1001 m (twice a knee radius measured off the skin at 0.0500, with the authored 0.0520 printed only as a cross-check): 2.8x margin, 0.1813 m of daylight. Worth recording because it was the reason I expected the stride could not grow, and it is now measured instead of feared. **(c) ALL THREE CLIPS CLOSE THEIR LOOPS EXACTLY: every one of the 24 foot deltas between phase 0.0 and 1.0 reads 0.0000 m**, and `CLIP_FIRST_KEY_AT_ZERO` is 1 on all three, so DW-34 holds in the shipped bytes rather than in the build script's intention. The idle's feet are pinned to **0.0006 m**, sixteen times tighter than the ~0.01 m its own build print claims, because most of that 0.01 is the VERTICAL root bob and the contract is about horizontal travel. **(d) NAMED, NOT FIXED: the walk's planted feet move 45 mm VERTICALLY during stance and dip to -0.0060 m**, so the foot axis passes below the ground plane and the drawn claw is about 16 mm under it. The cause is the same Root bob (+/-0.02 m) that the sway fix does not touch, since a root translation reaches every foot equally and cannot be cancelled by rotation. It is a look-versus-contact trade rather than a defect, and it is left for whoever owns the bob. Related instrument caveat, stated because it bounds the duty number: **walk duty reads 0.5333 against an authored 0.5000 and that gap is a ground-band artifact, not the clip.** The band cannot be tightened below the 45 mm of real vertical stance travel, and sweeping it 74% moved the headline slip by only 0.0012 m/s, so the slip is not living on the knob even though the duty is. COST: the .glb grows 1,056,804 to 1,089,924 bytes, which is the bite's keyframe data and no geometry, and no draw call, triangle, material or bone count moves. Commits `b84606a`, `0d023c6`. Screenshots `RN605_spider_bite_f11_c34` (wind-up, both fangs presented) and `RN605_spider_bite_f21_c34` (impact), one clip two frames apart under one light; `RN605_spider_bite_f21_chead`; `RN605_bite_sheet`; `RN604_walk_sheet`, re-rendered because the walk changed.

> **RN-491 to RN-498 (2026-08-01): THE FANG, AND THE CHANNEL THE MERGE THREW AWAY.** Reid, on the pelt build: "the fangs appear to have the texture as well. the fangs can be solid white with a sheen". He is right, RN-465 had already flagged it as a known limitation, and the ask and the missing channel turn out to be THE SAME THING: a sheen is a specular, a specular is low roughness, and roughness is precisely what `SpiderFlock.load`'s merge discards. **(1) RN-491, THE CHANNEL, AND IT COST NO LEDGER SLOT.** `web/src/render/materials/PartMaterial.ts` bakes each source material's authored roughness, metalness and an `of_bare` flag into ONE vec3 vertex attribute `aPartMat`, exactly the way the merge already bakes colour: **the proof that per-vertex data survives that merge was always sitting one line up in the same loop.** It declares NO material and installs NO `onBeforeCompile`; it exports GLSL that `FurShader.ts` splices into the ONE hook RN-463 argued for and spent. **THE DW-10 LEDGER BOTH WAYS: it stays at 4 ShaderMaterial + 3 hooks. Built as its own hook it would have been 4 + 4, which is what RN-455 priced it at, BEFORE FurShader existed.** RN-455's price was quoted against a world that no longer holds, and re-taking that measurement instead of inheriting it is the whole saving. **(2) RN-492, THE RULE, AND IT IS NOT A FANG SPECIAL CASE.** Effective response for a part is `authored role value x family ORM channel`: the FAMILY MAP supplies the variation across the surface, the AUTHORED ROLE supplies the level. The merged material's own roughness and metalness become the denominator of that ratio, so they now decide nothing except the fallback when the channel is off, and they must be NON-ZERO, which `assertPartMatBase` checks rather than hopes. A `bare` part additionally drops the family's albedo, strand normal and tiling AO, and takes its own colour and roughness FLAT. **(3) RN-493, WHAT THE PLAYER BODY AND THE MACHINES INHERIT, WHICH IS THE POINT.** Every merged single-material asset after this one has the same shape of problem: the suit is fabric plus a GLASS VISOR plus METAL FITTINGS, a machine is painted steel plus glass plus rubber, and the part one family cannot describe is reliably the part a player looks at. A lane inherits this by calling `bakePartMat` in its merge loop and `injectPartMat` from whatever hook it already has, and authoring the response in `of_lib.PALETTE`, which every asset already uses. **No role table on the client, no name parsing, no per-asset branch:** the bare flag travels as a glTF material `extras` entry written from a Blender material custom property (`export_extras=True` was already on) and lands in `material.userData` via GLTFLoader, VERIFIED by reading the exported .glb JSON rather than assumed. **WHAT IS STILL NOT SOLVED, PLAINLY: this is one family plus an escape hatch, not per-part FAMILIES.** A suit whose fabric and whose metal each want their own tiling family needs multi-family sampling in one shader, which is a real slot and is not built. **(4) RN-494, THE FANG.** `Fang` is the first member of `of_lib.BARE_ROLES`, roughness 0.30 to **0.18** (section 2.1's 0.15 with margin, and deliberately far from the shell's 0.745..0.95: hard wet keratin against soft velvet is what makes both read), colour C9BCA2 to **D5D1C6**. **THE COLOUR WAS TAKEN FROM THE MATERIAL AND NOT FROM THE RENDER, and the first version of it was the other way round and was measurably wrong.** EDE8DC measured a fang whose MEDIAN pixel was 255: the form was gone and the specular had nothing left to be brighter than. But tuning it down against the STUDIO would have been RN-456 again, because the studio is Standard + High Contrast, which CLIPS, while the game is ACES, which rolls off. So the number comes from neither render: keratin and ivory sit at 0.60 to 0.70 diffuse reflectance and D5D1C6 is linear 0.646/0.622/0.556. **(5) RN-495, THE INSTRUMENT, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART AGAIN.** `--diag <stops>` is a new MEASUREMENT mode: exposure dropped, look removed, so the frame is a plain sRGB encode of scene radiance and the linear value can be read back out and put through the client's own ACES arithmetic. It exists because the picture mode structurally cannot answer this question: a part whose albedo is 20x the shell's clips at ANY exposure that renders the shell correctly, so a WHITE part photographs as a white slab whatever its roughness is. **Read back at -3 stops the fang is scene-linear p05 0.191 / p50 0.728 / p99 0.958 against the shell's 0.0362, and through three's ACESFilmic at exposure 1.2 that predicts display 145 / 222 / 230 against the shell's 44: unclipped, and a 5x value step.** **THE HONEST HALF: p99/p50 in display is 1.04.** A 0.65-albedo DIELECTRIC cannot show a strong specular against its own diffuse (F0 is 4%), so the sheen reads as a crisp terminator and a defined lit face rather than a hot spot. A hotter glint needs a DARKER fang, which trades away "solid white", or a real specular term, which is a slot. Priced, not built, and not claimed in a screenshot. **(6) RN-496, THE EYES, AND THEIR COST IS THE DECISION.** Reid did not ask for them. The ratio rule is mechanism-wide and therefore hands `EyeGlow` and `EyeDark` their AUTHORED 0.10 roughness for literally zero extra code, zero extra bytes and zero extra instructions, so that half is taken: the six near-black secondary eyes now read wet by a specular instead of only by value, which is the thing RN-455 wrote down as impossible. **The `bare` flag for them is NOT taken**, because that is a table row and a look change nobody asked for, and it is one row away whenever someone wants it. **NAMED AND NOT FIXED:** the studio treats the eyes as flat (no `ROLE_FAMILY` row) while the client's one material samples the fur maps on every vertex including theirs; that divergence predates this pass and the bare flag would close it. **(7) RN-497, THE STUDIO STOPS LYING BY DELETION.** `CLIENT_MERGE_ROUGH_METAL` is GONE. It existed to force one roughness and one metalness onto every role so a studio render showed what the merge could actually draw; the merge now carries them, and `authored x ORM` is exactly what `surface_preview` already wired with no forcing, **so the honest preview and the plain preview became the same thing.** What replaces it is `--nopart`, THE STUDIO'S `?partmat=0`, which restores the pre-RN-491 collapse so the before/after pair is ONE FLAG APART ON ONE BUILD UNDER ONE LIGHT instead of two commits apart: a control that only exists in the client cannot photograph the thing it controls, and Reid is in the game. `surface_preview` reads `of_lib.BARE_ROLES` rather than keeping a second copy. **(8) VERIFICATION, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT COVER.** No client, no build, no server, no probe, no browser: Reid is on 4200 and this pass never went near it. What WAS proved offline: the injection is not a no-op, run against three 0.185.1's ACTUAL shipped `meshphysical` source in node, **8 of 8 anchors hit, 0 missing, vertex +70 chars, fragment +685, and `?partmat=0` a total no-op at 0 chars on both stages**; the check has a NEGATIVE CONTROL that fires by name (one anchor broken to `roughnessmapX_fragment` gives `anchors missed: fragment:roughness`, reverted, green). **Failure mode 1 is named in the file BEFORE any measurement and is the reason that check exists: a `String.replace` whose needle is absent returns the string unchanged and reports nothing, so a dead channel looks EXACTLY like a pass that did nothing** (which is the current state, so it is the one signature that must be distinguishable). `injectPartMat` therefore counts its replacements and publishes the misses, and `partMatStats()` is published beside `furStats()` so "the hook is installed" and "the channel reached the geometry" are TWO assertions. **BOTH FLAG DEFAULTS ARE FIXTURES AND ARE ASSERTED AS SUCH:** `partmat` absent gives `flagPresent false, enabled true`, and `furState()` now publishes `flagPresent` too (RN-150: `Number(null)` is 0). **`?fur=0` REMAINS THE STOCK-PROGRAM CONTROL, AND RN-498 IS THE BUG THAT NEARLY COST IT.** The first version of this pass gated `bakePartMat` on `partmat` alone, so with `?fur=0` the hook was gone and the attribute was still written: a dead ~52 KB per-vertex buffer that no program binds, and a control that no longer matched the build before FurShader.ts existed. **It was caught by re-reading the claim I had just written in this file against the code, not by any check**, which is worth recording because nothing in the gate set could have found it. The rule is now stated where it belongs: `PartMaterial` does not read the `fur` flag, because it does not know which hook a given asset splices it into and a merged machine will have a different one, so **the CALLER bakes only when a hook that reads the attribute will actually be compiled**. `SpiderFlock` reads `furState().enabled` once before the merge loop. **THE WIND AND MOTION LAG WERE NOT TOUCHED AND ARE STILL UNVERIFIED** (RN-465); the only change near them is that a bare part no longer sways, which fixes a LATENT bug in the shipped build, because `furOut` is inferred from the normal and a fang is a three-sided cone that read as the FURRIEST thing on the creature. **INVARIANTS: 28 bones and both clips untouched; the ENVELOPE IS 1.6036 TO THE DIGIT and three.js xyz [4.5233, 1.5975, 3.9470] unchanged, because nothing geometric moved; validate 51/51 PASS, lod0 4,346 of 4,400, total 8,038 of 8,200, materials 6 of 6; `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance; `texgen selftest` 37 of 37 and `texgen check` 79 of 79 with texgen.py UNMODIFIED; spider.glb rebuilds byte-identical (md5 5e2eb445), and player_body, assembler and boulder_iron all rebuild byte-identical under the of_lib change, which is the control that the `of_bare` custom property and BARE_ROLES touch no other asset's bytes; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean with a negative control red BY NAME (TS2304 `PART_ATTR`) and silent after revert; contracts.json edited as literal text, 17 insertions 1 deletion, no round trip.** ATTRIBUTION: at c34 the whole change is a **65 x 127 px box, 0.14% of the frame**, and the rest of the creature does not move. NO SUBAGENTS: the pass was small and the machine was Reid's. Screenshots `RN491_fang_{before,after}_rest_f0_{chead,c34}` (ONE FLAG apart on one build under one light, `--nopart` being that flag) and `RN491_diag_e-3_rest_f0_chead` (the measurement frame, committed as evidence that the clip is the INSTRUMENT and not the material).
