> **RN-461 to RN-465 (2026-08-01): THE SHELL BECOMES A PELT, AND THE HALF REID ASKED FOR THAT BLENDER STRUCTURALLY CANNOT SHOW.** Reid: "make it more hairy. it looks like its made of shiny stone. it should look like it has almost like a fur. very short fur. the fur should react to movement and the wind too." **He is right and the brief was wrong, and the correction is worth more than the art:** a sharp specular highlight is the single strongest HARD SURFACE cue there is, and the WIDER the roughness band the harder a thing reads, so RN-455's measured 0.242 to 0.792 band was excellent machinery aimed at beetle carapace when the subject is a tarantula. **(1) RN-461, THE MAP, AND EVERY TERM INVERTS ONE ABOVE IT.** Family renamed `chitin` to `fur`. Roughness HIGH EVERYWHERE: effective **0.745 to 0.95**, still clearing section 2.1's 0.15 band but sitting at the TOP of the range instead of straddling the middle, so no part of the surface is near a specular. The punctate pits and bristle sockets are **GONE**: they were the right detail for a shell and were also the COARSEST thing on the map, which is backwards for a surface whose whole character is that it is finer than its neighbours. In their place `_hair_layer` lays **real, discrete, tapered STRANDS along a wobbling flow field**, two layers at 20 and 40 cells over a 0.30 m tile (15 mm and 7.5 mm apart), bucketed 3x3 the way `_worley` buckets its points so cost stays linear in texels. **Strands rather than filtered noise because every generator in that file is isotropic BY CONSTRUCTION** (worley is distance to a POINT, fbm is a lattice of scalars) **and no amount of tuning grows a direction on an isotropic field**; a highlight stretched along the flow is what fur has and felt does not. Albedo darkens at the ROOT and lifts at the TIP. **(2) RN-462, THE GEOMETRY.** 14 hand-typed 185 mm SPINES became **116 seeded 46 to 86 mm hairs** (46 abdomen, 22 carapace, 6 per leg), seated on the ring profile by arithmetic (`profile_at` / `surface_at`, roots pushed 25% inside) rather than checked by hand, which is the only way a hundred is possible: **RN-246's floating scree avoided by construction.** lod0 3,530 to 4,346 (budget 4,400), **+816 x MAX_RIGS 8 = +6,528 worst case, 0.7% to 1.2%** of RN-71's 555k to 1.0M frame; cumulative against v1, 1,060 to 4,346 is 2.6% to 4.7%. Draw calls STILL unchanged (the merge). **THE LEG HAIR RANGES SKIP THE KNEE AND THAT IS THE ADMIN CAP, NOT A PREFERENCE:** the knee apex is the highest point on the creature, the first version put a hair straight up off it and the asset measured **1.6710 against 1.6036, a 4% growth in DRAWN SIZE bought by decoration**; constrained to the femur and tibia below the knuckle, the envelope is unchanged to the digit. **(3) RN-463 to RN-465, THE MOTION, AND IT IS UNVERIFIED.** `web/src/render/materials/FurShader.ts` is **ONE new live `onBeforeCompile`** (DW-10: 4 ShaderMaterial + 2 hooks to **4 + 3** with the flora wind on), spent and argued in the RN-52 style. It carries three things a map cannot: **RIM SCATTER** (short fur reads almost entirely at the SILHOUETTE and at grazing angles, where the line of sight passes through many strands instead of off one, which is view-dependent and a texture by definition is not; added to OUTGOING radiance and not to diffuse, so it survives on the shadow side where a fuzzy edge is most visible); **WIND**, PropWind's ANALOGUE and deliberately not an extension (that hook is `#ifdef USE_BATCHING` and phases off `batchingMatrix[3]`, which does not exist on a SkinnedMesh, and its amplitude is linear in height above the prop base, which is right for a plant rooted in the ground and wrong for hair on a body) keeping its three rules: sim clock so pausing stops the breeze, phase from POSITION never spawn time, one shared hook object so the program cache key stays identical; and **MOTION LAG**, the cheap version, the flock's MEAN velocity differenced off the rig roots `SpiderFlock` already positions rather than adding a velocity to the sim for a rendering want, **with its limit written where it lives: eight creatures running different ways all stream their fur the same way, and per-rig lag needs a material clone per rig.** The displacement lands **after `<skinning_vertex>`, not after `<begin_vertex>`**: `transformed` is the BIND POSE until skinning has run, so displacing earlier makes the pelt swim against the animation. How far a vertex stands OFF the body has no attribute, so it is INFERRED from the normal, which varies slowly on the shell and points hard away on a three-sided hair. **`?fur=0` REMOVES THE HOOK ENTIRELY** rather than zeroing an amplitude, so the material keeps its stock program and the build is bit-exact to the one before this file existed: that is both the negative control and the perf isolator, and a zeroed uniform would be neither. **(4) WHAT IS NOT DONE, PLAINLY.** The static fur look IS verified in Blender and the pair is committed. **THE WIND AND THE MOTION LAG ARE UNVERIFIED**: both are functions of a clock and a velocity that exist only in the running game, Reid is playing, and no build-and-look loop was run. `furStats()` is published so the hook and the `?fur=0` BOOT DEFAULT can each be asserted in their own right (RN-150: an unexercised default ships off silently). Failure modes are named in the file header BEFORE anyone measures: fur swimming while the creature stands still is the clock off a wall timer; a pop on claim is a phase off spawn time; an even cartoon outline is the rim noise dead; a creature going black when the sun passes behind it is the rim added to diffuse. **ALSO OPEN:** the fangs wear the fur map, visible in the head close-up and unavoidable while the merge gives the creature one material, priced at RN-455 and unchanged. **INVARIANTS: 28 bones and both clips untouched; validate PASS, lod0 4,346 of 4,400, total 8,038 of 8,200, materials 6 of 6; `check_coplanar` 0 by absence; `texgen check` 79 of 79; spider.glb and all three fur PNGs rebuild byte-identical; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean with a negative control red BY NAME in both files and silent after revert.** Commit `28402f9`. Screenshots `RN462_fur_{before,after}_rest_f0_c34` (ONE FLAG apart on one build under one light), `RN462_fur_after_rest_f0_chead`, `RN462_scale_after`.

> **RN-453 to RN-459 (2026-08-01): THE SPIDER IS SKINNED, THE FIRST TILING FAMILY CARRIES AN ALBEDO, AND THREE RENDERS WERE SPENT TUNING A MAP AGAINST BLENDER'S VIEW TRANSFORM.** Reid on the v2 form: "now actually skin it". The look-dev values freeze is lifted for THIS ASSET ONLY (Admin, 2026-08-01) and stands everywhere else. **(1) RN-453, THE FAMILY, AND ITS SHAPE IS THE GENERAL THING, NOT THE SPIDER.** `chitin` is **normal + orm + a TILING ALBEDO** at metre UVs, 0.30 m tile, 384 px, opaque, mean-neutral. Every family before it is normal+orm and leaves base colour to the palette constant, on an argument (`MachineBatch` overwrites `diffuseColor` after `<map_fragment>`) that was always SPECIFIC to that hook, and ART-DIRECTION.md names flat vertex colour as a defect to unlearn. So the manifest, texgen, `surface_preview` and `Surfaces.ts` each grew the third field rather than growing a special case: `Surfaces.ts`'s load loop is now one branch PER DECLARED MAP instead of one branch per family shape, and `apply()`'s early `return` after the card path is gone. **That early return is why a family could never carry both, and leaving it would have loaded chitin as a CARD: one 0.30 m tile stretched over a whole creature, no normal, no roughness, and `hasMap` true throughout.** The player suit, the machines and the rocks take this shape unchanged. **(2) RN-454, THE FREQUENCY SPLIT, AND IT COST TWO RENDERS TO FIND.** v1 drove albedo, normal and ORM off ONE heightfield, so all three had identical frequency content: a worley at 7 cells over a 0.30 m tile is 43 mm cells at half the amplitude and the creature rendered as **a spider built out of cobblestones**. Pushing the relief fine enough to stop that ALSO deleted every feature it had at 7 m and it went **rock to dusty clay without passing through chitin**. The split that works: **ALBEDO carries pigmentation at 10 to 20 cm plus crease darkening; NORMAL carries relief below a centimetre (punctate pits and bristle sockets, an annulus with a hole in it off ONE worley field so the two stay concentric); ORM carries an effective roughness band 0.242 to 0.792** against section 2.1's 0.15 requirement and `panel`'s measured 0.032. Plate structure at 10 to 30 cm is the GEOMETRY's job and the geometry already has it. **(3) RN-455, TWO NEW ROLES, FREE.** `SpiderFlock.load` merges every `_LOD0` primitive into one geometry and bakes material colour to a vertex attribute, so a rig is 4 draw calls at 4 materials and 4 draw calls at 6. **COLOUR IS THE ONLY PER-PART CHANNEL THAT SURVIVES THAT MERGE**, so the two new roles spend it where it counts: `ChitinBand` is the dark seam under each tergite lip (a LIGHT band wide enough to see read as a tan patch glued on, which was version one of it), and `EyeDark` splits the six secondary eyes off the amber anterior pair, so an eye reads WET by value rather than by a specular the merge cannot give it. **Roughness, metalness and emissive do NOT survive: the near creature has exactly ONE roughness and ONE metalness, and the fang authored at 0.30 draws at 0.80.** The .glb states the intent; the client states the truth. **(4) RN-456, THE INSTRUMENT, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART IN THIS PASS.** The studio rig was on **Blender's AgX** with a flat 0.16 grey world and a 0.8-albedo floor while the game is **ACES at exposure 1.2, contrast 1.45, saturation 0.92**. Under AgX the chitin reads as dusty terracotta, **which is a statement about the view transform and not about the map**, and three renders were spent tuning against it. `render_creatures.setup_view_transform` now PRINTS its transform, look and exposure on every run (Standard, +0.26 stops, High Contrast: not ACES, and the docstring says so, but wrong in the same DIRECTION as the game rather than the opposite one), the world is a dim sky, the sun is 2 degrees so a shell's specular stays TIGHT, and the floor is substrate-coloured at section 2.1's own reference luma. The other half is `surface_preview.apply_all(force=...)` and `--merged`, which **force the CLIENT's single roughness and metalness onto every role so a studio pair previews what the game can draw**, because a render giving the fangs 0.30 flatters in the direction nobody double-checks. **(5) RN-457, THE MERGE.** `SpiderFlock` DELETED `uv` in the merge; a map bound to that material would have sampled 0,0 on every vertex and drawn one flat texel with every "is a map bound" check reading true. UVs now go through `Surfaces.copyUv`, which copies unconditionally (a mixed attribute set makes `mergeGeometries` return null and the `?? list[0]` swallows it, reducing the creature to its first primitive) and COUNTS. **(6) RN-458, THE PALETTE.** The creature roles moved out of `build_spider`'s runtime `PALETTE.update()` into `of_lib.PALETTE`, because texgen's own "no stale roles" gate refuses a family row for a role of_lib has never heard of: **a role that wears a surface is a first-class palette role.** `Chitin` came UP from 2B2126 to 4A3B36, and that is the single biggest value decision here: section 2.1 puts groundNear at luma 35 to 55 and **a creature at luma 36 has no separation from the ground it stands on at any sun angle.** **(7) RN-459, IN GAME: BOUND, PROVED; LOOKS RIGHT, NOT PROVED.** `probes/spiderskin.js` against a frozen build of `1b6b5b4`, one port of my own, killed immediately, Reid's 4200 untouched. **smoke PASS, 88 draw calls of 150, 8 rigs claimed. `uv.byConsumer.spider = 6`, synthesised 0, countMismatch 0: the UVs survive the merge.** The material is on `chitin` with all five slots bound, mapSize **384 not 1x1**, repeat **3.3333 = 1/0.30**, normal and orm in NoColorSpace, alphaTest 0, roughness 0.800, metalness 0.040. **Two-sided: unbinding every map moves 200 of 1296 tiles at peak 16.27 counts and changes the hash; rebinding is BIT-EXACT at 0 tiles moved.** **THE GAP, NAMED: `setMaps` unbinds EVERY family at once, so those 200 tiles are terrain, foliage, machines and creature together and cannot be attributed to chitin. Attribution needs a per-family unbind the API does not have.** **AND THE PICTURE FAILED TWICE, FOR TWO REASONS, NEITHER FIXABLE IN BLENDER: (a)** the aiming sweep is spiderwalk's "twelve headings, keep the one that moves most", which was true at RN-124 and is FALSE now because the flora lane's wind landed in between and **11,465 foliage instances move in every heading**: INSTRUMENTS.md's dominant failure, and the thing that moved was another lane's feature; **(b)** separately and NOT MINE, at `sundot 0.30` in this sandbox **the whole frame is a near-uniform pale cyan wash**, terrain, foliage and distance alike, which is not section 2.1's target and against which no creature material can be judged. `RN459_ingame_dusk.png` is committed as evidence of (b), not as a creature shot. **PROBE BUG WORTH RECORDING: `of.framehash()` returns an OBJECT and my first version compared the objects with `!==`, so "unbinding changes the frame" passed VACUOUSLY and the bit-exact check failed with `[object Object] vs [object Object]`.** The probe written to catch the vacuous-green class committed it; it now compares `.hash` and counts moved tiles. **INVARIANTS: validate PASS, materials 6 of 6, lod0 3,530 of 3,600, total 6,528 of 6,700; `check_coplanar` 0 by absence; `texgen check` 79 of 79 including a NEW tiling-albedo check (varies, level, and mean-equals-the-shipped-bytes) so a map that ships is a map that is examined; spider.glb and all three chitin PNGs rebuild byte-identical, the other four families' PNGs are untouched by the regeneration, and player_body, player_fp_arms, armour_set, a boulder, a conifer, an assembler and a prop atlas are all byte-identical under the of_lib palette addition; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean with a negative control run, `'chitinX'` red by name as TS2345 and silent again after revert.** VRAM: chitin is 3 maps at 384 px = **2.36 MB** by arithmetic. **NOT DONE, NAMED:** the in-game LOOK is unverified and owed; the far stand-in in `EnemyArt.ts` still wears none of this (RN-451's finding, client lane); a per-vertex roughness channel through the merge would give the fangs and eyes their own material response and costs one DW-10 `onBeforeCompile` slot, priced but not built; the player body and FP arms are next and inherit this family unchanged. Commits `1b6b5b4`, `3322071`. Screenshots `RN455_spider_{before,after}_rest_f0_{c34,chead,cabd}` (ONE FLAG apart on one build under one light), `RN455_scale_after`, `RN459_ingame_dusk`.

> **RN-411 to RN-443 (2026-08-01): THE VESSEL FORM PASS. THE WORST ASSET IN THE GAME WAS ONE MISTAKE MADE ONCE AND REPEATED IN ELEVEN PARTS, AND THE LANDER WENT TO ZERO WITHOUT BEING EDITED.** Blender only, no browser, no build, no dev server. Commit `263836b`. **(1) RN-411, THE ATTRIBUTION, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART IN THIS PASS.** `check_coplanar` had `rocket_parts` at **778** overlapping same-facing pairs, an order of magnitude worse than anything else in the game, baselined as a named debt at FS-75 and never looked at. In descending order of blame, summing to exactly 778: **704 (90.5%) A CAPPED TUBE ENDING ON A PLANE WHERE ANOTHER CAPPED TUBE OF A DIFFERENT MATERIAL ALSO ENDS.** `rocket_common.tube` caps BOTH ends, and every stack part is concentric capped tubes SHARING A MATING PLANE: a tank is a barrel at r 0.600 spanning z 0 to h plus a collar at r 0.625 spanning z 0 to 0.09, so both emit a bottom disc on z = 0 facing down, one entirely inside the other, in SteelLight and SteelDark. Neither is in front. **That is the mating face of every tank in the game resolving to whichever material the rasteriser visited last.** Nobody saw it because a mating face is covered in an assembled stack; what shows it is the top of the topmost part, the bottom of the bottom-most, and every part held on its own in the VAB. **50 (6.4%) A TRIM SOLID WHOSE EXTENT LANDS EXACTLY ON ITS HOST'S END PLANE**: the class L engine's hazard ring is `z 2.465, height 0.09` and 2.465 - 0.045 is exactly 2.42, the mount plate's own bottom face, while the class S engine has the identical ring at `z 1.545, height 0.05` inside a plate spanning 1.50 to 1.60, i.e. comfortably interior. One number re-derived on the scale-up and the other transcribed. **16 (2.1%) A LITERALLY COPIED WIDTH** (the solar array's cell and its backing both authored 0.56), **which is the cause the brief predicted and it is FOURTH**. **8 (1.0%) TWO SOLIDS BOTH STARTED ON A RADIAL PART'S MOUNT PLANE**, because "sits on the mount plane" is written `size s at x = s/2` and only one solid may own that face. **(2) RN-412, THE FIX IS A DELETION AND IT IS TRIANGLE-NEGATIVE.** `tube(caps="lo"|"hi"|"none")` drops a cap that is sealed inside another solid; the disc was never geometry anyone could see. `_CAP_ORDER` measures of_lib's face order at import against a probe cylinder rather than assuming it, so a reorder upstream raises by name instead of silently deleting a side quad out of every tank. **rocket_parts 778 to 0, launch_pad 86 to 0, lander_landed 118 to 0.** **The lander is the proof: it is assembled out of `rocket_common`, it received NO edit of its own, and its 118 went to zero with the modules.** A fix at 24 call sites could not have done that. All three `ALLOWED` rows removed, so they are now held at zero the way the four clean machines are. **(3) RN-437, THE SIX PAD CAUSES**, largest first: a propellant tank and its own skirt ring both bottom-capped at `DECK_Z` because both were derived from the deck when only one stands on it (25, fixed by seating the tank 0.12 m INSIDE its skirt, which is what a skirt is for); the hazard paint band re-deriving the concrete cap's own Y boundary in a second function (24, fixed with a `PAINT_MARGIN`, and nobody paints to the cut line anyway); the hazard curb anchored to `HOLE` with a zero net standoff, then still landing on it by LENGTH after the offset was fixed (22, finally closed by running the curb as a CLOSED FRAME so every end face lands on another Hazard face and same-material overlaps are invisible by construction); an under-table girder written `2 * MT_HW` where `MT_HW` is defined as `TR_HW` (8); swing-arm trim anchored to the published `UMB_X` socket (4); a clamp hose hand-tuned to the same sum as a strap face (3). **(4) RN-413 to RN-434, THE FORM, under `docs/web/ART-DIRECTION.md`.** New vocabulary in `rocket_common`: `bell` (an open-mouthed shell swept through a real expansion CURVE instead of two straight cones with a kink, with cooling-channel FLUTES that cost zero triangles because the columns already existed, and a short inner sleeve because you cannot see further than that up a nozzle); `weld` and `blanket` (capless rings, 2*seg triangles for a hard line all the way round); `truss` (splayed struts you can see through); `feed_line` and `fitting` (plumbing on ONE bearing). Engines got turbopumps that are plumbed, two gimbal actuators on two bays of six rather than a ring of six, and chamber-throat-injector as three visibly different diameters. Tanks are welded from about 0.75 m plate courses with insulation that STOPS, and carry four fittings on four unrelated azimuths so no quadrant repeats. The pod got a shield made of tile courses whose rim OVERHANGS the hull, panel breaks, a hatch with hinges and a latch, a SECOND window not opposite the first, and two RCS clusters. Decouplers got a parting line, a det-cord raceway and pusher springs. The booster got field joints, a cable raceway down its 4.85 m casing and separation motors. **(5) RN-412's SECOND GATE, AND IT EARNED ITS KEEP FOUR TIMES ON ITS FIRST DAY.** `assert_class_envelope` measures the pile the moment it is poured and refuses BY PART NAME with the offending extent printed, and `fitting` / `stud_ring` / `hull` check a fitting's TRUE world AABB (a yawed box reaches `hx|cos a| + hy|sin a|`, so a 0.16 m lug at azimuth 157 sits at x -0.580 and REACHES -0.671). It caught the pod at 1.2580 (a chine proud of a class radius that was already AT the bounding box, fixed by narrowing the hull so the shield rim overhangs it), the booster's joint bolts 7 mm proud, the class L decoupler's pushers 20 mm proud, and **the vernier wrong in BOTH axes at once, 0.354 x 0.292 against 0.360 x 0.280, which is a PARITY argument rather than a typo: `seg` divisible by 4 puts columns on the axes, but the flute lands on ODD columns, so where a bell is the widest solid on a part `seg` must be divisible by 8.** **(6) THE FOUR AZIMUTHS ARE ARITHMETIC, NOT TASTE.** A fitting on a barrel is centred near r 0.64 on a class whose radius is 0.625, so it clears only between about 28 and 62 degrees off an axis; 33 / 118 / 214 / 302 are in that band and are also 10 to 13 degrees off the nearest stringer at either class. **(7) I COMMITTED THE COPIED-WIDTH DEFECT INSIDE THE FIX FOR THE COPIED-WIDTH DEFECT.** The first draft of the solar array's cell interconnects gave them the cell's own 0.030 at the cell's own x centre: 72 new same-facing pairs, in the same function, on the same day, in the fix for that exact cause. The build after it read 90 and named all of them. **A gate is not a formality; the person who most recently proved they understand a defect is not immune to it.** **(8) THE PRICE, STATED.** `VesselView` and `VabView` each clone a PER-PART mesh and **neither ever sets `castShadow`**, so a rocket part is drawn once and costs nothing in the three cascades; and there is no 3D parts palette, `VabPanelHtml` renders the catalogue as DOM buttons. On `render_vessel.py`'s reference vehicle (11 stack parts, 2 boosters, 2 radial decouplers, 4 fins, 2 RCS, 2 panels): **6,132 triangles before, 10,102 after, +3,970, which is 0.15% of StatsProbe's 2.7M alert ceiling.** File total 7,904 to 11,924 against a raised 13,000; `lander_landed` LOD0 1,478 to 3,880, ONE instance in the world. Nine parts are still under the budget they already had, because deleting 704 buried caps paid for the weld seams. **(9) CONTROLS.** All 51 glb hashed and rebuilt byte-identical BEFORE any edit; `validate_glb --all` 51/51 at both ends; `check_mating vessel` **ALL MATES AND TILES EXACT**, every socket, standoff, diameter and anti-parallel dot at +0.000000000; `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance on all 51 with all three of mine at 0 same. **Negative control, two-sided: `caps=` removed from `fuel_tank`, `rocket_parts` 0 to 246 and `lander_landed` 0 to 68, gate exits 1 and names both; reverted, both back to 0 and the after-bytes restored byte-identical across the whole 51.** `contracts.json` and `of_lib.py` are shared with three lanes: only my two rows differ from HEAD, verified by row-wise JSON compare against `git show HEAD:`. Index empty before and after, `git diff --cached --name-status`. Zero em dashes verified in Python per file. **(10) TWO PROCESS INCIDENTS, BOTH CAUGHT, BOTH WORTH WRITING DOWN.** A `json.load` / `json.dump` round trip of `contracts.json` reformatted 4,370 lines of three other lanes' rows; reverted and redone as regex edits against the literal text, 14 lines. Then `git checkout -- contracts.json` restored an OLDER revision than the sibling lane's new HEAD and silently reverted their spider row (`validate_glb` caught it: 50/51, failing spider, "no contract entry"); redone from `git show HEAD:` and re-verified row-wise. **`git checkout --` is not a safe undo when HEAD has moved under you mid-pass.** `render_launch_pad.py` also overwrote the eight committed `W12_pad_*` screenshots with its own before-pass, recovered with `git checkout --` only because they were tracked, and now takes `OF_PAD_PREFIX`. **NOT DONE, NAMED:** **the launch pad's ART pass** (its 86 defects are fixed and its six causes are attributed above, but no form work was done: LOD0 is at 2,564 of 2,600 and it is the one asset here that DOES cast into three cascades, so any raise is a 4x price and wants its own argument); **`LaunchPad_LOD1` and `LOD2` never draw at all**, because `MachineBatch` selects `_LOD0` only, exactly as `NodeBatch` does for harvest nodes, so 704 triangles of that file are dead payload; the in-engine frame cost of this raise is priced but NOT measured; and **ALL MATERIAL VALUES (albedo, roughness, colour) ARE UNTOUCHED and owed to look development**, including the fact that the tank's insulation blanket currently reads only through the SteelDark / SteelLight contrast, which is look-dev's number and not mine. Screenshots `RN436_{before,after}_vessel_{heroes,close,assembly,catalogue,engines,adapter_joint}`, `RN437_{before,after}_pad_{hero,human_deck,trench,clamps}`.

> **RN-451 to RN-452 (2026-08-01): THE SPIDER STOPS BEING A CLAY BLOB ON STILTS, AND "THE FAR SWARM SHARES THE AUTHORED SHAPE" TURNS OUT TO BE TRUE OF FIVE NUMBERS.** First creature pass under `docs/web/ART-DIRECTION.md`. Reid saw v1 and said "a good start"; the target then moved to realistic, detailed, complex, with "clean" reclassified as a defect, so v1 is now measured against a harder brief and the studio pairs agree with the brief. **(1) RN-452, THE FORM.** v1 was five smooth `oval_tube`s, six axis-aligned CUBES for eyes and eight smooth wires, 1,060 triangles; rendered it is a clay blob on stilts with nothing to look at at any distance. v2 is 3,450: a carapace DOME sitting on an overhanging MARGIN brim (the leg roots now emerge from under a plate instead of out of the side of a balloon), a fovea keel, five overlapping TERGITES, a real PEDICEL waist (v1 ran one underbelly mass under both body segments, which is most of why the silhouette read as a beetle), eight COXAL SOCKETS bound to the Thorax so the leg turns inside a sleeve that does not move with it, a paturon behind each fang (v1 had a fang and no jaw), PEDIPALPS, EIGHT domed eyes in the two rows a spider has, six SPINNERETS and an anal tubercle, thirty BRISTLES of which two are broken off, three leg SPINES on the front pairs and two on the rear, and legs at twice the mass with the knee as a PINCH between two knuckles. **TWO OF THE THREE MECHANISMS COST NOTHING, WHICH IS THE POINT.** `rig_common.oval_tube` takes a `smooth_sides` flag (default True, so **all four rigged assets rebuild byte-identical under the parameter alone**, verified before any art edit): a smooth-shaded 8-gon claims a cylinder it does not have and lights as one even gradient, and the SAME triangles flat-shaded give eight hard lit values, which is most of the difference between chitin and clay. The tergite banding is two rings 18 to 20 mm apart with a radius drop, i.e. **rock_form's bedding ledge (RN-241) wrapped round a body**: an annular lip, watertight, and cheaper than separate scute plates. **(2) RN-451, THE FINDING, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART.** The brief and this asset's own contract both say the far swarm shares the authored shape, so silhouette work reaches every creature on screen while skinning cost falls only on the nearest eight. **IT DOES NOT.** `EnemyView` draws `EnemyArt.creatureGeometry()`, five boxes and sixteen limb boxes built in TypeScript, and the two representations share exactly **HIP_Y 0.62, KNEE_Y 1.55, KNEE_R 1.05, FOOT_R 2.4 and AZ 35/70/110/145**. The claim boundary swaps animation and not shape only to the extent of those five numbers; they are frozen here and are the reason the knee had to be a pinch rather than a knuckle, and everything else on the creature was free. **Re-authoring the far stand-in against this form is a client-lane follow-up and no Blender edit can do it.** **(3) THE PRICE, STATED.** lod0 1,060 to 3,450 (budget 3,600), total 1,960 to 6,380 (6,700). `SpiderFlock.MAX_RIGS` caps the skinned pool at 8, so worst case is **+19,120 triangles in a fight against the 555k to 1.0M per frame RN-71 measured, 1.9% to 3.4%**, beside the rock lane's accepted 1.7% to 3.0%. **DRAW CALLS UNCHANGED AND `max_materials` STAYS 4, AND THE REASON IS A SECOND FINDING**: `SpiderFlock.load` merges every `_LOD0` primitive into ONE geometry and bakes material colour into a vertex-colour attribute, then draws with one material per enemy TYPE, so **this file's material count never reaches the draw-call count**. A rig is 4 calls (main plus three shadow cascades) whatever is in the .glb, and a fight stays at RN-124's measured 107 of 150. Also inherited from RN-243: **`SpiderFlock` selects `_LOD0` only and `EnemyView` uses none of the file, so LOD1 and LOD2 are 2,930 triangles of dead payload**, flagged and not deleted because the contract lists them. **(4) THE ADMIN CAP HONOURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** At `bodyRadiusM` 1.25 (the largest melee type) the shot sphere is 2.50 m across and the authored body mass is already 1.895 m long, 2.37 m drawn: **under 130 mm of headroom, so the abdomen may not take it.** The mass went into the legs, which `EnemyArt`'s own comment says are silhouette and not target. **The top of the creature came DOWN, 1.6054 to 1.6036 over the ground**, and the declared Y span reads 0.0032 larger only because the new tarsal claw reaches 5 mm nearer the floor. Leg jitter is capped at 0.040 for exactly this reason: 0.052 * 1.04 is the last knee radius under v1's measured height. **(5) `render_creatures.py`, NEW, AND IT EXISTS FOR THE TWO SHOTS A SINGLE-ASSET RENDER STRUCTURALLY CANNOT TAKE.** `render_check.py` frames a 1.80 m player at 4 m and renders one .glb; a 4.53 m creature is off three sides of every view in that table, and **"massive" is a RATIO, so it cannot be photographed with one asset in the frame**. `scale` mode puts the player beside the spider at the scale the client actually draws it; `sheet` mode bakes N depsgraph-evaluated poses into ONE frame, so a clip that stopped binding after a re-author stands in the rest pose beside five that do not, in one image under one light. **THE INSTRUMENT BUG IT COST, WORTH RECORDING:** Blender's glTF importer parks a 2 m `Icosphere` in a `glTF_not_exported` collection as the imported armature's bone display shape; the view layer excludes it, so **nothing that renders an import directly has ever seen it, `render_check.py` included**, and baking a pose LINKS the copy and escapes the exclusion. It drew a white dome over every body, and the honest first reading of dark legs around a white body on a ONE-material asset is "the bake lost its materials", which is wrong and cost a material audit. Its own hide flags are all False and say nothing; the collection is the only thing that names it. **INVARIANTS: 28 bones, both clip names and both durations untouched (DW-34); `validate_glb.py` PASS on all 21 checks including `bones`, `skin_weights` (every vertex, sum 1.0000), `rest_pose` 6.46e-07 and `anim_t0`; `check_coplanar` 0 by absence, still not in the allowance table; two builds byte-identical, before and after the docstring rewrite.** **NOT DONE, NAMED:** ALL VALUES (albedo, roughness, colour) UNTOUCHED and owed to look-dev, and the four `of.PALETTE` creature roles are v1's; the player skin follow-on (RN-125's texgen fabric family plus a `PlayerRig` map attach) is still not started and `texgen.py` and `surfaces.json` were not opened; **the rocket lane overwrote `contracts.json` from a stale copy 90 seconds after I landed my row and dropped it out of the WORKING TREE (HEAD was safe), restored surgically, and it is the sweep family's sixth member: a filtered blob protects the commit and nothing protects the file afterwards.** Commit `8efe864`. Screenshots `RN451_spider_{before,after}_rest_f0_{c34,cside,cfront,ctop,chead,cknee,cabd}`, `RN451_scale_{before,after}`, `RN451_walksheet_{before,after}`.

> **RN-371 to RN-378 (2026-08-01): THE MACHINE FORM PASS, PART 1. A ROCK IS DETAILED BY FRACTURE AND A MACHINE IS DETAILED BY ASSEMBLY, SO IT NEEDS ITS OWN VOCABULARY, AND THE COPLANAR GATE HAS TO SURVIVE IT STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN CAREFULLY.** Blender only, no browser, no build, no dev server: other lanes are live in the client. **(1) RN-371, THE INSTRUMENT, `tools/blender/render_machines.py`.** `render_check.py`'s machine views were computed for a 4.4 m frame width and crop an 8 m assembler, it has no floor, no view at the height a player stands, and no way to draw more than one copy of anything. **A FACTORY IS NOT LOOKED AT ONE MACHINE AT A TIME**, so this tool's `line:` shot places the machines on the client's OWN 1 m site grid (even footprints on integer centres, belt tiles on half integers) with real belt tiles between them, both curve handednesses, and the player body in frame as the only honest scale reference for a 3 m to 8 m object. `studio:` is two bearings on a neutral floor, `face:` is the standing-eye view at the port side which is the frame the game actually shows, and `det:` is 1.3 m of the front-lower CORNER including the ground contact, because a bolt head is 0.03 m and is four pixels in a whole-machine frame. **Both framing bugs RN-306 found in `render_flora.py` are fixed at birth rather than inherited**: `hide_render` is set BOTH ways so a second shot in one invocation is not an empty frame, and every view frames by whichever of height and footprint is LARGER. A third was found here and is written down: resolution is PER SHOT, because every distance in the file is solved from the sensor fit and Blender fits the 36 mm sensor to the larger pixel dimension, so the first `face:` put an 8 x 4 machine across 43 percent of a 720 x 640 frame. **(2) RN-372, THE VOCABULARY, `tools/blender/machine_form.py`, NEW and new for `rock_form.py`'s reason**: `of_lib.py` is read by every lane and all 51 assets, and industrial assembly detail has no business being visible to a tree. Eighteen greeble types over a `Face` abstraction with in-plane (u, v) coordinates, so one description of a detail band serves +Y, -Y, +X, -X and the roof. **THE COPLANAR GATE IS HELD BY TWO PROPERTIES, NOT BY CARE, and this is the part worth keeping.** The four clean machines are at ZERO by their ABSENCE from `check_coplanar`'s allowance table, and greebles are precisely how coplanar pairs get made; the catalogued root cause is two parts dimensioned off one landmark where the landmark is a width COPIED rather than DERIVED. So (a) nothing is ever placed ON a face: `Face.part` puts a part's front at `plane + proud` and its BACK at `plane - proud * 0.55`, and there is no call in the module that can land a part flush; and (b) **the proud height is a property of the greeble TYPE and not of the call site**, so two parts can only meet on a plane if they are the same type, and a type has one role, and a same-material overlap is invisible by construction and deliberately uncounted. **A caller cannot re-create the defect by passing a number, because the number is not a parameter.** Measured: assembler same-facing 0 before, 0 after, across 2,292 added triangles. **(3) TWO THINGS THE FIRST DRAFT GOT WRONG, BOTH NOW STRUCTURAL.** Nothing in this project CUTS geometry: there is no boolean anywhere, so the first module had a `sink()` that put a box inside a solid body, which would have authored four maintenance hatches nobody could see. A machine gets a recess the way `build_assembler._mouth` already got one, by standing a COAMING proud of the surface. And winding is not a rule an author can follow: the in-plane basis is (Y, Z) on an X face and (X, Z) on a Y face and those have opposite handedness against their own normals, so six axis-and-sign cases are six chances to ship an invisible backfaced panel on one side of one machine. `machine_form.oriented` tests the SIGNED VOLUME of the closed solid instead, which is one test that cannot be wrong. **(4) RN-373 to RN-377, THE ASSEMBLER, 684 to 2,976 triangles at LOD0.** Sixteen plate seams for ninety-six triangles, because a strap that spans the body shows on the face it enters AND the face it leaves (`build_smelter`'s rib trick, named and pointed at plating). **FOUR FACES THAT NOW DIFFER**, which is what gives the machine a front: instruments, a cable run, a canopy over the outlet and an eave with five gussets on the front; a feed hood leaning the OPPOSITE way on the rear intake, so the two intakes and the one outlet are distinguishable by shape from across the base; a ladder, a step and a bracketed landing on the side intake; a louvre coaming and a hinged, latched, bolted hatch on the service side. Sixteen anchor pads bolting the machine to its own plinth, which is what closed the one frame in the pass that showed nothing: the player-eye detail view was a picture of an empty yellow band and an empty dark slab. Kick plates at the foot of all four faces, **TWO OF THEM DENTED, and that is this lane's whole answer to wear**: look development owns every albedo and roughness value in the game, so a scuff cannot be authored here, but a dent can, and a dent is the cause a scuff is the effect of, for the same twelve triangles the box it replaces cost. A four-sided railing with posts, rails and toe boards in place of four boxes, because a parapet is SOLID and a railing lets the sky through at the exact height a player sees a roofline against it. **THE LADDER IS THE BEST TRIANGLES IN THE PASS AND THE REASON IS SCALE, NOT DETAIL: it is the only greeble whose size a player already knows.** It stops at 2.42 and arrives at a bracketed landing, and that is arithmetic rather than taste: the collar is 0.20 proud from 2.90 to 3.20 and a stringer stands 0.139 proud, so a ladder taken higher DISAPPEARS inside the collar for 30 cm and reappears as a stub. **(5) RN-378, THE BUDGET RAISE, ARGUED AND PRICED IN THE ROW.** lod0 1100 to 3050 (actual 2976), total 1800 to 3500 (actual 3428). `MachineBatch.ts:195` selects `_LOD0` ONLY and never switches by distance, and `MachineBatch.ts:211` sets `castShadow` against three CSM cascades (`Quality.ts:23`), so **every machine triangle is drawn FOUR times a frame and the whole raise is paid on every assembler on screen**: +2,292 each, +9,168 over four, **+1.4% of the 660,385 triangle frame `probes/portcost.js` measured over a 79-building base** and 0.34% of the 2.7M `StatsProbe` alert. Assemblers are the rarest machine in a base; portcost's own reference base is 22 smelters and 57 belts and no assembler at all. **RECORDED AND NOT ACTED ON: LOD1 and LOD2 are DEAD PAYLOAD on EVERY machine, exactly as RN-243 found the harvest nodes' are, because nothing selects them.** Kept because `lod_nodes` lists them; flagged to the client lane. **(6) INVARIANTS.** All 18 machine and structure assets rebuilt BYTE-IDENTICAL before any edit, which is what makes "before" a measurement rather than a file date; `validate_glb --all` 50/51 after (the one failure is the sibling rocket lane's in-flight `rocket_parts`, not this lane's); `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance on all 51 with the assembler at 0 same; the 8 x 4 x 8 declared box now asserted BY THE BUILD (`machine_form.assert_inside`, axis named and overshoot in metres) rather than found afterwards in the shipped bytes; sockets and the collision proxy untouched and verified 6/6; **`contracts.json` is shared and the rocket lane held uncommitted edits in it, so the commit staged a FILTERED blob whose only difference from HEAD is the assembler row**; real index verified empty before and refreshed to HEAD after; zero em dashes verified in Python per file. **ALL MATERIAL VALUES UNTOUCHED AND OWED TO LOOK DEVELOPMENT.** Commit `5182a3a`. Screenshots `RN371_before_*` against `RN372_after_*` (`_a`, `_b`, `_face`, `_det`) plus `RN371_before_line` / `RN372_after_line`. **NOT DONE: the smelter, miner, box, belts, generator, power pole, inserter, structures set, survival smelter and primitive furnace.**

> **RN-331 to RN-352 (2026-08-01): THE LOOK-DEVELOPMENT PASS. THE CLIENT ALREADY HAD ACES, SO THE PASTEL WAS SOMEWHERE ELSE, AND THE PLACE IT WAS IS THAT THE GROUND UNDER A FOREST WAS PAINTED THE COLOUR OF THE FOREST.** First pass under `docs/web/ART-DIRECTION.md` that owns colour, and the deliverable meant to outlive it is section 2.1, THE CALIBRATED TARGET. **(1) RN-331, THE FINDINGS, STATED BEFORE ANY CHANGE, BECAUSE THE BRIEF'S OWN HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG AND IT IS WORTH RECORDING THAT IT WAS.** The brief predicted that "pastel is very often an unmapped or naive curve plus a lifted black point". Measured: **the curve was never the problem.** `CompositeGlsl` has carried the ACES RRT+ODT fit, matrices and all, copied character for character out of three's `tonemapping_pars_fragment`, since RN-10; `Renderer.ts` has set `ACESFilmicToneMapping` and `outputColorSpace = SRGBColorSpace` since W0. Output is sRGB-correct and is NOT double-encoded: the composite writes its own `srgbEncode` and its fragment source contains no `<colorspace_fragment>` or `<tonemapping_fragment>` include, so three's `WebGLProgram` has nothing to substitute and cannot add a second one. The grade runs in DISPLAY space after the encode (RN-10 fixed that once already) at contrast 1.06, saturation 1.08, **lift +0.012, which is a lifted black point and is the one half of the brief's prediction that was true**, and vignette 0.16. **The half that was badly wrong is exposure.** It sat at three's default 1.0 and had never been calibrated against anything: measured across three sites at local noon, `hiFrac`, the share of the frame above luma 200, read **0.00 per cent at Hills, 0.00 at Plains and 0.86 at Forest**. At every site and every daylight elevation the top fifth of the display range was EMPTY while the black point was lifted off zero. That is a frame with no highlights and no blacks, which is what "pastel" is as a number, and it is an exposure fault rather than a curve fault. **(2) RN-332, THE ABANDONED LANE'S WORK: ADOPTED, EXTENDED, AND ONE OF ITS CLAIMS OVERTURNED.** A stopped lane left uncommitted `curveMix` (a slope-matched S that rolls the toe and shoulder so the ends stop clipping), the URL grade flags, `PostDefaults.ts` and `probes/lookdev.js`. I re-derived its numbers on my own build rather than trusting them and **they hold**: at Hills noon, world-band iqr 87 to 108 and mean saturation 0.533 to 0.458, both past a measured instrument floor of 0. The work is kept because its reasoning is sound and its negative control is exact (the restore is bit-identical at eleven rungs of twelve, and `?curve=0` reaches the pre-RN-207 straight line without a rebuild). **Its last unfinished check is overturned in section 5.** **(3) RN-333, THE SHIPPED RESPONSE CURVE.** exposure 1.0 to **1.2**, contrast 1.06 to **1.45** on the S rather than the straight line, saturation 1.08 to **0.92**, lift 0.012 to **0**. The saturation cut is what makes the contrast rise a VALUE change rather than a chroma change: a per-channel contrast raises chroma on its own, and at 1.45 with the old saturation 1.08 Hills noon measured saturation 0.527 to 0.578, i.e. the opposite of what the art direction asks for. At 0.92 the same frame reads chroma 56.8 against 57.7 while value contrast rises 29 per cent. **(4) RN-334, THE FOURTH SITE CONTRADICTED THE OTHER THREE, WHICH IS RN-64's LESSON ARRIVING ON SCHEDULE.** The abandoned lane measured Hills, Plains and Forest, all green biomes. I added **Beach, the site world-gen's own survey calls "the one to show Reid first"**, and two checks went red there and nowhere else: the pivoted contrast moved tiles 13,866 up against 383 down (a desert at noon lies almost entirely ABOVE the 0.5 pivot, so a contrast increase can only brighten it) and the masked night ground FELL, p50 31 to 28 and mean 27.2 to 24.9. An exposure sweep inside one page settled it: 1.20 / 1.08 / 0.98 gave Forest midnight masked-ground p50 2 / 1 / 1 and mean 4.82 / 4.22 / 3.76 against 4.06 before, so **1.20 is the only candidate that does not spend RN-152's starlight floor**, while Beach noon reads p50 172 / 163 / 155 against 150. The trade is published rather than hidden: **1.2 is kept and the price is that Beach noon reads brighter.** The root cause is stated and not fixed: one global exposure serves biome albedos spanning 0.03 (Forest, linear) to 0.58 (Beach red, linear), a 20x range, and no single value can be right for both ends of it. **(5) RN-335 / RN-336, THE GROUND-SKY MASK, AND THE NIGHT ANSWER IS NOT THE ONE THE PREVIOUS LANE GUESSED.** Its last words were that "night terrain got brighter but the night sky lost 1 to 2 counts, and my check conflated them". The conflation was real; the diagnosis was not. `lookdev.js` now builds a sky mask ONCE at noon, per column, as the maximal contiguous run from the top that is blue and bright, and reuses it at every rung: the geometry does not move between rungs, so a noon mask is the same partition at midnight, when no rule could find one, and it is the same partition before and after the grade, so a grade cannot move a pixel between populations and fake a difference. **Measured at Hills, elevation -23.8 degrees, over 461,209 masked sky pixels: the night sky reads mean 0.06 counts before and 0.07 after, with p50 and p95 both exactly 0 and 99.94 per cent of it under luma 24. It is black to the digit and had nothing to lose.** Both movements were the GROUND's, one at its median (7 to 9, up) and one at its own upper tail (37 to 35, down), and a percentile over the union of two disjoint populations cannot say which is which. The check now asserts the ground's MEAN and MEDIAN, which is what RN-152 actually bought, and publishes p95 and the sky beside it. The tail loss is a property of a contrast pivoted at 0.5 rather than a regression: at night every ground pixel lies below the pivot, so no global contrast can raise the median without pulling the tail toward it. **(6) RN-337, THE MASK'S OWN BAR MOVED, AND FOR A LEGITIMATE REASON.** The first bar was "the top row is 95 per cent sky", true at Hills (1.0000) and FALSE at Forest (0.8394), because a canopy hangs into the top of the frame and the 16 per cent that is not sky is leaves. Widened to 0.60 with the measurement recorded, because this is INSTRUMENTS.md's opening failure in miniature: a control that was right when it was written, against a world that then grew trees. **(7) RN-345 to RN-347, THE FOLIAGE AND THE FLOOR, AND THE PICTURE FOUND WHAT NO STATISTIC HAD.** Admin redirected mid-pass on Reid's verdict of the flora work: *"Forest floor looks the same before and after. Still plato-y smooth pastel."* Standing eye at the Forest site pitched 26 degrees down, `docs/screenshots/RN347_forestfloor_before.png` answers it in one look: **the forest floor is a large, smooth, saturated emerald SHEET with sparse tufts standing on it.** The tufts are the understorey, which two lanes had just re-authored for silhouette. The sheet is `BiomePalette.HEX`, it is most of the pixels, and nobody had touched it since W1. **The error is categorical rather than a matter of degree. `biomeAt` classifies CLIMATE, the table was filled in by naming the classes (forest is green, plains is green), and the terrain material draws the GROUND. The ground under a forest is litter, humus, root and soil; the green belongs to the 3.2 million detail cards per square kilometre standing ON it. Painting both the same green leaves the layer that exists to read as vegetation with nothing to read against, which is exactly why two passes of silhouette work changed nothing Reid could see: the silhouettes were correct and they were invisible.** Beach `c8b48a` to `b3a184`, Plains `5f7d38` to `6d6a47`, Forest `2f5230` to `41392b`, Hills `6f7346` to `6b6650`; every one moves down in saturation and toward the substrate, and **Mountains, Polar, Regolith, MoonHighland and CraterFloor are deliberately untouched and are the control, so any measured move at those sites is a bug in this change.** Measured on the money shot, world band: mean saturation **0.590 to 0.439** and mean chroma **25.8 to 13.0**. Beside it, two smaller terms with one owner each so a later change stays attributable. **RN-345**, a family-level `FOLIAGE_TONE` in a new `render/instancing/FoliageTone.ts` (saturation and value about the colour's own luma, leaf 0.62 / 0.86 and grass 0.70 / 0.94, `?foliagetone=0` the exact negative control, boot default 1 through an explicit `null`-safe parse per RN-150). **RN-346**, `ScatterLook.tintFor` widened: value band 0.80..1.18 to 0.66..1.26, the dry drift un-squared (the median instance sat at t = 0.19, i.e. 81 per cent of the way back to the one green) and a second independent CHROMA axis added, because dry is a WARM drift and a field varied only along it is a LINE through colour space where a real understorey is an area. The extra hash draw is re-SEEDED rather than given a new index, since `k * 8 + 12` would alias exactly onto instance `k + 1`'s `+4`. **THE FOLIAGE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST TO THE NEXT LANE: the two albedo cards carry NO HUE AT ALL.** Decoded from the shipped bytes, `of_grass_a.png` opaque means are R 188.1 / G 187.1 / B 186.2 and `of_leaf_a.png` R 199.6 / G 199.4 / B 199.2, with a maximum absolute R-minus-B of 6 counts. They are grey VALUE textures; every green in the game arrives from `of_lib.PALETTE`, whose five foliage roles sit in a band of HSV saturation 0.519 to 0.551 across hue 85 to 107 degrees. And because `Surfaces.ts` divides `material.color` by the manifest's scalar `albedo_mean`, **raising a card's mean value is a no-op on final brightness BY CONSTRUCTION: the only things that survive that division are the card's spatial VARIANCE and its HUE, and both are currently near zero.** That is where the next foliage pass has the most room, and it is one file. **(8) RN-338 / RN-339, SHADOW AND AMBIENT OCCLUSION AT WALKING DISTANCE, AND BOTH TERMS RUN AT ABOUT A THIRD OF THEIR NOMINAL REACH EXACTLY WHERE THEY MATTER.** Cascade 0 spans 22 m in a square box of 1.44 times that, so on the high tier it is **15.47 mm per shadow texel** with a PCF kernel one texel wide, and the 22 m boundary into cascade 1 is a **3.64x resolution cliff with no blend**. The AO and contact terms are clamped in SCREEN space, `aoMaxScreen` 0.09 and `csMaxScreen` 0.05, and with `uProj[0][0]` at 0.9743 for the shipped 60-degree vertical field of view those clamps **BIND at every pixel closer than 4.87 m and 4.38 m respectively**. The effective world radius collapses to `0.185 * z` for AO and the march length to `0.103 * z` for contact, so the ground under a standing player's own feet at z of 1.5 to 2 m gets an AO radius of **0.28 to 0.37 m against a nominal 0.9 m** and a contact march of **0.15 to 0.21 m against a nominal 0.45 m**. The clamps exist to bound the face-against-a-wall case and their comments say so; what the derivation shows is that they bind in the ORDINARY standing case, which is not what they were written for. Both are fixed-tap terms (36 horizon taps, 8 march steps), so the cost of widening them is texture-cache locality and not arithmetic. **NOT CHANGED THIS PASS, and named as the first thing the next look-dev lane should price.** Load-bearing for any AO retune: `AO_APPLY_FS` is a full-screen MULTIPLY over the final colour and `TerrainShader` sums ambient and direct into one value with no channel to reach into, so **AO attenuates DIRECT SUN on terrain**; `aoStrength` 0.9 and `csStrength` 0.72 are the whole of the compensation and their comments say exactly that. **(9) RN-340, THE MATERIAL-RESPONSE CENSUS, decoded from the shipped PNGs rather than read off the generator.** Effective roughness is `material.roughness * ormG`. **`panel`, which is every machine, plate, steel and suit surface in the game, has a p05..p95 effective roughness band 0.032 wide**, which under a moving sun is indistinguishable from a constant, and that is the plastic read on the manufactured half of the world. **Metalness is a decoration and not a signal: three of the four ORM maps are literally constant 255 in blue**, so the only spatially varying metalness anywhere is `panel`, and 91.1 per cent of that is at identity. **And `coarse`, the family with the most screen area (rock, soil, sand, regolith, coal), has the LEAST relief in the set: mean normal tilt 7.7 degrees and p95 14.8 degrees, against bark's 22.8 and 65.7.** Ore is the one family that behaves like a material (roughness standard deviation 0.174, driven by an explicit glint mask). Five materials draw with Lambert and have no specular term at all, two of them large-area: the ore-patch decal and the near voxel terrain. **The terrain itself is pure Lambert with no specular lobe, no roughness and no metalness**, which is the single largest surface in the frame that cannot respond like a material, and it needs a DW-10 argument rather than a tweak. **NOT CHANGED THIS PASS; the `coarse` normal strength is the cheapest of these and is one constant in `texgen._coarse_masks`.** **(10) CONTROLS AND HYGIENE.** One binary per claim; `npm run sync-assets` before every served comparison; port 4200 never touched; my own port killed by PID and confirmed gone before serving, and **the `--strictPort` trap fired once and was caught** (the new preview refused to start, the OLD server kept answering 200, and the tell was that the frame still had the old floor in it). Freshness proved against HEAD rather than by boot line: served wasm byte-identical to `HEAD:web/wasm/dist/of-core.wasm`, bundle "client expects 21" against `OF_ABI_VERSION = 21`. tsc proven alive with a negative control before it was relied on (`TS2322` by name at `PostDefaults.ts:173`, then reverted byte-identical). Zero em dashes verified in Python per file. Index empty before and after via `git diff --cached --name-status`. **`run.mjs` carried ANOTHER lane's uncommitted `trees` / `treedensity` / `spires` / `forestdetail` whitelist entries, so it was committed as a FILTERED BLOB of HEAD plus my own hunks and their work was left in the working tree for them.** **(11) NOT DONE, NAMED, IN THE ORDER I WOULD DO THEM. (a)** The AO and contact screen clamps in section 8, priced at Forest and Plains. **(b)** The two card-mask defects Admin ruled mine and I did not reach: `of_leaf_a.png`'s bottom row is 22.7 per cent opaque so every leaf strip is cut away at its base, and `_grass_strips` authors 11 blades on a 0.0455 half-pitch at 0.058 to 0.082 half-widths so the roots overlap into a MAT with zero transparent columns. Both are in `texgen.py`, whose entire input closure is itself and which is committed and clean, so regeneration is safe; note that `GRASS_BAND_HALF_U = 0.045` in `props_common.py` is coupled to the blade pitch, so every Grass-bearing asset needs a rebuild if the blade COUNT changes, and that grass coverage measures 0.5949 against a check floor of 0.55, i.e. 0.045 of headroom before narrowing a blade trips the gate. **(c)** Card HUE and within-card value variance, per section 7. **(d)** The `coarse` normal strength. **(e)** The terrain's absent specular lobe. **(f)** A dark scribble artefact on the near ground, plainly visible in every floor frame and attributed in `docs/screenshots/RN347_forestfloor_{notex,norelief}.png`.

> **RN-301 to RN-311 (2026-08-01): THE UNDERSTOREY, AND THE MONEY SHOT SAYS THE FOREST FLOOR IS DECIDED BY A TABLE THIS LANE DOES NOT OWN.** Second pass under `docs/web/ART-DIRECTION.md`. Blender only, no browser, no build, no probe, no dev server: Reid was gaming. **(1) RN-301, THE INSTRUMENT, and it is the finding.** `render_flora.py` gained `floor:`, a standing-eye (1.66 m, 34 mm) view of a 694 m2 patch populated **BY DENSITY, NOT BY COUNT**: each species gets `per_km2 * area / 1e6` off a table transcribed from `Registry.ts` (`BIOME_PROPS[3]`, `GROUND_DETAIL`, `CANOPY_FOREST`, `DENSITY_SCALE = 6` applied). `group:` could not see this layer at all, since it frames a 16 m fir from 5.4 m up across a band 26 to 78 m out, in which a 0.30 m fern is two pixels: INSTRUMENTS.md's own trap, a thing measured only where it cannot show reporting its own absence. The renders also now wear the SHIPPED alpha cards (`of_grass_a.png` / `of_leaf_a.png` through the authored UVs at the shipped `alpha_test`), because a foliage strip is a CARRIER for a mask and a render of the untextured strip is a picture of something the client never draws. **The frame's verdict: `docs/screenshots/RN301_before_floor.png` is a GRASS FIELD, not a forest floor. In that patch the four Forest biome props place 27 instances against the detail layer's 2,244. The character of the ground under a forest is 98.8 percent `GROUND_DETAIL`, which Forest shares verbatim with Plains and Hills.** **(2) RN-302 / RN-303, ZERO-TRIANGLE SHAPE ON THE ONLY LAYER WHERE A TRIANGLE CANNOT BE SPENT.** `GROUND_DETAIL` sums to 3.2 million per km2 after the scale, so one triangle on `Detail_GrassCardA` costs 6,873 inside the 45 m LOD0 radius and the whole file's budget is 245. `props_common.blade` therefore gained `twist` (rolls the cross section about the blade's own bearing, so a strip leaves the ground face on and presents an edge partway up) and `kink` (displaces the centreline perpendicular to the plane it arches in, because bend and droop BOTH act in one vertical plane and a tuft was otherwise a set of flat fans); `pc.tuft` gained `twist`, `kink`, `droop_var` and `lean_var` as per-blade magnitudes. **All four draw from `_hash01`, never from `nxt`: one extra draw off the seeded LCG shifts every position generated after it and thirty-odd callers stop rebuilding byte-identical.** **(3) RN-304, THE GROUND LAYER, 245 TRIANGLES BEFORE AND 245 AFTER, to the unit, every card unchanged.** Seven cards opted in. `Detail_GrassCardC` carries the largest droop and twist because DRY IS A SHAPE BEFORE IT IS A COLOUR: dead grass lodges and folds, and straw standing as straight as the green beside it is a recolour pretending to be a species. The forb takes the largest `kink` and the smallest `twist`, because rolling the file's widest paddle edge on deletes the thing it exists to be. IoU: GrassCardB 0.466 to 0.307, GrassCardC 0.246 to 0.217, FlowerSprig 0.392 to 0.278, BroadleafForb 0.570 to 0.492; area cv up on all seven (GrassCardA 0.048 to 0.116, B 0.045 to 0.104). **GrassCardA's IoU went the WRONG WAY, 0.417 to 0.504, and is reported as such**: its width and area both moved more, so the mass is genuinely varying, but at `segs` 2 the twist has one interior level to act on and the plan filled out rather than changing shape. **(4) RN-305 to RN-308, `props_forest`, FOUR PROPS AND THE FOREST-FLOOR MATERIAL THEY HAD NONE OF.** Litter, fallen branches, an exposed root mat, more moss and small dry stems, all folded INSIDE the four stems the biome table already names and INSIDE the roles each prop already pays for: **no new stem** (`PropLibrary.register` calls `addGeometry` for every `_LOD0` in a file whether or not a table names it) and **no new role** (`acquire` takes one instance slot per part per placement, and the fern is the densest biome prop in the biome), so the pass is provably zero extra draw calls and zero extra instances without a browser to measure in. The snag's trunk became five swept rings with a coherent azimuthal ridge and a root flare (`taper_bands` `offsets` / `ridge` / `flare`, all free, none of which had reached this file) where it had been ONE `hc.taper` band with a `lean`, i.e. a tilted dowel; its stubs, roots and fallen limb are `tree_common.limb` sweeps, because a stack of horizontal rings flattens into a ribbon as it reaches out. The log bends in PLAN as well as sagging, at 8 sides instead of 7 (a 0.5 m trunk at 7 sides rendered as a hewn beam). The mushroom stalks went from FOUR sides with two caps to SIX sides with none, **for the same twelve triangles**, and the caps' variety is a ring table and therefore free. IoU: DeadTree 0.522 to 0.356, FallenLog 0.593 to 0.489; **the fern was already 0.336 and the brief's premise that it repeated is falsified, its defect was width, 0.042 to 0.139 cv**; MushroomCluster 0.412 to 0.441, worse, with width cv 0.052 to 0.097. The price, stated: +218 triangles of asset across four props at 25,200 / 9,000 / 2,520 / 1,560 per km2, which is 243 LOD0 instances inside the 45 m switch and about 8,700 triangles, 0.76 percent of the 1,139,594 measured at RN-183. **(5) RN-309, `bush_scrub`, THE WORST IN THE SET AT IoU 0.745 AND WIDTH cv 0.0201.** A projected width that moved two percent over a whole revolution. Three causes, each sufficient alone: five ELLIPSOIDS (each a solid of revolution), summing to a round plan, fitted into a SQUARE 1.00 x 1.00 footprint. Now three stems off a flared base with foliage clothed ALONG their length (RN-278's lesson: one mass per limb renders as a table top, and a table top rotates perfectly well) plus two thin bare dead stems, one snapped pale. **0.745 to 0.580 IoU, 0.0201 to 0.1435 width cv, 270 of 300 LOD0.** The first cut put every mass in the middle third and rendered as a green slab hovering over a stump, and the second made the dead stems 3.4 cm thick and 0.86 of the height, which read as two chopsticks: **the picture refused both, and neither refusal was visible in any statistic.** **NOTE FOR THE PLAN: `bush_scrub` IS NOT REFERENCED BY ANY `Registry.ts` TABLE** (ROADMAP-W11:415), so this is a real fix to an asset that does not draw. **(6) RN-310, THE WG-13 GATE IS RETIRED BY REID'S RULING, NOT BY ANYONE JUDGING IT WRONG.** Reid: *"there should be no scenery trees. all trees should be minable."* One family cannot be distinguished from a family that no longer exists, so WG-13 to WG-17 are superseded and `assert_bare_trunk` is gone; bare-trunk fractions are unconstrained on every tree in the project and root flare, low branching and forks may go where the form wants. **The gate was correct while the rule stood**: it caught the broadleaf pair at 0.014 of height apart. **THE LESSON THAT OUTLIVES THE RULE, and the reason that near-miss was invisible: A CONSTRAINT THAT SEPARATES TWO FAMILIES CANNOT BE CHECKED BY MEASURING ONE OF THEM.** What survives is the MEASUREMENT, still printed every build (pine 0.597, fir 0.623, broadleaf 0.586), plus an assertion that a canopy tree has SOME foliage, which is a build defect and not a design rule. Negative control: predicate inverted, build refused BY NAME on all three, reverted, glb byte-identical. The wider consequence of the ruling, that these three become harvest nodes rather than scatter props, is a Registry / world-gen / gameplay change and is flagged rather than started. **(7) THE ASK, AND IT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN SUGGESTED, `docs/screenshots/RN311_ask_floor.png`.** A `FOREST_DETAIL` list in `Registry.ts` distinct from `GROUND_DETAIL`, summing to the SAME 538,000 pre-scale and, at the shipped per-card triangle counts, the SAME 61,900 triangles in the patch (+0.16 percent), weighted off upright blades and onto the broad, low and dry shapes: GrassCardA 180,000 to 90,000, B 105,000 to 30,000, C 70,000 to 120,000, BroadleafForb 75,000 to 150,000, SedgeRosette 60,000 to 110,000, FlowerSprig 24,000 to 14,000, PebbleScatter 24,000 unchanged. **No new stem, no new asset, no new geometry, one `const` in one file**, and it changes the ground more than every geometry edit in this pass put together. **(8) THE SUBAGENT FALSIFIED THE GRASS-CARD HYPOTHESIS I WAS ABOUT TO ACT ON.** `GRASS_BAND_HALF_U = 0.045` samples about one blade of the card's eleven, and widening it looked like free density. It is not: `of_grass_a.png` has **ZERO transparent columns**, every one of its 256 columns is a single opaque run reaching the bottom row, so it is a bottom-anchored MAT with a serrated top edge and any u band of any width returns the same 0.6009 opaque fraction. Widening only collapses the per-blade variance (sd 0.120 to 0.024 at half-width 0.25) and buys no extra visible blade at all. **Zero of 113 grass strips across two files land on a gap; the 0.045 band is correct and was left alone.** The real defect is upstream in `texgen._grass_strips`, which authors 11 blades on a 0.0455 half-pitch with 0.058 to 0.082 half-widths, so the roots overlap into a mat and the mask carries NO lateral silhouette information. Owed, and NOT taken this pass, because regenerating `of_grass_a.png` moves a shared generated artifact that every textured contract hashes. Second finding from the same audit: the LEAF card's bottom row is only 22.7 percent opaque, so every Leaf/LeafDry strip (`Detail_GrassCardC`, `Detail_BroadleafForb`, the sprig heads, `Beach_DuneGrass`) is cut away at its base and roots into thin air. **(9) TWO INSTRUMENT BUGS FOUND BY USING IT, both in `render_flora.py` and both pre-existing.** `lod0_objects` only ever set `hide_render` one way, so a SECOND `studio:` in one invocation rendered an empty frame; and `studio:` framed by HEIGHT alone, which put the camera 1.7 m from a 2.86 m log. Both were invisible to the pass that wrote the tool because it rendered one tall tree per launch, and both are the understorey's problem specifically: this is the layer where footprint routinely exceeds height. **CONTROLS:** all 51 glb rebuilt and hashed before any edit, all 51 byte-identical; after every edit, the 9 untouched `props_common` consumers plus the sibling lane's two rebuilt byte-identical, and only the 3 intended files moved; the shared-module change also proved inert by a pure-python control running seven distinct `tuft` call shapes and three `blade` shapes through HEAD's module and the working tree's and comparing floats exactly, with a negative control showing `twist` does move them; `props_canopy.glb` byte-identical across the gate removal; `validate_glb.py --all` 51/51 at both ends (the `props_mountains` failure the brief warned of was the sibling rock lane's and they closed it in `54c5b38` mid-pass); `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance on all 51 with all four touched assets at 0 same and 0 naive; `contracts.json` rows touched are only this lane's; index empty before and after, verified with `git diff --cached --name-status`; zero em dashes verified in Python per file. **ONE SUBAGENT, pure python, no Blender, to keep Reid's machine quiet.** **NOT DONE AND NAMED in section 7: the `FOREST_DETAIL` registry ask, the `texgen` grass-mat and leaf-base defects, the in-engine price of RN-274 and of this pass, and the blocky `blob` / `canopy_mass` primitive that both the bush and the mushroom caps are still made of.**
>
> **RN-241 to RN-247 (2026-08-01): THE ROCK GEOMETRY PASS. FIVE SCALES OF FRACTURE, FOUR MINERALS THAT BREAK FOUR DIFFERENT WAYS, THE MOUNTAINS SPIRE BACK AS A ROCK YOU CAN HIT, AND A DECORATION RULE THAT HAD BEEN MEASURED ON ONE SIDE OF A SCALE.** Reid: "the rocks still look atrocious", and the studio-floor pairs agree. **(1) RN-241, THE VOCABULARY (`tools/blender/rock_form.py`, NEW, and new rather than an edit to `harvest_common.py` or `props_common.py` because one extra draw from either of those streams moves the bytes of twenty assets this pass has no business touching).** RN-73 got ONE fracture plane, ONE cleft and ONE overhang per mass; rendered, those four boulders are convex low-poly gems, empty inside 1 m. Five scales now, and FOUR OF THE FIVE COST NOTHING: the fracture plane (kept); the SHEAR PLANE, a vertical plane over a height band that outlying vertices are projected orthogonally onto, so two adjacent clipped columns span a quad lying exactly IN it, which is a second family of flat break faces for zero triangles and, banded by height, is a corner knocked off rather than a slice through; the BEDDING LEDGE, two rings 5% of the height apart with a radius step, 2n triangles for a horizontal line through the silhouette; RIM BITES and FLANK BITES, seeded single columns pulled in hard on the crown and on the body, zero triangles, and the flank bites are what stop the profile between the ledges reading as a cone; and PITS, a facet inset and pushed in along its own normal plus an optional pan in the crown, the only mechanism that is invisible at 30 m and the only one that survives at 1 m. Plus welded CLASTS (base inside the host, so what shows is the part that broke off; positioned by `radius_at()` from the host's own ring profile rather than by a typed coordinate) and RUBBLE. **`ring_jit` deliberately relaxes RN-73's exact-frustum rule**: re-rolling a small radius factor per (ring, column) bends each side quad so the exporter splits it into two triangles with different normals, DOUBLING the lit facet count for zero triangles. The old rule was a readability argument and ART-DIRECTION says those get restated. **BOTH EXACTNESS CLAIMS ARE MEASURED IN THE BUILD**: 75 fracture planes over the four boulders at max out-of-plane **0.000e+00 m**, 17 sheared masses at max **5.551e-17 m**, and `mass()` raises with the number printed. **NEGATIVE CONTROLS, both run, both reverted and both verified**: a crown vertex lifted 4 mm fails with `fracture plane is not planar: 4.000e-03`, a clipped vertex nudged 2.5 mm fails with `shear face is not planar: 1.822e-03`, and after reverting, `rock_form.py` is md5-identical and all four .glb rebuild byte-identical. **(2) RN-242, FOUR MINERALS, FOUR FRACTURE BEHAVIOURS, which is FORM and not hue.** RN-76 gave four arrangements of one mass. Now: stone SHATTERS (three blocks, shear planes on one SHARED bearing because they were one rock a moment ago); iron CLEAVES (ring jitter 0.025 so its facets stay hard and large, two deep shears meeting in an arris, the fewest pits); copper never cleaved, it WEATHERED (bulging NODE4 profile, no shear anywhere, the deepest rim bites, six welded nodules, vug pits); coal is FRIABLE AND BLOCKY (five to six columns, two stacked ledges, the most pits, and a ring of crumbs it has shed around its own foot, which no other kind gets). The RN-156 seam ROLES and colours are untouched: values are lighting-dependent and are owed to look-dev. **(3) RN-243, THE BUDGET RAISES, argued in the rows.** lod0 200 to 460 (stone, actual 426), 220 to 290 (iron, 258), 220 to 570 (copper, 526), 240 to 420 (coal, 382). **The price is stated rather than waved at: `NodeBatch` selects `_LOD0` only for a harvest node, so the whole raise is paid on every rock drawn.** Worst case Mountains at ROCK_DENSITY_KM2 640 over the 170 m ring, about 58 stone nodes: 142 to 426 is about 16.5k triangles against the 555k to 1.0M RN-71 measured per frame, i.e. **1.7% to 3.0%**. Recorded in the same comment: **nothing selects a node's LOD1 or LOD2, so 60% of each node file is dead payload** (flagged to the client lane, not deleted, because the contract lists it). Detail falls with depletion (Half drops half the clasts, Low drops them all and halves the pits). **check_coplanar went 0 to 13 on stone mid-pass and back to 0**, and the cause was real: every mass stands on z = 0 and they interpenetrate on purpose, so two flat bases in different roles are coplanar, same-facing and overlapping. Buried under a rock, except that this game digs tunnels. `rock_form.BASE_DZ` staggers the bases by index, two orders above the checker's PLANE_EPS and two below a pixel. **(4) RN-244, THE SPIRE, RESTORED AS A HARVEST NODE** (`nodes/rock_spire.glb`, node_kind `Rock`, 626 tris Full). WG-68 retired the 3.40 m decor prop and named the loss; this is the form. `node_kind Rock` so a client lane adds it as a SECOND `NodeArt.ART[NODE_KIND.Rock]` entry the way the two trees already alternate: one line, no new resource. Frost shattering works along JOINTS, so it is a stack of blocks with OFFSET joints and a broken top, not a taper (the first build stacked them concentrically and photographed as a rocket). **THE JOINT GAP AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT SETTLED IT**: seating a lift at the nominal top of the one below is wrong twice over (a tipped block re-seats on its LOWEST corner; a fracture plane is TILTED so its own lowest point is well under rz), and both errors point the same way, so the stack shipped with daylight through it in two places. **Rendered, that is a black slot across the shaft, indistinguishable from the shadow the bedding ledges on the same shaft make on purpose: the eye could not arbitrate and the arithmetic could.** `crag_common.seam_margins` read **+0.086, -0.035, -0.007**; now **+0.126, +0.085, +0.114**, and the build refuses to export on a non-positive margin (control: reconstructing the first seat heights puts it RED at joint 2 by name). **2.60 m authored, not 3.40, and that is not a reduction**: a decor prop is one size and a node is placed at 0.75 to 1.5, so these stand 1.95 m to 3.90 m and the tallest is taller than the retired one ever was; 3.40 would also have cost a 36% vertical stretch of every pit and clast, because `Parts.fit` forces the box. **The hit socket is derived from the PLAYER**: `min(0.55 * height, 1.15)`, because 0.55 of its own height lands over the player's head on anything this tall. INSTRUMENTS.md's 8 m machine, paid in advance. **(5) RN-247, THE FINDING, AND IT OUTRANKS THE ART IN THIS PASS. `DECOR_ROCK_MAX_H` and the decoration it gates were measured on OPPOSITE SIDES OF A SCALE and only one side was priced.** A node is placed at a UNIFORM scale, so ROCK_SCALE_MIN is the right factor for the smallest node. A scatter prop is not: `ScatterLook.scaleFor` multiplies a non-foliage prop's HEIGHT by up to `MINERAL_H_HI` 1.24 on top of `1 +/- jitter`. So an authored h is DRAWN at up to **h * 1.55**, and the real authored cap is `0.27 / 1.25 / 1.24` = **0.174 m**. **`Hills_ScreePatch` at 0.24 m is drawn at up to 0.372 m and `Plains_PebbleA` at 0.18 m at up to 0.279 m: both above the threshold WG-68 cleared them against.** `crag_common.check_decor_height` is that derivation as a BUILD GATE, two-sided: red by name on 0.240 and 0.180 with the drawn height printed, green on the 0.155 that ships. The node side is unchanged and re-derived: 0.90 * 0.40 * ROCK_SCALE_MIN is still 0.27 m, and the spire's own Low is 2.60 * 0.28 = 0.728 m authored, so it does not become the new minimum. **(6) RN-245 / RN-246, THE SMALL STUFF.** `Mtn_RockSpire` and `Mtn_TalusChunk` REMOVED from the Mountains atlas (grep over `web/src` finds two lines of Registry comment and no table row), replaced by three sub-cap forms because a scree slope in one prop is a repeating stamp: `Mtn_ScreeSheet` (14 plates), `Mtn_TalusFan` (7 chunky blocks) and `Mtn_FrostShards` (9 platy splinters, each spun about Z, because rx/ry are world axes and an anisotropic fragment without that rotation combs the field). The atlas now has NO collision proxy: it is all ankle debris. `Hills_ScreePatch` re-authored off `pc.chips` cones and lowered to 0.168 m, which also took props_hills from **12 coplanar same-facing pairs to 0**. **`sink` is 0 in every decoration and the reason cost a render**: on a pile that is ENTIRELY fragments, `Parts.fit` shifts everything up by the DEEPEST burial, so every shallower fragment hovers. The first Hills frame is a scree field of floating chips. **INVARIANTS: `validate_glb.py --all` 51/51** (rock_spire is the 51st); **`check_coplanar` 0 over allowance** with all four boulders, rock_spire, props_mountains and props_hills at 0; **every asset rebuilt byte-identical**, before the pass, after it, and again after re-wrapping comments. `ASSET-SPECS.md` corrected in the same commits (the node table, the boulder budget table whose ore-material column still named the RN-156-superseded ITEM metals, the atlas table, the collision table). **NOT DONE, NAMED:** `Plains_PebbleA` still over the corrected cap; nothing new draws until a client lane adds Registry rows for the three Mountains stems and an ART entry for the spire; the four boulders still ship ONE seed each, and `ART` is already a LIST per kind, so seeded sibling files are a cheap follow-up; ALL MATERIAL VALUES (albedo, roughness, colour) UNTOUCHED and owed to look-dev. Commits `10e0326`, `54c5b38`. Screenshots `RN241_<kind>_{before,after}_rest_f0_{surfnode,detnode}`, `RN243_lineup_{before,after}{,_low}`, `RN243_seeds_{stone,copper}_after`, `RN244_spire_after_{full,foot}`, `RN245_mtndecor_*`, `RN246_hillsdecor_*`, `RN247_crags_after`.
