> **RN-271 to RN-284 (2026-08-01): THE FOREST REPEATED BECAUSE EVERY TREE WAS A SOLID OF REVOLUTION, AND THE ENGINE WAS ALREADY ROTATING THEM.** First pass under `docs/web/ART-DIRECTION.md` (KSP was a MECHANICS reference and never an art one; the target is realistic, detailed, complex; "clean" is now a defect). Blender only, no browser, no build, no probe, no dev server: Reid was gaming and every build burst stutters his machine. Commits `521bf39` (canopy) and `484800e` (harvest trees). **(1) THE CAUSE, AND IT WAS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN.** RN-183 named the parasol repetition and could not say why RN-61's per-instance tint and scale did not touch it. RN-63 had the mechanism for a different family: *"rotation does not change a silhouette viewed from a level camera"*. `ScatterEmit.ts:153` yaws every scatter instance through a hashed full turn and `NodeBatch.ts:353` says harvest nodes differ "only in yaw", while all three canopy trees and both harvest trees were stacks of concentric rings and axis-centred domes. **The forest was not short of variety mechanisms; it was short of anything for them to act on.** That is why this is an ASSET fix and not a scatter fix. **(2) RN-271, THE INSTRUMENT, `tools/blender/flora_silhouette.py`.** Rotates an exported LOD0 about its own up axis, rasterises the outline, reports width cv, area cv, mean pairwise IoU between yaws, quarter-turn IoU, and the crown centroid offset. No renderer and no lighting, so a geometry pass can run ahead of look development. `--foliage` reports WG-14's bare-trunk ratio off the exported bytes. **Twelve yaws, 256 grid, HEAD against now: Canopy_Pine width cv 0.0539 to 0.1437 / IoU 0.845 to 0.465; Canopy_Broadleaf 0.0789 to 0.0828 / IoU 0.658 to 0.381; Canopy_Fir 0.0125 to 0.0975 / IoU 0.822 to 0.617; TreeConifer 0.0202 to 0.0686 / IoU 0.668 to 0.549; TreeBroadleaf 0.0424 to 0.0888 / IoU 0.607 to 0.501.** IoU 1.000 is the defect. The fir's projected width used to move 1.2 percent over a whole revolution, which is faceting and nothing else. **(3) RN-272 / RN-280 / RN-281 / RN-283, THE GEOMETRY.** Crowns are heavy BOUGHS (new `tree_common.limb`, a tapered tube swept along a polyline, and `tree_common.arc`, a path that rises, flattens and lets its outer end fall) at uneven azimuths and unequal reach, clothed ALONG their length by overlapping foliage clumps, plus a bare or broken limb on every tree. Reach carries TWO harmonics of azimuth: the first leans the crown off the axis (drives IoU), the second makes it elliptical in plan (drives width). Trunks: five rings for a curved taper instead of two for a constant one, per-ring `offsets` for a real sweep (`lean` interpolates ONE displacement and can only make a tilted dowel), a coherent azimuthal RIDGE running the length of the stem in the same direction as RN-100's bark fissures, and a lobed root FLARE. **Ridge, flare and sweep cost ZERO triangles: they reshape rings that already existed.** The conifer also gains three real boughs inside its crown, because RN-69's 121 blades all sprang straight off the trunk. **(4) RN-273, THE STRUCTURAL FINDING: `Parts.fit` INTO A SQUARE BOX NORMALISES AWAY PLAN ANISOTROPY.** It scales X and Y independently to fill `size` exactly, so the first draft rebuilt all three canopy crowns out of asymmetric boughs and still measured a plan aspect of **1.019** on the pine, with width cv 0.023 even though yaw-to-yaw IoU had already collapsed from 0.845 to 0.352. Footprints are now non-square and state the aspect the tree has (canopy 3.85x2.55, 2.90x2.20, 8.40x10.50; harvest 2.62x2.10, 4.35x3.55), with the MEAN held near the old value so forest density does not move as a side effect. `validate_glb` caught both harvest dimension changes BY NAME before the contract rows moved. **(5) RN-274, BUDGET RAISES, ARGUED.** props_canopy was at EXACTLY every budget it had (310/310 LOD0, 932/932 total, every part on its own maximum), so any structure at all needed a raise: Pine 296 to 742, Fir 248 to 334, Broadleaf 310 to 784, LOD2s 26/22/30 to 50/28/58, file total 932 to 1996. **The price, stated: canopy LOD0 draws inside WG-17's 78 m radius and casts into three cascades, so four draws per tree, and the Forest site holds roughly 75 of them in that radius; about 85,600 triangles becomes about 185,000, roughly +9 percent of a scene that measured 1,139,594 at RN-183. NOT re-measured in engine, because this pass had no browser.** The harvest trees needed no raise (conifer 721 to 791 of 900, broadleaf 592 to 634 of 1000). **(6) RN-275 / RN-284, THE WG-13 SCENERY RULE IS NOW A BUILD GATE, AND RE-MEASURING BOTH FAMILIES FOUND ITS MARGIN HAD GONE.** WG-14 measured the SCENERY fractions off the exported bytes and never published the HARVEST ones, so nothing in the project recorded that the broadleaf pair is separated by 0.11 of height and by nothing else. This pass moved the scenery broadleaf 0.632, 0.507, 0.558, 0.468, 0.516, 0.531 while nothing in the build said a word, and at 0.531 against the harvest tree's 0.517 the two were the same tree at two sizes on the one signal WG-13 exists to protect. `build_props_canopy.assert_bare_trunk` now measures the file it just wrote and refuses the build; the floor was raised to 0.58 and it refused twice (0.558, 0.578) before the number cleared. **Shipped: scenery 0.586 / 0.597 / 0.623 (broadleaf, pine, fir), harvest 0.517 / 0.091 (broadleaf, conifer), plus 2.1x the height and 2.4x the crown width on the broadleaf pair. Ratio margin 0.069 where it had reached 0.014.** Negative control: floor to 0.70, build refused BY NAME (`Canopy_Pine at 0.578, floor 0.70`), reverted, glb byte-identical. **THE GENERAL FORM: a rule that separates two families cannot be checked by measuring one of them.** **(7) RN-278, THE RENDERS, `tools/blender/render_flora.py`.** `render_check.py` frames a 1.8 m player and cannot draw more than one copy of anything, and ONE COPY CANNOT ANSWER THIS QUESTION: a lane that only ever renders one tree can ship a beautiful asset that still tiles. The group shot places instances with yaw and non-uniform scale drawn from the CLIENT's own distributions (ScatterEmit's full turn, `ScatterLook.scaleFor`'s 0.14 width jitter and 0.84 to 1.20 height band) inside WG-17's 78 m LOD0 radius; `studio:` frames a whole tree from two bearings; `base:` frames the bottom two metres from a standing player's eye, because a buttress is 3 percent of a whole-tree frame. **`RN271_before_group.png` against `RN271_after_group.png` is the money shot.** **(8) THE PICTURE CAUGHT WHAT THE NUMBERS COULD NOT, THREE TIMES, which is DW-7 in the direction this project usually gets wrong.** Draft one scored well on every silhouette statistic and rendered as flat discs on sticks, because one mass per bough leaves the limb bare for its whole length: a table top rotates perfectly well. Draft two over-corrected with vertical clump scatter and separated them into floating plates. The `base:` view's first framing was aimed at 0.72 m from 2.45 m and cropped the ground contact out, i.e. aimed just past the thing it exists to show. **(9) RN-276 / RN-277, HOUSEKEEPING WITH TEETH.** `build_props_canopy._bands` generated its rings with the same trigonometry as `tree_common.taper_bands`, so the new shape maths would have had two implementations; `_bands` is now a wrapper keeping only what was genuinely local to it, the cap policy and the UV policy. Two stale premises corrected in place rather than left to trip someone up: this file refused crossed-quad impostors because "there is no texture pipeline in this project", which RN-176 to RN-183 made false (the refusal survives on its other reason, that a crossed quad has no silhouette edge on), and the LOD2 band was documented as 150 m to 500 m when WG-17 pinned it at 78 m. **CONTROLS:** all four flora assets rebuilt byte-identical before any edit and after every one; the three untouched flora assets stayed byte-identical to HEAD across every `tree_common` change, which is what proves the shared-module extension inert; `validate_glb.py --all` 50/50 at the start and 49/50 at the end, the one failure being `props_mountains`, the sibling rock lane's in-flight `Mtn_RockSpire` retirement and not this lane's; `check_coplanar` 0 over allowance on all 51 with the four flora assets at 0 same and 0 naive; `contracts.json` and `of_lib.py` are shared with that lane and only rows this lane owns were touched; index empty before and after both commits, verified with `git diff --cached --name-status`, and every new screenshot confirmed staged as `A`; zero em dashes verified in Python on every file. **NO SUBAGENTS SPAWNED**, and one Blender at a time, sequential, to keep Reid's machine quiet. **NOT DONE AND NAMED: the understorey and ground flora** (`bush_scrub` is untouched and still measures IoU 0.745, the highest in the set; `props_forest`'s fern likewise), **the in-engine frame cost of the raise**, and **the geometry-versus-texture split under the new target**, all in section 7.

> **RN-176 to RN-183 (2026-07-30): THE IMAGE PIPELINE EXISTS, THE FLORA WEARS IT, AND THE FILL-RATE CLIFF DID NOT COME.** RN-101's deferral is closed, in three commits that each boot (`302c082`, `322c443`, `8cedf5a`). **(1) RN-176, THE DESIGN DECISION: textures reach flora by the SURFACES MANIFEST (sidecar), not GLB embedding.** Four reasons: the batch paths DISCARD glTF materials by construction, so an embedded image dies exactly where it is needed while the manifest already owns the role-to-family binding that survives both paths; the bark additive-family precedent; one shared map per family instead of per GLB; and the player-skin follow-on (RN-125) is already planned through this manifest, so one pipeline serves an alpha card AND a future opaque skinned map. The manifest schema gains a second FAMILY SHAPE, additively: an albedo card family carries `albedo` + `uv_space:'unit'` + `wrap {u:repeat, v:clamp}` + `alpha_test` + `albedo_mean`, no normal/orm/tile_m, version unchanged, and the client consumes whichever shape a family declares (the slice-A tolerance is structural: `f.normal.file` on an albedo family would have thrown inside `ready` and taken every family down). **(2) RN-177/178, THE TEXTURES:** texgen.py gains an RGBA writer (colour type 6, same pinned zlib) and two deterministic card families, `leaf` (frond, coverage 0.665, mean 0.7821) and `grass` (11-blade bundle, coverage 0.595, mean 0.734), 256 px, ~84 KB disk, 0.68 MB VRAM the estimator does not see. **Albedo is DILATED under the alpha** so bilinear/mip sampling cannot pull halo colour (negative control: undilated build fails the halo bound 251.7 vs 64, by name); **coverage sits ABOVE the 0.35 cutoff on purpose so far mips converge toward SOLID rather than eroding**: distant crowns keep the shipped look, which bounds far cost and far benefit at once, and that trade is the design. Selftest 36, check 65, six old PNGs byte-identical, new ones byte-identical across builds. **(3) RN-180, THE HARD HALF RN-101 NAMED, resolved one layer earlier than expected:** `copyUv` already carries UVs through both batch merges unconditionally, so the batch path needed NO map-survival surgery; what did not exist was card-space UVs on the meshes. Every foliage primitive (5 roles, 10 assets, through the shared helpers: blade strips base-v0 to tip-v1, grass on hashed narrow bands of the bundle, leaf full-card with hashed mirror, shells azimuth x3 wrapped, broadleaf quad cards, stump caps on a ring around the opaque centre so alpha cannot cut sapwood) now authors unit UVs, `of_lib.MeshBuilder` gained the mixed authored/projected path, and the whole rebuild was proven VISUALLY INERT before the switch: served-swap pair 0.21% against a 0.20% same-assets cross-load floor, invariants identical to the digit. Pre-edit rebuilds byte-identical first, all ten; positions byte-exact after (conifer: 4 prims re-split, identical triangle multiset). **(4) RN-179, THE VALIDATOR:** `uv_authored_materials` (range/span checks replacing the metre equality per listed material) and `textures` (family exists, carries albedo, size within declared max_px, **and alpha:true REQUIRES the family to declare alpha_test**, refusing with the grey-white failure mode named), all contract-driven, all shown red by name first. **(5) RN-181 to RN-183, THE SWITCH AND THE NUMBERS.** Role move atomic across texgen + client tables + manifest in one commit; client attaches sRGB map + alphaTest 0.35 + colour scaled 1/albedo_mean (three composes color x map x vertexColour x instanceTint, so an uncompensated map darkens every card by its own mean; baseColor captured so the toggle restore is exact). NodeBatch wind hook moved from `flat:` to `leaf:`/`grass:` prefixes, which also retired the latent water-pool sway. **Grass at walking distance: 15.29% of pixels move, 111,524 darker / 62,731 lighter** (both ways; darker lean is RN-78's tone-curve mechanism). **In-page matched pair, wind frozen, NO sim advance between captures** (the first draft ran the sim between captures and its 'restore' read 950 tiles of creature motion): off-to-on 5,889 of 14,400 tiles, up 2,472 / down 3,417, peak 121.9; **restore BIT-EXACT, 0 tiles, hash equal**. **Sky-through-crown (RN-72's instrument promoted to `tools/smoke/skythrough.mjs`): 3.94% to 4.50% (+13% relative) at a ~25 m conifer, beside a sky control box at EXACTLY 0 of 66,000 px.** Near crown: punched cutouts, leaflet serrations, no halo, no autumn cast (`RN183_crown_near.png`). **THE FILL-RATE CLIFF: measured A/B/A/B interleaved in one page at the Forest site (1,139,594 tris), frameMs p50 on 9.4/9.9 vs off 9.9/9.1, p95 12.6/12.7 vs 13.6/12.6: deltas straddle zero, verdict NO RESOLVABLE COST at this scene**, stated as method rather than a number invented from noise. **The grey-white silent drop shown red then green:** map assignment nulled in scratch, floratex fails by name, including 'moves BOTH ways' failing because colour compensation alone moves the frame ONE-sided lighter, so the probe distinguishes even the half-alive state; reverted byte-identical, green. Wind regression re-proof both ways on the textured build (probes/wind.js all green; `?wind=0` bit-exact stillness); leafvar composes (colour attribute and map are independent multipliers). `?leaftex=0` restores the pre-texture flora in one flag, asserted bare. **Invariants, walk site, one binary off/on: programs 35 to 37 (standard-material map+alphaTest variants; DW-10 UNCHANGED at 4 ShaderMaterial + 2 live onBeforeCompile), textures +2, calls 61/61, triangles 540,665/540,665, geometries 53/53, vramEstimate 104.2/104.2 (blind to shared maps; surfaces report 7.83 MB total incl. the ore lane's).** Clean `git archive HEAD` freeze boots smoke-PASS at abi 21. **TASK-4 FIDELITY ITEM, NAMED NOT FIXED: the mid-field canopy parasol repetition.** In every forest frame (`RN182_forest_on`, `RN183_crown_near` background band) the props_canopy trees repeat ONE umbrella silhouette by the dozen; RN-61's per-instance tint and scale ship and do not touch it because the repetition is SILHOUETTE. Scoped fix: 2 to 3 authored silhouette variants in build_props_canopy riding the existing per-stem registration (no new batch, no new material), a builder-and-rebuild pass of its own. **PROCESS INCIDENTS, all NUMBERS.md classes, all survived:** a reused port answered plausibly with another lane's server after mine lost a restart race (caught by a bundle-name sentinel; measurement moved to a fresh port); the ore lane's whole-file commit SWEPT my in-flight NodeBatch edit (content correct at HEAD, `8c4cd8e`) and my half-landed texgen role move, which broke their clean-archive boot and they unswept it at HEAD (`17411dd`), costing one re-apply; the first cross-load pair read 1.67% until wind and animation were frozen (floor 0.49% wind-frozen, 0.20% both-frozen). Filtered blobs kept every commit to my own hunks. Screenshots `RN182_forest_on`, `RN182_grass_close_{on,off}`, `RN183_crown_{on,off,near}`, `RNB_ctl_w0a0_{a,b}`, `RNB_uv_w0a0`.
> **RN-146 to RN-158 (2026-07-30): THE SECOND GROUND PASS. THE PALE DISC WAS A TOOL DECAL, THE RN-78 TEXTURE HAD NEVER DRAWN FOR A PLAYER, AND THE GROUND FINALLY HAS RELIEF THAT IS NOT WATER.** **(1) RN-146, THE PALE DISC, ATTRIBUTED AND FIXED (commit 2a4d661).** RN-81's washed-out disc was never a shader term: it was `LevelRing` at idleAlpha 0.16, the WG-22 levelling footprint, drawn on EVERY key-up tick following the feet forever, an unlit 10 m MeshBasicMaterial film (0.55, 1.0, 0.75 tint, bright rim band at 8.6 to 10 m) that ignored sun and atmosphere. Attribution was by ELIMINATION inside one page (`probes/discshot.js`): every terrain art, texture, post, atmosphere and shadow toggle left the near-band warmth droop of 19 counts intact, `?levelring=0` alone removed it, and the one-binary flag pair equals the fixed build to the digit (68 calls / 627,295 tris / 35 programs; the ring cost 2 draws, 192 triangles, 2 programs at rest). **TWO INSTRUMENT LESSONS PAID FOR ON THE WAY: a luma-only profile read ~0 on a plainly visible disc because the disc moves HUE (pale blue-green over olive), so the instrument grew a warmth (r-b) axis; and the disc had only ever been observed at teleport sites, so a walk-away frame was needed to prove it player-centred rather than world data.** The fix keeps both recorded intents: the active feet-fallback stays, and the idle preview persists a 3 s grace window (`LEVEL.idleGraceTicks`, armed by the key AND by `levelOnce` so the probe path agrees), then hides. `probes/levelgrace.js` is two-sided (rest hidden / application shows / grace expires) and fails by name on the pre-fix build; `level.js` green, with `levelOnce` now drawing the ring in its own frame because the old always-on preview was what its `ringVisible` assertion had been leaning on. **(2) RN-150, THE FINDING THAT OUTRANKS EVERY FEATURE IN THIS PASS (in commit 4286c04): `Number(null)` is 0 and 0 is finite, so `groundTexAmpFromQuery`'s fallback branch was UNREACHABLE and the RN-78 ground texture NEVER DREW A PIXEL in ordinary play; the RN-57 wet-sand band had the same dead default.** Every instrument forced the amp through `setTex` and measured the term perfectly while the shipped default stayed dark, and no invariant depends on the amp, so twelve green site pairs coexisted with a texture Reid never saw. Fixed via `ampParam` (a missing param is MISSING, never 0); `groundshot.js` and `reliefshot.js` now assert the BOOT default is live as part of their fixture, and that assertion goes red by name on the pre-fix build. **(3) RN-147 to RN-149, THE RELIEF (4286c04): `of_ground_relief.png`, four AUTHORED ASYMMETRIC height fields** (noise-warped sawtooth ripples with 78/22 stoss/lee, crack-network clods, terraced scree, stamped litter flakes), because RN-78 proved smooth fields fed to a bump read as choppy water and the difference between water and dirt is ASYMMETRY. groundtex.py asserts the property per channel with symmetric negative controls that fail by name (ripple derivative skew -1.608 vs a 0.8 bar, symmetric triangle -0.001; clod histogram skew -0.780 vs 0.5, fbm -0.091; scree/litter flat fraction 0.74/0.69, fbm 0.11). The shader term is a second separable `ofArtBump` call (sample UNCONDITIONAL beside g1/g2; per-biome `RELIEF_W` interpolated as vMatW; steep ground rides coverSel), with TWO fades and each is a measured scar: `OF_RELIEF_FINE_M` 0.45 for the footprint (the mip chain bounds the sampled VALUE but never its GRADIENT, re-photographed as moire arcs on the vnoise constant) and a 30 to 60 m distance fade completing inside the max-depth ring (the chunk UV's world size doubles per LOD step, and a coarser ring drew the ripples at double wavelength as a chunk-shaped patch). Default amp 0.08 MEASURED DOWN from 0.30 (grazing sun crushed every trough black; 92% of the beach frame moved, 71% darker). **Pairs at the site's own solved GRAZING morning AND local noon, one binary (`probes/reliefshot.js`): beach 86.5% moved (707k darker/279k lighter, the directional lee-shadow lean) vs 19.5% at noon; plains 63.6/20.2; forest 29.3/17.0; hills 66.1/48.2; invariants IDENTICAL both ways at every site to the digit.** VRAM: +5.59 MB (1024 RGBA + mips) that `vramEstimateMB` does not see, RN-78's precedent; 1024 stays (3.5 mm/texel against a 2 to 4.6 mm walking footprint: at the limit, and relief needs no more). Plains turf 0.19 to 0.24 and Forest clod 0.14 to 0.18 in MAT_W. TerrainShader's vertex stage and BAYER moved to TerrainVertex.glsl.ts / TerrainDither.glsl.ts purely for the 400-line cap, GLSL unchanged to the character, import sites re-exported. **(4) RN-156 to RN-158, THE ORE SEAM SPLIT (8c4cd8e), Admin's ruling implemented:** roles IronOre 6E7B8A / CopperOre 9A5228 / CoalSeam 1A1B1E, every one under the 0.5 metalness batching split ON PURPOSE (the old seams rode the ITEM roles into `coarse:metal` at metalness 1.0, a mirror whose only image is the sky: the RN-81 ice crown and near-black copper). New texgen family `ore` (384 px, 0.5 m tile): warped vein banding, crystalline grain, ORM roughness spread 0.42..1.0 because roughness VARIATION is what sells mineral; anisotropy asserted like bark's WITH the negative control bark never had (wrong-axis bands land at 0.35 vs the 1.50 bar, by name). Client half in the SAME commit (RN-100's rule): ROLE_FAMILY rows, the `ore` Family, `nodes:ore:matte` at roughness 0.72 / metalness 0.25. No geometry moved: BIN chunks byte-identical all four boulders, stone byte-identical whole, validate 50/50, coplanar 0 over, maps.js green. **DELIBERATELY NO THIRD onBeforeCompile: the brief's triplanar question dissolves because the boulders already wear box-projected family maps through `attachSurface`, and the deficiency was per-role PBR constants plus item colours, both fixed at ZERO hooks. DW-10 ledger unchanged both ways: 4 ShaderMaterial + 2 live onBeforeCompile, and programs identical off/on in every pair of this pass.** **(5) RN-151, THE SWEEP I COMMITTED AND UNSWEPT (17411dd), the pass's own worst moment:** my ore commit regenerated surfaces.json from the shared working texgen.py and LAUNDERED the flora lane's uncommitted RN-181 table move into HEAD without its client half; a clean archive booted with a five-role mismatch failing every smoke run. Repaired by restoring the five roles to FLAT_ROLES with their original reasons; the flora hunk returned to their working tree. **The general lesson, now in the commit for NUMBERS.md's family: a filtered blob is not enough when a COMMITTED ARTIFACT is GENERATED from a shared source file; the generator's input must be filtered too.** After the repair a clean HEAD freeze boots with zero console errors, wasm cmp-identical. **(6) RN-152/RN-153, THE NIGHT ADDENDUM (29f7bd8):** PH-86's first real night measured mid-field terrain at 0/255 (raw ~2/255, the grade's dark end crushes it). `terrainNightAmbient` writes base + blue-shifted starlight (0.055, 0.065, 0.095) into the SHARED `TERRAIN_AMBIENT` object once per frame from Systems, gated 0.03 to -0.05 of the one sun elevation everything reads: both terrain materials and the sky ground shell hold the Color by reference (RN-64's consistency), so the floor reaches every consumer with zero shader changes and zero programs. Night mid-field 5.5 to 7.6/255 against a 0.1 sky (silhouettes read; clearly night); `?starlight=0` restores the PH-86 black exactly (0/0.02); noon boxes identical on/off to the hundredth. The headlamp's gate keyed on sky occlusion only and had NEVER lit a surface night; now max(occlusion, night) over the same elevation band: lampCd 0 to 46 at midnight, and the lamp-off/on night pair moves 93,566 px, 93% LIGHTER at meanDelta 68 (an additive light can only add). **Headlamp.ts and player/LevelAction.ts were sanctioned crossings (Admin's addendum and the RN-146 brief), flagged for the owning controllers.** **A vacuous control caught mid-pass: the first day-identity pair ran with `--starlight=0` UNFORWARDED, both sides on; GP-156's greppable signature, found because the discriminating night run was cheap.** **NOT DONE, NAMED:** Mountains/Polar relief pairs not shot (no pinned site coordinates in the probe tables; Polar's near-zero weights are the design and are asserted only by table); the wet-sand band coming alive changes pond shorelines, flagged to the water lane; the FP arms stay day-lit at night (view-model hemisphere is occlusion-keyed only, pre-existing); ore GROUND_COLOUR / chip colours untouched (items unchanged per the ruling); the `nodes:ore:matte` batch is one more never-culled node batch (+4 draws, rides RN-100's debt); the ore boulders' iron seam at the RN158_ore_k3 camera sits in its own spire's shadow, so the iron after-frame undersells the split at that sun. > **RN-77 to RN-81 (2026-07-30): THE GROUND SKINNING PASS. THE TERRAIN GETS ITS FIRST TEXTURE, THE UNDERGROUND STOPS BEING GRASS-GREEN, AND THE PASS BURNED FOUR WRONG DIAGNOSES BEFORE THE RIGHT TWO.** **(1) RN-77, THE ASSET: `assets/textures/dist/of_ground.png`,** 1024x1024 RGBA, 1,436,496 bytes, generated by new `tools/blender/groundtex.py`, which IMPORTS texgen.py's deterministic primitives (hash noise, worley, the pinned-zlib PNG writer at bpp 4) and does not touch texgen or surfaces.json: the terrain does not go through Surfaces.ts, and a texgen family would upload two maps to every machine batch for nothing. Four channel-packed VALUE fields, R grass clump / G rock grain / B granular / A clod, every channel remapped so its MEAN is 0.5 and its p2..p98 span is exactly 0.72, because the mean is what the mip chain converges to and 0.5 is the modulation identity: minification retires the term into itself. **The first R channel was worley F1 and the offset-by-half sheet read as POLKA-DOT ground before it ever reached the terrain (failure mode named, then seen, then removed); the first _centre() used the median and shipped a 3% net brighten on skewed fields.** Tiling verified three ways: wrap-step <= worst interior step per channel in selftest AND on the shipped quantised bytes, plus offset-by-half sheets INSPECTED (no seam cross). Two builds byte-identical at sha 9ec9dfaddb79; selftest 15/15, check 16/16 over 1,048,576 texels; texgen's shipped set untouched, asserted by sha. **(2) RN-78, THE SHADER TERM, AND THE COORDINATE DECISION THE BRIEF ASKED FOR: the per-quad chunk UV CAN carry texture lookups at the near ring, and does.** Integer repeats per quad (16 and 5, i.e. 3.6 m and 11.6 m tiles at depth 14) make same-depth chunk edges phase-continuous by arithmetic; pM was rejected by RN-45's own numbers (0.03125 m quantum = ~9 texels of stair-step plus a dead uv derivative into the sampler's mip logic). Per-biome vec4 channel weights (`BiomePalette.biomeMatWeights`, interpolated across biome edges exactly as vBiomeColor), the rock-grain branch riding the SAME coverSel smoothstep as the rock albedo. **FOUR DIAGNOSES IN A ROW WERE WRONG AND EACH IS A KEEPER.** (a) An 89%-darker one-way split was blamed on HTMLImage alpha PREMULTIPLY and the load path moved to createImageBitmap premultiplyAlpha:'none' (kept: it is the correct loader for data-in-alpha), but the picture did not change. (b) A harsh speckle band at 15 to 60 m was blamed on the anisotropy budget and swept 16/8/4/1 WITH NO EFFECT, which is the finding: **a filtering defect that filtering settings cannot touch is not a filtering defect.** (c) The real first cause: **the texture2D calls sat inside if (texW > 0.0), NON-UNIFORM control flow, where an implicit-derivative fetch has UNDEFINED LOD** - mip-0 speckle at 40 m and deep-mip mush at 3 m in the same frame. Samples are now unconditional. (d) The band that SURVIVED that fix was the texture riding the RN-50 surface-gradient bump: a derivative scales a field by its own frequency, so filtering bounds the sampled VALUE but never its gradient, and at 2.4, at 0.8, and coarse-field-only at 2.0 every biome's walking frame photographed as CHOPPY WATER, because **a smooth metre-scale undulation IS liquid's visual signature whatever its height.** THE TEXTURE DRIVES ALBEDO ONLY; the bump ships as RN-50's vnoise, byte-for-byte of behaviour. Calibration measured down: weight sums 0.6 to 0.3 (the 0.6 table photographed as speckle soup), plus a 1.6x near boost inside ~20 m because dark biomes photographed nearly flat at the feet without it. **MEASURED, one binary, in-page setTex(0)-vs-setTex(1) pairs one settled toggle apart (probes/groundshot.js, which asserts its own fixture: texState() must report the 1024 map, not the 1x1 placeholder - a placeholder pair is bit-identical BY CONSTRUCTION and reads as a dead term when it is a dead fetch), 12 site/pitch pairs across Beach/Plains/Forest/Hills/Mountains/Polar at each site's own LOCAL NOON (a pinned t is a different local time per longitude; sitelook.js's lesson, relearned once at dusk).** The full per-site diff table is in the Completion Report; the split leans darker BY MECHANISM (the tone curve compresses the bright half of a linear-symmetric modulation) and the two-sided movement is the property assertion. Per-biome walking and mid-field pairs in docs/screenshots/RN78_*. **INVARIANTS at the RN-15 camera, props on, one binary, off/on: draw calls 82/82, triangles 1,001,122/1,001,122, programs 37/37, geometries 53/53, textures 94/94, vramEstimateMB 93.7/93.7.** The texture itself is +1 texture and **5.59 MB of VRAM (1024^2 x 4 x 4/3) that vramEstimateMB DOES NOT SEE** (it reads post targets and the chunk pool; RN-51's grab precedent), published here instead. Baseline drift since RN-71 (52 geometries, 88 textures) is other lanes' landed work plus this +1. **DW-10 LEDGER, stated both ways: 4 ShaderMaterial + 2 live onBeforeCompile after DW-38 ratified the sibling lane's wind hook; this pass adds ZERO to either count** - the lookups live inside the terrain's existing custom program, and programs 37/37 both ways is the proof the term did not fork it. ?groundtex=0 and ?groundtexamp= registered in run.mjs; __ofTerrainArt.{setTex,getTex,texState}. CASCADE_GLSL moved to new CascadeShadow.glsl.ts purely for the 400-line cap, GLSL unchanged to the character, re-exported from TerrainShader so RN-52's published import site holds. **(3) RN-80, THE UNDERGROUND: dug faces are coloured by DEPTH BELOW THE DESIGNED SURFACE** - BiomePalette.dugAlbedo (topsoil 0-0.4 m, subsoil to ~2 m, then the terrain's own rock constant so a deep cut arrives at the substance cliffs are drawn with), fed by VoxelSkin.faceColours from of_base_height (the DESIGNED relief, NOT the edited surface, which reports a pit floor at depth zero and would keep it grass), with an integer-hash vertex mottle quantised to 0.25 m so re-meshing a brick cannot re-roll the dirt. **CROSS-DOMAIN, FLAGGED LOUDLY: VoxelSkin.ts and the faceColours call site in VoxelMesh.ts are web/src/world/ files.** The brief assigns the dug LOOK to rendering under the DW-26 data/look split (the water precedent, RN-51); the rule itself lives in my BiomePalette.ts and the world/ diff is a consumer wiring of ~10 lines. Admin should log the ownership note. **MEASURED with probes/digskin.js, the same scripted 14-strike dig on TWO builds (this one and a HEAD control archived to a scratchpad tree, wasm cmp-identical to HEAD): pit-interior r/g 0.8884 to 0.9299 here against 0.8884 to 0.9064 on HEAD - 2.3x the shift toward earth, same lumaDrop (~40) both, dug 14/14 both, and the frames show the same cavity with warm brown walls here and grey rock walls there** (docs/screenshots/RN80_dug_{head,rn80}.png). rgBefore IDENTICAL to 4 decimals across builds is its own control: the r/g ratio is invariant under the scalar texture modulation, so the dig comparison is clean of RN-78. **(4) RN-81, THE ORE BOULDERS, EVALUATED AND DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED.** Close-ups of all four (probes/oreshot.js, deposit.js's approach and aim search; docs/screenshots/RN81_ore_*.png): the RN-73..76 geometry reads well, but the SEAM MATERIALS do not separate at a glance - OF_Iron photographs as pale blue-white that reads as ICE on the crown facets, OF_Copper and OF_Coal read near-black at any sun that is not overhead. The fix is a materials question and the ore roles are SHARED with the item chunks and machines through of_lib.PALETTE, so a unilateral per-boulder recolour is the two-authority failure; **needs an Admin decision: either per-boulder ore materials (OF_IronOre distinct from OF_Iron) or a palette change that moves the items with it.** No boulder bytes moved this pass; validate_glb untouched by construction. **PROCESS NOTES: one lane-named scratch dir (web/dist-rnskin, deleted at pass end), ports 4272/4273 own-killed before serving, 4200 never touched; tsc proven alive by a deliberate TS2322 before its silence was trusted; one backtick trap (INSTRUMENTS.md's tsc-outage entry) walked into inside the GLSL template literal and caught by the build; probes/groundshot.js's first fixture check was a HEAD fetch that itself failed every smoke run as a requestfailed ERR_ABORTED - the instrument was the failure, replaced by texState().** NOT DONE, NAMED: structured relief at clod scale needs authored asymmetric height fields (sharp-ridge/flat-facet, a groundtex.py extension), the ore-patch ground skin (game/OreField.ts, not mine) still reads as flat stain and would take the same texture treatment, and the ore seam materials await the Admin decision above. Commits recorded in the Completion Report; screenshots docs/screenshots/RN78_*, RN80_*, RN81_*.

> **RN-121 to RN-125 (2026-07-30): THE FIRST CREATURE THAT WALKS, THE GATE THAT PROVED THE MIXER, AND A PLUME THAT HAD TO REACH ORBIT TO BE MEASURED.** **(1) RN-121, THE GATE, ANSWERED BEFORE ANY ART.** Does this client play skeletal animation? YES, and it always did: `build_player_body.py` ships a 44-bone armature with 14 clips through `validate_glb.py`'s generic contract-driven rig checks (`bones`/`skin_weights`/`rest_pose`/`anim_t0` activate off contract keys, NO validator change was needed for a new rigged asset), `Loaders.ts`'s one GLTFLoader preserves skinning ONLY on the `PlayerRig` path, and exactly two `AnimationMixer`s ticked, both from `Avatar.animate` on `loop.simSecs`. What was missing was the flag and the instrument. **`?anim=0` (`Config.anim`) freezes every skeletal mixer**; the guard lives in `Avatar.animate` and in each consumer, never in `Systems.ts` (the frame loop knows nothing about it), and `avatar.animLive` is published so a zero-motion reading can be told from a dropped URL flag. `probes/animgate.js` asserts the CYCLE, not a magnitude: the Idle clip is exactly 2.0 s, so frames at t/t+1.0 must differ strongly and t/t+2.0 must nearly return, and the frozen build must show nothing. **Frozen build of `3486ab5`, smoke PASS: live FP 66 tiles over 2.5 counts at half period, 0 at full; TP 23/0; frozen 0/0 with max residual 2.47.** **INSTRUMENT FINDING: this scene is NOT bit-exact across `of.run` even fully frozen.** 13 to 32 tiles at up to 2.72 counts move per simulated second, identical between `?anim=1` and `?anim=0`, unmoved by `?gameplay=0`, `?interp=0`, `?shadows=0`. Camera-side micro-motion, bounded, source an open question recorded in the probe; the claim is two-sided ABOVE the floor (threshold 3.0 against signal 16 to 138). Also re-earned: writing a runner's stderr log INTO `web/` triggers the Vite watcher and kills the run with "Execution context was destroyed". **(2) RN-122, THE SPIDER ASSET** (subagent-built to an exhaustive brief, `build_spider.py`): 28 bones (Root/Abdomen/Thorax/Head + Coxa/Femur/Tibia times eight legs), LOD0 1,060 of 1,100, total 1,960 of 2,600, 4 materials, 4.53 x 1.59 x 3.96 m at unit scale, drawn at scale = `EnemyTypes.radiusM`. **Spider_Walk (0.8 s) stance is a constant-rate ramp on the 2.100 m foot lever: 27.28 deg in 0.400 s IS the 2.5 m/s the client retimes against, derived not tuned. Spider_Idle (2.0 s) pins the feet to 7.1 mm residual.** validate 50/50, coplanar 0 by absence, two builds byte-identical, weight sums 1.0000 exactly. **(3) RN-123/124, THE CLIENT HALF, AND THE LADDER.** `SpiderFlock.ts`: at most 8 skinned rigs that the CLOSEST creatures are promoted into (claim 80 m, release 100 m, sticky), each ONE SkinnedMesh because the authored primitives are merged at load with material colour baked to vertex colour: 4 draw calls per rig, at most +32 in a fight, per-type tint as a per-type material colour multiplying the bake. Mixers tick from `Enemies.step`'s fixed dt. The far swarm stays in `EnemyView`'s one BatchedMesh, whose stand-in is now an eight-legged spider with the AUTHORED proportions, so **the claim boundary swaps ANIMATION, not SHAPE** (RN-3's ladder applied to creatures). Load is LAZY on first live swarm and a failure degrades LOUDLY to the batch. **WHY A THIRD MIXER SITE IS NOT DW-8:** DW-8 bans per-object mixers at factory scale O(100k); a swarm is capped by /core at 100 and this pool at 8, each body the player's subject. **MEASURED (candidate build, isolation `props=0 water=0` because the flora lane's wind and the water lane's shimmer otherwise own any motion metric), `probes/spiderwalk.js` valid both modes, smoke PASS:** claimed 8/8 on a 50-Skitterer wave; **batch 46 + rigs 8 = creatures 50 + nests 4 EXACTLY, refused 0**; Spider_Walk on every marcher at timeScale capped 2.2 (Skitterer wants 3.2: A-13's trade, 31% slip accepted over unreadable legs, RECORDED); Spider_Idle on biters; **action time advances across `of.run` live and holds exactly 0 frozen (the cheapest exact mixer-tick statement)**; at the swarm's own heading a half walk period moves **92 of 1,296 tiles over 2.5 counts (max 90.9) against the frozen run's best-anywhere 1 (max 3.1)**; full-frame pngdiff 19,479 px (1.71%) at 4,183/15,296 split vs frozen translation-only 8,937 at even split. **Fight-scene invariants: 107 draw calls of the 150 budget, 55 geometries, 44 programs, 104.2 MB.** Walk-camera invariants IDENTICAL under the flag to the digit: 67/702,795/53/37/80/104.2 both ways, and **programs 37 both ways is the no-fork proof.** The first probe draft teleported INTO the swarm and was eaten (`RN124_spiders_live` of that draft, kept: spiders looming over the corpse IS Reid's "dwarfed" brief). **(4) RN-125, THE PLUME.** Per-nozzle length/width/core-share flicker on smoothed value noise, amplitude follows throttle, opacity flutter; NO shader, matrix writes on meshes that already rewrite every frame; **DW-10 UNCHANGED at 4 ShaderMaterial + 1 onBeforeCompile (+1 more when the flora wind is on, theirs)**; `VesselView.root` parenting untouched. Flicker clock is per-frame BY CHOICE (turbulence is a look; nothing reads it); `?anim=0` zeroes it and restores pre-change rendering exactly. **THREE INSTRUMENTS FAILED BEFORE ONE WORKED:** whole-frame diff of a burn pair moves half the frame on ground scroll; a plume BOX still moves 76% frozen because the flame is additive over scrolling terrain; only a burn IN ORBIT (the orbit cheat) puts flame on black sky. There: **live 5,804 px (17.27%, mean 71.1, two-way 1,578/4,226) vs frozen 978 (2.91%, even), hull control box 1.2%.** Also pinned on the way: **the launch clamp ZEROES thrust** (engine lit, no flame, CLAMPED at 14% throttle), so any two-throttle plume comparison must fly. **(5) OPEN, NAMED:** the player SKIN slice (generated fabric family in texgen + `PlayerRig` map attach by the surfaces manifest, the sibling lanes' PNG rules applied to the suit) was not reached; the box-region diff LANDED as `web/tools/smoke/boxdiff.mjs` on Admin's close-out instruction (run from `web/`; always publish a control box beside the subject box); the throttleUp ramp saturates so a held mid-throttle was not reachable; `bodyRadiusM`'s ranged pin (reach > 2.6 -> 1.05 m) catches the MELEE Colossus and pins the game's largest creature at a 1.05 m body, which caps how massive its spider can honestly be drawn: a cross-lane contract question for Admin, not edited here; an attack/bite clip is the spider's next clip; skinned rigs cast static-pose shadows only in the sense that all casters do, but no shadow of a WALK cycle was separately measured. Commits `3486ab5`, `eac4104`, `35c8b58`, `d6fca3a`, `ac08fcf`. Screenshots `RN121_gate_{live,frozen}`, `RN122_spider_{front,side,midwalk}`, `RN124_column_{near_a,near_b,far}`, `RN124_spiders_{live,frozen}`, `RN125_{orbit_burn_a,orbit_burn_b,ascent_full,pad_clamped_lit}`.

> **RN-102 (2026-07-30): THE LEAVES STOP BEING ONE FLAT GREEN, AND THE PROBE FALSIFIED THE FIRST VERSION BEFORE A FRAME WAS LOOKED AT.** Per-vertex tip tint in the foliage bake: the plant's body loses 10% of red (cool, self-shadowed interior) and its top 40% loses 16% of blue (warm, sun-dried tips), smoothstepped, written into the colour attribute that was already uploaded. Zero draw calls, zero VRAM, zero triangles, zero programs, zero new bytes: **the fifth payout of the already-on-the-GPU attribute** (RN-50's UV, RN-61's instance colour, RN-62's mineral bake, RN-96's position.y, this). **THE FINDING: a normalized Uint8 vertex colour is a MULTIPLIER and cannot exceed 1.0.** The first version raised r at the tips; `probes/leafvar.js` measured 30 tiles, ALL darker, peak 0.86, because "+16% r" clamps to nothing wherever the bake is already bright, which is everywhere above the base band, i.e. exactly the tips, and only the blue cut survived. **A brighter tip is expressible in this encoding only as a DARKER INTERIOR**, so that is what shipped, and the predicted toggle direction flipped from lighter to darker BY MECHANISM rather than by tuning the assertion until it passed. **THE GREEN CHANNEL IS THE INVARIANT, by design:** only r and b are modulated, so `setLeafVar(false)` recovers the untinted bake on a concatenated BatchedMesh buffer by copying g into r and b, no geometry needed, and the restore is asserted BIT-EXACT. **Measured, RN-15 camera, wind frozen, zero-floor matched pair: 2,418 of 14,400 tiles move, all darker (a strictly dimming multiplier can only darken, RN-62's direction rule), peak 2.38, 99.71% inside the props-hidden mask; `?leafvar=0` bakes the pre-RN-102 bytes exactly and the toggle on them is asserted a no-op (0 tiles, identical hash).** Grass close-up pair 15,437 px (1.35%), 14,401 darker; the named failure mode (autumn, a dying field) checked for and absent: tips keep full brightness and warmth, bodies sit cooler behind them. Invariants unchanged both ways: 82 calls, 1,001,122 triangles, 37 programs, 53 geometries, 104.2 MB. Harvest crowns NOT included (NodeBatch bakes an albedo tint, not a gradient, and RN-61 already gave those trees per-instance variety). **DW-38 landed in DECISIONS.md** (Admin ratification of the RN-96 ledger change, 4 ShaderMaterial + 2 live onBeforeCompile, with the `?wind=0` off-switch documented in the entry). PropLibrary.ts cap debt and the Tree_Fall caution are in section 7. Isolation `?leafvar=0`, runtime `__ofProps.setLeafVar`. New: `web/tools/smoke/probes/leafvar.js`. Screenshots `RN102_grass_{before,after}.png`. Commits `b6c5339` (DW-38), `f37a4cb`.

> **RN-96 to RN-99 (2026-07-29): THE FOLIAGE MOVES, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT PROVES IT HAS AN EXACTLY ZERO FLOOR.** Reid asked for "grass blowing in a gentle breeze and leaves on the tree", and it ships as VERTEX SHADER work, never a mixer (props_common.py:41 already said so in 2026-07). **(1) THE MECHANISM.** One shared `onBeforeCompile` (`web/src/render/instancing/PropWind.ts`) assigned to the eight foliage prop batch materials (`isFoliageMaterial`, the SAME predicate the base bake imports, so a rock can never inherit a breeze) and to `nodes:flat:matte` (the harvest trees' merged leaves; the five node kinds are trees plus four boulder kinds, so `flat` cannot hold a non-plant). Injected after `#include <begin_vertex>` where `transformed` and `batchingMatrix` are both in scope; amplitude is LINEAR IN `transformed.y`, metres above the prop's own base (both normalize() paths bake ground-pivoted geometry), so **displacement is zero at the ground BY CONSTRUCTION** and "grass detaches from the ground plane" is unreachable; reach is `min(h,1) + 0.15*max(h-1,0)`, so a 0.6 m grass tip moves ~4 cm and a 12 m canopy crown ~18 cm, the same order as the authored (and never-read) `Tree_Sway` clip's 17 cm. Trunks are never hooked (Bark is not foliage), which retires "the whole tree slides". Per-instance phase is a dot on the batching matrix TRANSLATION, position not spawn time, so a probe's matched pair survives streaming order; time is `Loop.simSecs` pushed in `Systems.ts` beside `TerrainMaterials.update`, so pausing the sim stops the breeze. **(2) DW-10 BOTH WAYS: 4 ShaderMaterial + 2 live onBeforeCompile with wind on, 4 + 1 with `?wind=0`,** because the flag never assigns the hook and the program set is stock. The RN-52-shape argument: a sway moves vertices so post cannot do it; no existing custom shader draws a foliage triangle; a ShaderMaterial replacement would forfeit three's lighting, shadows, batching and Surfaces bindings to buy nothing. One shared function object means one program cache key: **programs read 37 with wind on AND off.** **(3) THE INSTRUMENT.** `of.framehash` renders synchronously and advances no ticks; `__ofWind.freeze(t)` pins the wind clock; so two captures at two frozen wind times differ by NOTHING except the sway, and the floor is not small, it is zero. `probes/wind.js`, RN-15 camera, 160x90 tiles, freeze pair 1.6 s apart: **6,698 of 14,400 tiles move, 3,196 lighter against 3,502 darker (BOTH WAYS, the silhouette rule), 98.98% inside the props-hidden mask; props hidden, two wind times hash IDENTICAL; `windamp=0`, hash IDENTICAL (the hook alone moves no pixel); `?wind=0`, zero hooks and bit-exact stillness.** Invariants IDENTICAL both ways: 78 calls, 1,001,122 triangles, 37 programs, 52 geometries, 104.2 MB. **Zero draw calls, zero VRAM, zero triangles, zero programs, zero new attributes** (the amplitude coordinate was already on the GPU, the fourth time this lane has cashed that cheque). **(4) MY SKY CONTROL WAS BROKEN THE SAME WAY RN-45's WAS, AND THE REPLACEMENT ASSUMES NO GEOMETRY.** The first probe asserted a fixed top-of-frame "pure sky" band moved zero tiles; it failed with 512 moved, because at this camera the hillside rises and canopy crowns stand in those rows (98.98% of them inside the prop mask, so nothing had escaped; the band had stopped being sky, INSTRUMENTS.md's opening entry). Replaced by the props-hidden identity above. **At the spawn clearing that same control INVERTS BY DESIGN and becomes the harvest-tree measurement:** `propsVisible` does not hide `NodeBatch`, so with the scatter hidden and the arms frozen, **127 tiles still move, 55 up / 72 down, peak 23.9: pure crown sway of `nodes:flat:matte`** with everything else excluded by construction (`trees:true` mode). **(5) STATED LIMITS, NOT OVERSIGHTS:** cast shadows do not sway (the depth material is not hooked; at <=18 cm the static shadow is below notice, and hooking three's depth material would spend real complexity on it); a floating-origin rebase (4 km) re-seeds phases, a re-decorrelation and not a pop; normals are not bent by the sway; screenshot pairs across page loads (RN99_*, RN100_*) sit on the ~0.13% cross-load floor and are illustration, the in-page freeze numbers are the claim. Isolation `?wind=0 ?windamp=`, runtime `__ofWind.{state,set,tree,freeze,thaw}`, all in `run.mjs` (committed as a FILTERED BLOB, HEAD plus only my hunk, because the ground lane's uncommitted `groundtex` hunk lives in the same list). Commit `c555fc7`.

> **RN-100 (2026-07-29): EVERY TRUNK WEARS BARK INSTEAD OF ROCK, AND THE FISSURE DIRECTION IS DERIVED FROM THE UV PROJECTION RATHER THAN GUESSED.** Bark/BarkLight were `coarse`, i.e. every trunk wore the ROCK pitting at 0.75 m tile. New texgen family `bark` (384 px, 0.6 m tile, 640 texels/m, +1.50 MB with mips): vertical meandering fissures, plateau ridges, breaks and knots, occlusion in the fissures, roughness 0.84 to 1.0. **Box-projected UVs put world-vertical in v for BOTH horizontal-normal cases** (of_lib `_project_uvs` and validate_glb's equalities agree), so fissures run along v and come out vertical on every trunk side; texgen selftest asserts the anisotropy (6.95, need >1.5) and the negative control (fissures rotated horizontal) fails BY NAME at 0.06. Client `ROLE_FAMILY` moves in the same commit because `verifyAgainstManifest` makes a disagreement a failed smoke run. **Controls: pre-change regeneration byte-identical for every existing png, and still byte-identical after** (only surfaces.json and the two bark files differ); maps.js green UNTOUCHED, because it enumerates no family names, which is the difference between a probe that rots and one that generalises. **Measured, HEAD build against this build, wind FROZEN at t=40 on both sides so the sway cannot pollute a texture pair: forest trunk close-up 30,221 px (2.65%), 13,231 darker / 16,990 lighter; forest mid-field at LOCAL NOON 7,315 px (0.64%); RN-15 camera 5,926 px (0.52%), 3,305 / 2,621.** The darker lean at a low sun and the lighter lean at noon are both the relief working (occlusion vs lit facets). **The first forest pair was shot at NIGHT, WG-53's fixed-t trap** (a body-frame t is a different hour at every longitude); retaken with forestsite.js's noon solver, now also in probes/wind.js. **Invariants: triangles IDENTICAL 1,001,122, programs 37 both ways, calls 78 to 82** (+4: the new `nodes:bark:matte` batch across main plus three cascades, **and it draws even where no harvest node stands** because the node batches are never frustum-culled; a bounded cost, stated), geometries 52 to 53, surfaces VRAM 4.17 to 5.67 MB, `vramEstimateMB` unchanged 104.2 (it does not include the shared surfaces, which is its own recorded gap). The trees-site pair reads 0.11%, UNDER the cross-load floor: two trunks are a few pixels at that camera, the honest limit, same shape as RN-73's. Commit `15f8133`.

> **RN-101 (2026-07-29): LEAF AND GRASS TEXTURES ARE A MEASURED DEFERRAL, NOT AN OMISSION.** The classic answer for the three-triangle canopy blades is an alpha-tested leaf texture. Not taken this pass, four reasons in order of weight. (1) **No image pipeline exists to carry it**: zero of 49 shipped GLBs embed a texture, `of_lib` creates no image node, `NodeBatch` DISCARDS glTF materials entirely (an authored map reaches nothing without batch-key surgery), `PropLibrary`'s batch key would collide two alpha states into one batch, and `contracts.json` has no texture or byte budget key. The two contract comments rejecting alpha cards ("there is no texture pipeline") are now STALE as premises, since DW-35 shipped texgen, and are re-openable; the plumbing above is what actually gates it. (2) **The fill-rate hazard is real and unmeasurable without building it**: ~121 overlapping double-sided blades per conifer at 462 trees in the RN-15 frame is the known way alpha test gets expensive, and no honest number exists until the pipeline does. (3) **RN-69 just won the open-canopy read with GEOMETRY**; alpha cards are a second answer to the same question, and this project's costliest bugs are second answers. (4) The flat_roles reasons are recorded per role and the grass one ("sub-pixel blades at any real viewing distance") still holds at mid-field; the honest arithmetic the deferral does NOT get to lean on: at walking distance (8 m) a 0.6 m blade spans ~100 px, so a texture WOULD resolve there. **What it would take, scoped for a future pass: of_lib image-texture support with embedded PNGs, a texture/byte budget key in contracts.json, PropLibrary batch-key extension, NodeBatch material preservation for mapped roles, and an alpha-test A/B fill-rate pair at the forest site.** Related debt, CORRECTED BY ADMIN and restated in section 7: **Tree_Sway superseded; Tree_Fall status UNPROVEN.** My original framing called both clips dead and was wrong about the second: Tree_Fall is shaped like the harvest felling animation and DW-34's impact-frame contract may reference it, so it must be proven unreferenced by the clip-name contract before any deletion list, not merely unreferenced by the sway path. **ScatterLook.ts: the brief sanctioned its move to `web/src/render/`; it has been there since RN-71.**
>
> **RN-71 to RN-76 (2026-07-28): THE BOULDER LEAVES THE TOPOLOGY, `ScatterLook` LEAVES `world/`, AND I COMMITTED A FILE WHILE ITS AUTHOR WAS STILL WRITING IT.** **(1) RN-72, THE MISTAKE, AND IT IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER.** RN-70 landed `tree_broadleaf.glb` at 138,592 bytes and published LOD0 748. Neither describes a finished asset: I validated a 127,900 byte build, staged, and by the time git read the file the subagent had written it twice more. **HEAD carried a broadleaf I never validated, at a triangle count I never measured.** Nothing broke, because every intermediate passed the same contract and the tree drew, and that is exactly why it survived. **A file being rebuilt on a timer by another process is not a file you can stage, and "I ran the validator two minutes ago" is not the same claim as "I validated what I committed".** The conifer escaped by luck (its author had finished; HEAD's blob IS byte-identical to a fresh rebuild). Corrected in `01912ca`: real final is LOD0 **592**, total 1,346 of 2,600, rebuild byte-identical. **THE MEASUREMENT THAT SHOULD HAVE BEEN RN-69's HEADLINE**, because it measures the property rather than a consequence of it: per image column take the topmost and bottommost foliage pixel and count background pixels strictly between them, i.e. **sky seen THROUGH the crown as a fraction of the crown's own area. Conifer 7.87% to 17.23%; broadleaf 0.27% to 11.86%.** The conifer's 7.87% before was not see-through at all, it was notching at the silhouette edge where one tier skirt overhung the next, with no interior hole anywhere. Corrected broadleaf pixel pair, original against FINAL: 11,505 moved (1.68%), 5,425 darker / 6,080 lighter (RN-70 published 3,455/6,888 for the intermediate). **AN INVARIANT THAT MOVED AND IS EXPLAINED:** `main()` computes ONE fit scale from `Full_LOD0`'s raw AABB and replays it on every depletion variant, so changing the hero silhouette rescales trunk, stubs and Stump by a few per cent, **identically across all four variants**, so "a swap must not shift the trunk by a millimetre" holds and both trees still measure exactly to the validator's 5 mm. **(2) RN-71, THE MOVE, AND TWO SELF-INFLICTED INSTRUMENT ERRORS.** `ScatterLook.ts` goes to `web/src/render/`. **`of.framehash` DOES NOT SURVIVE A PAGE LOAD**: bit-exact within one page (RN-47, RN-51 both leaned on that correctly), but the SAME binary gave eight different hashes at four sites in two runs while triangles were identical to the unit every time. A cross-build hash comparison proves nothing in either direction. What does survive, identical on both builds at four sites across three biomes: **triangles 1,003,938 / 555,713 / 654,099 / 936,116, draw calls 78/66/62/78, geometries 52, programs 37, VRAM 93.7 MB, batches 17, props 42, grows 10, refused 0, and prop instances 43,085 / 17,965 / 1,774 / 43,085.** The instance counts are the ones that matter: if the move had reseeded anything, per-cell placement is where it would show. **THE PIXEL CLAIM WAS WRONG FIRST TIME**: the move read 6,818 px (1.00%) against a 550 px (0.08%) floor, twelve times over, because the two scratch builds were serving DIFFERENT broadleaf assets after RN-72's swap. With byte-identical model assets the floor is **913 px (0.13%)** and the move is **421 px (0.06%)**, i.e. the move changes fewer pixels than the binary changes against itself. **Second error, worth recording because the fix looks obvious afterwards: aligning two builds by copying one's `dist/assets` over the other's BREAKS IT**, since Vite puts the hashed JS chunks in `dist/assets` beside the model directories. Only the model subdirectories may be aligned. **(3) RN-73 to RN-76, THE BOULDER.** `hc.lobe` is one radius per (ring, azimuth), so it cannot express a cleft, an undercut or a fracture plane. **FRACTURE PLANE: yes**, new local `_cleaved()` ends a mass in an n-gon whose height is `z = rz * (1 + tilt.x*u + tilt.y*v)`, linear in the vertex's own normalised position, so the facet is planar BY CONSTRUCTION whatever the jitter does; **measured out-of-plane deviation 0.000000 m** on every up-facing crown of every kind, five per Full boulder. **OVERHANG: yes, all four**, a whole mass rotated about Y base-included then re-seated on its lowest corner; measured down-facing facet height **0.138 / 0.189 / 0.184 / 0.245 m** against 0.9 to 1.1 m totals. **CLEFT: yes**, a `notch` pulling one column of every ring in (each notched crown has exactly ONE reflex vertex and stays a simple polygon) plus interpenetrating tipped pairs; deepest trough **0.427 / 0.410 / 0.493 / 0.706 m**, up to 71% of height. **RN-68's `Parts.fit` FINDING CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: it re-pins the pile AABB minimum to zero unconditionally, so a deliberate sink is impossible and the overhang comes from re-seating rather than sinking, which leaves `fit()` nothing to undo. **The four kinds are now four forms** (cluster, wedge, pair, slab), differing by arrangement, mass count, tip angle, cleft placement and break direction rather than by seed; masses deplete from the END so the mass carrying the seam always survives to `_Low`. **IT CAME IN CHEAPER: LOD0 164 to 142/156/148/134, totals 574 to 518/564/534/478.** **MY BRIEF HAD TWO WRONG BUDGETS and the file was obeyed instead**: `contracts.json` holds stone at 200 LOD0 and **2** materials and coal at 240, not the 220/3 I wrote. In-engine, one binary, assets swapped in the served bundle: **3,186 px moved (0.47%), 1,549 darker against 1,637 lighter, max 238**, the near-exactly even split being the property assertion. **HONEST LIMIT: that clears the 0.13% floor by 3.5x but the camera holds only a handful of ore boulders and the frame is dominated by scatter pebbles; a better one was not found.** Invariants: draw calls 66 to 66, geometries 52, programs 37, VRAM 93.7 MB, instances 17,965 both ways, triangles 556,199 to 553,383. `validate_glb.py --all` **49/49**; all four unchanged rebuilds byte-identical first. `harvest_common.py` NOT touched, so no other asset's bytes moved; `_cleaved` stays local because promoting it would move the beach and cave scatter rocks and it carries boulder-shaped parameters a canopy blob has no use for. **`ASSET-SPECS.md` line 2404 said "the four ore boulders: one form, four dressings", which my own change made false, so it is corrected in the same commit** rather than left to trip someone up. **DW-10 UNCHANGED at 4 `ShaderMaterial` + 1 live `onBeforeCompile` across all of RN-64 to RN-76.** Commits `01912ca`, `c5c680c`, `e28b018`.
>
> **RN-69 / RN-70 (2026-07-28): THE CANOPY OPENS, SO SKY COMES THROUGH A TREE FOR THE FIRST TIME.** The conifer was five stacked `tc.taper_bands` skirt tiers plus four lobes; the broadleaf four `canopy_mass` spheroids. Both are SOLIDS OF REVOLUTION, so no sky has ever shown through a canopy anywhere in this game, and the union of concentric sweeps is an outline of circular arcs. Not reachable by jitter, by a phase offset or by a fragment shader, which is the honest converse of the RN-45 strata win. **TRIANGLES WERE NEVER THE BLOCKER AND THE NUMBERS SAY SO BOTH WAYS: conifer LOD0 584 to 721 of a 900 budget (file total 1,682 of 2,600), broadleaf 452 to 748 of 1,000 (total 1,428 of 2,600).** The fix spends a third of headroom that was sitting unused. **WHAT REPLACED THE TIERS:** the conifer crown is about **121 SEPARATE BLADES**, five verts and three triangles each, hung in whorls off the trunk with **nothing at all between neighbouring blades**. Blade reach varies by nearly 2x inside one whorl and consecutive whorls disagree on their nominal radius on purpose, so the outline is sawtoothed and no two whorls are concentric; each blade kinks twice so the flank has two slope changes instead of one straight cone edge; **the bark trunk is now visible THROUGH the crown** and a green leader runs past the trunk top, which is the strongest conifer cue in the asset and was previously buried inside the tiers. Leaf role is per BLADE rather than per horizontal band, so the crown mottles instead of striping. **MEASURED, one binary, assets swapped in the SERVED bundle, HUD excluded, 684,800 px: conifer yaw 90 moves 7,254 px (1.06%), 2,078 darker against 5,176 lighter, max 238; conifer yaw 118 moves 12,047 (1.76%), 4,256 against 7,791; broadleaf yaw 90 moves 10,343 (1.51%), 3,455 against 6,888.** **THE LIGHTER LEAN IS THE ASSERTION AND IT WAS PREDICTED BEFORE MEASURING**: a silhouette change moves pixels both ways, but OPENING a canopy must move them predominantly LIGHTER because sky now reaches pixels opaque leaf held, and all three come out 2.0 to 2.5 lighter for every darker. A predominantly darker result would have meant the canopy got DENSER and would have falsified the pass. The conifer pair is clean of the broadleaf change: the served bundle still carried HEAD's broadleaf for those two frames. **Invariants: draw calls 82/81 unchanged, geometries 52 unchanged, VRAM 93.7 MB unchanged, DW-10 unchanged. Triangles 555,813 to 559,101 and 558,065 to 561,353, i.e. +3,288 BOTH TIMES**, the same instance count drawing a heavier LOD0 rather than anything appearing. `validate_glb.py --all` **49/49**, both assets keeping their exact authored boxes to 5 mm, their ground pivots, 12 LOD nodes, both clips live at t=0, and the conifer at exactly 5 of 5 allowed materials. **LOD1 and LOD2 UNTOUCHED.** **STILL OPEN, and it is the boulder half of the same finding:** every rock is `harvest_common.lobe()`, a single-valued radial sweep topologically incapable of an overhang, cleft or undercut, and all four ore boulders are the SAME 164 triangles with different material dressing. RN-68 worked WITHIN that topology for the pebble because 42 triangles allows nothing else; a boulder at 164 has room to leave it, and nobody has tried. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN69_{before,after}_y{90,118}.png` and `RN70_{before,after}_y90.png`. Commit `8d7150c`.

> **RN-68 (2026-07-28): THE NEAR PEBBLE STOPS BEING A FOUR-SIDED PYRAMID, AND THE BUDGET THAT ACTUALLY BOUND IT WAS NOT THE ONE IN THE BRIEF.** The scatter read as dozens of identical grey pyramids on sand at walking distance and it was literally that: `pebble_scatter` built SEVEN stones, each `hc.lobe(seg=4, rings=((0.0, 1.00),))`, and a single-ring lobe is a base quad plus an apex fan. Four sides, one point, six triangles, seven copies. **RN-45's far lobe is UNTOUCHED and stays the LOD2**, because this is two assets and not one better lobe: the 45 m case needs vertical extent (a horizontal facet presents 3.5% of its area at a 2 degree grazing view) and the 5 m case needs a low part-buried chip, and tuning one lobe only moves which distance is wrong. **THE BINDING BUDGET WAS `max_tris_total`, NOT `max_tris_lod0`.** The brief said 42 of 60 with 18 spare; the same `contracts.json` block sets `max_tris_total` 245 and the control build's total is EXACTLY 245, because RN-48 tightened that number to the built total. `validate_glb.py:311` checks it, so the file had ZERO slack: a 52-triangle version was built, measured and thrown away, and the shipped shape lands on exactly 42. No budget was raised. **WHAT IT IS NOW:** four stones at 22 + 8 + 6 + 6. The broad chip is `seg=6` with `rings ((0.0, 1.00), (0.72, 0.74))`, and the second ring IS the fix, because `lobe()` always ends in an apex fan and the only way to not have a point is to hand that fan a large ring and very little height. Aspect ratio 3.6 to 5.1 wide-to-tall against the old 1.5 to 2.0, **and that ratio is the whole reason the old one read as a pyramid**: `Parts.fit` stretches the pile to the card's authored 0.10 m height whatever is authored, so seven small stones were inflated into 9 to 19 cm wide by 6 to 10 cm tall cones. **BURIAL CANNOT COME FROM A NEGATIVE z**, now in the docstring so nobody retries it: `build_atlas` calls `parts.fit(size, base_z)` and `Parts.fit` re-pins the pile AABB base to zero, so a sink is either undone on the deepest stone or lifts every other stone off the ground. **MEASURED, one binary, the asset swapped in the SERVED bundle so nothing else can differ, HUD and hotbar excluded, 684,800 px: pitch -42 moves 16,196 px (2.37%), 5,828 darker against 10,368 lighter, max 233; pitch -22 moves 9,445 (1.38%), 4,836 against 4,609; pitch -9 moves 13,646 (1.99%), 6,335 against 7,311.** THE SPLIT IS THE ASSERTION AND THE COUNT IS ONLY THE MAGNITUDE (RN-63): a silhouette change has to move pixels BOTH ways or it is not reshaping anything, and one coming out 98% darker would mean it had shaded something instead. **Invariants: draw calls 78 to 78, geometries 52 to 52, VRAM 93.7 to 93.7 MB, DW-10 unchanged. Triangles 907,000 to 906,958 and 1,035,584 to 1,035,500, i.e. -42 and -84, exactly one and two cards' worth, and IDENTICAL at pitch -9.** The LOD0 geometry is the same 42 triangles, so those deltas are per-instance frustum culling: a chip with a different bounding box crosses the frustum edge where a cone did not, which is RN-63's 42 triangles with a different subject. Controls: an unchanged rebuild came back BYTE-IDENTICAL at 33,368 bytes before any edit; two rebuilds of the new shape are byte-identical to each other at 32,984; `validate_glb.py --all` is **49/49** with `tris_total 245 <= 245`. **NEW INSTRUMENT: `web/tools/smoke/pngdiff.mjs`**, the numeric frame diff the last three RN passes each built by hand. It decodes in a browser because this repo has no PNG decoder installed and `playwright-core` is already a dependency, and it reports the darker/lighter SPLIT rather than only a count. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN68_{before,after}_p{-42,-22,-9}.png`. Commit `4e3a2fd`.

> **RN-64 to RN-67 (2026-07-28): THE PROPS GET A GROUND UNDER THEM, AND THE 3.2x THAT SENT ME LOOKING FOR IT WAS THE FIRST-PERSON ARMS.** **(1) RN-66 IS THE FINDING AND IT IS AN INSTRUMENT ONE.** RN-60 reported the prop band swinging 71.4 counts across a sun sweep where the ground swings 22.4, a 3.2x disagreement, and every conclusion after it rested on that number. The band it called "ground" was defined as the tiles props never touch, at pitch -10. At that pitch those tiles are the sky above the horizon and the ANIMATED FIRST-PERSON ARMS along the bottom, and the arms are drawn in the view-model pass, which has its own sun, its own hemisphere at 1.1 in warm colours and no scattering integral at all, so the control barely responds to the sun BY CONSTRUCTION. **Measured at the same site with the same sun: the band reads 110.7 counts at dawn against 137.7 at its brightest when the camera is pitched -10, and 27.2 against 110.4 when it is pitched -30. Terrain does not brighten fourfold because the camera tilted twenty degrees.** Against real ground the same quantity is **0.10 of normalised response, not 0.44**. `proplight.js` now excludes the arms BY THEIR OWN SIGNATURE rather than by a screen rectangle (a rectangle is a constant that goes stale, which is INSTRUMENTS.md's opening entry): they are the only thing that moves while camera, sun and world are pinned, so two captures one settled frame apart differ exactly where they are. And it asserts the property RN-60 needed: **a sweep that takes the sun to 2.5 degrees removes the direct term, so any surface that sun lights must lose most of its light, and a control band keeping four fifths of its peak at dawn is not that surface.** It **fails BY NAME on RN-60's own configuration (0.8043)** and passes at -30 (0.246). **I WROTE ONE ASSERTION BEFORE THAT AND IT WAS WRONG,** recorded because the mistake is the useful part: "the control sits higher in the frame than the props standing on it" reads like sky contamination and is in fact what happens whenever the camera looks DOWN, since distant ground is at the top and the near understorey at the bottom. A rule whose answer cannot express the failure is the set-versus-order entry with a different subject. Left in as a published field, not as a check. **(2) RN-64, THE GROUND HALF OF THE ENVIRONMENT, AND THE TRACED MECHANISM WAS INVERTED.** The handed-over diagnosis was "sky above and nothing below", so props have an empty lower hemisphere where `TerrainShader` has `ground * (1 - skyView)`. First half right, second half not: **the lower hemisphere is not empty, it holds the sky model MARCHED THROUGH THE PLANET**, which accumulates a real reddish in-scatter worth about **seventeen times** the true ground bounce at the Hills site. Every prop, tree, pebble and machine is lit from below by an integral of atmosphere taken inside the rock. So the fix makes props DARKER on dark ground and BRIGHTER on bright ground, **and the sign flipping with the biome is the assertion rather than the magnitude, because that is what a ground bounce IS and what no tuned constant could do**: same code, same constants, matched pairs at dawn, **Hills (0x6f7346) prop band 17.696 to 13.980; Polar (0xe8eef2, snow) 115.124 to 118.849.** **(3) WHERE THE RADIANCE COMES FROM AND WHY IT IS NOT A TUNED CONSTANT.** Term for term `TerrainShader`'s own `ground`: the albedo is the biome's palette entry from `biomeColorArray`, indexed by the `/core` `biomeAt` classifier under the observer, i.e. `vBiomeColor` and not a tint; the bracket is `uAmbient + skyAmb + sunT * 1.45 * max(dot(up, sunDir), 0)`, computed by the shared `Atmosphere.glsl` model IN THE SHADER and not on the CPU. The three surface constants move into new `TerrainAmbient.ts` and are **shared BY REFERENCE** with both terrain materials on `createAtmosphereUniforms`' precedent; `TerrainShader`'s two `1.45` literals now interpolate the same exported constant and emit the same characters, so the compiled program is unchanged. **The one term not carried over is `shadow`**: a cascade lookup needs a position and this is one radiance for the whole hemisphere, so it is the UNSHADOWED flat ground, and the error errs toward too much bounce inside a shadow where the direct term is already gone. **(4) RN-65, THE WIRING DIAGNOSIS, AND WHY THE FIRST DESIGN WAS THROWN AWAY.** It was a second mesh interposed into the sky scene for one capture, which is the stronger confinement, and it rendered correctly (forced visible it paints the lower hemisphere with the horizon exactly where it belongs). **A FORTY-fold change in its radiance moved the prop band by 0.05 of a count.** A changed input that does not change the output is a wiring diagnosis and never a small effect, so the ground half moved onto the one mesh the capture has always worked from and the graph question disappeared. `?iblgroundamp=` stays as the instrument that made that call: at 0 / 1 / 8 the prop band reads **13.748 / 13.984 / 15.510**. **(5) INVARIANTS, RN-15 camera, ONE binary, `?iblground=0` against default: draw calls 78 to 78, triangles 1,021,918 to 1,021,918, geometries 52 to 52, textures 88 to 88, programs 37 to 37, VRAM est 93.7 to 93.7 MB, ibl rebuild 0.6 to 0.7 ms, `groundBuilds` 0 to 5** (a COUNTER not a flag, on RN-58's `grabs` precedent). **DW-10 UNCHANGED at 4 `ShaderMaterial` + 1 live `onBeforeCompile`**: ground mode is a UNIFORM branch inside the sky shader that already existed, not a define and not a second shader, and **programs reading 37 both ways is what proves the branch did not fork the program**. **(6) WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, STATED PLAINLY.** Reid's complaint is that props read too dark, and at a dark biome this change makes them darker because it removes light that was never physical. The residual prop-against-ground response gap after the instrument is fixed is 0.10 normalised at Hills and up to 0.25 at a second camera, and **it is NOT the ground bounce**: the ground bounce is multiplied by `1 - skyView`, which is zero for the flat facets that dominate terrain, so it was never what kept the terrain lit at dawn. The remaining suspects, in the order I would take them, are the prop sun being `3.0 * smoothstep(elev, -0.12, 0.15)` with a hand-lerped orange where the terrain sun is `1.45 * ndl * ofAtmoSunTransmittance`, and the env map's sky half being uncalibrated against the terrain's `uSkyAmbient` 0.32. **At Reid's 9-degree candidate site `k` is already 1.0, so the elevation dimmer is not the mechanism there.** Isolation: `?iblground=0 ?iblgroundamp=`, both registered in `run.mjs`. New: `web/src/render/materials/TerrainAmbient.ts`. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN64_{hills,polar,rn15cam}_{0,1}.png`. Commit `21fbfc0`.

> **RN-59 to RN-61 (2026-07-28) - TREES AND ROCKS, AND THE PASS OPENED BY DISPROVING ITS OWN HYPOTHESIS.** Scope: silhouette, form and variety at walking (0-15 m), mid-field (15-60 m) and horizon (60 m+). **(1) THERE IS NO FOREST, and this is the finding of the pass.** Living trees exist ONLY as 14 harvest nodes in one spawn clearing, on a golden-angle spiral from 7 m to ~57 m (`NodeField.ts:136`), and the Forest biome's scatter atlas contains no live tree AT ALL, deliberately (`build_props_forest.py:9-12`: a scatter conifer "would read as a harvest node the player cannot chop"). So beyond ~57 m of spawn there is nothing to accumulate into a treeline: **no silhouette work can ever produce a horizon mass**, because there are no trees out there to form one. That is placement, not asset quality; Admin scoped it to world-gen as a separate lane. **(2) THE NEAR-BLACK BOULDERS ARE NOT A LIGHTING MISMATCH, and I predicted they were.** New probe `probes/proplight.js` sweeps the sun and finds prop pixels BY DIFFERENCE (capture with props shown and hidden; tiles that moved are prop tiles, tiles that moved not at all are pure ground, mixed tiles discarded), so neither band is a hand-picked rectangle. Two controls green: the visibility toggle returns the bit-identical frame, and both surfaces genuinely respond. **Two sites mattered and one would have given a confident wrong answer in either direction.** At high sun the Mountains site reads prop 96.37 against ground 96.13 (agreement to a quarter of a count) while Hills reads 60.97 against 123.41 - the Hills gap is ALBEDO, grass being brighter than rock. Every baseline frame was shot at sunT 0.30 where the two models agree to 0.02 of full range, so the dark boulders are albedo and material (`OF_RockDark` 0x57534C). R8 doing real work on a lighting claim rather than a geometric one. **(3) BUT A REAL SYSTEMIC DISAGREEMENT EXISTS AND IT IS ABOUT THE INDIRECT TERM, NOT THE SUN.** Reproduced at both sites, worst normalised gap 0.42 and 0.48, always at LOW sun; the prop band swings 71.4 counts across the sweep at Mountains where the ground swings 22.4, i.e. **3.2x**. `TerrainShader` gives itself a sky-ambient integral plus RN-30's bounce; props get three's ambient and an IBL and nothing else. **Every prop, tree, pebble and machine crushes toward black at dawn and dusk while the ground stays lit.** Bears directly on Reid's live golden-hour spawn choice: one candidate site sits at 9 degrees maximum sun elevation, which would make that defect permanent. Next commit, reachable via `SkyIbl` and `envMapIntensity` without touching `Systems.ts`. **(4) RN-61: the clone army is gone.** `NodeBatch` never called `setColorAt` at all, so 14 trees were two meshes at a literal scale of 1.0 in one colour, differing only in yaw. Added `NodeBatch.tint` (build-time only) plus one colour PER PLACEMENT applied across that placement's slots - trunk and canopy are separate material batches, so tinting them independently would drift a tree's bark from its own leaves and read as a broken asset. Deliberately NOT a call into `ScatterLook.tintFor`: the flower branch would put a 1.70/1.50/0.38 yellow on a whole conifer, and that value range (0.80-1.18) at tree size reads as a lighting bug. Per-instance tree size 0.86-1.14, bounded because **`art.radiusM` and `art.hitUpM` do not scale with it**, so a big and a small tree are chopped from the same distance; at +/-14% that is under aim tolerance, at +/-40% it would be a gameplay bug wearing an art change's clothes. Verified numerically because the eye would have said "no change": **2.48% of scene pixels (13,514 of 544,000) move by >6 counts, max channel delta 180**. Invariants: draw calls 65 -> 65, triangles 641k -> 641k, VRAM 93.7 -> 93.7 MB. **(5) THE ROOT CAUSE OF THE ROCK SAMENESS, STATED PRECISELY.** Every rock in the game is `harvest_common.lobe()`, a single-valued radial sweep about a vertical axis, **topologically incapable of an overhang, cleft or undercut**; all four ore boulders are the SAME 164 triangles with different material dressing. Canopies are closed solids with zero sky-through. Neither is reachable by a fragment shader, which is the honest converse of the RN-45 strata win (a cliff IS terrain, so one edit reached every face; a boulder is a genuine prop and what is wrong with it is its outline). **DW-10 ledger UNCHANGED at 4 ShaderMaterial + 1 live `onBeforeCompile`, and this pass is not expected to spend a slot.** **(6) THE THIRD ALREADY-UPLOADED ATTRIBUTE NOBODY WAS READING.** `PropGeometry.bakeColour` is gated on `isFoliageMaterial`, so `OF_Rock`, `OF_RockDark`, `OF_Regolith` and `OF_Bark` carry a constant-255 vertex colour with nothing writing it - after RN-50's per-quad UV and the node instance colour, that shape has now paid out three times and is worth a positive entry in `INSTRUMENTS.md`, which is currently all failures. **DEAD WEIGHT, recorded so nobody spends a day on it:** every `_LOD1`, `_LOD2` and `_Stump` in the node files is contract-required and **never rendered** (`NodeBatch` draws LOD0 at every distance), and `bush_scrub.glb`, `water_pool.glb` and `oil_seep.glb` ship, validate and are never loaded. Trees are NOT triangle-constrained: conifer 584 of a 900 budget, broadleaf 452 of 1000, 14 trees = 8,176 triangles in a ~1 M frame. Commits `53106ee`, `2e410d5` (compare-and-swap; the gameplay lane's `8ab129d` landed between them and was not reverted). Screenshots `RN59_base_{rn15,trees,tree_near,tree_far}.png`, `RN61_{before,after}.png`.
