> **RN-50 (2026-07-28): THE DETAIL BUMP SHIPS, ON A COORDINATE THAT WAS ALREADY ON THE GPU, AND THE PER-CHUNK PHASE IS A CAPABILITY WE DID NOT NEED YET.** Admin asked me to MEASURE both the UV route and world-gen's per-chunk phase before committing to either. The UV route wins for this term. WG-56 found that every chunk already carries a per-quad normalised UV, in the wire format, in the batch attributes, written per slot, uploaded and interpolated every frame, and that `TerrainShader.ts` contained the string `uv` **exactly zero times**. It is the well-conditioned coordinate `pM` is not, because it is LOCAL: one uint16 step is 0.883 mm on a depth-14 chunk, a quarter of a ground pixel at 2 m, against `pM`'s quantum of nearly nine pixels there. **MEASURED, one binary, RN-15 camera, 1280x720, props hidden, matched pairs one settled frame apart, noise floor exactly 0.0000: the bump moves 75.4% of the near band at a mean of 6.32 counts, 67.6% of the mid at 5.35, 51.7% of the far at 3.88, and 0.0% of the horizon at 0.0007.** The horizon reading is the one that matters: the term fades itself on the PIXEL FOOTPRINT, so it is gone before a triangle is a few pixels wide, and that is now measured rather than argued. The arcs are gone (`RN50_uvbump.png` against `RN45_iso_bump.png`, same camera, same flags, bump alone). **I WALKED INTO WORLD-GEN'S TRAP AND CAUGHT IT BEFORE MEASURING.** The obvious version keeps the macro field in the bump's height and merely adds a UV octave; that scores 0.00% dead quads, identical to a correct fix, and is still visibly wrong, because a quantised octave ANYWHERE in the sum poisons the derivative of the whole sum. `hArt` now feeds the COLOUR and never the bump, and every octave the derivative sees is on the UV: two, at 14.0 and 5.3 over the quad (4.1 m and 10.9 m at depth 14), neither a power of two because a lattice aligned to the 32-cell grid would draw the grid. **THE HONEST COST, and why this does NOT replace the phase:** the UV normalises over the QUAD, so the detail's world size doubles at every LOD step. Tolerable for THIS term only because it is well conditioned solely within about 20 to 45 m, where the streamer is at max depth, so the step falls outside the band. Any future field needing a derivative at range still wants the phase. **RN-45's ARITHMETIC CORRECTED: the quantum is 0.03125 m, not 0.0625** (I quoted the worst case; a generic point on a 600 km sphere sits one binade lower). Conclusion unchanged, and the corrected figure explains the SHAPE: 8.77x the ground pixel at 2 m and 0.55x at 8 m, which is why it was a near-field band that STOPPED rather than a fade. **TWO ENVIRONMENT DEFECTS, both of which cost a measurement.** (1) `npx vite build` SKIPS `sync-wasm`, which `npm run build` runs as a prebuild step; another lane bumped the client to ABI 19 mid-session, my builds kept a wasm at 18, a binary silently failed to boot, a `--out` screenshot never wrote, and I compared two images that were THE SAME FILE and concluded an amplitude change had done nothing. **Identical output from a changed input is a wiring diagnosis, never a coincidence**, and that rule paid three times tonight. (2) The disk filled to 7.2 MB free of 931 GB from accumulated `dist-*` directories across every lane; I deleted only my own. **`Scatter.ts` is 398 lines and UNDER the 400 cap**, better than the 409 it started the night at, repaying the 3 lines RN-48/49 added. No file was split. **NOT DONE: water polish (surface motion, glint, refraction across the waterline, shoreline) is still untouched, and `WaterSurface.ts` is under `web/src/world/` which is world-gen's, so the surface half needs an ownership ruling; the post-stack half is mine and is the next thing I would take.**

> **RN-51 to RN-58 (2026-07-28): THE POND STOPS BEING A SHEET OF GLASS. ONE DW-10 SLOT BUYS ALL FOUR OF SURFACE MOTION, GLINT, REFRACTION AND THE SHORELINE, AND THE WET BEACH COSTS ZERO BECAUSE IT IS TERRAIN.** Reid asked for the pond and will go swimming first thing. **RN-51, THE OWNERSHIP MOVE:** `WaterSurface.ts` leaves `web/src/world/` for `web/src/render/`. World-gen keeps the LEVEL and the BASIN (`water_field.h`, `WaterOracle`, `levelAt`/`depthAt`/`shorelineM`), which is surface authority and stays in one place per DW-26; the look, the geometry and the vertex attributes are mine. **THE SLOT SPENT, AND WHICH OF THE FOUR NEEDED IT: ONE `ShaderMaterial`, and it is not four effects.** They are four questions about ONE fragment (which way is this facing, how much sun does it mirror, what is underneath it, how close is the edge), all answered from the same perturbed normal and the same water depth, so a second program would have to recompute the normal to ask its question. **The DW-10 ledger goes 3 + 3 to 4 `ShaderMaterial` plus 3 `onBeforeCompile`. REFRACTION WAS THE ONE THAT LOOKED LIKE IT NEEDED A SECOND SLOT AND DID NOT: what it needs is not a program, it is a TEXTURE**, the scene as it stood before the water drew. One `copyFramebufferToTexture` at `onBeforeRender`, which is the only instant at which the bound target holds the sky, the far scaled scene and every near opaque and does NOT yet hold the water. A pass costs a program; a grab costs a copy. This is RN-47's argument reaching the OPPOSITE answer for a good reason: the submerged view wanted a per-pixel distance the depth buffer already had, so it was post; the surface wants the normal it is already computing, so it is a material. **RN-57, AND IT IS RN-45's FINDING REPEATING: A CLIFF IS TERRAIN, AND SO IS A WET BEACH.** The waterline darkening goes into `TerrainShader`, already a custom shader, so it spends NO slot and reaches the shore, the submerged bed and any future body for no draw call, no triangle, no texture and no byte of VRAM. **THE COORDINATE IS THE CORRECTNESS ARGUMENT, TWICE, AND BOTH TIMES RN-45/RN-50 WAS APPLIED BEFORE THE ARTEFACT RATHER THAN AFTER.** (a) The ripple's gradient is ANALYTIC in `aPlane`, metres from the pond centre: **not one `dFdx` appears in the water shader.** `aPlane` spans +/-11 m so float32 resolves it to about a micron, against planet-centred metres' 0.03125 m quantum that RN-45 measured into concentric arcs. (b) The wet band measures height above the water from `vRelief` (metres above datum, a per-vertex attribute) and NOT from `length(pM) - levelRadius`: algebraically identical, and only one is computable, because a difference of two 6e5 radii resolves the 0.55 m fade into about eighteen steps and draws contour bands parallel to the shore. The LATERAL gate is allowed to use `pM`, since 0.05 m of jitter on a 5 m smoothstep is invisible. **Knowing which of two coordinates a term can afford is the skill; using the good one everywhere would have cost a varying nobody needed.** **THREE MORE PROPERTIES RATHER THAN TUNINGS.** The reflected sky is `ofAtmoScatter` along the reflected ray at `tMax` 1.0e9, so DW-22 holds (no hand-picked blue to disagree with the sky above it) and RN-30's confinement holds (an ESCAPING ray cannot pick up the boundary-layer aerosol). The refraction offset is scaled by the vertex's water depth, which is zero at the shoreline, so shoreline bleed is impossible AT the boundary by construction rather than fixed by a depth test. The fine ripple octaves fade on the PIXEL FOOTPRINT and the discarded slope variance is added back as specular roughness, because dropping a sub-pixel ripple unpaid turns a sheet of glitter into flickering dots that the bloom pyramid then amplifies. **EVERY FRAME GLOBAL IS SHARED BY REFERENCE WITH THE NEAR TERRAIN MATERIAL** (atmosphere record, planet centre, sim clock, cascade splits, ambient, sky-ambient), so there is no new per-frame push, no edit to `Systems.ts` or `Boot.ts`, and "the water agrees with the shore" is a property of the object graph. `CASCADE_GLSL` is EXPORTED from `TerrainShader` rather than copied: a second copy is a second authority on which cascade a range belongs to, and the visible failure would be a glint still lit inside a shadow the shoreline is already in. `WATER_SIGMA` and `WATER_TINT` move out of `PostConfig` into `WaterOptics.ts` and both sides read them, because looking DOWN into a pond and looking UP from inside it are one integral seen from two sides. **MEASURED, ONE BINARY, `of.framehash` (NOT screenshots: the water animates on `uTime`, so two screenshots are a tick apart and the surface has genuinely moved; `framehash` renders synchronously so two calls cannot have a tick between them). Noise floor BIT-EXACT: identical hash, 0 of 14,400 tiles, peak 0.0000. At the pond, sun 4 degrees up, 1280x720, 160x90 tiles: ripple 4,983 tiles / mean 3.10 / peak 65.6; refraction 6,820 / 5.76 / 67.0; glint 164 / 0.097 / 72.3; foam 59 / 0.013 / 11.2; wet band 4,754 / 2.45 / 25.0; all four together 6,823 / 7.19 / 120.1.** **INVARIANTS, MATCHED PAIR AT THE RN-15 CAMERA, before build archived from `3c9191c`: draw calls 70 to 70, triangles 1,001,340 to 1,001,340 (IDENTICAL, and it reproduces RN-49's published figure to the digit), geometries 52 to 52, VRAM estimate 93.7 to 93.7 MB, and programs 40 DOWN to 39**, because the custom material replaces a stock `MeshStandardMaterial` that was also compiling an environment-mapped variant. **THE GRAB IS 7,372,800 BYTES AND IS PUBLISHED SEPARATELY, because `vramEstimateMB` is built from the post targets and the chunk pool and does NOT see it**; it is allocated lazily inside `onBeforeRender`, which three calls only for objects that survive the frustum cull, so a body with no water, a camera looking away, `?post=0` and `?msaa=` all pay zero. `grabs` is a COUNTER (483 frames at the pond) and not a flag, because "it ran once at boot" and "it runs every frame" are different claims. **THE REGRESSION CLAIM IS BIT-EXACT: at the RN-15 camera, 121 km from the pond, toggling the wet band moves 0 of 14,400 tiles with an IDENTICAL hash.** The basin gate confines it exactly, and that is a stronger statement than any screenshot pair. **FOUR DEFECTS, ALL MINE, AND THREE OF THEM WERE INSTRUMENTS.** (1) The shader did not compile: `CASCADE_GLSL` was concatenated ABOVE the uniform block and GLSL requires declaration before use. (2) **The static check that should have caught it printed `(none)`**: it compared the SET of uniforms used against the set declared, and a set has no order, so it was asked a question whose answer could not express the failure. Nothing but a compiler checks a compiler. (3) **THE GLINT WAS MEASURED TWICE WHERE IT CANNOT EXIST.** Four fixed bearings gave peaks of 0.10, 0.13, 0.91 and 1.13 counts and TWO "failures"; three of those cameras had the sun behind them, where a correct specular is SUPPOSED to be invisible, which is RN-46's `wetCells: 0` error with the sign flipped. And even aimed correctly it read about 0 on a 64x36 grid, because a 20x20 px tile averages a sparkle path away; at 160x90 the same term reads a peak of 72. **The claim is now TWO-SIDED: present with the sun up, and BIT-EXACTLY absent below the horizon, which no tuned threshold can imitate.** At a HIGH sun (49 degrees) the glint reads 1.67 counts and that number is RECORDED in the probe so nobody later "fixes" what is geometry. (4) **The foam was a solid white ring 3.2 m wide.** It bounds a band of DEPTH, and depth converts to distance up the shore through the beach slope: 0.34 m at this pond's 6 degrees reached 3.2 m inland and photographed as a wash over the sand. **`?waterfoam=0` is what ATTRIBUTED it rather than a guess**, which is the whole reason standing rule 7 wants one flag per term. Shipped at 0.10 m with a much higher noise gate, which reads as a broken line of surf. **ALSO FOUND, NOT MINE, AND IT AFFECTS EVERY LANE: `__ofPost.state().sun` FREEZES BELOW THE HORIZON**, because `ShadowRig.update` does `if (!this.active) continue` before it moves the light and `Frame.publishSun` derives the vector from that light. A probe read the identical elevation 0.5486 for two different times of day, which is the tell. Harmless where the post stack uses it (the contact march is gated on the same rig), not harmless in an instrument; `of.stats().sky.elevationDot` does not freeze. **FRAME TIMINGS ARE NOT PUBLISHED. Two lanes were live and the invariant table is the whole cost claim.** New: `web/src/render/WaterSurface.ts` (moved), `web/src/render/materials/{WaterShader,WaterMaterial,WaterOptics}.ts`, `web/tools/smoke/probes/water.js`. Isolation: `?water=0 ?waterripple=0 ?waterglint=0 ?waterrefract=0 ?waterfoam=0 ?wetsand=0` plus `?rippleamp= ?glintamp= ?refractamp= ?foamamp= ?wetsandamp=`, all registered in `run.mjs`; runtime `window.__ofWater.{set,reset,tune,state,grabBytes,grabs}` and `__ofTerrainArt.{setWet,getWet}`. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN58_pond_{before,after}.png` (same camera, one binary, terms off against on), `RN58_glint.png` (a real sun path at a low sun), `RN58_waterline.png`, `RN58_floatline.png` (the float line, NOT a submerged camera: see the not-done below), `RN58_nofoam.png` (the attribution), `RN58_rn15cam_{before,after}.png`. **NOT DONE, AND NAMED: there is still no shot of the surface from genuinely UNDERNEATH it.** `pondshot --swim` settles the capsule at the float line with `headUnderM` -0.29, i.e. the head is ABOVE water, so that capture is a waterline shot and not a submerged one; a submerged camera needs a dive input the probe does not drive. RN-47's own list of not-dones (unattenuated first-person arms, a laterally unbounded water plane, no tint on a free camera) is unchanged. The near-field water seen at a very grazing angle from under a metre away is a flat pale wash with no ripple detail, which is partly correct (the wavelengths exceed the visible patch) and has not been separately measured.

> **RN-48 to RN-49 (2026-07-28): RN-46 SHIPPED DEAD IN THREE SEPARATE WAYS AND I PROVED IT WORKING WITH THE WRONG HALF OF THE EVIDENCE. THE UNDERSTOREY BECOMES SEVEN CLUSTERED SPECIES FOR ONE DRAW CALL AND NO EXTRA DENSITY.** **(1) THE POND REJECTION NEVER RAN, IN ANY BUILD.** RN-46 reported `wetCells: 0` at two DRY sites and called that a result. It is not: **zero at a dry site is equally consistent with the filter working, with it never running, with the oracle never being consulted, and with the whole feature being unreachable. A filter is proved by the case it is supposed to CATCH, never by the case it is supposed to ignore.** `probes/pondscatter.js` goes to the pond and asserts the positive half, and found THREE defects stacked: **(a) the flag was inverted** (`?scatterwet=1` means wet scattering is ALLOWED, so Boot handed the oracle over in exactly the configuration that then refuses to use it); **(b) `depthAt` takes a normalized DIRECTION and was being handed the absolute 6e5 m position**, which is why `DebugTerraform.water` divides by the length before every call and answered correctly all along; **(c) the radius guard was ineffective on an assumption I never examined, that a water level sits near the datum.** It does not: the pond is at the Mountains spawn, so its level radius is 604,667 m while the Hills site stands at 600,861 m, and every cell over most of the planet paid a WASM call to be told it was dry. Replaced by a **BASIN GATE**, one dot product per chunk against the oracle's own published 22 m disc, where the Hills site is 121,813 m away. It **fails SAFE**: a future non-disc water model leaves `disc` null, the gate is skipped, every cell is queried again, and correct-but-slow beats silently-dry (the DW-26 trap). **MEASURED at the pond, camera aimed by SEARCHING the actual aim ray rather than assuming a yaw convention: wetCells 0 to 431, foliage coverage over the water 0.3123 to 0.0716 (-77%), instances 18,789 to 18,001; the `?scatterwet=1` control restores 0 / 0.3087 / 18,789.** The probe publishes the oracle's own reading at two points so a zero can be ATTRIBUTED rather than guessed at (3.40 m at the pond centre, 0.13 m at the camera in the shallows); **my first two diagnoses of this were both guesses and both were wrong, which is why that field exists.** Every symptom read healthy throughout: `deliveredFraction` 1.0001 and the two halves of the pair identical TO THE INSTANCE. **(2) SEVEN SPECIES, AND THE DENSITY BUDGET IS UNCHANGED TO THE UNIT.** `Detail_BroadleafForb` (30 tris LOD0 / 4 LOD2, OF_Leaf), `Detail_FlowerSprig` (30 / 5, OF_Grass plus OF_LeafDry heads), `Detail_SedgeRosette` (36 / 4, OF_Grass), authored to RN-45's rule (LOD2 rebuilt at `segs=1` and widened, never decimated). Byte-identical control run first at 18,288 bytes; validator green; 48/48 assets; **`max_tris_total` went 340 DOWN to 245**, the exact built total, because 340 was slack against a real 136 and slack in this budget is slack that gets spent at one to two orders of magnitude more instances than any biome prop. **GROUND_DETAIL sums to 538,000 before and after, DRY_DETAIL to 116,000 both ways: the new species are paid for out of the old ones.** Variety must not become cost by default while DW-5 is not relaxed. **(3) THE CLUSTERING IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS, and without it the species would have been a cost with no picture.** Adding species to an unclustered scatter makes the ground MORE uniform, not less: four species salt-and-peppered and seven salt-and-peppered both average to one flat texture at any distance, and the second is merely a more expensive way to get there. Each 8x8 cell patch (about 14 m at the shipped 1.808 m near cell) draws a DOMINANT species from the SAME weighted table, so a common species dominates many patches and a rare one dominates few, and 55% of instances take it. Not 100%, because a patch that is one species reads as a planted crop, which is exactly what RN-30 spent a pass removing from the grass height. **KNOWN: the patch lattice is chunk-local and restarts at chunk boundaries. That is another patch edge rather than a seam, and the honest fix needs the same high-precision per-chunk phase the detail bump needs, so it is blocked on the same world-gen work.** **COST AT THE RN-15 CAMERA, one binary, 1280x720: triangles 972,049 to 1,001,340 (+3.0%), draw calls 69 to 70 (+1, the one new OF_Leaf batch), programs 40 to 40, geometries 51 to 52, VRAM 93.7 MB unchanged, foliage coverage 0.5438 to 0.5629 (+3.5%).** Coverage up more than triangles is the trade working: the forb and the rosette are wider than the blades they displaced. Pool refusals 0, `cellsCapped` 0, `chunksCapped` 0, backlog 0. **Across the night the frame is 1,087,719 to 1,001,340 triangles, i.e. 7.9% FEWER than the pass started with, while going from four species to seven and adding the terrain surface art.** **THE ASSET LANE'S FIRST CUT VALIDATED AND WAS VISUALLY WRONG** (sprig heads and sedge straps rendered as hairlines, the sub-pixel failure `build_props_plains` already documents); caught by rendering it, fixed before it shipped, triangle counts unaffected. **`Boot.ts` IS COMMITTED AS A FILTERED BLOB**, HEAD plus only my two hunks, built and asserted in Python (`resumeWorld` absent, both of mine present), because it still carries the vessel lane's uncommitted wiring. New: `web/tools/smoke/probes/pondscatter.js`. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN48_pond_after.png` (a clean bed), `RN49_species_after.png` (visible stands of forb, grass and straw). **NOT DONE: water polish (surface motion, glint, refraction across the waterline, shoreline treatment) is untouched, and `WaterSurface.ts` lives under `web/src/world/` which is now world-gen's, so the surface half needs an ownership ruling before I take it; the post-stack half is mine and is the next thing I would do.**

> **RN-45 to RN-47 (2026-07-28): THE LOD2 CARDS, THE TERRAIN'S FIRST SURFACE ART, THE POND, AND FOUR BROKEN INSTRUMENTS OF WHICH THREE WERE MINE. THE REORDER: ITEM 1 AND ITEM 3 OF THE BRIEF ARE THE SAME SHADER EDIT, BECAUSE IN THIS ENGINE A CLIFF IS TERRAIN AND NOT A PROP.** Admin ranked rocks and cliffs first and the terrain material third. They were merged and done as one, and the argument is structural rather than a preference: the rock props are metre-scale boulders inside a 170 m scatter ring, while every actual cliff, cut bank, ravine wall, crater rim and mountain flank on the planet is drawn by `TerrainShader`'s steep-slope branch, which was the single constant `vec3(0.30, 0.28, 0.26)` and nothing else. Bedding put into that constant reaches every cliff face on Forge; new boulder meshes reach a few hundred instances. **(1) RN-45a, THE LOD2 DETAIL CARDS, BUILT HERE RATHER THAN REQUESTED.** Blender 5.0 is on this machine and the shipped `detail_cards.glb` already carried `Khronos glTF Blender I/O v5.0.21` in its own bytes, so the exporter could not drift; the control was run first and **an unchanged rebuild came back BYTE-IDENTICAL at 13,616 bytes**, which is what makes every later diff attributable. Authored as CALLABLES and never as a decimate, because `props_common`'s own header already says a collapse decimator eats a grass blade whole and leaves a shard: blades at `segs=1` (one triangle each, a base pair and a tip), four of them instead of six or eight, and 2.4x to 2.7x wider so the covered AREA survives the dropped blades. **Shipped: 4 / 4 / 4 / 6 triangles against 18 / 40 / 18 / 42, LOD0 unchanged to the triangle, 48/48 validate, `lods 8 present`.** The pebble scatter is a single four-sided lobe rather than three flat chips, and that is measured rather than aesthetic: at 45 m with the eye 1.8 m up the ground is seen about 2 degrees off edge-on, so a horizontal facet presents 3.5% of its area and a scatter built from them would vanish at exactly the range the LOD exists to serve. **MEASURED, ONE BINARY, `?proplod2=0` as the control, the RN-15 camera at 1280x720: triangles 1,087,719 to 972,049, i.e. -115,670 and -10.6% of the whole frame, at 69 draw calls, 40 programs, 51 geometries and 93.7 MB VRAM on BOTH sides, with foliage screen coverage 0.5533 to 0.5438 (-1.7%).** The 1,087,719 reproduces RN-30's published figure to the digit, which is what says the control is the right control. **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT CONTROL WAS WRONG IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION:** `?proplod2=0` initially applied to every batch, read 2,303,735 against 972,049, and would have let this pass claim 1.33 M triangles. Most of that gap is the BIOME props' LOD2, which has been shipping since W4. A control that removes more than the change removed is not a control. **(2) RN-45b, THE TERRAIN SURFACE ART: A MACRO VARIATION FIELD AND ROCK STRATA SHIP; THE DETAIL BUMP IS A MEASURED NEGATIVE RESULT AND IS DEFAULTED TO ZERO.** New `web/src/render/materials/TerrainArt.glsl.ts`, called from the existing terrain `ShaderMaterial`. **The DW-10 ledger is UNCHANGED at 3 `ShaderMaterial` plus 3 `onBeforeCompile`**: this edits a custom shader that already existed and adds no program, no draw call, no texture and no byte of VRAM (69/40/51/93.7 identical with the terms on and off). The noise phase is PLANET-CENTRED METRES and not engine space, because engine space rebases and a field keyed on it would swim across the ground every time the floating origin moved. **TWO INDEPENDENT CONFINEMENTS, and they are not the same reason:** a compile-time `#ifndef OF_SCALED` keeps it out of the far scaled scene where a 12 m octave is far below one pixel (confinement by the call graph, RN-30's rule), and a distance fade completing at 4 km against a ~15 km handover is what prevents a SEAM, since a ridge modulated on one side of the handover and flat on the other is the failure a define cannot catch. **MEASURED, one binary, five states each one settled frame apart through `window.__ofTerrainArt`, props hidden, RN-15 camera, 1280x720, two repetitions bit-identical: the macro field moves 99.6% of the near band, 89.9% of the mid, 81.3% of the far and 54.2% of the horizon, by means of 5.86 / 4.99 / 4.65 / 3.51 counts, and ADDS 0.72 / 1.25 / 3.59 / 2.43 counts of 16x16 block-mean spread against a measured noise floor of 0.0000.** At the Mountains spawn the same term reads block-mean spread gains of x1.42 / x2.07 / x2.27 / x1.32. **THE LEVEL IS THE CONTROL AND IT DID ITS JOB TWICE.** A variation layer must raise the SPREAD and leave the LEVEL where it was, and the first tint pair (0.86,1.00,0.90) against (1.12,1.03,0.84) had a per-channel mean of (0.99, 1.015, 0.87), i.e. a 13% net blue reduction applied to the whole planet, which the probe caught as a 5.03% drop in the mid band's level while its spread rose. The pair is now reciprocal about 1.0. The residual per-band level shift is NOT a bias and that was established rather than argued: it scales LINEARLY with amplitude (mid -2.79% at 1.0 against -1.37% at 0.5, a factor of 2.04 where a tone-curve Jensen term would give 4), the field's own mean is 0.000056 over 200,000 samples computed offline in float64, and **the shift FLIPS SIGN between sites (-2.79% at Hills, +2.63% at the Mountains spawn)**, so it is which lobe of the field that patch of ground is sitting on. That is not a defect, it IS the effect. **I was wrong twice about this mechanism before measuring it, and both wrong explanations were plausible.** **(3) THE DETAIL BUMP DOES NOT WORK ON THIS PLANET, AND THE REASON IS ARITHMETIC.** A Mikkelsen surface-gradient bump from screen derivatives, needing no tangents and no UVs and costing two `dFdx` pairs on a field already evaluated. It produced a field of concentric moire arcs across the ground within about fifteen metres of the eye, **and no number saw it: it moved 35% of the near band with a healthy peak either way. A screenshot caught it (`docs/screenshots/RN45_iso_bump.png`), which is DW-7's rule and RN-15's.** A footprint fade was tried FIRST and did nothing, which is what forced the real diagnosis. `pM` is float32 and about 6e5 at Forge's surface, so one ULP is 2^(19-23) = **0.0625 m**, while at the pinned camera one pixel covers **4.3 mm of ground at 2 m and 21.5 mm at 5 m**: the quantum is 3 to 15 times the pixel footprint, whole runs of adjacent pixels sample the SAME quantised position, `dFdx` of the field is exactly zero across them and steps at the quantisation boundaries, and those boundaries are surfaces of constant range from the eye, which is why the artefact is arcs centred on the player. It is not fadeable: the term is well conditioned only once the footprint clears the quantum (about 20 m) and starts aliasing its own 4.2 m octave once the footprint passes a third of that wavelength (about 45 m), and a 25 m annulus is a band of ground rather than a surface treatment. **Left in the build behind `?bumpamp=1` because the measurement is the deliverable. THE MACRO COLOUR TERM IS UNAFFECTED and ships, because it reads the field's VALUE, where 0.0625 m against an 11.9 m finest octave is 0.5% of a wavelength; only the DERIVATIVE is destroyed.** **DEPENDENCY ON WORLD-GEN, stated rather than faked: sub-metre terrain detail of any kind needs a per-chunk phase reduced modulo the octave period on the CPU in float64 and carried with the integer cell index, so the shader adds a small local offset to a small local coordinate and never forms a 6e5 intermediate. That is a terrain-chunk format change.** **(4) ROCK STRATA, the cliff half, at essentially zero cost.** Beds are quantised from `vRelief` (metres above the datum, already a varying), WARPED by a low-frequency noise so they tilt and thicken instead of reading as a contour map, given a per-bed tone and an iron-versus-grey hue from a hash of the integer bed index, and separated by a dark parting at each contact, which is most of what says "layered" at 100 m after the tonal difference has washed out. Bed thickness 2.35 m. **SELF-CONFINING: it only multiplies the rock term, which the slope smoothstep already gates, so flat ground receives exactly nothing and needs no second guard.** Measured at 18% to 22% of the near band with peaks of 105 to 145 counts and EXACTLY ZERO in the mid, far and horizon bands at both sites. **HONEST LIMIT: I did not find a camera in this seed with a large cliff face in the mid or far field, so the term is demonstrated at close range only.** **(5) RN-46, THE SCATTER LEARNS THE POND EXISTS.** WG-35 to WG-42 cut a real pond into `sampleDesignedHeight`, so the bed is ordinary ground to the scatter: legal slope, a biome, and therefore grass cards, pebbles and snow patches four metres under water. Rejected per cell at `WET_REJECT_M` = 0.02 m through `WaterOracle.depthAt`, which is the ONE place "is there water here" is answered (a second copy of that rule in the scatter is the DW-26 failure exactly). **The affordability is a RADIUS guard, not a cache: a body has one water level, so a cell standing above it cannot be wet whatever the disc layout is, and only cells failing that pure arithmetic pay for a WASM call.** Counted as `wetCells` in `stats()` rather than silent (DW-28), and it reads 0 at both dry test sites, which is the negative half of the pair. `?scatterwet=1` restores the old behaviour. **(6) THE THREE FLAGGED FIXES, AND TWO OF THEM WERE NOT WHAT WE THOUGHT.** `?grassshort=` is wired, Boot came free and it cost the one line it was predicted to cost, closing the last unisolated claim in the ground-art programme. `ShadowRig.ts:59-73`'s false comment is corrected in the code and now says plainly that the block is a NO-OP, why (three filters shadow casters against the VIEW camera's layers at r185 line 511, never the shadow camera's), what actually puts the player in the map (`CameraRig` enabling those layers on the near camera), and why the two lines are kept anyway. **`probes/post.js` was NOT inverted, and that hypothesis was mine and Admin's and it was wrong.** Its `check` helper is correct and all three failures were genuine. **The CONTROL had gone stale, and RN-15 is what staled it:** the probe's attribution ratio compares a machine column against the SAME ROW of open ground, that threshold was calibrated on a BARE hillside at RN-10 (0.0267 edge darkening, ratio 13.2), and RN-15 then planted an understorey over it at 0.55 screen coverage. A grass card is an occluder and a 0.9 m AO radius resolves it, so the denominator became a second copy of the numerator's physics. The fix takes a second profile with `of.propsVisible(false)` and asserts attribution there, keeping the shipped frame for the checks that belong to it; **no threshold was tuned. It now PASSES for the first time: control centre 0.3335 against edges 0.0007, ratio 476.4, where the shipped frame reads 1.4658.** Two further real defects fell out: the ratio's `Infinity` on a zero denominator PASSED the attribution check on a dead effect, and the peak-band search ran over bands the file's own comments call invalid. **(7) FOUR INSTRUMENTS WERE BROKEN TONIGHT AND THREE OF THEM WERE MINE, ALL THE SAME SHAPE: A CONTROL THAT DEPENDS ON SOMETHING THAT MOVED.** (a) The macro claim was measured over a band 55% covered in grass, so a real term read as a 0.956 DECREASE. (b) The sky control was a band of the shooting frame, which is sky at Hills and is mountains at the 4,668 m spawn, so the probe reported the term had escaped into the sky when the BAND had stopped being sky; it is now taken at pitch +72 over the top 45% of the frame, which is sky at every site and every altitude, and reads **mean 0.0000 and peak 0 over 648,000 px at BOTH sites**. (c) The lower frame of that same up-look moved by a mean of 0.78 with a peak of 194: the animated first-person arms, which `probes/post.js` already documents and excludes. (d) A noise floor was added and the FIRST version of it read EXACTLY equal to the effect it was bounding, to four decimal places, on every band, because the state was not reset before the floor capture. **Two numbers agreeing to the last digit is a wiring diagnosis, never a coincidence.** With it fixed the floor is 0.0000 and every claim is asserted against it rather than against an unreachable zero. **(8) RN-47, THE UNDERWATER VIEW, AND THE APPROVED DW-10 SLOT WAS NOT SPENT.** Admin approved one scene shader slot for water. It was built as a POST pass instead, so the ledger is untouched: RN-10 established post programs cannot be infected by the depth policy (no projection, no depth test, no `gl_FragDepth`), and a post pass also reaches the terrain, the props, the machines and the scatter at once with no per-material edit, using the depth buffer a scene material would have had to recompute. Beer-Lambert per channel with red absorbed roughly 22x harder than blue (Smith and Baker 1981), the water surface treated as a PLANE because curvature over a 22 m pond is under a millimetre, and the exit distance `headUnderM / max(dot(rd, up), eps)` giving the same confined-layer structure the aerial perspective already uses. **Measured, eye 1.346 m under, 921,600 px: red over blue 1.0493 to 0.5813, mean red -44.34 against mean blue +3.36, 81.2% of pixels moved, peak 152.** **The negative controls are BIT-EXACT rather than approximate**, because screenshots cannot reach zero here (sim time advances between captures) so they were taken on `of.framehash()`, which renders and reads back synchronously: dry at 300 m gives the identical hash `3035253034` both ways, and WADING at 1.0 m depth with `headUnderM` -0.62 gives the identical hash `1014643489`, while the same instrument submerged gives two different hashes. The wading control is the sharper one: a pass keyed on "is the player wet" passes the dry control and fails that. Above water the pass is SKIPPED rather than multiplied by one. **Cost: +3 post draw calls and +3 programs when submerged, ZERO when dry, +3.5 MB allocated lazily on the first submerged frame.** It also caught a framebuffer feedback loop the same way RN-10 did: the first shape reported `ran: true`, counted its draws and moved exactly zero pixels while the driver emitted 256 `GL_INVALID_OPERATION` per frame. **NOT DONE, and named: the first-person arms are not attenuated (they are drawn after the interpose point, so it needs a second interpose); the water plane has no lateral bound, so a level ray inside a shallow puddle stays submerged past the bank; a free camera is never tinted because `swim()` needs a capsule; and there is still no surface seen from beneath.** **NOT COMMITTED, DELIBERATELY: `web/src/app/Boot.ts`.** It carries the vessel lane's uncommitted `resumeWorld` call, whose `ResumeBoot.ts` is still UNTRACKED, so a commit of Boot.ts alone would not build. My two hunks there are three lines and will ride with that lane's commit. Every other file is committed path-limited. New: `web/src/render/materials/TerrainArt.glsl.ts`, `web/src/render/post/UnderwaterGlsl.ts`, `web/src/render/post/UnderwaterPass.ts`, `web/tools/smoke/probes/{terrainart,artshot,underwater}.js`. Isolation: `?terrainart=0 ?macrovar=0 ?terrainbump=0 ?strata=0 ?macroamp= ?bumpamp= ?strataamp= ?proplod2=0 ?grassshort=0 ?scatterwet=1 ?underwater=0 ?uwext= ?uwtint= ?uwscatter= ?uwpath=`, all registered in `run.mjs`. Runtime handles `window.__ofTerrainArt.{set,get,reset}` and `window.__ofUnderwater.{set,state,setTune}`. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN45_world_{before,after}.png` (the RN-15 camera, so RN-15, RN-30 and this pass are all comparable), `RN45_terrain_{before,after}.png` (props hidden, which is the only way to photograph a ground material), `RN45_iso_{macro,bump}.png`, `RN_underwater_{on,off}.png`. **FRAME TIMINGS ARE NOT PUBLISHED. Four lanes were building and probing on this machine and the ring drifts by more over a minute than any of these effects costs. The invariant table is the whole cost claim and it is not noise sensitive.**
>
> **RN-30 (2026-07-27): THE GROUND STOPS BEING FLAT, AND THE AERIAL-PERSPECTIVE TERM LANDS ON THE SECOND ATTEMPT BECAUSE THE CONFINEMENT CHANGED RATHER THAN THE TUNING.** Three changes, one binary pair plus a one-binary isolation series, and the headline is that all three sky controls read **exactly 0.0000 mean and 0 peak over 276,480 samples**. RN-15 bought 3.7x the foliage by taking the understorey out of the shadow pass, and the picture paid for it: every grass shadow in the frame went with the 12.3 ms. **(1) SHADOW CONTACT COMES BACK AS TWO COMPLEMENTARY TERMS, NEITHER OF WHICH IS A CASCADE, AND THE REASON IT IS NOT A CASCADE WAS CHECKED IN THREE'S SOURCE RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `WebGLShadowMap.renderObject` filters casters with `object.layers.test( camera.layers )` at r185 line 511, where `camera` is the VIEW camera passed to `shadowMap.render( shadowsArray, scene, camera )`, **not** the per-light shadow camera, so `light.shadow.camera.layers` is never consulted anywhere in the shadow pass and layers cannot select a cascade. `Object3D.onBeforeShadow` does fire per object per light but at lines 535/549, i.e. AFTER the `castShadow` test, so it cannot veto a draw either; the only supported per-cascade signal is `_frustum.intersectsObject`, and the understorey is ONE `BatchedMesh` whose bound covers the whole ring, so every cascade intersects it. "Understorey in cascade 0 only" needs a fork of three's shadow map. **This also convicts a live comment in my own lane's code: `ShadowRig.ts:59-73` asserts exactly the layer behaviour disproved above and enables `LAYER_PLAYER_BODY`/`LAYER_PROPS` on cascade 0's shadow camera to buy a restriction it does not buy.** The props and the player reach the shadow map because `CameraRig` enables those layers on the NEAR camera. The code is harmless, the comment is false, and the 45-to-27 draw-call measurement recorded beside it was real with another cause. So: **(1a) A BASE-CONTACT GRADIENT BAKED INTO THE FOLIAGE VERTICES, at literally zero cost.** A plant is darker where it meets the ground because the ground and its own neighbours occlude the sky there; that is an ambient-occlusion fact about the ASSET, so it belongs in the asset's vertices. `PropGeometry.bakeColour` writes 0.42 over the bottom 38% of each geometry's own height, smoothstepped, into the per-vertex colour attribute **that already existed and was a constant 255**. No draw call, no triangle, no shader, no uniform, not one extra byte. It survives per-instance tinting because three composes `vColor = 1` then `vColor.rgb *= color` then `vColor *= getBatchingColor(...)`, so the gradient and `PropLibrary.tint` MULTIPLY (`color_vertex.glsl.js`, read rather than assumed). **Measured: near band mean darkening 0.0323, p99 0.3705, 17.9% of pixels moved over 236,195 px; mid band 0.0348 / 0.3861 / 25.4%; 7 batches carry it.** **(1b) SCREEN-SPACE CONTACT SHADOWS, `post/ContactGlsl.ts` plus `post/ContactPass.ts`, two full-screen passes.** A depth-buffer march toward the sun bounded to 0.45 m of WORLD distance, which is what makes it a contact shadow rather than a shadow: it physically cannot see a hill, so it cannot disagree with the cascaded maps about one. It divides from cascade 0 by RANGE exactly as the baked ORM map divides from SSAO by spatial frequency, and cascade 0 at a +/-15.8 m box and 15.5 mm per texel cannot resolve a 3-triangle blade against the soil under it. **Measured: near band mean darkening 0.1012, p99 0.6581, 40.4% of pixels moved over 237,762 px; mid band 0.0521 / 0.3744 / 42.4%.** The sun direction is derived from `shadowCascade0`'s own position minus its target, which is not a second authority because ShadowRig already merged the sun and cascade 0 into one light; when the rig is built disabled the vector is zero and the march skips, so `?shadows=0` also removes contact shadows, which is correct rather than incidental. **(2) THE UNDERSTOREY STOPS READING AS A CROP FIELD, and the durable half of the fix is structural rather than a smaller number.** RN-15 had already retuned the height jitter once for this (1.48 down to 1.30) and it did not hold, because the jitter COMPOUNDS with the distance upscale: `Detail_GrassCardB`'s authored 0.60 m reached 0.60 x 1.3 x 1.30 x 1.32 = **1.34 m**, chest height on a 1.8 m character, and a field of 1.3 m blades has no middle distance in it. The band drops to 0.40 to 0.82 AND `DETAIL_FAR_GROW` is demoted to a HORIZONTAL upscale, so height is no longer a factor in that product at all. **Density-weighted mean understorey height 0.4262 m to 0.2811 m (-34.0%); tallest card in the world 1.34 m to 0.64 m (-52%).** Coverage is held rather than spent: the width band gains 1.18x, and measured screen coverage moves 0.5503 to 0.5456 across three repetitions each, i.e. -0.9%. Capping a product by shrinking one factor has to be repeated every time either factor moves; taking a factor out of the product holds. **(3) AERIAL PERSPECTIVE SHIPS, AND THE ONLY THING THAT CHANGED FROM THE REVERTED ATTEMPT IS THE CONFINEMENT.** The first attempt put the aerosol INSIDE `ofAtmoScatter` and relied on a 400 m scale height to keep it off the sky; that is confinement by HEIGHT, near-horizon sky rays lie inside the layer for tens of kilometres, and it failed its own control (sky saturation 0.494 to 0.410, sky red 81.9 to 97.0). **The seam that works already existed and nobody had named it: every escaping ray in this codebase passes `tMax = 1.0e9` and every terminating ray passes a real metre distance.** So `ofAtmoAerial` is a SEPARATE ENTRY POINT called from exactly one site, `TerrainShader`'s aerial-perspective line, which is the only call with a finite distance to geometry. The sky quad and the terrain's own upward sky-ambient ray both pass 1.0e9 into the other function and cannot reach it. "The sky did not move" stops being a tuning result and becomes a property of the call graph, **and a `#define` keyed on "is this the terrain material" would have got it wrong, because the sky-ambient ray IS a terrain fragment issuing an escaping ray.** Two further things had to be right. The optical depth is ANALYTIC (`L * H / (h1 - h0) * (exp(-h0/H) - exp(-h1/H))`, the closed form for a linear height profile) rather than marched, which is what makes it safe from orbit: a viewer 100 km up collects about H worth of dense air however long the ray is, where a midpoint or Simpson rule weights its samples by the FULL path length and would produce an enormous optical depth for a scaled planet. And the layer sits on the TERRAIN, heights measured from `min(h0, h1)`, not on the datum: measured from the datum the same term is 2x weaker on an 860 m hillside than at sea level and vanishes on a mountain, so one pair of constants would have to be retuned per site. Shipped at sigma 4.5e-4 per metre (Koschmieder 8.7 km), H 400 m, grey tint (0.38, 0.39, 0.43), g 0.35 with a 0.55 isotropic multiple-scattering floor. **The tint is grey because of the first attempt's finding, which is the useful one: a blue-biased coefficient does not haze, it DARKENS** (far-band red 70.2 to 57.0 while saturation moved 0.004), because it extinguishes red hard and refills with saturated blue. The g and floor pair was set to compress the phase function's dynamic range: g 0.55 spans 45:1 between the solar and anti-solar directions, which would make the distance alternately glow and darken as the player turns; g 0.35 with a 0.55 floor spans 2.8:1 and the haze is brighter than the ground it veils in every direction, which is what haze means. **(4) A DEFECT IN MY OWN INSTRUMENT, and it is the reason the first tuning read as doing nothing.** `probes/groundart.js`'s "far band" is frame rows 0.42 to 0.52, and at the pinned Hills camera the horizon sits at about 0.28, so that band is ground **30 to 80 m away**: near enough that no atmosphere on any planet would change it. The term was measured against the wrong range and read 0.3% of pixels moved. A `horizon` band at 0.29 to 0.38 was ADDED rather than the old one moved, so the RN-15 numbers stay comparable. **RESULT AT THE LOCKED RN-15 CAMERA (frozen builds `RN30-before` / `RN30-after` per BT-27, `vite preview`, 1280x720, sandbox, walk, seed 0xbf00d01, lat 12 / lon 150 / yaw 300 / pitch -10, sunT 0.30, RTX 4060 Ti, three repetitions each, interleaved BEFORE/AFTER): horizon saturation drop 0.0254/0.0322/0.0258 to 0.2148/0.2141/0.1994, i.e. mean 0.0278 to 0.2094, a 7.5x; horizon blue shift -0.0551/-0.0475/-0.0548 to +0.1199/+0.1191/+0.1019, i.e. THE SIGN FLIPS (distant ground was redder than ground underfoot and is now bluer); horizon band mean red 118.57 to 132.48 and mean blue 66.22 to 95.13; RN-15's own near-against-far saturation drop -0.185 to -0.139, still negative and correctly so, because that band is 30 to 80 m. Screen coverage 0.5580/0.5582/0.5346 to 0.5449/0.5429/0.5489. Draw calls 67 to 69 of a 150 budget, triangles 1,087,115 to 1,087,719 of a 2.7 M alert, programs 36 to 38, geometries 50 to 50, VRAM 92.8 to 93.7 MB. Props placed 45,854, instances 48,511, `deliveredFraction` 1.0018, pool refusals 0, `cellsCapped` 0, `chunksCapped` 0, all unchanged on both sides. Frame p50 11.3/10.5/12.3 to 10.6/10.9/9.9, p95 20.7/17.3/20.9 to 18.3/18.1/25.9, p99 26.9/19.5/29.8 to 28.2/20.8/42.5, worst 39.2/21.1/46.0 to 73.1/24.6/71.1.** **THE FRAME NUMBERS ARE PUBLISHED AS UNRESOLVED AND THAT IS THE HONEST READING, not a hedge.** An interleaved A/B/A/B series inside ONE binary with medians (RN-10's method) gives p50 all-on 11.4, contact-off 10.6, aerial-off 10.3, both-off 10.7, which is **non-monotonic**: both-off is slower than contact-off, and the within-label spread for "all on" alone is 10.0 / 11.4 / 11.5. Four other lanes are building and probing on this machine tonight and the frame ring drifts by more over a minute than any of these effects costs. What IS measurable and is not noise: **+2 fixed draw calls, +2 programs, +0.9 MB of R8 buffer**. An earlier quiet-machine reading had the BEFORE binary at p50 5.8 and I nearly reported a 4.8 ms regression from it; re-measuring the same binary later gave 10.2, which is the whole argument for paired repetitions rather than one A then one B. **THE ORBITAL CONSEQUENCE IS REAL, QUANTIFIED AND DELIBERATE, and it is stated rather than hidden because the same terrain program draws the scaled far planet and a ray from orbit to the ground is a TERMINATING ray.** The vertical column through the layer is exactly sigma x H = 0.18 by construction, so 16% haze. Measured at `?scenario=orbit`, 300 km, the term toggled inside one settled frame: planet disc saturation 0.4017 to 0.2789, mean red 100.23 to 120.66, 27.9% of the frame moved with a peak of 56 counts, and the disc is 34.7% of the frame. At `?scenario=ascent`, 12 km: saturation 0.4511 to 0.4266, mean red 91.68 to 99.53. The picture reads as a planet with air over its land rather than as a grey ball (`docs/screenshots/RN30_orbit_{before,after}.png`), and the scale height is the knob if that judgement changes: it does essentially nothing to a ground-level horizontal ray and is entirely what the orbital column costs. **ISOLATION, and it is weaker than this lane's standard for two of the three, for an ownership reason rather than a technical one.** `?contact=0` plus `?cslength= ?cssteps= ?csthick= ?csstrength=` are query flags (standing rule 7). The other two are RUNTIME handles instead, `window.__ofProps.setBaseShade` and `window.__ofAtmos.setAerial`, on the `Surfaces.ts` precedent, because everything that configures the scatter and the atmosphere is passed by `Boot.ts` and the VAB lane owns Boot this round. That turned out to be the STRONGER instrument for the two claims that are matched pairs (one settled frame apart, so camera, sun and streamed chunk set cannot differ), but **the grass HEIGHT has no runtime control at all and its before/after is a binary pair**; `?grassshort=` is one line in `Boot.ts` and `Config.ts` and is a coordination item, not a design gap. **NOT DONE, DELIBERATELY, AND THE LEDGER IS NOT SPENT.** Admin approved a DW-10 shader-ledger entry for an `onBeforeCompile` giving props aerial perspective, conditional on a working term shipping. The term shipped, so the approval stands available, and **I am declining to spend it on the measurement rather than on the principle**: props reach `RADIUS_M` = 170 m, and at 4.5e-4 per metre the optical depth at the ring's outermost instance is 0.0765, so a prop out there is drawn at 100% of its own radiance where the terrain at the same range is at 92.6%. A bounded 7.4% worst-case discrepancy is not worth a slot in a cap of five, and it shrinks if the scatter radius ever does. **PRE-EXISTING AND NOT MINE: `probes/post.js` returns `valid: false` on BOTH binaries with an identical three-item `fails` list** ("AO is not a global multiply", "AO darkens the machine column far more than the SAME ROW of open ground", "open ground in the peak row is essentially unchanged"), while its underlying numbers are healthy and moved the right way (centre-over-edges 1.5975 to 1.7359, p99 0.6676 to 0.7046). The strings read like the probe's PASS conditions inverted. `grass.js`, `cutbank.js`, `maps.js` and `tools/smoke/boot.mjs` are green on the after binary. New: `web/src/render/instancing/PropGeometry.ts`, `web/src/render/post/ContactGlsl.ts`, `web/src/render/post/ContactPass.ts`, `web/tools/smoke/probes/contact.js`, `web/tools/smoke/probes/aerialrange.js`. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN30_world_{before,after}.png` (the RN-15 camera, so all three passes are comparable), `RN30_contact_{on,off}.png` (one binary, `?contact=0`, pitch -4), `RN30_orbit_{before,after}.png`.

> **RN-15 (2026-07-27): THE GROUND. 3.7x THE FOLIAGE FOR 40% FEWER TRIANGLES AND A THIRD OFF THE FRAME, AND THE THING THAT PAID FOR IT WAS NOT A NEW ASSET, IT WAS TWO STALE RENDER-POLICY DECISIONS.** Reid: "the terrain, trees rocks, ground, etc look like shit still. Im talking satisfactory (the game) level graphics", while being explicitly HAPPY with the rocket and the pad, which is what said the gap was WORLD art and not the renderer. **THE BUDGET WAS FOUND BEFORE ANY OF IT WAS SPENT, and that is the whole shape of this pass.** At a fixed Hills camera (lat 12, lon 150, yaw 300, pitch -10, 1280x720, sandbox, one binary) the isolation flags said: **`?props=0` removes 1,383,872 of the frame's 1,804,969 triangles and 15.0 ms of its 18.0 ms p50; `?shadows=0` removes 1,203,460 triangles and 14.5 ms; `?detail=0` removes 1,003,112 triangles and 12.3 ms for 9,738 understorey cards.** That last is about 103 triangles for a card whose LOD0 is 18 to 42, because **every card was being drawn FOUR times**, once in the near pass and once in each of three shadow cascades. A 0.36 m card's cast shadow is a few pixels under a tuft that is already casting one. **(1) THE UNDERSTOREY LEFT THE SHADOW PASS, and it needed its own batches to do it.** `castShadow` is a property of the MESH and `Detail_GrassCardA/B` share `OF_Grass` with `Plains_GrassTuftA/B`, so there was no way to spend it per prop; `PropLibrary` now registers the shared atlas into `<material>:detail` batches (`DETAIL_SUFFIX`), read off `SHARED_ATLAS` rather than passed in, so Boot.ts kept its one-line change. Two things fell out free and both were wanted: the understorey got its OWN pool ceiling instead of sharing the one that was full at 16,384, and `stats().perMaterial` now reports the two layers separately with a `casts` flag, so a triangle count that does not say which batches were in the shadow pass can no longer be read. **(2) PER-INSTANCE FRUSTUM CULLING WENT BACK ON FOR THE UNDERSTOREY, AND THIS IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER: the comment in `PropLibrary` said culling "costs far more than it saves", it was RIGHT, it was measured at 9,340 props, and DENSITY WALKED IT OVER THE LINE without touching a line of that code.** The scatter is a RING centred on the player, so about three quarters of it is behind the camera and every one of those instances is still a `multiDrawElements` range the driver walks. Measured, same binary, `?propcull=0` as the control: **triangles 1,916,689 to 1,087,115, p50 22.8 to 11.3 ms, p99 33.6 to 20.2 ms, and screen coverage 0.5545 against 0.5563, i.e. the same picture.** This is DW-19's lesson pointed at a second constant: a measured trade is only valid at the scale it was measured, and the failure mode is silent. **WHAT THE BUDGET BOUGHT: understorey density 3.6x** (GROUND_DETAIL 906,000 to 3,228,000 instances per km2), **a graded ring instead of a hard edge** (`DETAIL_FULL_M` 30 m at full density falling linearly to 0.18 at `DETAIL_RADIUS_M`, which went 55 to 78 m, because the ring EDGE was the second thing the eye found after the bare ground and a ring that ends is a ring you can see the edge of at any radius), **per-instance colour** (`BatchedMesh.setColorAt` value jitter plus a hue drift toward dry plus 5.5% flowers, all the same card geometry so no new asset, no draw call and no triangle), **non-uniform scale** so height and width are drawn separately, and **CONTACT BLENDING**: five understorey cards tucked around the base of every prop the registry marks `collides`, which is the same set as "has a silhouette that meets the ground", so the skirt follows the asset table rather than a second list. **RESULT AT THE LOCKED CAMERA, three repetitions after against two before: props placed 12,379 to 45,854, instances 15,020 to 48,511, foliage screen coverage 0.4118/0.4178 to 0.5563/0.5568/0.5586, draw calls 72 to 67 of a 150 budget, triangles 1,804,969 to 1,087,115 of a 2.7 M alert, frame p50 17.4/18.0 to 10.8/10.9/11.1/11.3 ms, p95 23.9/25.2 to 16.1/16.5/16.6, p99 29.0/44.7 to 19.1/20.2/20.4/21.5 against a 25 ms alert the BEFORE was outside and the after is inside, worst frame 61.0/72.9 to 21.7/23.8/24.8/26.2. Pool refusals 0, `cellsCapped` 0, `chunksCapped` 0, VRAM 92.8 MB.** `MAX_CAPACITY` went 16,384 to 65,536 and that is a memory guard and nothing else (DW-28): a slot is about 100 B of typed array and 80 B of texture, a batch only reserves what it has grown into, and the batch that actually reached the new range is `OF_Grass:detail` at 32,881. **TWO DEFECTS OF MINE, both found by instruments this project already had. (a) `probes/grass.js` failed at `deliveredFraction` 1.5146**: the contact skirt PRODUCES cards it never REQUESTED, which breaks RN-7's ratio in the direction that looks harmless, and a ratio that can read 1.5 can no longer be trusted to read 0.98 when something is genuinely being dropped. A skirt card is not a density draw, its request is exactly `CONTACT_CARDS` per colliding prop, and counting it that way restored 0.9997 while still catching truncation. **(b) A PICTURE CAUGHT WHAT NO NUMBER DID**: the card height jitter topped out at 1.48 and COMPOUNDS with the distance upscale at 1.45, so a card authored at 0.58 m could reach 1.2 m, put the understorey at the same height as the tufts it is meant to sit under, and made a meadow read as a cornfield. Two multipliers that were fine apart and wrong together. **AERIAL PERSPECTIVE WAS BUILT, MEASURED AND REVERTED, and the measurement is the deliverable.** The gap is real and is quantified: near band against far band IN ONE CAPTURE (so exposure, sun elevation, tone curve and colour grade are equal by construction and range is the only free variable) reads **saturation -0.149, i.e. the DISTANT ground is MORE saturated than the ground at the player's feet.** There is no aerial perspective at playable range, and the reason is that the Rayleigh term is CORRECT and therefore useless here: Earth's molecular scattering moves a ridge by about one part in a hundred over the 200 m to 3 km a player looks across. A boundary-layer aerosol term was added to the SHARED model (DW-22, never three's `FogExp2`, which would have been free and would have been a second authority disagreeing with the terrain integral at every range). It did not work and it failed in two informative ways. Biased blue like Rayleigh it DARKENED rather than hazed (far band mean red 70.2 to 57.0, saturation 0.7072 to 0.7008, i.e. it moved by 0.004), because a blue coefficient extinguishes red hard and refills with saturated blue; grey coefficients plus an isotropic multiple-scattering floor of 0.22 on the phase improved it (red 70.2 to 60.9, blue over red 0.393 to 0.460) **and then failed its own control: the SKY band moved, saturation 0.494 to 0.410 and mean red 81.9 to 97.0.** The 400 m scale height was supposed to keep it off the sky structurally and does not, because near-horizon sky rays sit inside the layer. Shipping a half-tuned change to the one model the sky, the near terrain and the far scaled planet all read is precisely the cross-lane cost this log keeps recording, so **it is fully reverted and `Atmosphere.glsl.ts` is byte-identical to main.** The sky control is what made that call possible and it is kept in the probe. **NOT DONE, deliberately:** the colour grade (item 5) already implements cool shadows against warm sun (`shadowTint` [0.97, 0.995, 1.035], `highlightTint` [1.035, 1.005, 0.96]) and retuning another pass's measured output on the last run of a session is a poor trade; new rock and cliff meshes (item 4) are an art-lane job; **props still get NO aerial perspective at all while the terrain behind them does**, which is a real inconsistency I found and did not fix, because the honest fix is an `onBeforeCompile` on the prop materials and that bumps the DW-10 shader ledger, which is an Admin decision and not mine. New: `web/src/world/ScatterEmit.ts`, `web/src/world/ScatterLook.ts`, `web/tools/smoke/probes/groundart.js`. Isolation: `?propcull=0` (standing rule 7). Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN15_world_{before,after}.png` (same seed, site, camera, viewport and probe; the before from a pre-change build of the same tree) and `RN15_nocull.png`.
>
> **RN-10 (2026-07-27), THE POST-PROCESSING STACK (DW-35 item 1). THE ENTIRE POST STACK WAS ONE LINE OF ACES TONEMAPPING; IT IS NOW AMBIENT OCCLUSION, BLOOM, A COLOUR GRADE AND FXAA, FOR +15 FIXED DRAW CALLS, +27.7 MB AND +1.0 ms p50.** New `web/src/render/post/` (8 files), driven surface `web/src/app/DebugPost.ts`, probe `web/tools/smoke/probes/post.js`. **THE FOUR-PASS RIG IS NOT TOUCHED AND THE STACK IS NOT A FIFTH PASS.** It brackets the four (bind an HDR target first, resolve to the canvas last) and interposes at exactly ONE point inside them: after the NEAR pass and BEFORE its `clearDepth()`. That instant is the only one in the frame at which the depth attachment holds the near 1:1 scene and nothing else, which is what makes depth-only ambient occlusion correct here: the sky writes no depth, the far scaled pass was cleared, and the view model is not drawn yet. Moving that one call one statement later would hand AO a cleared buffer, every pixel would read as background, the effect would vanish, and every frame-time number would stay healthy. So `probes/post.js` asserts occlusion CONTRAST, never that AO ran. **REVERSED-Z IS HANDLED BY UNPROJECTION, NOT BY A HAND-ROLLED LINEARISE.** `post/DepthGlsl.ts` inverts whatever projection `DepthPolicy` built, so the reversed flip undoes itself; `DepthPolicy` gained `clearValue()` (0 reversed, 1 otherwise, and getting it backwards makes AO skip the world and process the sky) and `logFC()` for the log branch, which is a closed-form `w` recovery because an inverse projection applied to a log-encoded sample is silently wrong rather than visibly wrong. All three depth modes are COMPILED VARIANTS switched by `#define`, so a mode that is not selected cannot run. **AO IS GTAO FROM DEPTH ALONE: no normal buffer, no second geometry pass.** The alternatives were MRT out of every scene material (which means editing the terrain, atmosphere and starfield shaders DW-10 caps at five) or an override-material prepass (which doubles the near scene's 53 draw calls against a 150 budget). Normals come from four depth taps. **DW-10 LEDGER: scene custom shaders UNCHANGED at 3 `ShaderMaterial` plus 3 `onBeforeCompile` sites; post adds 9 full-screen programs (AO, its denoise, its upsample, its apply, two bloom downsample variants, the bloom upsample, the composite and FXAA) that CANNOT be infected by the depth policy, because they have no projection, no depth test and no `gl_FragDepth`.** **MEASURED, one binary, one camera, one settled frame pair via runtime toggles: AO peak row-band centre darkening 0.3528 against 0.0267 in the SAME ROW of open ground, ratio 13.2; median lit pixel 0.0000; p99 0.5974.** Same row is the control: it holds range, sun elevation and the lighting gradient equal and leaves proximity to a machine as the only free variable, which is RN-8's circular-pit argument applied to occlusion. **COST, as PAIRED repetitions rather than one A then one B: master switch +1.0 ms p50 (5.7 to 6.7), reproduced across two independent runs; the per-effect deltas fall INSIDE their own run-to-run spread and are published as unresolved rather than as numbers.** Draw calls 53 scene + 15 post = 68 of a 150 target, and the two are published SEPARATELY (`PassTimings.sceneCalls` / `.postCalls`) so a fixed full-screen cost cannot quietly eat another lane's headroom. VRAM 75.1 to 102.8 MB, and `StatsProbe` now reads it LIVE off the renderer instead of from `Boot.ts`'s boot-time snapshot, which was sized for a 300x150 canvas and read 0.9 MB for a 27.7 MB allocation. **FOUR DEFECTS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, ALL FOUR MINE, AND THREE OF THEM LOOKED FINE.** (1) **A FRAMEBUFFER FEEDBACK LOOP**, 256 `GL_INVALID_OPERATION` per frame: the AO apply targeted the scene while sampling the depth texture that is one of that framebuffer's attachments. Fixed structurally, by resolving the depth-aware upsample into its own full-resolution buffer first, so the one pass that touches scene colour samples exactly one texture the scene framebuffer does not own. This is the only one of the four that failed loudly, which is why it is not still there. (2) **THE COLOUR GRADE CRUSHED THE SHADOWS**: contrast 1.06 about a pivot of 0.4135 applied to the ACES output, where mid-grey is 0.18 and not 0.41. Sky red **37.9 to 7.9 of 255**, invisible in any whole-frame mean because the crush is confined to the dark end. The grade now runs AFTER the sRGB encode where the pivot means what it says; sky red reads 29.7. (3) **MY FIRST BEFORE/AFTER PAIR WAS INVALID AND LOOKED LIKE A RESULT.** The build recipe places machines by trying pitches until a ghost is legal, so two runs consume different amounts of sim time, and the sun moves with sim time. The pair differed by a warm-to-cool shift across the whole hillside that read exactly like a colour grade and was entirely sun angle. `of.setTime()` is now re-pinned after every data-dependent wait and immediately before the captured frame. (4) **MY OWN PERCENTILE METRIC PASSED ON PIXELS THE EFFECT NEVER TOUCHED.** `darkening = 1 - with/without` divides by nothing on an already-black pixel and reads 1.0, so the probe reported p99 and max darkening of exactly 1.0000 and PASSED its "the 99th percentile moves a lot" check. A ratio metric needs a floor on its denominator, always. **AND ONE THAT WAS NOT A DEFECT AT ALL: I read a green wash across the ground in the after image, and I was wrong.** Measured region by region, the ground is 1.5% darker and its green bias is present IDENTICALLY in the before. It was a sequential-viewing illusion; only measuring the two captures settled it. That is WG-28's lesson pointed the other way: looking at the thing is necessary and is not sufficient. **THE TWO OCCLUSION SOURCES (the texture lane's baked ORM map and this one) ARE SEPARATED BY SPATIAL FREQUENCY, STRUCTURALLY.** `aoRadiusM` 0.9 m on a half-resolution buffer cannot resolve a panel gap, and the probe proves it by re-running the entire measurement at a 5 cm radius and requiring it to produce nothing. The first version of that control asserted "a machine face darkens less than 6%", measured 10.1%, and would have been repaired by moving the threshold; it was sampling the smelter's own recessed panels, which are decimetre-scale and are legitimately this term's business. **STANDING RULE 7 IN FULL: `?post=0` restores the pre-lane path exactly** (no render target, no composite, three's own ACES straight to the canvas, 0.9 MB), and `?ao=0`, `?bloom=0`, `?grade=0`, `?aa=0` isolate one effect each, plus `?aoradius= ?aostrength= ?aoscale= ?aoslices= ?aosteps= ?bloomstrength= ?bloomthresh= ?exposure= ?msaa= ?fxaalod=0`. **`?clear=` and `?scenario=zfight` force the stack OFF by default**, because both read RAW PIXEL VALUES: magenta through ACES, an sRGB encode, a split tone and a vignette is not magenta within `Loop.countHoles`'s 12-count tolerance, so every crack and hole probe would read ZERO holes and PASS. That is Boot.ts:114's existing argument applied to a layer that recolours every pixel. **NO TEMPORAL JITTER ANYWHERE**, because this project asserts that a settled frame equals the previous one (FrameDiff's second difference; `wires.js` requiring five identical draw-call reads): the AO rotation is a pure function of `gl_FragCoord` with a period of exactly 4 px, and the denoise kernel is exactly 4x4. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN9_{contact,terrain}_{before,after}.png`. **HONEST LIMIT: this is one lane of DW-35 and it is not Satisfactory yet.** No screen-space contact shadows, no depth of field, no temporal AA, and the largest remaining gap is the materials ceiling DW-35 names second, which is RN-9's lane and not this one.
>
> **RN-9 (2026-07-27), THE SHIPPED TEXTURES REACH THE SCREEN. Three changes, all three landed, and the hand-off's one predicted failure mode turned out not to exist in this three version.** Commit `87f9846` shipped two tiling PBR surfaces and nothing changed on screen because no client path consumed them (ASSET-SPECS 2.9). **(1) `uv` now copies through all three `normalize()` functions UNCONDITIONALLY**, via one shared `Surfaces.copyUv` rather than a fourth copy-paste, because the conditional form is what reintroduces the mixed-attribute merge that returns `null` and degrades to one drawn primitive. Measured across the three paths: **294 geometries copied real UVs, 0 synthesised, 0 count mismatches**, and per consumer, because a single total would have let the machine path pass on the nodes' UVs (`machines 105 / nodes 55 / props 134`). **(2) The maps are attached** at all three sites with `RepeatWrapping`, `repeat = 1/tile_m` (0.667 for `panel` at 1.5 m, 1.333 for `coarse` at 0.75 m), `NoColorSpace`, anisotropy 16 (three clamps to the device max at upload), one ORM texture in three slots. **(3) `NodeBatch.familyOf` buckets by (family, metalness)**, so the node batches went from `matte`/`metal` to **`coarse:matte` / `flat:matte` / `coarse:metal`**: bark and rock keep the rock map, **Leaf, LeafDeep, LeafDry, LeafLight, Grass, Ice and Oil land in `flat:matte` and carry nothing**. **THE NORMAL MAP IS DEMONSTRABLY READ, NOT MERELY ATTACHED, and that is the assertion the structural half cannot make.** Toggled inside ONE settled frame (`window.__ofSurfaces.setMaps`, the `PropLibrary.setVisible` pattern, so camera, sun, streamed set and terrain cannot differ between captures) and split `normal` from `orm` so the difference is ATTRIBUTABLE: **normal map alone moves the ground box by mean 2.78 counts over 288,000 px with 18.6% of pixels moving more than 3 counts and a peak of 375; ORM alone 1.87 / 23.7%; both 3.37 / 24.7%. The negative control is a sky box in the same captures at exactly 0.000 mean and 0 peak over 72,000 px**, which is what makes the ground number the maps' and not a global exposure wobble. Ground mean luminance 92.41 -> 91.80, i.e. very slightly DOWN, which is the direction ORM-multiplies predicts (wear and grime can only take a surface below its palette constant). **`aoMap.channel = 0` is set and asserted, and the hand-off's prediction that three defaults it to a nonexistent `uv1` is OUT OF DATE for r185**: `Texture.channel` defaults to 0 and `WebGLPrograms` reads `material.aoMap.channel`, so the predicted silent-tint failure could not have occurred here, verified in `node_modules/three/src/textures/Texture.js:118` and `WebGLPrograms.js:273`, not assumed. The line is kept because the ORM image is ONE object in three slots. **THE MACHINE BATCH'S CUSTOM SHADER EDIT WAS THE ONE THING PIXELS COULD NOT REACH** (the walk scenario places no machines), so it is asserted off the REAL shader string three hands `onBeforeCompile`: `roughnessmap_fragment` 2605, `metalnessmap_fragment` 2639, `normal_fragment_maps` 2707 all resolve before the `emissivemap_fragment` hook at 2827, and `aomap_fragment` at 2996 is applied after it, on all four `MachineBatch` instances (`factoryMachines`, `beltCargo`, `structures`, `launchpads`). That is the ordering claim ASSET-SPECS 2.8 rests on when it refuses an albedo map, checked rather than remembered. **COST, from two builds of the same tree differing ONLY by this change, same seed and same input tape: draw calls 60 -> 64 of a 150 budget, and the +4 is exactly the one extra node bucket times the main pass plus three shadow cascades; triangles 849,513 -> 849,513 unchanged; geometries 45 -> 46; programs 32 -> 34; frame p50 6.60 -> 6.70 ms and p99 10.40 -> 10.20 ms. Texture VRAM +4.17 MiB measured** (3.125 MiB base plus the 4/3 mip chain), which `stats().vramEstimateMB` does NOT include because that counter is pool bytes only and stayed at 102.8 both sides, flagged, not fudged. **THE CLIENT'S ROLE TABLE IS CHECKED AGAINST THE SHIPPED MANIFEST at runtime** and disagreement is a `console.error`, because the table has to be a synchronous copy: `NodeBatch.build()` buckets a material the instant the glTF lands and a decision waiting on a fetch would be a race whose losing branch is a rock map on a leaf card. 31 roles, 0 mismatches, 0 unknown. `PropLibrary.load` awaits the manifest instead, being async and the one path that keeps a material per role name. **The probe caught its own defect first, which is the point of standing rule 11:** the first run reported `meanAbsDelta 0` on every box, and the reason was `samplePx: 0`, it read `bmp.width` after `bmp.close()`, which zeroes it, and it had never called `drawImage`. Every difference is reported next to the sample count it was computed over, and `ok` requires both a nonzero sample and a lit ground, so a measurement over nothing cannot read as a passing sky control. New: `web/src/render/instancing/Surfaces.ts` (the one place the role -> family decision lives, plus the runtime toggle and the probe report), `web/tools/smoke/probes/maps.js` (six asserted properties), screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN_maps_{before,after}.png`. **FLAGGED TO THE POST LANE: there will shortly be two occlusion sources in the frame.** The baked AO is now bound on 12 materials at `aoMapIntensity` 1.0 and their SSAO is coming; multiplying both double-darkens contact regions. The baked map was deliberately NOT weakened to pre-compensate. **NOT MINE, observed in passing:** `npm run check` is red on the current tree for other lanes' reasons (six `src/` files over the 400-line cap, `FlightMode` 554, `LaunchPad` 549, `BuildMode` 454, `FlightSession` 448, `GameplayActions` 427, `Gameplay` 418, plus `tsc` errors in `MapBoot`/`Persist`/`MapView`/`Discovery`); all four files this lane owns are within cap and typecheck clean. An FXAA shader in `web/src/render/post` emitted `warning X4000: use of potentially uninitialized variable (f_ApplyFXAA)` during an intermediate build, which `run.mjs`'s warning filter treats as a hard failure; it did not reproduce in the final build.

> **RN-7 (2026-07-27) prior head, kept below.**
>
> **RN-7 (2026-07-27) - THE SHIPPED ART REACHES THE SCREEN. Six defects, and five of them reported success.** Reid's overnight art lane delivered a rebuilt player, 26 clips, clump grass, belt cargo and armour, and almost none of it was drawn. **(1) THE SCATTER PLACED NOTHING NEAR THE PLAYER, and this is the find of the night.** `Scatter.sample` quantised its per-CELL prop count with `Math.round(expected)`. DW-19 took the near terrain cell from 7.2 m to 1.808 m on 2026-07-26, so `expected` became `118920 * 1.808^2 / 1e6` = **0.389, and `Math.round(0.389)` is 0**. Every chunk under the player scattered exactly nothing while the per-chunk `want` read 399 and every instrument read healthy: a terrain LOD improvement silently deleted the ground cover, and the only surviving props were on coarse chunks far away. **Measured, same seed, same site, same camera, same binary via `?scatterfair=0`: delivered fraction 0.0182 -> 0.9971, props placed 159 -> 8,697, and the number that matters, FOLIAGE SCREEN COVERAGE 0.56% -> 28.87%** over a 600x600 px box at pitch -25 (`of.groundCover` differences the same settled frame with the layer on and off, so camera, sun, streamed set and terrain cannot differ between the two captures). Fix: spend the fractional part as a probability keyed by the SAME per-cell hash everything else uses, so determinism is untouched and realised density equals requested density at any LOD depth. **(2) `PropLibrary.ts:33 CAPACITY = 7000` per material batch, fixed, no growth, exhaustion counted into a field nothing printed: a direct DW-28 violation.** Pools now double to a 16,384 ceiling, and `registerPool` puts the foliage on the same HUD line as the machines, so a refusal shouts `POOL FULL: n NOT DRAWN`. **(3) BOTH SCATTER CAPS WERE SILENT.** They are counted now, and `chunksCapped` earned itself immediately: at the shipped understorey a depth-14 chunk wholly inside the detail ring wants 3,106 props against `MAX_PER_CHUNK` 2,600, a 16% shortfall on exactly the chunk the player stands on. **(4) `props/detail_cards.glb` was declared in `Registry.ts` and never passed to a loader** (A-2): the ground-detail understorey had never been drawn. It is now a second density tier confined to a 55 m ring, 9,503 m2 against the full ring's 90,792, so a card costs 10.5% of what a shrub costs. **(5) DW-34, THE DEAD HOLD, LANDED ATOMICALLY.** The exporter wrote the first key at Blender frame 1, so `seconds = frame / fps` put every clip's first sampler input at 1/60. Root cause was NOT the exporter's `export_anim_slide_to_zero`, which only reaches the sampled path and would have fixed the two characters while silently leaving every machine, tree and belt at 1/60; it is `of_lib.pose_clip` / `add_clip_multi`, fixed with one `clip_frame()` helper. **Browser-measured, driven, off three's own `AnimationClip`: clips opening with a dead hold 26 of 26 -> 0 of 26, worst hold 16.6667 ms -> 0.0000 ms, `Run` duration 0.416666657 s -> 0.400000006 s, hitch at 4.5 m/s 7.50 cm -> 0.00 cm per cycle.** Every published impact index moved down one and moved in the SAME commit: pickaxe 17 -> **16**, axe 18 -> **17**, dig 16 -> **15**, read out of the exported sampler, and `SWING_CLIPS` in `AnimGraph.ts` is now the one place they are stated. `validate_glb.py` gained `anim_t0`, which asserts the first sampler input is **exactly 0.0** (an equality, because `frame/fps` into float32 makes frame 0 exactly 0.0, so a tolerance is only somewhere to hide) and which failed exactly the 21 animated assets before the rebuild. Four unanimated witnesses rebuilt byte-identical, including `armour_set.glb` whose build script was edited. 48/48 validate. **(6) FOUR FP CLIPS AND THE WHOLE AXE WERE UNREACHABLE (A-6).** `FP_CLIPS` mapped `jumpStart`/`jumpLoop`/`jumpLand`/`fall` to `null` with a comment claiming that was correct; the arms went dead still in the air. `swing` mapped unconditionally to `Swing_Pickaxe` and `holdTool` had only ever been called with the pickaxe, so `Swing_Axe`, `FP_Swing_Axe` and `crude_axe.glb` shipped, validated and could not be reached. **Driven proof: chopping a tree now reads `playing Swing_Axe`, `playingFp FP_Swing_Axe`, `crude_axe.glb` in BOTH hands, sampled mid-swing (`swingLeft 0.417`), and mining ore reads `Swing_Pickaxe` on the same run; unmapped states 0 on both rigs, 26 of 26 clips reachable.** **ARMOUR IS ON THE AVATAR (A-10), and the probe caught the A-9 trap on the way.** `PlayerRig.equip/unequip` rebinds each piece to the BODY's skeleton. The first version took ONE match per slot and bound **284 of 904 triangles** while every slot still had something on it, because `Armour_Chest_LOD0` is four primitives and GLTFLoader splits it into `_0.._3`. Fixed with an anchored `_LOD0(_\d+)?` match: **904 triangles drawn, 4 slots, 44 == 44 bones, `sameSkeleton` true on all four, frame delta 3,616 = 904 x 4 passes and exactly reversible.** **CUT BANKS ARE NO LONGER BLACK (task 109).** RN-6's suspicion was right and the mechanism is narrower than "ignores three's light list": `TerrainShader.ts:211` is `albedo * (uAmbient + skyAmb + sunT * 1.45 * ndl * shadow)` and `skyAmb` is traced along `up`, not along `n`, so it is identical for every fragment. A near-vertical face at a 69 degree sun gets `ndl` = 0 and keeps **2.5 / 3.5 / 5.9 per cent** of what flat ground gets, per channel. Isolated with a CIRCULAR 10 m pit, where depth, range, albedo rule, relief band and facet steepness are equal at every bearing by construction so the only quantity that varies is `dot(N, sunDir)`: **azimuthal luminance spread 155.3 counts**. Fix is 8 ALU ops, no new shader and no new uniform: a `skyView` split plus a `ground` bounce term whose radiance is the flat-ground branch already computed at that point, so a flat fragment is algebraically unchanged (floor 154.8 -> 155.4). **Darkest wall against the sunlit floor in the SAME frame, 0.135 -> 0.313 against a derived tolerance of 0.18** (sky diffuse on a vertical face is about 13% of horizontal, halved for seeing half the dome, plus about 11% bounced). Headlamp regression zero on one identical tree: `tunnellit` lamp lift **16.99 before and after**. **COST, and it is a real trade: draw calls 45 -> 53 of a 150 budget, triangles 493k -> 1.39 M of a 2.7 M alert, frame p50 1.5 -> 5.3 ms, p99 2.6 -> 15.6 ms, worst 39.9 ms.** The worst frame is one chunk's scatter build and is the named follow-up; per-update sampling is already amortised to one chunk and pools start at 2,048 rather than 256 to stop a doubling storm (22 reallocations in one 55 m walk -> 2 per batch). Gates: **29/29 ctest**, `npm --prefix web run check` green at **180 files**, `tools/smoke/reload.mjs` PASS. New probes `tools/smoke/probes/{grass,avatar,cutbank}.js`; isolation flags `?scatterfair=0`, `?propgrow=0`, `?detail=0` (standing rule 7). Screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN_grass_{before,after}.png`, `RN_cutbank_{before,after}.png`, `RN_avatar_{before,after}.png`. **NOT DONE, and both are ownership rather than difficulty: A-3 and A-4 live in `web/src/game/NodeArt.ts` and `NodeBatch.ts`**, which a progression lane owns tonight, so `water_pool.glb` and `oil_seep.glb` are still undrawable, every harvest node's `_LOD1`/`_LOD2`/`_Stump` is still dead weight, and `NodeBatch.ts:67 CAPACITY = 128` is still a silent DW-28 ceiling. **A-5 needs Reid, not a lane**: `walkMps` 4.6 against `RUN_THRESHOLD_MPS` 3.0 means the `Walk` clip can essentially never play, and whether 4.6 m/s (16.6 km/h) is the intended default walking speed is a feel decision. **A-11 needs art**: armour is body-only because `armour_set` carries the 44-bone rig and the view model is a different 27-bone rig. | **Last updated:** 2026-07-27 (RN-7).
