> **RN-6 (2026-07-26) prior head, kept below.**
> `rendering-controller` · **Reports to:** Admin · **Phase:** WEB (three.js, DW-1 pivot) · **RN-6 (2026-07-26), KILLED THE FIELD OF BLACK SPIKES OVER TERRAFORMED GROUND, AND THE AIM RAY THAT STOPPED SHORT OF IT.** The reported defect (`docs/screenshots/W8_base.png`) was **the near voxel mesh**, not fill geometry, not winding, not the headlamp and not a material. **The attribution in the brief was wrong and so was every one of its four candidate causes**; the isolation that settled it is `?voxelnear=0`, which removes the mesh from the scene and takes the entire spike field with it at a fixed site and camera. **Cause:** `exposedFaces` answers "every solid-to-air face in this box", which is right for a mesher that owns the world and wrong for one that SUPPLEMENTS the heightfield. A 6 m levelling op has a 13 x 25 x 13 m dirty region, so it made the mesh draw /core's 1 m solidity shell over ~25 m of ordinary ground; that shell disagrees with the smooth `surfaceRadius` the terrain chunk draws by up to half a cell diagonal (DW-26), so its cube corners poke through, and at 2 to 3 m from the eye a 1 m face fills a fifth of the screen. Nothing about it is specific to fill: **a dig showed it too, over a couple of metres instead of twenty-five.** **Fix (`web/src/world/VoxelSkin.ts`, new):** keep a face only where the heightfield CANNOT express it, i.e. the solid cell stands above the derived surface or the air cell sits clearly below it. Measured on a driven level: **924 faces exposed, 904 dropped, 20 drawn.** Filtering to "faces of an EDIT" was tried first and is not enough, and the reason is worth keeping: `levelArea` flips only the cells on the wrong side of its target, so its edits are a SCATTER and their faces are isolated cubes standing out of a terrace the chunk had already drawn correctly. **Colour:** the mesh's per-vertex albedo now comes from `BiomePalette.terrainAlbedo`, the terrain fragment shader's own palette entry, slope-to-rock smoothstep, snow band and relief scaling. **Light stays Headlamp's:** shading it with the terrain's actual ShaderMaterial was tried, matched perfectly in daylight and BROKE the tunnel, because that program computes its light from `uSunDir` and ignores three's light list, so the lamp had nothing to act on (lift 3.46x -> 1.12x, `lampDoesTheWork` false). Lambert + imported colour keeps both; lamp lift is now **11.5x**. **Obstruction (the user's own complaint, "annoying when you dig"):** `VoxelWorld.raycast` marched `solidAt`, the quantised shell, so the aim stopped on invisible rock in front of the ground: **17 of 40 driven aims stopped early, worst 3.8 m, on a shell face up to 0.52 m above the surface.** `SurfaceOracle.solidForAim` is `solidAt` with its one quantised term swapped for the smooth surface and both edit sets untouched (`added || (r <= surfaceRadius && !removed)`), so a tunnel is still hollow and its wall is still rock. Worst driven strike error **0.20 m against a 0.25 m march step**. **Numbers:** levelled pad vs untouched terrain, same seed, same site, matched range (8.7 / 8.8 m) and matched local slope (0.573 / 0.428 m), 240 px samples: **RGB 96.5/96.6/85.8 vs 143.7/145.5/134.7 (worst channel 33.6% off) becomes 132.7/135.8/124.9 vs the same ground (7.6%), tolerance 12%.** Draw calls with a base and a levelled pad in frame **49 -> 45 of 150**; p99 unchanged. **Isolation flags shipped (rule 7): `?voxelskin=0`** (the W5 mesh exactly: whole shell, flat brown Lambert), **`?voxelnear=0`** (not in the scene at all), **`?aimshell=1`** (march the raw shell). Probes `tools/smoke/probes/{voxelskin,padshot,beltfloat}.js`; screenshots `docs/screenshots/RN_base_{before,after}.png`, `RN_pad_{before,after}.png`, `RN_pad_after_novoxel.png`, `RN_dig_{voxel,novoxel}.png`. Regression green: `tunnelwalk` (8/8 grounded, 8/8 rock overhead, 8/8 column closed), `tunnellit`, `dig`, `level` (all 7 verdicts). **Found and NOT fixed:** `tunnelpersist.stillWalkable` is red on this branch and is NOT this lane's (identical with `?aimshell=1`); three call sites in `web/src/game` pass `edits = 0` to `_of_surface_radius` while two others pass the live handle. · **Last updated:** 2026-07-26 (RN-6). Prior WEB history lives in `docs/web/STATUS.md` and `docs/web/ARCHITECTURE.md` 15.2; the UE-era status below is FROZEN (DW-6, tag `ue-frozen-2026-07-05`).

ONE surface everywhere, voxel-only digging, deform store DEMOTED, the two root-caused hacks REMOVED.** UE-side only (`OFPlanetTerrain.cpp`, `OFAutomationManager.cpp`), commit `181ba86`; streaming/rebase, build grid, automation line, harvest, sprint, FP/TP camera, foliage NOT regressed. **The swaps:** (1) **EnsureInit**, the streamer's mesh-lowering fn is now bound to the VOXEL-DERIVED far-field lowering (`derivedLoweringAt` over `Impl->Voxels`, the top-anchored open-column depth), NOT `TerrainDeform::depthDugAt`. a miner bored to 80 m (bottomed) while the auto-line made 134 iron; save→dig→load restored the exact saved state. **GOTCHAS:** dig radius must be ≥~1.5 cell pitches or the brush catches NO cell (floored in C++); a deep (>~30 m) pit/shaft renders BLACK in a downward PIE capture (frame a shallow wide bowl from a low angle); adding `persistence.h` shifted UE unity grouping → renamed OFSurvivalCharacter's anonymous-ns `kRawIron`/`kIron` to `kSc*` (anon namespaces merge per unity TU). Commits `55e2d7d`/`de5ce01`/`0399726`/`c0130ec`; screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.6_{dig_hole,miner_shaft,miner_actors}.png`. · **M4.4 (2026-06-16), HARVEST NODES GLUED TO THE GROUND + STABLE id-keyed STREAMING (fixed the FLOAT + POP bugs on the biome nodes).** On `PlanetSurface` the harvest features (trees/rocks/ore/oil) FLOATED above the ground and "popped" (despawn/respawn) every step. **Two independent bugs, both UE-side only in `OFPlanetTerrain` (no `/core` edit; collision/walking `21ac998` + slope `b9fe759` NOT regressed; harvest E untouched):** (1) **FLOAT** = the deposit position uses `/core`'s DESIGNED relief (`sampleDesignedHeight`) but the chunk COLLISION is the RAW `sampleHeightField`, so nodes floated by the relief-modulation gap → **FIX `SnapNodeToGround`: line-trace each placed node straight DOWN onto `ECC_WorldStatic` and drop its base onto the hit (+1 cm)**; the cached `NodePositions` is recovered from the snapped location so rebases stay grounded. (2) **POP** = `GenerateLocalDeposits` re-quantized every cell id each step because the lon lattice spacing `dLon = stepM/(R·cosLat)` BREATHES with latitude → **FIX: a SINGLE GLOBAL angular lattice with CONSTANT `dAng = stepM/R` in both axes; cells = global integer indices `(iLat,iLon)`, id = `hashCombine(iLat,iLon)`** (a physical spot → one id forever; only the loop EXTENT scales by `1/cosLat`, never the spacing/id). The streaming loop already kept in-range nodes, despawned only out-of-range, and `ReanchorDeposits` shifts by the origin delta on rebase. **PIE-verified CHEAPLY (no long drive) via 3 new `BlueprintCallable` diagnostics (`DiagNodeClearances`/`GetLiveNodeIds`/`StepObserverForTest`):** all 40 resident nodes clearance = 1.0 cm above the chunk collision; a continuous ~55 m / 6-step walk held id persistence 100/95/100/100/97/100 % (only 0–2 EDGE nodes churn per step at the 700 m range boundary, never the nearby set). **GOTCHA: the streamed PMC collision is NOT hit by Python `line_trace_single`/`_for_objects`/sphere traces even under the grounded player, verify PMC grounding from C++ (`LineTraceSingleByChannel(ECC_WorldStatic)`); `current_floor.blocking_hit` confirms the capsule is grounded (`FHitResult` struct fields aren't attribute-accessible in this UE Python build).** Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.4_{nodes_grounded,nodes_stable}.png`. · **M4.3 (2026-06-16), SOLID COLLISION GROUND + PHYSICAL SMOOTH WALKING (fixed the FLOAT + TELEPORT bugs) + per-biome Megascans surfaces.** In real PIE gameplay the player + objects FLOATED in space (no ground) and running TELEPORTED instead of moving smoothly. **Root cause = the old "pin the player to a computed eye-height each tick" model:** the streamed chunk `UProceduralMeshComponent`s had NO collision, so the capsule had nothing to stand on (it floated, pinned to the `/core` surface height by a per-tick `SetActorLocation`), and that per-tick re-pin + the re-pin after every floating-origin rebase is what read as teleporting once you actually moved. **FIX (UE-side only, no `/core` edit, slope fix `b9fe759` NOT regressed):** (1) build chunk sections WITH collision (`bUseComplexAsSimpleCollision`, synchronous cook so the body exists frame-1, `QueryAndPhysics` + block Pawn), solid ground; (2) put the player in `MOVE_Walking`+gravity and DROP them onto the collision (removed the per-tick pin), physical CharacterMovement walking; (3) **invisible rebase compensation**, on `consumeRebased()` shift the player by the SAME world delta the terrain re-anchors by (`OldFrameRot.RotateVector(lastDelta×scale)`) so their position relative to the ground is unchanged (no jump, no re-pin); (4) safety net only on a >2 km catastrophic tunnel while falling. **PIE-VERIFIED IN MOTION:** standing → `clear=2.2 cm` above the mesh, `is_falling=false` (feet on solid ground, not floating); driving the real WASD path → **15 consecutive intervals all dX≈94.6 cm (rock-steady constant velocity, zero teleport spikes) + smooth dZ≈−2.7 cm** down a slope. **Task 2, per-biome Megascans GROUND surfaces:** the user added `/Game/RockyGround` (rocky-ground scan), `/Game/Materials_TreeBark` (9 conifer-bark MIs), `/Game/WaterMaterials`, `/Game/B3D` (pine-branch atlas). `M_PlanetSurface` now BLENDS grass↔rock surfaces per biome, a per-vertex `BiomeSurfaceWeight` (0=grass…1=rock) packed into vertex ALPHA (blurred to feather boundaries), `lerp(grass,rock,A)×VertexColor.RGB` for base/normal/roughness; tree trunks use the 9 `MI_TreeBark_*` conifer instances (per-node). Grass biome PIE-verified textured (real surface relief); rock biomes wired (not yet walked-to). **Gotcha re-bit: `VertexColor` RGB is the DEFAULT output (`""`), NOT `"RGB"`, wiring `"RGB"` silently fails → black ground.** Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.3_{standing_on_ground,walking_smooth,planet_biomes_textured,ground_check}.png`. **Residual:** no streamed-foliage scatter on the planet yet; no dedicated snow/soil scan (Polar tints rock bright). · **M4.2 (2026-06-16), FIXED THE PERPETUAL ~40° SLOPE BUG (level ground under the player).** On `PlanetSurface` the player stood on a constant tilted hillside everywhere (tilted horizon) instead of level ground. Root cause: `ComputeFrameRot` (the world-from-body root rotation in `OFPlanetTerrain.cpp`) had TWO compounding bugs, (1) **wrong handedness:** `East = U × North` made the `{East,North,U}` basis left-handed, and (2) **wrong matrix layout:** the body axes were the matrix ROWS, but UE's `FMatrix(rows)+ToQuat()+RotateVector()` needs them as COLUMNS to map `RotateVector(U)=+Z`. Together radial-up landed near −Y (the ~40° tilt). FIX: `East = North × U` (right-handed) + body axes as COLUMNS (transpose layout) + north pole `(0,1,0)` per `/core latLonToDir`. **Empirically verified in PIE: `R·U=(0,0,1)`, `R·North=(0,1,0)`, `R·East=(1,0,0)` → level ground, level horizon, world +X=east/+Y=north (matches the existing WASD un-rotation, no other change).** PIE-confirmed level on the forest start AND across a ~64 km auto-walk (biomes cycled, engine coords stayed bounded ~133k UE / ~1.3 km between rebases, no jitter, floating-origin rebase intact, `FrameRot` recomputed per rebase). A prior uncommitted attempt fixed only the layout (left ground inverted, U→−Z) and was never verified, reworked + verified here. UE-side only, no `/core` edit. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.2_{level_ground,horizon_level}.png`. **ALSO M4.2, REAL MEGASCANS TEXTURES WIRED IN (the M3.1 blocker is cleared, the user finally Add-to-Project'd Fab packs):** two packs imported, `/Game/BarkWillow` (willow-bark scan + 6 PBR textures) and `/Game/OWD_Flowers_Pack` (450 assets: flower + grass meshes, a `Stones/` rock surface, a `Landscape/` grass GROUND surface; ~468 MB total). Wired: **planet ground** `M_PlanetSurface` gains the grass albedo (brightness-preserving detail, normalized to mean≈1 then ×biome VertexColor so the per-biome palette still drives colour) + grass Normal; **ore boulders** `M_OreRock` (both maps) gains the `T_Stone_07` color/normal/rough/AO with the per-kind tint param preserved; **tree bark** = `M_Bark_Willow` on the trunk (one C++ line in `OFResourceNode.cpp`, both maps); **SurvivalTest meadow** dressed with 332 real `SM_Flower_*` + 464 `SM_Grass_*` meshes (replacing the basic-shape proxies). NO soil/snow/wood surface set was imported, planet biome variety tints the single grass surface; flagged as the gap. PIE-verified both maps; screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.2_megascans_{planet,survival}.png`. · **M4.1 (2026-06-16), POLISHED PLANET SURFACE + HARVESTABLE BIOME DEPOSITS ON THE PLANET.** The whole-planet surface now reads as a real, legible place AND the player can mine real biome-distributed resources on it (UE-side only, no `/core` edit; commit `5d8bd30`). **Task 1 (looks):** fixed the wash-out, PostProcess manual-exposure bias 10.5→8.2 (was clipping bright Mountain/Polar biomes to pure white), sun 8→5.5, skylight 1.8→1.05, bloom→0.25; **muted LINEAR biome palette** (snow caps 0.50, only on the highest peaks) with **per-vertex biome classification + a 2-pass box blur** that feathers biome boundaries into a transition band (no more hard diagonal seam); **skirtFraction 0.5→0.9** seals coarse↔fine LOD cracks; capsule already stands radial-upright in the frame-aligned root (verified); start moved to a Forest landmass for a green-coastline headline shot. **Task 2 (harvest):** `AOFPlanetTerrain::UpdateDeposits()` streams `AOFResourceNode`s around the player **parented under the rotated root** (same `(pos−origin)×scale` anchor as the chunks → they tilt + re-anchor on every floating-origin rebase, appear/disappear as you walk), wired to the existing survival harvest (E)+inventory; PIE-verified a forest start spawned 20 trees/6 rock/2 iron/2 oil (forest biome bias) and harvesting granted +1 wood. **HONEST `/core` density finding:** the planet-wide `BiomeResourceField` is too SPARSE to be walkable (~634 deposits / 600 km planet → ~14 km to the nearest node), so placement uses `GenerateLocalDeposits()`, the SAME `/core` biome rules (`biomeAt`+`biomeResource`) evaluated on a dense local grid, biome-proportional + deterministic, instead of the sparse field. Flagged to Admin (world-gen needs a density knob); no `/core` edit. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.1_*`. · **M4.0 (2026-06-16), PLAYER NOW STANDS + WALKS ON THE REAL STREAMED PROCEDURAL PLANET (the signature "seamless surface" step).** New map `/Game/Maps/PlanetSurface` + new actor `AOFPlanetTerrain` (OrbitalFoundryCore plugin, pImpl over `/core` `worldgen::SurfaceObserver` + `TerrainStreamer`) render the resident `TerrainChunk`s as biome-coloured ProcMesh sections **at real scale (1 m = 100 UE units)** with the player pinned near the engine origin. **The floating-origin crux is solved frame-aligned:** the actor root carries a `world-from-body` rotation (radial up → world +Z) so the local patch renders HORIZONTAL and plain gravity/world-Z cameras work; chunks attach with relative pos `(centerUniverse − floatingOrigin) × scale`. **All 3 milestones landed + PIE-verified:** M1 player stands on real curved biome terrain (snow mountains / green plains / ocean basins, ~200 chunks / ~240k verts resident); M2 walking drives `SurfaceObserver::move` → `updateStreaming` adds/drops chunks as biomes change; M3 `consumeRebased()` → `onOriginRebased` + re-anchor + frame-recompute + re-pin kept engine coords **bounded near 0 over a ~180 km walk (99 rebases, no jitter/blowup)**. Commit `ad472c5`; screenshots `docs/screenshots/M4.0_*`. This is the first real exercise of the `terrain_stream.h`/`surface_walk.h`/`biome.h` contracts through a renderer. **Residual:** LOD-seam cracks at coarse↔fine boundaries (skirt covers most), capsule not yet radial-up-aligned, atmosphere/exposure can blow out white biomes, all polish. · **M3.1 (Fab/Megascans) finding:** **Fab/Megascans asset acquisition is UI-ONLY, there is NO scriptable/Python/bridge path to download a Fab asset (acquisition = the Fab panel's "Add to Project" button only).** The Fab plugin ships + is enabled + the user is signed in, but the import entry point `UFabBrowserApi::AddToProject(signedUrl, meta)` is `UFUNCTION()` *without* BlueprintCallable (invisible to Python, confirmed live) and needs a short-lived signed CDN url minted by the Fab web panel's JS after a user click. Python only exposes the *post-download translator* (`InterchangeMegascansPipeline`/`MegascanImportType`), not an acquirer. So real-Megascans-materials is gated on a ~2-min user action (precise shopping list below in §6); once assets land under `/Game/…` an agent can wire them. Full evidence recorded in `ue/CLAUDE.md` ("Fab / Megascans import is UI-ONLY"). · **M3.1b (2026-06-15):** FP **held-tool view-model** landed (pickaxe/axe now visible + tracks the view in first person; fixed a pre-existing FP black-slab bug = the hidden body's self-shadow) + polish (metallic tool heads `M_Metal`, crude-oil pool `M_Oil`, minimal crosshair/interaction HUD). **The Megascans texture wire-in (M3.1) is STILL pending**, the assets were never actually Add-to-Project'd in Fab (AssetRegistry empty; launcher log shows only a search), so the slice keeps the M3.0 authored materials. Commits `30ebe1a`+`2e7082c`. · **Status:** **SURVIVAL SLICE GRAPHICS/ATMOSPHERE PASS LANDED (M3.0), the survival slice went from flat untextured "Play-Doh plastic" to a textured, atmospherically-lit, Valheim-leaning stylized-realistic look.** The biggest single fix: **Lumen GI was OFF** (`r.DynamicGlobalIlluminationMethod=0`=None, killing all bounce light), re-enabled in `DefaultEngine.ini` RendererSettings (+Lumen reflections). Built a full sky/lighting/post stack on `SurvivalTest`: warm low-angle key sun + real-time-capture SkyLight + Sky Atmosphere + Valheim-style Exponential Height + Volumetric Fog + a VolumetricCloud + an unbound PostProcessVolume (desaturated grade w/ cool-green shadows & warm highlights, bloom, histogram auto-exposure, AO, vignette). **StarterContent turned out NOT to be installed** (only one moss texture exists on disk, the brief's "StarterContent IS installed" premise was wrong; the prior pass was right), so authored textured PBR materials **install-free** from engine noise/detail textures: `M_Meadow_Textured` (grass+dirt ground w/ detail normal, the micro-surface normal is the key de-plastic factor), `M_GrassTuft` (two-sided foliage + subsurface) on ~1800 dressing pieces, `M_Water` (reflective + panning ripple normals), upgraded `M_FactoryColor` (tree/tools/furnace/grass) + `M_OreRock` (textured rough rock) in place. This is the worldgen→UE-mesh pipeline proven end to end (coarse single-mesh preview; quadtree-streamed LOD + dual-camera/floating-origin still ahead). > Read alongside: [MASTER_PLAN](../MASTER_PLAN.md) · [AGENT_ARCHITECTURE](../AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md) · [ADMIN](ADMIN.md) · **[Spike 1 rendering design](../spikes/spike1-rendering.md)** (the buildable plan)

## 1. Mission
Make surface→orbit→interplanetary→surface look seamless and run fast. Own the "rendering magic" that sells continuous traversal, plus the techniques that let a dense 3D factory render without melting the GPU.

## 2. Scope & owned subsystems
- **Scaled space**, distant celestial bodies via a second camera at huge scale; swap to real terrain on approach.
- **Multi-range depth**, logarithmic or cascaded depth buffer (draw a 1 m object and a 100,000 km planet in one frame, no z-fighting).
- **Terrain rendering**, consume world-gen's cubed-sphere quadtree; stream/blend LOD; horizon curvature.
- **Atmospheric scattering**, Rayleigh/Mie post-process; the sky-fades-to-black sell of leaving a planet.
- **Factory rendering at scale**, GPU instancing of identical parts/items; LOD that collapses distant belts to flowing textures/decals; cull/abstract un-observed item meshes.
- **Materials, lighting, VFX**, engine pipeline (Lumen/Nanite under D-001), thrust/dust/weather FX.
- **Non-goals:** what to render (world-gen, factory-sim, physics produce the data); UI/HUD (→ gameplay).

## 2.1 THE CALIBRATED TARGET (RN-352). What "correct" looks like now, as numbers

**Written 2026-08-01. Every art, asset and rendering lane calibrates against this
section. It is not taste; every line is a measurement with a way to re-take it.**

Without a written target the direction decays back to whoever last had an
opinion, which is how this project spent several passes measuring well against
the wrong thing. If you disagree with a number here, take the measurement again
and change the number, in this file, with the reading beside it. Do not work
around it.

### The one command that re-takes every number

```
cd web && npm run sync-assets && npx vite build --outDir dist-<lane>
npx vite preview --outDir dist-<lane> --port <yours> --strictPort --host 127.0.0.1
node tools/smoke/run.mjs --url=http://127.0.0.1:<yours>/ --scenario=walk \
  --width=1600 --height=900 --evalfile=tools/smoke/probes/lookdev.js \
  --evalargs='{"site":{"name":"forest","lat":-19.85,"lon":-72.7853,"yaw":300,"pitch":-10}}'
```

`probes/lookdev.js` reports, per sun elevation, the whole frame and the frame
split into **masked ground** and **masked sky** (the mask is built once at noon
and reused; see RN-335). Add `"pose": true` for a single PNG through the
shipping path, and pipe it through `tools/smoke/writeshot.mjs`.

### 1. The response curve

| | value | why it is that |
|---|---|---|
| tone curve | ACES RRT+ODT fit, three's own | one curve for the composite and for any material three tonemaps itself, so an A/B never measures "a different tonemapper" |
| exposure | **1.2** | the largest value that leaves shadow occupancy alone (`loFrac` moves under 0.8 points at three sites) and the smallest that keeps RN-152's night floor |
| contrast | **1.45**, on the slope-matched S (`curveMix` 1) | the straight line clips both ends: at 1.06 every display value over 0.972 was already white |
| saturation | **0.92** | a per-channel contrast raises chroma on its own; this is what makes the contrast a VALUE change |
| lift | **0** | a lifted black point is the first thing ART-DIRECTION.md names |
| colour space | sRGB encode in the composite, `outputColorSpace = SRGBColorSpace` | no double encode: the composite's source has no `<colorspace_fragment>` include for three to substitute |

**Negative controls, both exact and both one flag:** `?curve=0` restores the
RN-10 straight line, and `?grade=0` makes the whole grade an identity mix.
`?post=0` restores the pre-stack path.

### 2. Reference luminances

Standing eye (alt 2.0 m), yaw 300, local noon, 1600 x 900, the shipped grade.
`groundNear` is a 140 px box at 0.5 / 0.66 of the frame; `sky*` boxes are placed
off the mask's own horizon row. **These are the calibration. A change that moves
one of them by more than a few counts owes an explanation.**

| site | groundNear luma | groundNear RGB | masked-ground p50 | masked-sky p50 | ground `sat` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Forest (floor, pitch -26) | 35 | (24, 44, 19) before RN-347, brown after | 33 | 191 | 0.44 |
| Hills (pitch -10) | 43 | (40, 49, 20) | 50 | 152 | 0.53 |
| Plains (pitch -10) | 55 | (45, 67, 21) | 51 | 158 | 0.62 |
| Beach (pitch -10) | 167 | (189, 166, 111) | 167 | 174 | 0.36 |

**Shape targets, which matter more than the absolute values because they survive
a retune:**

- **`hiFrac` must not be zero.** Before this pass it was 0.00 at Hills and Plains
  noon: the top fifth of the range was empty at every site and every daylight
  elevation. It now reads 0.056 / 0.040 / 0.060 at Hills / Plains / Forest noon.
  A frame with no highlights is the definition of the flat read.
- **`loFrac` must not move much when exposure does.** That is the test that a
  contrast gain was not bought by brightening the image out of its own shadows.
- **The masked ground's `iqr` goes UP and its `sat` goes DOWN.** Value does the
  work, hue does less. Both must hold at every site and every day elevation, or
  the claim is site-specific (RN-64, and RN-334 where the fourth site disagreed).
- **Night: assert the masked GROUND's mean and median, never a whole-frame
  percentile.** At Hills midnight the sky is mean 0.06 counts with p95 = 0, so a
  whole-frame p95 at night is a statement about the ground wearing a costume.

### 2b. DUSK reference luminances (RN-515). The half of the cycle 2.1 had none of

**Written 2026-08-01. Same command, same grade, same standing eye (alt 2.0 m,
yaw 300, pitch -10, 1600 x 900), same four sites, binary `b45bdd1`. Rungs are
`of.stats().sky.elevationDot` on the RISING side; `probes/dusk.js` takes them.**

Section 2.1 shipped with noon and midnight and nothing in between, and roughly
half the interesting light in a 60-minute cycle lives in between. That absence
is why a frame nobody had measured could be called a dusk fault for a day.

`groundNear` is the same 140 px box at 0.5 / 0.66 of the frame the noon table
uses. `warm` is meanR minus meanB in counts, POSITIVE IS WARM, and it is the
axis this section did not have: a luma-only row cannot tell a warm dusk from a
cold one, and "pale cyan" is a claim about hue.

| site | elev | groundNear luma | groundNear RGB | warm | world mean | world warm | world sat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Forest | 47.4 | 23.2 | (26.7, 23.4, 13.3) | +13.4 | 42.1 | +3.8 | 0.41 |

| Hills | 1.4 | 2.8 | (1.7, 3.5, 1.9) | -0.2 | 21.3 | +4.9 | 0.41 |
| Plains | 59.2 | 57.7 | (60.4, 61.5, 31.1) | +29.4 | 60.0 | +26.6 | 0.51 |
| Plains | 17.6 | 24.0 | (23.9, 26.3, 12.1) | +11.8 | 38.3 | +12.3 | 0.51 |

**Shape targets for dusk, which is what a later lane should actually hold:**

- **`groundNear.warm` STAYS POSITIVE at every site down to about 1 degree.** The
  ground a player stands on gets warmer light as the sun sets, never cooler,
  because `sunT` extinguishes blue about seven times harder than red at grazing
  airmass. **A negative reading here is the assertion that would have caught a
  real cold wash, and this section had no such assertion until now.** The one
  value that reaches zero is Hills at 1.4 degrees (-0.2), where the direct term
  is gone and only ambient is left.
- **`groundNear` falls monotonically with elevation at every site.** Beach falls
  145 to 17, Plains 58 to 6, Hills 37 to 3, Forest 23 to 2. A rung that does not
  fall is a term that is not following the sun.
- **`skyLow.warm` is between -29 and -56 at every site and every rung.** The sky
  is blue and the ground is warm, and the SEPARATION between them is the thing
  worth protecting; a frame where both read the same hue is the failure mode.
- **The instrument floor is 0.05 counts of mean and 0.06 of warm at every
  daylight rung**, rising to 0.58 and 0.40 at the lowest two, where the
  understorey and the creatures are the only things left moving.

**ONE NUMBER HERE IS OWED AND IS NOT FIXED, NAMED SO IT IS NOT REDISCOVERED.**
`groundNear` at Plains 3.5 degrees is luma 7.9 while the frame's own world band
sits at 37.5, so the ground under the player's feet is four to five times darker
than the frame around it and resolves into nothing. The mechanism is that the
direct term carries `ndl`, which on flat ground is the elevation dot and goes
linearly to zero, while `ofAtmoAerial`'s haze carries only `sunT` and merely
reddens. Nothing balances them. **Not changed this pass**, because the honest fix
is a judgement about how dark a playable dusk should be, and that is Reid's.

**AND ONE INSTRUMENT LIMIT, because it would otherwise be read as a result.**
The sky mask's rule is CHROMATIC (`B - R > 8` and luma `> 50`), so as the sun
drops the detected horizon row climbs: at Plains it moves from row 292 at noon
to row 193 at 1.5 degrees, a hundred pixels, with the geometry perfectly still.
**Any box placed relative to that horizon therefore cannot carry an elevation
trend**, which rules out `groundFar`, `groundMid` and `skyLow` for cross-rung
comparison and leaves `groundNear` and the world band, which are fixed fractions
of the frame. `probes/dusk.js` publishes the horizon row on every rung so this
is visible rather than inferred. A second defect in the same family was fixed on
the way: lookdev.js centres `groundFar` at `hz + 14` with a half-height of 26,
so twelve of its fifty-two rows are SKY, and at Hills at dusk that box read 152
while the ground under it read 7. `dusk.js` places it a full half-height below
the LOWEST column's horizon and publishes the old placement beside it as
`groundFarOld` so the noon rows above stay comparable.

### 3. Colour: what belongs to whom

**The terrain is the SUBSTRATE and the scatter is the VEGETATION, and they must
never be the same colour.** This is the rule RN-347 established and it is the
single largest thing in a ground-level frame.

- `BiomePalette.HEX` is soil, litter, sand and stone. Vegetated biomes sit at HSV
  saturation **0.25 to 0.35**. Anything above 0.40 is the old error.
- The green lives on the 3.2 M detail cards per km2 that stand on it. A plant
  that cannot be told from the ground it grows out of has no silhouette,
  whatever its geometry says.
- Mountains, Polar, Regolith, MoonHighland and CraterFloor were already substrate
  colours and are **the control**: a change that moves those sites is a bug.

### 4. Material response: roughness and metalness conventions

Effective roughness is `material.roughness * ormG`; effective metalness is
`material.metalness * ormB`.

- **A family's effective roughness p05..p95 band must be at least ~0.15 wide.**
  Below that it is a constant under a moving sun. **The rule stands; the two
  numbers this bullet carried from 2026-08-01 to 2026-08-13 were both wrong, and
  the campaign that quoted them was scoped on them. See RN-1470.** Measured off
  the shipped bytes on 2026-08-13, per role, `palette_roughness * ormG/255`:

  | family | effective band, per role | verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | `stone` | Rock 0.549, RockDark 0.467 | widest in the game |
  | `suitplate` | Plate 0.268 | |
  | `coarse` | Soil 0.325, Sand/Regolith 0.309, Coal 0.293, Rubber 0.277, **Iron 0.130, Copper 0.114** | RN-1471 widened it; the two metal-chunk roles are still short |
  | `ore` | CopperOre 0.319, IronOre 0.226, CoalSeam 0.175 | |
  | `suitfab` | SuitGrime 0.317, SuitDark 0.242, Suit 0.224 | |
  | `panel` | Hazard 0.245, SteelDark/SuitAccent 0.224, Accent 0.204, Steel 0.184, **SteelLight 0.143** | |
  | `fur` | Chitin 0.168, ChitinBand/Under 0.169 | high and narrow ON PURPOSE (RN-461) |
  | `bark` | Bark 0.138, BarkLight 0.133 | short, and nobody has flagged it |

  **The two roles that still fail are palette-side, not map-side, and that is the
  transferable lesson.** `SteelLight` (0.143) and `coarse`'s `Iron` (0.130) and
  `Copper` (0.114) all wear a map whose span is fine and a palette roughness that
  is the lowest of their family's set (0.35, 0.40, 0.40). A narrow band is the
  PRODUCT of the map's span and the constant, so widening a shared map to rescue
  one role over-polishes the other six that wear it. Fixing these three is a
  palette edit in `of_lib.py`, priced as a re-surfacing of everything those roles
  touch, and it is deliberately not done here.
- **Metalness must carry information or be honestly constant.** As of 2026-08-13
  four families vary it: `panel` (span 0.580), `suitplate` (0.651), and the two
  RN-1474/RN-1475 additions `paintchip` (0.722) and `rust` (0.384). `coarse`,
  `bark`, `ore`, `fur`, `stone` and `suitfab` are honestly constant at 255 with a
  declared reason each in `ALLOWED_CONSTANT`. The old "three of four ORM maps"
  count predates six of the ten families.
- **Normal-map mean tilt: `coarse` is 7.7 degrees and it has the most screen area
  in the game.** Bark is 22.8. A surface family with no relief cannot respond to
  a moving sun no matter what the grade does. **`coarse` no longer has the most
  screen area: `stone` (mean 17.18, max 74.31) took the host rock at RN-742, and
  what is left on `coarse` is soil, sand, regolith, rubber and the loose item
  chunks.** The 7.7 figure is still the field's own and still low.

> **THE STALENESS TRAP THIS SECTION SET, recorded because it cost two lanes and
> is the most reusable thing on this page.** The `panel` 0.032 bullet was written
> on 2026-08-01 in the same commit range as RN-331..352. `RN-553` re-authored
> `_panel_masks` and opened the band to 0.184 **the same day, four commits
> later**, with the widening named in its own commit subject. Nobody edited the
> bullet back, and `of_panel_orm.png` changed three more times afterwards. Twelve
> days on, that one sentence had become: the scope audit's "THE headline defect",
> the ART-CAMPAIGN plan's line 13 and its A2b task, and **the stated CAUSE in
> RN-1415** for why raising the PMREM cube bought nothing. Three documents and one
> lane's conclusion, all resting on a number that had been false since the
> afternoon it was written. The `ore` "0.28 to 0.37" in the same bullet was never
> right at all under any palette this repo has shipped, and looks like a copy of
> item 5's unrelated "0.28 to 0.37 m" AO radius three bullets down.
>
> **The rule that follows: a measured number in this file is a claim about bytes
> at a date, and a lane that quotes one to justify work must re-measure it first.**
> It is ten seconds of arithmetic off the shipped PNG. Two lanes spent a day each
> on the strength of not doing it.
- **Foliage cards carry VALUE only.** `of_grass_a.png` and `of_leaf_a.png` have a
  maximum absolute R-minus-B of 6 counts. Because `Surfaces.ts` divides
  `material.color` by the scalar `albedo_mean`, **only the card's spatial
  variance and its hue survive; its mean level cannot.** Author variance and hue
  into the card; author level into the palette or `FoliageTone.ts`.

### 5. Contact and occlusion, and the number that is currently wrong

- Cascade 0: **15.47 mm per shadow texel** over 0 to 22 m on the high tier, PCF
  kernel one texel. The 22 m boundary is a **3.64x** step with no blend.
- **AO and contact are clamped in SCREEN space and the clamps BIND inside 4.87 m
  and 4.38 m.** At a standing eye the AO radius is 0.28 to 0.37 m against a
  nominal 0.9 m, and the contact march is 0.15 to 0.21 m against 0.45 m. Both
  are fixed-tap, so widening them costs cache locality, not arithmetic. **Price
  this at Plains (the most expensive frame) before changing it.**
- `AO_APPLY_FS` multiplies TOTAL radiance and the terrain has no ambient channel,
  so **AO attenuates direct sun on terrain**. `aoStrength` 0.9 and `csStrength`
  0.72 are the entire compensation.

### 6. The traps that cost this pass time, so they cost the next one none

- `__ofPost.state().sun` **freezes below the horizon**. Use
  `of.stats().sky.elevationDot`.
- A luma-only instrument reads ~0 on a hue-only change. A grade moves both.
  Decode RGB.
- **`--strictPort` fails the NEW server and the OLD one keeps answering 200.**
  Kill by PID and confirm the port is free before serving. It fired here.
- A flag's DEFAULT is a fixture. `Number(null)` is 0, so parse a missing
  parameter as MISSING and assert the boot default in its own named check.
- Measure at **four** sites, not three. Three green biomes agreed with each other
  and Beach contradicted all of them (RN-334).


## 3. Key design decisions
| # | Decision | Rationale | Status | Date |
|---|----------|-----------|--------|------|
| RN-1520 | **THE PMREM RAISE BOUGHT NOTHING BECAUSE THE ENVIRONMENT HAS NO BRIGHT SOURCE IN IT, AND THAT IS NOW A NUMBER RATHER THAN AN INFERENCE** | RN-1415 took the cube 64 -> 256 and moved the canonical machine box 20.52 -> 20.43; RN-1470 then withdrew its stated cause (`panel`'s band is ~0.4 wide, not 0.032), leaving the observation with no explanation. A resolution raise is a claim about the ANGULAR FREQUENCY CONTENT of the source cube, and nothing in this repo had ever read that: every other measurement of the sky is downstream of ACES and an 8-bit framebuffer, both of which destroy precisely the quantity in question. `OFRenderer.cubeRadiance(scene, size)` is the new seam method that reads it, as RGBA16F cube faces decoded to `Float32Array`, with `NoToneMapping` and linear output for the duration exactly as `PMREMGenerator` does, so it measures WHAT THE PMREM SEES and not a kinder copy. **Measured on a real Windows D3D boot at a 256 cube, RN-64's ground half raised, `probes/ibldiag.js` `pass: true, fails: []`: mean 0.1399, p99 0.5366, MAX 1.1178, `peakRatio` (max/mean) = 7.99, and `brightFrac` (texels above 10x the mean) = 0.0000 out of 393,216.** Not one texel of the whole sphere is an order of magnitude above its own average. A real sky's sun-to-sky radiance ratio is 1e4 to 1e5. At `iblSize` 64 a texel is 1.4 degrees and the sun disc is 3.15 degrees across, so **the only high-frequency feature in the cube was already resolved at 64 at 2.25 texels wide**, and 256 gives nine texels across a feature whose local contrast over the sky beneath it is 4.8x. That is the whole of RN-1415's null, and it replaces the withdrawn cause. | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1521 | **THREE OF THE FIVE SUSPECTS ARE REFUTED BY DIRECT MEASUREMENT, NOT BY ARGUMENT, AND THE MIRROR ARM IS WHAT REFUTES THEM** | `?ibldiag=mirror` overrides every machine `BatchedMesh` material to metalness 1.0 / roughness 0.02 after `assertMachineBase`, because **a mirror IS the environment map, displayed**. Verified armed rather than assumed (failure mode (c) named in advance): `overrides: 14` and every published row reads `metalness 1, roughness 0.02` live. **Suspect (1), the environment not reaching instanced machine materials: REFUTED.** All 14 rows read `envMapIntensity: 1` and `ownEnvMap: false`, with `scene.environment` non-null at `environmentIntensity: 1`; and the mirror moves the canonical box hard, luma **19.76 -> 24.54**, iqr **16.96 -> 31.32** (+84 per cent). **THOSE TWO FIGURES ARE THE CORRECTED PAIR AND THE FIRST COMMIT OF THIS LANE HAD THEM WRONG**: it compared the mirror against the SHIPPED frame (20.43 / 15.30) rather than against `?machinemat=0`, which the mirror arm required beside it, so the pair was two variables wide and `?machinemat=0` alone is worth -0.67 luma and +1.66 iqr of it. See RN-1526, which removes the second flag entirely and re-measures. The direct refutation of suspect (1) is RN-1527's `noenv` control, not this row. **Suspect (3), metalness 0.45 halving the specular story: NOT THE BINDING CAUSE.** It is worth a great deal (the mirror arm is that number) but RN-1200's per-part channel already restores the authored 0.85 on Steel, and neither arm's null depends on it. **Suspect (4), tone mapping compressing the highlights: REFUTED.** `hiFrac` is **0** in every arm including a mirror under a 200x sun; nothing is being clipped, because there is no highlight to clip. | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1522 | **THE FIRST READING OF THIS INSTRUMENT WAS WRONG BY 2.6x AND IT WAS THE CUBE SIZE, NOT THE SCENE** | At a 32 cube the same capture reported `atSun` 0.4305 against a cube max of 0.7314, i.e. "the sun is DIMMER than the horizon haze", which would have been a far more dramatic finding and is false. A 32-cube texel subtends 2.8 degrees and the disc is 3.15 degrees, so the texel straddles the disc's falloff and box-filters its peak away; at 256 (0.35 degrees per texel) the same disc reads 1.1158 and IS the cube maximum. **The rule, and it generalises past this file: a cube-map instrument cannot report a feature narrower than about three of its own texels, so any peak read off a low-resolution capture is a lower bound and must be re-taken at a resolution the feature survives.** Recorded rather than quietly corrected, because the wrong version of this number was on its way into a report. | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1523 | **`sunIntensity` IS DOCUMENTED AS "RADIANCE SCALE FOR THE SUN DISC" AND HAS NEVER REACHED THE SUN DISC. THAT IS THE DEFECT, AND IT IS SMALLER THAN IT SOUNDS** | `Atmosphere.glsl.ts:30` names the field exactly that; `createAtmosphereUniforms` spends it on `uSunColor`, which `ofAtmoScatter` multiplies the scattering integral by, and grep across `web/src` finds no other consumer. The disc itself is a `THREE.Sprite` in `SkyPass` whose peak radiance is its texture alpha times the LDR colour `0xfff3d6`. **Measured by difference across the `?ibldisc=` arm at a 256 cube: the disc's own contribution at its centre is 0.8859 over a local sky of 0.2319, against a declared 15.0, so the sun in this game's environment is 1/16.9 of the number the parameter beside it states.** On the presented frame this is invisible because ACES clips both to white; in the environment capture it is the whole of RN-1520. Named a defect on the brief's own test (a sun missing from the IBL is a defect), and priced honestly in the row below rather than fixed by reflex. | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1524 | **THE KNOB SHIPS AND THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE, BECAUSE THE FIX WAS MEASURED AND IT BUYS 0.01 OF A COUNT** | `?ibldisc=<k>` multiplies the sun sprite's radiance FOR THE DURATION OF ONE IBL CAPTURE and for nothing else, on `setGroundMode`'s exact precedent: raised, all six cube faces rendered inside the same synchronous call stack, lowered, so no presented frame can observe it. **k = 1 is the shipped identity and an absent flag gives it, verified: the canonical machine box reads luma 20.43 / p05 5.79 / p50 19.23 / p95 39.12 / iqr 15.30 / loFrac 0.704 on this branch, bit-identical to RN-1479's recorded row, and forestfloor groundNear reads 27.32 / p95 75.35 against RN-1479's 27.32 / 75.35.** The default is NOT raised, on three measurements. (i) On the shipped material at k=15 the box moves 20.43 -> **20.44** (p95 39.12 -> 39.18). (ii) On a verified MIRROR it moves 24.54 -> 24.54 at k=15 and 24.54 -> **24.55** at k=200, `hiFrac` 0 throughout. (iii) The sun is already carried by the `ShadowRig` directional light, which has its own GGX lobe, so raising the IBL disc double-counts it; the size of that double count is measured too and it is small, `meanRatio` 1.0051 at k=15, i.e. the sphere-averaged diffuse irradiance rises 0.51 per cent. **A change that moves nothing is not a fix, and shipping it as the default would have made a real defect look addressed.** | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1525 | **SUSPECT (5) IS CONFIRMED AND IT IS WHY FIXING RN-1523 ALONE CANNOT WORK: THE DISC IS 35x TOO WIDE, SO ALMOST NO SURFACE REFLECTS IT INTO ANY CAMERA** | The sprite is `scale 0.055` with `sizeAttenuation: false`, which is angular-size preserving under any projection (the NDC extent and `tan(halfFov)` cancel, checked against the 90-degree cube camera as well as the presented one), giving a disc **3.15 degrees across against the real sun's 0.53**. Its solid angle is 2.4e-3 sr, i.e. **1.9e-4 of the sphere**, and for a mirror the set of surface normals that reflects it into the eye is that same fraction; on the canonical pose, a near-vertical plate viewed near-horizontally under a sun at elevation dot 0.45 contains none of them. `docs/screenshots/RN1521_machine_mirror_disc200.png` is the picture: a metalness-1.0, roughness-0.02 machine under a 200x sun, reading as flat blue sky-reflecting plate with no highlight anywhere in frame. **RN-1527 supplies the stronger form of this: the canonical box receives no direct sun AT ALL, so the question of a reflected lobe was never the binding one there.** **THE PRICED NEXT STEP, AND IT IS LOOK-AUTHORING RATHER THAN A BUG FIX: narrow and brighten the disc TOGETHER, conserving irradiance.** Taking it to 0.53 degrees and raising radiance by (3.15/0.53)^2 = 35x is the same total energy with 35x the peak and a highlight six times narrower, which is what makes a curved plate catch it. That change is also visible in the presented sky, which is a different lane's judgement and is not a null to be silently corrected here. | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1526 | **THE MIRROR ARM WAS HALF-ARMED AND REPORTED ITSELF ARMED, AND A VERIFIER CAUGHT IT: THE PER-PART CHANNEL ASSIGNS `roughnessFactor`, IT DOES NOT SCALE IT** | A fresh-context verifier ran `?ibldiag=mirror` with `?ibldisc=15` and `=200` and got luma 20.44 to 20.61 / iqr 15.31 to 15.51 against this lane's reported 24.54 / 31.32, and correctly refused to merge on it. **Reproduced exactly and the verifier's reading was right: `?ibldiag=mirror` ALONE renders the canonical box at luma 20.43 / p95 39.12 / iqr 15.30, BIT-IDENTICAL to the shipped frame, while `machineMatState()` reported `overrides: 14` and every material row read `metalness 1, roughness 0.02` live.** The cause is that `PartMaterial`'s injected GLSL ASSIGNS both factors from the per-vertex channel rather than scaling what the material carries, so with the channel on `MeshStandardMaterial.roughness` and `.metalness` are dead uniforms and `iblDiagOverride` writes numbers no fragment ever reads. **This is MachineMat's own failure mode (a) ("the injection is a silent no-op") wearing a green light, and the instrument this lane built to catch it counted the CPU-side write rather than anything downstream of it.** The first commit's arm happened to carry `?machinemat=0` beside it in every run, which is why the lane never saw the null; the flag was documented as required and that is precisely the shape that gets run without it. **FIXED, and not with a sentence: `machineMatEnabled()` now folds `iblDiagMirrorOn()` into the one predicate the bake gate and the injection gate already share, so the arm carries its own precondition, and `machineMatState().mirrorForcedOff` publishes why the channel is off.** After the fix, the verifier's exact invocation `?ibldiag=mirror` with no second flag reads **24.54 / 65.78 / 31.32**, reproducing this lane's figure, and `?ibldiag=mirror&ibldisc=200` reads 24.55 / 31.28, leaving RN-1524's conclusion unchanged. The shipped frame with no flags is 20.43 / 39.12 / 15.30, unmoved. **THE TRANSFERABLE RULE: an arm that needs a second flag beside it to mean anything will be run without it, so the precondition belongs in the code and not in the comment; and a counter that counts an assignment is not evidence that anything reads it.** | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1527 | **THE CANONICAL MACHINE BOX IS 99.7 PER CENT IBL-LIT AND RECEIVES ESSENTIALLY NO DIRECT SUN, WHICH MAKES IT UNABLE TO REGISTER A SUN-DRIVEN CHANGE OF ANY KIND** | `?ibldiag=noenv` is the control this lane shipped without and the verifier's question deserved: `SkyIbl` assigns `null` instead of the PMREM texture and changes nothing else (the capture still runs, the ground half is still raised, the rebuild still counts), so "what is the environment worth on this subject" is one subtraction on one binary at one pose. **Measured: the shipped machine box goes 20.43 -> 0.06 luma with the environment removed, while the same frame's world box only goes 32.41 -> 22.08.** The frame is still fully lit; the machine is a black silhouette in it (`docs/screenshots/RN1527_machine_noenv.png`). A verified mirror goes 24.54 -> **0.00**, which is what a metalness-1.0 material with no environment must be. **So suspect (1) is refuted as hard as it can be: the environment is not merely reaching these materials, it is 99.7 per cent of their light.** RN-1415 called this box "the most IBL-dominated subject in the game" and that is now a number rather than a description. **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE CAMPAIGN, AND IT IS BIGGER THAN THIS LANE: RN-1200's rectangle frames a face the sun does not reach, so no change to the sun, to the sun's specular, to the disc, or to any direct-light term can ever move it.** That is a second and independent reason A0 owes the machine shot another box, on top of RN-1479's ("the columns are outside it"): this one needs a box on a SUNLIT face, or the campaign's gateway measurement is a rectangle that is blind to the light by construction. **RN-1528 and RN-1529 surrendered unused.** | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1470 | **THE CAMPAIGN'S HEADLINE DEFECT WAS FIXED TWELVE DAYS BEFORE THE CAMPAIGN WAS SCOPED, AND FOUR DOCUMENTS PLUS ONE LANE'S CONCLUSION WERE BUILT ON THE STALE SENTENCE** | A2b was dispatched to widen `panel`'s effective roughness band from 0.032 to >= 0.15 by copying `ore`. Measured off the shipped bytes before touching anything: `panel` is **0.184 on Steel** and 0.143 to 0.245 across its six roles, and `ore` is 0.175 to 0.319, so panel already beat the family it was told to copy on five of six roles. `RN-553` re-authored `_panel_masks` on 2026-08-01 and opened the band, **the same day section 2.1's bullet was written and four commits later**, with "0.032 roughness band opened to 0.184" in its own commit subject; the bullet was never edited back and `of_panel_orm.png` changed three more times afterwards. Independently verified by a fresh-context agent (§7 rule) that wrote its own PNG decoder rather than reusing this lane's and regenerated the family byte-identically from source before measuring, and which found the provenance commit. **What this invalidates:** the scope audit's "THE headline defect", `ART-CAMPAIGN-2026-08-13.md` lines 13 and 36, and **RN-1415's stated CAUSE** for the PMREM null result, which is withdrawn below. The `ore` "0.28 to 0.37" was never true under any palette this repo has shipped and appears to be a copy of item 5's unrelated "0.28 to 0.37 m" AO radius. **The reusable rule is now a standing note in section 2.1: a measured number in a doc is a claim about bytes at a date, and quoting one to justify work obliges re-measuring it, which is ten seconds of arithmetic.** | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1471 | **THE BAND THAT ACTUALLY NEEDED WIDENING WAS `coarse`, AND THE CAUSE WAS A SMOOTHSTEP THAT NEVER REACHED ITS RAILS** | With panel measured healthy, the same sweep found the two worst effective bands in the game were `coarse`'s: **Copper 0.075 and Iron 0.086**, half of section 2.1's bar. `_coarse_masks` keyed on `_smoothstep(0.15, 0.85, _clamp01(height))` over a raw field whose mass sits between 0.3 and 0.7, so the ramp was near-linear across the occupied range, never reached either rail, and the 0.26 coefficient was never spending 0.26. Keyed on a normalised copy (`hn`, added to `_coarse_height`'s aux **without touching `out`**, so the normal map and AO are byte-identical) with the knees moved inside the distribution, plus a washed-fines zone term and a packed-fines hollow term: ormG span **0.2157 -> 0.3255**, Soil/Sand/Regolith/Coal/Rubber now 0.277 to 0.325, and the family's under-0.60 fraction goes 0.0% to 2.3% so it can finally glint at all. This is `_panel_masks`'s own recorded lesson ("the thresholds are where the band lives, not the coefficients") arrived at independently on a second family. **Iron 0.130 and Copper 0.114 still fail, and so does `SteelLight` at 0.143: all three are PALETTE-side** (constants 0.40, 0.40, 0.35), and widening a shared map to rescue one role over-polishes the six that wear it. Recorded and refused, priced as a palette edit | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1472 | **THE TWO MISSING TILING ALBEDOS, AND `coarse`'s IS ALMOST PURELY VALUE BECAUSE SEVEN ROLES SHARE NO HUE** | The audit named `bark` and `coarse` as lacking albedo maps. Both authored, both reading **neither `height` nor `aux`**, following `_stone_albedo`'s rule and RN-454's cobblestone scar: on `bark` a pigment keyed on the fissures would draw them a third time after the normal and the AO and read as a painted-stripe trunk, which is the worst case in the set because bark's relief is the only strongly directional one. `bark` gets vertically stretched weathering zones (a 3x compressed v sample, wrapped, which is the cheapest honest direction out of an isotropic generator), organic staining, and **lichen as two fields MULTIPLIED not summed** so a colony is a patch with holes rather than a smooth blob, leaning COOL green-grey and giving the file its first cool stain against three warm ones. `coarse` is the interesting constraint: it serves **seven roles from Sand F0 to Coal 1C1C1F**, and no hue is true of all of them (a warm damp stain reads as rust on the iron chunk and as nothing on coal), so it authors grain segregation, settled dust and damp darkening in VALUE and confines hue to a 0.045 lean on the damp term alone. Both bands sit at or above their family's coarsest relief feature so the maps never overlap in frequency. This leaves `ore` as the only tiling family with no albedo | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1473 | **THE EDGE-WEAR MASK IS NOT CURVATURE, CANNOT BE, AND SAYS SO** | The brief asked for a curvature/edge-wear mask channel. Real edge wear keys on MESH curvature and this project has none available: every surface here is box-projected (`of_lib.MeshBuilder._project_uvs`) and campaign decision 5 refuses per-asset unwrap and AO baking precisely because they would end DW-5's byte-identical gate. So `_edge_wear` returns **local convexity of the MAP**, and the honest claim it makes is "this bolt head, this flake, this plate lip is exposed", not "this is the corner of the smelter". **It is the `_ao` signal with its sign flipped**, reading the same `height - blur` at the same radius, so occlusion and exposure are one number read once each and cannot disagree about where a crevice is: the module's one-heightfield rule extended to a third derived channel. **No family may ship it raw**, and the docstring enforces the reason: exposure is uniform over the tile, so keyed on it alone every chamfer in the game wears identically and reads as a pattern rather than as history. Each consumer multiplies it by its own low-frequency "has this actually been handled" field and smoothsteps the product, which is `_plate_wear`'s bimodality lesson reused. Selftest 7j/7k: the mask separates bolt tops from groove floors by 0.25, and a flat field is the negative control that collapses it to a constant | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1474, RN-1475 | **TWO NEW FAMILIES FOR THE D-020 VOCABULARY, AND THEY MOVE THE SAME CHANNEL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS** | `paintchip` and `rust`, both 512 px at panel's 1.5 m tile (inherited, not rechosen: same subject, same distance, and they will share a frame, so a different tile would put two plate rhythms at two scales on one structure). **`paintchip` is a coating failing on SOUND steel**: a 21 mm chamfer three times panel's bevel (the exposure peak is only as wide as the geometry that makes it), a 0.050 paint film added AFTER the wear is computed so the circularity is broken in the physical order (fabricate, paint, use), and metalness going **UP** as paint leaves, span **0.722, the widest in the game**. **`rust` is the steel ITSELF failing**: `_stone_planes` reused at a third of the tilt because a lifted oxide flake and a rock arris have the same requirement (that form's alternative, `(1-worley)**2`, is flat exactly where two cells meet), spall craters, and metalness going **DOWN** because oxide is a dielectric and there is progressively less metal to read. Its albedo is the one map here that READS its aux, and the docstring argues the inversion explicitly: stone's pigment must not know about stone's facets because pigment and fracture are independent in rock, whereas the colour of a patch of rust IS caused by how deeply it has corroded, so authoring them independently would put light fresh scale inside deep craters. **Both ship UNREFERENCED** (the `leaf`/`grass` precedent), and **both section headers state a required palette pairing**, because the maps are mean-neutral and the ORM multiplies: `rust` on a grey role renders grey rust, `paintchip` on a low-metallic role cannot show bare metal, and both are silent failures. Also measured and recorded honestly: dropping rust's near-texel-frequency grit octave saved only 9% (510 to 465 KB) against a predicted third, because the cost is the fracture field itself, which IS the family; the lever on that number is resolution or KTX2, not octave tuning | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1476 | **A MANIFEST FAMILY NO ROLE WEARS IS NO LONGER DOWNLOADED, DECODED OR RESIDENT, AND THE RULE IS DERIVED RATHER THAN LISTED** | `Surfaces.ts`'s family loop is EAGER: it fetched and uploaded every family the manifest declared, which was free while the manifest only listed families in use. RN-1474/RN-1475 ended that: two unreferenced 512 px families carrying three maps each cost about **3 MB of transfer and 8 MB of VRAM to draw exactly nothing**, against a 7.9 MB whole-game texture budget, i.e. a 30% payload regression for zero pixels. `REFERENCED_FAMILIES` is computed from **`ROLE_FAMILY`'s own values**, not hand-listed, because a "not yet wired" list is a second table to drift and this file already carries one copied table plus a verifier (RN-951) whose entire existence is that copied tables drift; the moment a lane points a role at `rust` it loads with no other edit. The two families attached by STRING LITERAL rather than through a role lookup (`MachineBatch`'s 'panel', `SpiderFlock`'s 'fur') are unioned in explicitly, because leaving them implicit is a trap that works until a role move silently un-references a family a hard-coded call site still attaches, failing as an untextured machine with no error. The skip is **announced** on the console, since a texture that failed to load and a texture nobody asked for look identical from there | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1477 | **THE OBVIOUS KTX2 ENCODER WOULD HAVE SILENTLY ENDED THE BYTE-IDENTICAL GATE, SO NO ENCODER WAS INSTALLED AND NO `.ktx2` SHIPPED** | A2a shipped the KTX2/BASIS loader with zero assets and this lane owed the first real conversion. Both viable project-local encoders were found and exercised on real maps. **The native `basisu@1.16.3` CLI is not byte-reproducible across Windows and Linux** for ETC1S or for UASTC+RDO (different sha256 even with `-no_multithreading`, so it is MSVC-vs-GCC codegen and not thread count), and **UASTC+RDO is non-deterministic run to run on one machine**: three runs, three hashes, three sizes. The pure-wasm **`ktx2-encoder@0.6.0` is byte-identical on both hosts for both codecs** (same wasm under both) at a ~3% size penalty, needs no C++ toolchain, and needs no PNG decoder dependency either because `texgen.py` already owns one and can hand it raw RGBA. Since development is explicitly split (Windows for judged frames, the Proxmox VM for everything else) a native-CLI pipeline would have made the DW-5 rebuild gate hold only when every encode happened on one OS, which is the exact hazard `texgen.py` owns its own PNG encoder to avoid. **This is a devDependency decision for Admin/build-tooling, not a texture decision, so it is reported rather than taken**, and it is the one job in the brief this lane did not land | OPEN, NEEDS ADMIN DECISION | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1478 | **RN-1203 IS CLOSED: EVERY MACHINE PART NOW WEARS THE FAMILY ITS OWN `.glb` DECLARES, BY ONE `BatchedMesh` PER FAMILY, AND THE SAMPLER BUDGET IS WHAT REFUSED THE OTHER DESIGN** | `MachineBatch.makeMaterial` called `attachSurface(m, 'panel', ...)` unconditionally, so the authored role never reached `familyForRole`. **The `flat`/card roles fold back into `panel` and everything else is routed**, so `MachineGeometry.gatherTiers` now buckets a template's primitives by `familyForRole(roleOfMaterialName(mat.name))` and returns one merged geometry PER FAMILY PER TIER; `MachineLayers.buildLayers` gives each family its own `BatchedMesh` and its own `attachSurface`, i.e. the same call `RuinSites` (RN-1422) and `PlayerRig` already make, with all of D-016's mean-neutral machinery, the `setMaps` toggles and `surfaceReport` inherited for free rather than reimplemented. **THE ONE POOL SURVIVES INTACT AND EVERY CALLER IS UNCHANGED**: a slot is added to every layer in the same order so slot n means the same machine everywhere, and a layer with nothing to draw for a template points at three degenerate vertices (`absentGeometry`, whose attribute set is COPIED from a real one because `BatchedMesh` throws on a mismatch and `normalize` is the only authority on that set) and stays invisible; the id disagreement that would put one machine's parts on another is checked and logged rather than assumed away. FS-40's `drawnKeyAt` keeps reading three's own state because the absent geometry is in no `geomKey`. **MEASURED OFF THE SHIPPED BYTES, the five machine pools author FOUR tiling families between them and no pool authors more than three**: `factoryMachines` panel+stone+coarse, `beltCargo` coarse+panel+stone+bark, `structures` and `launchpads` panel+stone, `station` panel+coarse+suitplate. 5 meshes become 14; the machine shot's draw calls go **77 to 85, +8, which is exactly `factoryMachines`'s two extra layers times the eye plus three cascades**. **THE SAMPLER ARITHMETIC IS WHY THIS IS NOT ONE MATERIAL WITH A BAKED FAMILY INDEX, which is what §7 proposed:** measured live on the D3D11 path, `MAX_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS` is **16**, not 32; one machine program binds **5** surface samplers (three allocates a unit per sampler UNIFORM, so one ORM image occupies roughnessMap+metalnessMap+aoMap) plus **1** PMREM env plus **3** cascade shadow maps = **9 of 16, unchanged by this pass**. Each extra family on one material costs albedo+normal+ORM = 3, so `factoryMachines` would need 15 of 16 and `beltCargo` would need **18, over the limit on Reid's own GPU**, and A4's `rust`/`paintchip` would be unwireable on any machine that also carries stone. The cost therefore goes where there is headroom, draws, and is counted rather than assumed. `render_machines.py`'s regex-read `metalness: 0.45 / roughness: 0.55` **did not move**: a family changes which maps a material wears, never the base they modulate | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |

Before to after, left column (300,0,470,600) **iqr 8.25 -> 11.50, +39.4 per cent**, luma 18.51 -> 18.20, sat 0.426 -> 0.443; right column (1225,0,1320,560) **iqr 6.94 -> 10.00, +44.1 per cent**, luma 17.30 -> 16.54. **POSITIVE CONTROL, because "the columns changed" is still an inference:** `?tile=stone:0.12` (RN-953's sweep) moves the left column's iqr 11.50 -> 10.11 and leaves RN-1200's box **bit-identical again**, which localises the `stone` family to the columns and to nothing inside the box. **THE ACTION THIS OWES A0: the machine shot needs a second named box on the hearth columns, or the campaign's own gateway measurement is a rectangle that cannot see a material change by construction** | LANDED, ACTION OWED TO A0 | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1405 to RN-1414 | **A0: THE FIVE CANONICAL ART FRAMES EXIST, AS ONE PROBE RATHER THAN AS FIVE REMEMBERED INVOCATIONS** | `web/tools/smoke/probes/artframe.js` is the shot manifest: one `SHOTS` table carrying every field of every shot (pose, sun, standoff, bearing, readout box as frame FRACTIONS so it survives a resolution change), so "reproduce frame 3" is one argument. **It captures through `of.screenshot()` and not `run.mjs --out`, and that is forced rather than preferred:** `--out` is a PLAYWRIGHT PAGE screenshot, so the first frame of this pass came back with the objectives panel, an interaction tooltip and a progress bar over the subject; `artshot.js`'s hideUi walks the canvas's siblings and is correct at the instant it runs and cannot survive HUD nodes that mount LATER (the tooltip appears when the aim lands on a harvest node, which is after the pose is set). Every frame in this pass carries its own `postState`, `shadow`, `ibl` and `render` block, so a pair can be SHOWN to be one variable apart rather than asserted to be. **Three framings were rejected with their reason recorded so the next lane does not re-run the sweep:** the ruin at bearing 35 puts a canopy conifer in front of the cella, at bearing 200 it is a clean silhouette and FULLY BACKLIT so no material claim can be judged on it, and bearing 60 at 34 m keeps a sunlit face, a shaded front and the debris field; the base at bearing 210 stands the eye behind a trunk filling the right third; the base at dot 0.12 came back at world luma 15.6 with loFrac 0.86, a true reading of §2b's named open problem and not a frame anybody can judge a material on, so it is taken at dot 0.20, which is `lookdev.js`'s OWN dusk rung and therefore the same hour as the §2b table | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |

| RN-1415 | **THE PMREM ENVIRONMENT IS NOW THE QUALITY TIER'S AND NOT A LITERAL 64, IT COSTS NOTHING, AND IT BUYS NOTHING YET. The reason is the audit's own headline: `panel`'s effective roughness p05..p95 band is **0.032** wide at about 0.55, so the specular lobe is broad enough that a 64 cube already resolves it. **A 256 environment cannot show a variation the material does not have.** It ships anyway because it is free and because it is a PREREQUISITE for A2b: the moment the panel band widens to the >= 0.15 the ore family already has, the environment has to be able to carry the sharp end of it. So "a 256 environment cannot show a variation the material does not have" is false as stated, and the closing sentence's premise, that shipping this is a prerequisite for A2b widening the band to what ore has, is doubly wrong: the band was already wider than ore's on five of six roles and had been since 2026-08-01. The null RESULT stands exactly as measured (64 vs 256 moves the machine box 20.52 to 20.43); what is withdrawn is the explanation, and the real reason the IBL raise buys nothing on that subject is now UNDIAGNOSED and is open work. | RN-1420 | **SHADOW SOFTENING IS REFUSED WITH A PRICE, TWICE OVER: `PCFSoftShadowMap` NO LONGER EXISTS AND VSM COSTS 2x THE FRAME AND LEAKS LIGHT** | Cascade 0 is 15.47 mm per shadow texel over 0 to 22 m with a ONE-TEXEL PCF kernel (§2.1.5, now published as `shadow.texel0M` and confirmed at 0.01547 by the runtime), so every contact edge in a walking frame is a hard 15 mm step, which is the largest "reads like a render" tell in a ground-level frame. **(a)** three r185 has DEPRECATED `PCFSoftShadowMap` and answers it with "Using PCFShadowMap instead." on the console, which the smoke runner correctly fails the run on: setting it would have been a silent no-op behind a warning, and it was caught in the first minute only because the runner treats a WebGL warning as a failure. **(b)** VSM is the only soft filter r185 still has, and it was measured rather than adopted, ONE binary, the RN-1200 machine scene, one flag apart, same pose (miss 0.20 m both) and same pinned sun (dot 0.4479 both): `?shadowsoft=0` gives box luma 20.43 / p95 39.12, near pass 6.5 ms, frame p50 9.4 ms; `?shadowsoft=1` gives box luma 25.43 / p95 71.51, near pass 14.2 ms, frame p50 19.2 ms. That is **2.18x the near pass and 2.04x the frame, putting p50 over the 16.6 ms budget**, and it LIFTS the shadowed subject 24.4 per cent with p95 up 82.8 per cent, which is VSM's own light bleed and is a fidelity regression rather than a softening. **Default FALSE on every tier.** The switch and `shadow.radius`/`blurSamples` stay in the build so the next lane re-PRICES it rather than re-discovering it, and so both rows above are reproducible from one build. The honest remaining levers are a shadow-map size raise (2048 to 4096 is 48 to 192 MB of VRAM) or a cascade-0 split cut, and the second one moves the per-cascade texel that `ShadowLod.ts` and every asset lane's LOD ladder are priced against, so neither belongs in a light-foundation pass | LANDED (refusal) | 2026-08-13 |

Measured here by serving a SECOND dist whose `assets/textures/surfaces.json` carries the seven pre-fix sRGB-domain means with `version` still 2, so the two arms are one file's contents apart on one binary and the per-family ratios reproduce rendering.md's recorded factors exactly (stone 2.3362, panel 2.1322, fur 1.8511, grass 1.4328, suitfab 1.3767, leaf 1.3276, suitplate 1.2206). **Machine box (panel-dominated): luma 10.79 to 20.52, +90.2 per cent, p50 9.37 to 19.23, loFrac 0.952 to 0.698.** Forest groundNear (a terrain box with grass and leaf cards standing in it, and terrain does not go through `Surfaces.apply`): luma 24.79 to 27.34, +10.3 per cent, with p95 57.36 to 75.64, +31.9 per cent, which is the right shape, the lit card tips moving far more than the mean. **NOTHING CLIPS: `hiFrac` is 0 in both boxes in both arms**, so the correction did not overshoot and no family needs pulling back. The retune D-016 owed is therefore **NIL, stated as a measurement rather than skipped**. **AND ONE THING THE ARM FOUND THAT WAS NOT BEING LOOKED FOR: the ruin box moves 72.96 to 73.20, 0.3 per cent, i.e. THE RUIN IS NOT ON THE SURFACE-FAMILY PATH AT ALL.** `RuinSites.ts` draws through `loadGlb` + `selectLod` directly, not through `MachineBatch` and not through `attachSurface`, so the structure high-water mark ships with no albedo, normal, roughness, metalness or AO map and reads as flat untextured grey in the A0 frame. That is a wave A4/A6 item and it is bigger than a retexture: it is a missing wiring | LANDED | 2026-08-13 |
| RN-1250 | **THE TERRAIN BOUNCE DOES REACH MACHINES, and the expectation in my brief that it does not is falsified rather than confirmed** | The brief asked me to test it rather than accept it, so this is reported first. `iblgroundamp` exists for exactly this question (RN-64: "so that 'this term measures near zero' can be told apart from 'this term does not reach its consumer', which are the same reading and have opposite fixes"). Game-lit, `probes/machinemat.js`, sun pinned at dot 0.45 (err 0.0021), one seed, one camera, machine box 268,550 px at `505,20,1160,430`. **`--iblgroundamp=0` / shipped / `--iblgroundamp=6` gives machine mean 6.403 / 9.033 / 24.853 and p50 2.63 / 6.66 / 18.96.** So RN-64's ground shell is **29.1 per cent of all the light on the camera-facing face**, and its signature is a fill's: removing it costs 60 per cent of the median and only 10 per cent of p99. **`MachineBatch` missed the per-part MATERIAL path (RN-1200) and did NOT miss the ambient path**: it is on `scenes.near`, which is `SkyIbl`'s target 0, at `environmentIntensity` 1.0 under open sky, and nothing writes `envMapIntensity` anywhere in the client. The bounce is wired, correct and already the dominant term, so **no amount of additional fill is the fix** | LANDED (finding) | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-1251 | **The stock materials' ambient floor was 16.6x, 18.8x and 52.9x too small on its downward half, was ORIENTED where the terrain's is deliberately not, and never moved at night; it is now `TERRAIN_AMBIENT` itself, held by reference** | `TerrainAmbient.ts` opens with "two materials now need the same three numbers and a transcribed copy is a second authority". There was a THIRD consumer holding different numbers since W4: every stock `MeshStandardMaterial` in the near scene (the machines, the structures, the pad, the belt cargo, the station, the rocks, the props, the player's own body) is floored by one `HemisphereLight` whose two colours are hex literals in `Headlamp.ts` (`SKY_HEMI = { sky: 0x334466, ground: 0x101008, intensity: 0.35 }`). Read off the running client, linear: terrain floor **0.03000 / 0.03400 / 0.04500**, hemisphere sky half **0.01159 / 0.02023 / 0.04650**, hemisphere ground half **0.00181 / 0.00181 / 0.00085**. Three separate faults and only the second is obvious. **(a) It is oriented.** A hemisphere light delivers `mix(ground, sky, 0.5 + 0.5*dot(n, up))`, so a face turned down gets the ground half alone, while `uAmbient` has NO weight on it at all (RN-1017 named it as the one unweighted term in the terrain expression) and its stated job is "what stops a fully shadowed slope reading as a hole". A floor that falls away exactly where the surface is darkest is not a floor. **(b) The ground half is zero with a rounding error**, and it is not standing in for the bounce, which arrives separately and is measured working in RN-1250. **(c) It does not move at night**: `terrainNightAmbient` writes starlight into the shared object every frame, so on an airless night the ground beside a machine is floored at 0.085/0.099/0.140 and the machine at 0.00181/0.00181/0.00085, a factor of 47, 55 and 165. `StockFill.ts` publishes the open-sky ENDPOINT and Headlamp's cave lerp is untouched, so the buried endpoint is unchanged in both arms and only what an open-sky surface receives can differ. No new uniform, no new program, no shader edit: DW-10 unchanged at 4 ShaderMaterial + 4 hooks | LANDED | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-1252 | **AND IT BUYS 2.1 PER CENT, WHICH IS THE POINT: the machines are not dark for want of light, and the sequencing gate ART-DIRECTION.md sets is now DISCHARGED** | Same rig, same pin, same box. Shipped floor against `?stockfloor=0`: mean **9.033 -> 9.220**, p50 **6.66 -> 6.83 (+2.6%)**, p75 **13.79 -> 14.42 (+4.6%)**, p99 **50.96 -> 51.38 (+0.8%)**. The SHAPE is a fill's, the middle lifting by six times what the bright tail does, and the SIZE is nothing. Two more levers measured on the same box, to bound the whole question rather than to leave it open: **`?ao=0` is worth +24.9 per cent** (mean 11.281, p50 9.12, p99 60.15) and `?contact=0` **+2.8 per cent**, and both of those terms multiply TOTAL radiance by their own admission in `PostDefaults.ts`, on a surface this pass has measured to be 100 per cent indirect. Summed, every lighting lever in this domain moves a close-up machine wall from 9.0 to about 11.6 counts of 255 against a sunlit ground at 105. **The residual is the material, and it is arithmetic rather than opinion:** the camera-facing panels are `Steel` (linear 0.2542/0.2831/0.3185) and `SteelDark` (0.0685/0.0802/0.0953) at authored metalness 0.85, times the panel ORM's blue channel (mean 0.958) = **0.81**, so the effective DIFFUSE albedo is `vColor * (1 - 0.81)` = **0.013 to 0.061**, against a biome albedo near 0.25 on the ground in the same frame. That is a factor of 4 to 19 and it is the ten. **A shaded vertical wall IS supposed to be about ten times darker than sunlit dirt. What is not supposed to happen is that the wall's own albedo is another 4x to 19x under the dirt's.** So the asset lane's material and palette pass is not blocked on this domain, and re-authoring is no longer the boulder-albedo mistake, because the light it would be compensating for has been measured and found adequate | LANDED (finding) | 2026-08-03 |

| RN-1017 | **The airless night is one term, and the term is the only one in the expression weighted by nothing** | `lit = albedo * (uAmbient + skyAmb*skyViewEff + ground*groundView + sunT*(1.45*ndl*shadow))`. On an airless body at night `skyAmb` is 0, the bounce has no sunlit ground to gather, and the sun term is extinguished. `uAmbient` is the ONE term with no weight on it: not sky view, not horizon occlusion, not shadow, not the normal. Every other term has one. Two consequences worth separating. **(a) The two halves of that constant have different physical natures and share one delivery mechanism.** `AMBIENT_DAY` (0.030, 0.034, 0.045) is deliberately a safety net, "what stops a fully shadowed slope reading as a hole", and being unweighted is arguably its job. `STARLIGHT` (0.055, 0.065, 0.095) is 1.8x to 2.1x LARGER and is the dominant night term, and it is genuinely SKY LIGHT, so it inherits an exemption that was written for something else. Weighting it by `skyViewEff`, which is already computed two lines above, would give the airless night ambient-occlusion form for no new uniform and no new program. **(b) THE HEADLAMP LIGHTS NOTHING AT ALL ON AN AIRLESS PLAIN AT NIGHT, and the first version of this row understated it.** I wrote "it reaches props, rocks and the player's arms and never the terrain", then tested it, and the arms half is wrong. Measured with the fixed `?lamp=0` (RN-1011) at Cinder, sun -26.6 degrees, `lampCd` provably **46 against 0**: with props and rocks and trees in frame the flag moves **20,593 pixels**; with `--rocks=0 --trees=0` and props off it moves **exactly 0**, in the terrain box AND in the arms box, which was my positive control and which is why the second run is reported rather than the first. Three reasons compose: `TerrainShader` reads no three.js light at all (`lights: true` is there only because three throws without it); the SpotLight is added to the NEAR scene while the arms are in the VIEW MODEL scene; and the arms' fill is `lerp(caveVm, VM_HEMI, skyVis^2)`, so under open sky `skyVis` is 1 and `lampK` is multiplied straight out of it. **So a player standing on an airless plain at night with the headlamp on sees the ground exactly as dark as with it off.** That is gameplay-visible, not only an art note. Both fixes proposed, neither built; (b) is a DW-10 conversation rather than a free change. | **Proposed** | 2026-08-03 |

| RN-1006 | **The swing goes to PI, and pi is DERIVED rather than tuned: a ripple field is 180-degree periodic, so pi is exactly full orientation coverage at the smallest seam that buys it** | 1.05 rad is +/- 30 degrees, and a frame of lines all within 30 degrees of one mean IS a frame of lines running one way. Rotating parallel crests by pi maps the field onto itself, so the set of DISTINGUISHABLE orientations has measure pi: a peak-to-peak swing of pi covers all of them, and every radian past pi re-covers directions already reachable **while raising the largest possible angle difference between neighbouring cells**, which is precisely what a seam costs. Pi is therefore the unique maximum-coverage minimum-seam point and needs no defence as taste. The picture agrees, which is the half that decides: at 4.5 rad the crests visibly CURL at close range (side D's named failure), at pi they do not, at either the walking pose or a 0.35-to-5.5 m one. | RN-1005 | **The direction field has TWO scales and they multiply, so neither could be documented or swept without the other; both were `#define`s and one docstring was a version behind** | `REL_CELL` (cell edge, tile units) and `REL_CELL_NOISE` (angle-noise frequency on the cell lattice) together set the correlation length of the direction field, and the swing alone cannot compensate for it: at 14.4 m a walking frame covering ~30 m of usable ground held about TWO independent directions and therefore read as one. Both promoted to `uReliefCell` / `uReliefCellNoise` on RN-843's precedent, `?reliefcell=` and `?reliefcellnoise=`, with **the two `#define`s deleted rather than left behind**, because a dead define that still compiles is how a lane ends up sweeping one authority while the shader reads the other. **The UPPER bound on the noise is structural, not taste:** `ofRelVnoise` is sampled at `c * noise` for integer cell index `c`, so at noise = 1.0 the argument is always an integer, `fract` is always 0, and the interpolation returns `ofRelHash(c)` exactly. **At 1.0 the construction degenerates into the per-cell hash it exists to avoid**, every seam becomes maximal, and the 1.0 frame confirms it by eye. Shipped at 0.5, two samples per period, the coarsest non-degenerate setting, correlation 7.2 m. **Two stale docstrings corrected on the way:** `REL_CELL` still said "2.0 is 7.2 m" and "8 cells per chunk" after 92a4fb0 lowered it to 1.0, and `REL_CELL_NOISE` still said "8 tile units or 28.8 m" when the product was 14.4. | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |
