But the frame carries **long straight dark streaks running across the upper slope, and they are IDENTICAL in the before and after halves.** That is a different artefact with a different cause, it is unmeasured, and the leading suspect is `ofArtStrata`, which draws rock bedding as a function of `vRelief` and therefore draws contour-parallel lines that are straight in projection on a smooth slope. **Not diagnosed here and not claimed as fixed.** `RN1001_mtn_walk_y0.png`. | **Open, filed** | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-961 | **The ripple pass, shipped: a rigid per-cell rotation, because constant-theta-in-a-cell removes the shear identically rather than bounding it** | RN-955's diagnosis was that rotating about the UV origin is not a rotation of the pattern. The sharper statement, and the one the fix is built on: **the shear came from theta VARYING, not from where the rotation was centred.** The map `p -> R(theta(p)) * p` has Jacobian `R + (dR/dtheta)(grad theta)p^T`, and that second term is the whole disease. Hold theta constant inside a cell and it is IDENTICALLY ZERO: the map becomes `p -> R*(p-a)+a`, a rotation about a point, an isometry. Not a bounded shear, an exact one. So side C cannot fire on it by construction rather than by tuning. What it costs instead is a SEAM, paid down two ways: the angle is a value noise sampled AT the integer cell index, so it is **constant within a cell and continuous between cells** (those sound opposed and are not, and a per-cell hash would have made every seam a maximal jump); and two half-offset grids are blended by centrality, so where one grid is at a seam the other is at a cell centre. Costs no fetch for the angle, which is ALU. The gradients are differenced INSIDE each grid's rotated frame and blended with the value's own weights, because differencing the blended height would carry a `grad(w)` term belonging to the blend rather than the ground and would light a ridge along every cell boundary. `?reliefswing=0` is the negative control. | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-962 | **Side B could not adjudicate the fix, and the reason is that a screen-space orientation statistic is not a world-space one** | The instrument's four sides guarded the RN-955 failure perfectly (lambda 38.26 to 37.55 px, cv 0.122 to 0.126, tau 0.077 to 0.090, A p50 0.5886 to 0.5631: all flat, all accept) but side B, the one that was supposed to show the BENEFIT, moved 37.31 to 35.76 degrees, i.e. slightly the wrong way. Two independent confounds, and neither is a defect in the fix. **At a close pose there is nothing to measure**: at 2 m and -62 degrees the frame covers about six metres, and real sand ripples ARE parallel over six metres, so B was flat (16.98 to 18.96) no matter the swing. **At a wide pose PERSPECTIVE swamps it**: a projection maps one world direction to widely different screen directions across the image, and the term OFF already scores 37.31 degrees. So B measures the camera as much as the ground. Sides A, C and D are local or relative and are immune; B is the only global comparison across the image and is therefore the only one projection can reach. Fixing it means unprojecting each window to the ground plane and comparing WORLD directions, which is real work and is not done. **Recorded rather than worked around: the verdict on this pass came from A, C, D and the picture, and B abstained.** | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |

| RN-959 | **An instrument that rejects the current build is broken, not strict, and a positive control is part of the instrument rather than an extra** | The tempting way to harden a gate is to add sides to the claim until nothing gets through. That is not conservatism. A gate that fires on the SHIPPED build sends the next lane hunting a defect that is not there, and the cost of that is a whole pass spent chasing monotony that was never a bug. So `winaniso.py` carries the shipped beach as a fixture that must be **accepted**, and it is the load-bearing half of the fixture set: the whorl half only proves the gate can say no. The same argument applies one level up, to the CLAIM rather than the fixtures. Sides C (wavelength stable) and D (crests do not curl) are both satisfied by changing nothing at all, so four sides without a positive control would have scored a perfect pass on the unfixed build: a detector of 'did anything change' wearing four hats. **A positive control is a field a CORRECT fix would produce, and the instrument must fail loudly when it rejects one.** `gentle curve` is direction-varying AND wavelength-preserving, which is the shape the ripple pass is aiming at, and the selftest refuses to run if it is rejected. | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-960 | **A guard whose verdict nothing acts on is not a guard, and I proved it inside my own commit procedure on the night I was handed a correction to it** | RN-958's doc edit asserted on an anchor string, the assertion FAILED correctly (the header read 'RN-954 to RN-956' and I anchored on 'RN-954 to RN-957'), and the commit went ahead anyway with the controller file simply absent from the index. The verdict was right and reached nothing. This is the same shape as every vacuous guard catalogued tonight (the `?? 0` fallback that supplied the value making its own assertion trivially true; the `git rev-parse HEAD` captured into a variable while the update-ref used a literal that re-resolved) and it generalises well past commit procedures: **a check is only a check if a failing verdict changes what happens next.** Two concrete rules that follow. **A staged-file list built from a variable is not evidence that every file in it was modified**: verify the staged set against the intended set before `write-tree`, or make the write and the commit one step that cannot half-succeed. And **a guard in a script must terminate the script**, which means not trusting `set -e` to reach inside a heredoc, because it did not. | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-958 | **The instrument gets its third and fourth sides, a positive control and real pixels; and my own prescription for the third side was wrong** | RN-956 named the missing property as 'local wavelength must be preserved'. Building it showed that is not sufficient either, by the exact counter-example it was meant to catch: `sin(2*pi*r/L)` has `|grad phase| = 1` everywhere, so a **perfect concentric fingerprint has exactly constant local wavelength**. What separates ripples from a whorl is the crest's TURNING RATE in the only scale-free unit available, radians per wavelength travelled: `tau = |grad theta| * lambda`. Unit-checked rather than scored: rings of radius R must read `tau = lambda/R`, and do, to 7 percent at R = 600 and R = 1200 px, two radii an octave apart so the check constrains the scaling and not one number. Taken on the DOUBLE-ANGLE UNIT FIELD from a structure tensor, never on theta, because differencing a wrapped angle manufactures a spurious gradient at every wrap. The claim is now four-sided: A each window still a corduroy, B the windows disagree, C the wavelength is stable, D the crests do not curl. **On the real frames C is the primary catch**, median wavelength 41.30 px to 5.21 px and IQR spread 0.173 to 0.572, which vindicates the original instinct even though the reasoning for it was incomplete; D is the backstop for the idealised whorl C cannot see. **A POSITIVE CONTROL IS PART OF THE INSTRUMENT AND NOT AN EXTRA**: C and D are both satisfied by changing nothing, so without a field that is direction-varying AND wavelength-preserving the thresholds could be tightened until everything was rejected, and it would be a detector of difference in the costume of a detector of quality. Grounded in real pixels too: `fixtures/RN958_ripple_frames.png` holds the shipped beach, which must be ACCEPTED, and the reliefrot=1.20 whorl, which must be REJECTED. | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-954 | **The corduroy is the sand-ripple channel doing exactly what it was authored to do, and no edit to the generator can fix it** | Measured off the shipped `of_ground_relief.png` with an orientation tensor over the power spectrum: R (sand ripple) **0.9705 at -0.22 deg**, G (clod) 0.0133, B (scree) 0.1247, A (litter) 0.0091. `RELIEF_W[1]` (Beach) weights R at **0.42**, seven times the next channel, and Ocean at 0.30, which is exactly the two biomes Reid reports it on. Wind ripples ARE a corduroy and the generator's docstring asks Beach to be ripple-marked, so R at 0.97 is the feature working and a pass that treated it as a defect would have deleted a real material. What is wrong is that the DIRECTION is a global constant: sampled at `vChunkUv * 16.0`, every beach on the planet ripples the same way. **The tile is periodic by construction, so any orientation baked into it is the orientation everywhere.** That is a structural argument rather than a measurement, and it PREDICTS RN-850's spectral result rather than merely agreeing with it: a peak in one bin from 1 to 64 texels with 2.4 degrees of movement over a 64:1 range is what a field with no orientation freedom looks like. | RN-955 | **Rotating a sample UV about the UV ORIGIN is not a rotation of the pattern, and the frame said so in one look** | `ruv = R(theta(p)) * p` with theta varying over 11.5 m. The frame came back as **concentric fingerprint whorls** with visible stair-stepping, dramatically worse than the parallel ripples it was meant to fix. **Reverted whole rather than shipped behind a default-off flag.** RN-843's precedent (promote to a swept uniform, refuse a new default) does NOT apply here: RN-843's mechanism was correct and its VALUE was unsettled, whereas this mechanism is known wrong, and a known-wrong term in a per-fragment path costs cos, sin and a mat2 on every fragment while it is off and invites the next lane to turn it up. What survives is the diagnosis, the pose probe and the instrument. The correct formulation must keep the map near-isometric: rotate about a LOCAL anchor, or blend fixed orientations (which are shear-free because theta is constant), i.e. tile bombing rather than a UV rotation. | Accepted | 2026-08-03 |

Both the limb and the arc now use the geometric normal. (2) It was CUT OFF at /core's ceiling, which left a hard straight edge across the night side, because `exp(-h/H)` is still 6e-3 there and **6e-3 against a black sky is a visible 13/255 after the tone curve**: "too small to see in linear" and "too small to see against black" are different claims and only the second one governs here. The exponential is the whole falloff now, and by the ceiling it is 2e-5. (3) It had no line-of-sight PATH factor, so it was uniform along the terminator: a flat orange slab rather than an arc. What reaches the eye is (density at the shadow line) times (how much of that air the sight line crosses), and the second factor is 1/cos(emission), which is exactly what puts the glow where the terminator meets the limb. **No amount of retuning a brightness fixes a term with no shape**, and I spent two frames trying before naming it; `RN845_defect_twilight_slab.png` is faults (2) and (3) together, kept as the counter-example. | RN-850 | **The anisotropy instrument RN-843 asked for exists and validates cleanly, the null it returns is the same null, and the reason has changed: at Beach the artefact is NOT in the support and cannot be reached from `RELIEF_GRAD_UV` at any value** | Delegated, briefed with the trap named first. **VALIDATED BEFORE USE:** through the exact pipeline, pure white noise reads A 0.0083, an isotropic mottle 0.0660, and a pure corduroy 0.9993 at its exact authored angle, a factor of 15 of separation across essentially the whole scale. **THE FORESHORTENING TRAP IS PRICED RATHER THAN ASSUMED AWAY:** the same isotropic synthetic reads A 0.7495 at the 8-degree depression `reliefsweep.js` shipped and 0.0497 at the worst corner of the -85-degree camera used here, so at the old camera three quarters of the index would have BEEN the camera. The relief term is isolated by SUBTRACTING the amp-0 arm's power spectrum from each rung's, with both frames captured with no await between them so they are pixel-registered by construction. **THE RESULT.** Neither Cinder nor Beach has an interior minimum: both point at the sweep's edge, 64 texels, where dRms is down 70 and 73 per cent, which is the amplitude answer RN-148 already rejected. | RN-840 | **An airless body is an atmosphere PROFILE question and not a flag, and the fix is one boolean becoming two: does the sky box EXIST, and does the INTEGRAL run** | `Boot.ts` called `forgeAtmosphere(body.radiusM)` for every body, so the only body-derived input was the radius and Cinder got Earth's coefficients. The tempting fix, `?atmos=0`, is not a vacuum model: it deletes the sky box, and the box carries RN-64's ground shell, which is the entire ambient of every stock material. That is why the moon had only two states. Splitting `SkyOptions.atmosphere` (build the box) from `SkyOptions.scattering` (`uAtmosOn`) gives the third: box present, integral off, so `ofAtmoScatter` returns 0 above the horizon while the ground shell still paints sunlit regolith below it. **`iblGroundOn` deliberately dropped its `scattering` term**, and that omission is the load-bearing line. Measured on Cinder at 12 km: `air {box:true, atmosOn:0, coreSeaLevelDensity:0, coreSpaceAltitudeM:0}`, `ibl {groundBuilds:12, ground:true}`, 838 star pixels at a 33 degree sun, masked sky p50 exactly 0, sky pass 0.10 ms. | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-841 | **The bounce source is the ground BESIDE a fragment, so it must not carry that fragment's own shadow, and what settles it is CONSISTENCY rather than photography** | `ground` is the radiance of the flat ground around the point and it was multiplied by `shadow`, the cascade sample for the point ITSELF, so a fragment in shadow was told its neighbourhood was shadowed and its bounce went to zero. That is wrong wherever a shadow is smaller than the field it sits in, i.e. every rock shadow, every cut bank and every crater rim: the thing casting the shadow is standing in sunlight, and on an airless body it is the only thing lighting what it shades. The decisive argument is not Apollo, it is that **SkyAtmosphere's ground shell already drops this term and says so in its own comment**, and `TerrainAmbient.ts` exists precisely so the props' idea of the ground cannot drift from the ground's. They had drifted on this term and this was the side that was wrong. COST, NAMED: inside a shadow LARGER than the bounce's gather distance this over-lights, bounded by the ground-view weight and in the direction of too much fill where the direct term has already gone. `?bouncelit=0` restores the old expression exactly. | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** (shadow p50 4.00 to 5.21 alone, to 8.43 with RN-842; sunlit +4.7 per cent) | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-842 | **`skyView` was a flat-plane assumption, and a vacuum is where a flat-plane assumption becomes the image; the correction is MEASURED from the body's own height field at boot, never written down** | `0.5 + 0.5*dot(n, up)` is exact for a facet on an infinite tangent plane and wrong on any landscape. With air the error hides because skyAmb and the ground radiance are comparable; with none, skyAmb is exactly 0 and the entire ambient rides on `(1 - skyView)`, which the assumption drives to 0.033 on a 21 degree slope. `HorizonOcclusion.ts` samples the body's OWN oracle at boot (24x24 centred differences over an 8 m support, 576 samples, **5.3 ms on Cinder and 3.9 ms on Forge**) and forms `omega = (2/pi)*atan(medianSlope)`, clamped at 0.45. MEDIAN and not RMS, on WG-146's heavy-tail corollary: a crater field's slope distribution is dominated by rims and a mean would describe nowhere a player stands. `skyViewEff = skyView*(1-omega)` and `groundView = 1 - skyViewEff` **sum to exactly 1 for every normal and every omega**, so the term cannot brighten a frame on its own, only move irradiance out of a channel that is zero in a vacuum into one that is not. Measured: **Cinder omega 0.1693** (median slope 15.23 deg), **Forge omega 0.0000** (median slope 0.00 deg, because Forge's field is genuinely flat at that scale), so this is a no-op on the calibrated planet BY MEASUREMENT rather than by hope. The baseline is argued, not assumed: `probes/slopestat.js` takes the full 2 m to 1000 m ladder and the two bodies separate hardest at 8 m (0.149 against 0.034) and converge by 40 m, which is the reading that says 40 m measures regional tilt rather than local shelter. At omega 0 both weights are algebraically the old expressions, which makes `?horizonocc=0` exact rather than approximate; it also SUPPRESSES the boot measurement, so it controls the mechanism and not one value the mechanism produced. | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-843 | **R17 and Reid's etched ground are ONE bug and it is in `RELIEF_GRAD_UV`; I promoted the constant to a swept uniform and then REFUSED to ship a new default, because my own sweep metric cannot tell "less artefact" from "less signal"** | Attribution is settled: `?groundreliefamp=0` removes 95.5 per cent of the high-pass tail on Cinder's crater floor and 99.2 per cent on Forge's forest floor; `?terrainspec=0` removes 37 per cent, so the lobe is innocent; and there is no sign flip, both bodies show a two-sided perturbation. `?reliefgrad=0` (the pre-RN-741 screen derivative) is 2.0x to 10.5x WORSE, so RN-741 was a real fix that did not finish. The cause is the SUPPORT. `RELIEF_GRAD_UV = 0.0311` is derived from `RELIEF_FINE_M = 0.45 m` on the claim that 0.45 m is the finest authored wavelength, and the texture disproves it: `of_ground_relief.png` is 8-bit RGBA 1024^2 (header bytes 24 and 25, read directly) with gradient energy to the texel (3.5 mm) and channel decorrelation lengths of 0.12 to 0.39 m. At the shipped 31.85 texels the difference is **86 to 96 per cent uncorrelated with the field's own gradient** and goes NEGATIVE on Beach one octave out, so the support stamps its own 113 mm scale onto the lighting: the corduroy in `RN843_beach_t32.png`. **WHY NO NEW DEFAULT.** Swept over 1 to 64 texels the two bodies point OPPOSITE ways (Cinder monotonically prefers small, Forge Beach monotonically prefers large), and looking at the frames says why: `over8` counts CONTRAST, and at 1 texel the near beach gains a fine ripple that reads as real wet sand while the mid field keeps banding. The metric scores that ripple as artefact. **This is WG-146 again: the instrument saturates on the amount of contrast when the question is whether the contrast is organised into lines.** The instrument that would decide it is the ANISOTROPY of the high-pass field, which a contour artefact has and an isotropic mottle does not. SHIPPED: the uniform, `?reliefgraduv=`, `probes/reliefsweep.js`, and the attribution. NOT SHIPPED: a number chosen on a metric I had just shown to be confounded. | **Diagnosed and unblocked; default deliberately UNCHANGED pending an anisotropy instrument** | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-844 | **`--sundot` is not dead, it is solved once at boot against the SPAWN, and the fix is a first-class API plus publishing the solve rather than deleting the flag** | I was told the flag was vacuous and asked to make it work or remove it. Measured instead: at the boot site, asks of 0.28 / 0.55 / 0.92 deliver elevationDot 0.286 / 0.551 / 0.920, i.e. it works exactly as named. `elevationDot` is `dot(sunDir, localUp)`, so a phase that is one elevation at the spawn is a different one elsewhere; at Cinder's crater floor those same three asks land at -0.778 / -0.815 / -0.706, **three nights inside a 0.109 band out of 0.64 of requested range**, which is exactly why two very different values produced frames a lane read as identical. Deleting the flag would have destroyed a working instrument on the strength of a misreading. Shipped instead: `of.setSunElev(dot)`, which solves against `observer.up` AT THE MOMENT OF THE CALL (reusing `SkyPass.solveSunT`, the same solver Boot runs, so the two paths cannot drift) and returns `{wantDot, gotDot, err, t}` so an unreachable target is visible rather than silently rounded to the nearest achievable; and `stats().sky.sunSolve`, which publishes what the boot solve was aimed at and where. **The refusing case is reachable and was proven in the same loop**: at the pole `dirForT` pins the sun's y at 0.42 before normalising, so `dot(sunDir, up)` is the constant 0.3872 at every phase, and an ask of 0.90 returns `err 0.5112` while an ask of 0.3872 returns `err 0.0000`. It immediately caught a real experiment-design fault in another lane's plan: **the crater-floor site's maximum sun is 0.16**, so "noon at the crater floor" does not exist and any grazing-versus-noon pair taken there is a null result caused by the site. | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** | 2026-08-03 |
| RN-821 | **The station is ONE INSTANCE OF A `MachineBatch`, and the alternative was not a style choice: a raw `THREE.Group` would have made RN-761's shadow-safe authoring decoration** | `gatherTiers` + `addLadder` + `attachShadowLod` is the ONLY code in this project that turns an authored rung into a rung a cascade actually draws, and it takes a `BatchedMesh` only. Proof it was the right call, from `__ofShadowLod.report()` after the change and never available before it: `station:station_hull` dev [0, 139.87, 278.37] mm drawing tiers 0,0,1 for a marginal 3.0x, `station:station_int` dev [0, 52.80] mm drawing 0,1,1 for 2.0x, both agreeing with `check_shadow_lod.py` to the penny. The merge also collapses eight glTF primitives across eleven materials to one draw per stem. WHAT IT COSTS, NAMED: `OF_Glass` loses transparency and `OF_EmissiveState` becomes the batch's status role on a flat texel. The interior is unskinned by design, so the merge is discarding placeholder nuance, and the skin pass knows what it is buying back. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-823 | **The station's visibility gate is the near camera's own far plane, read off the camera, so there is no tunable constant in the file** | "Do not draw it from the ground" invites a distance constant, and a constant is a threshold hiding a physical quantity. The near camera is built with `far = DepthPolicy.nearFarPlaneM()`, 100 km on reversed or log depth and 30 km on plain; a station 400 km down is BEHIND it and could not rasterise a fragment if it drew. So the rule is `eyeDistM <= farPlaneM + boundM`, with the far plane off `rig.nearCam.far` and the bound off the batch's own merged geometry. Measured: 395,330.591 m against 100,000, not drawn; 4.316 m, drawn. Both terms are published in `stats()` rather than the verdict, because "visible: false" is equally consistent with a correct cull, a station that never installed and a pose that never arrived, and the three want opposite fixes. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-824 | **Whole-mesh frustum culling stays OFF, which is a deliberate refusal of the cheaper-looking option** | `MachineBatch` sets `frustumCulled = false` with the factory's justification ("always within a few tens of metres"), which does not hold at 400 km, so turning it on looked obviously correct. A `BatchedMesh` bounding sphere is three's own bookkeeping across `setMatrixAt`, and the failure mode of trusting it is the station VANISHING FROM AROUND A PLAYER STANDING INSIDE IT, which the brief forbids by name. Against that, the cost of leaving it off is one draw call issued while looking away, inside a gate that has already excluded every frame the player is not at the station. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-825 | **Two templates, not one, and `LOD_MATCH` is the whole reason** | Tier 0 is `/_LOD0(?:_\d+)?$/` and BOTH stems answer to it, so handing `gatherTiers` the whole root merges `Station_LOD0` and `StationInt_LOD0` into one rung and produces no interior ladder at all. That failure would have LOOKED FINE: one draw call, correct silhouette, and `StationInt_LOD1`'s 52.80 mm silently averaged against the hull's 139.87 into a number that earns nothing. Two holders, two ladders, and the interior keeps the cascade 1 it earns. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-826 | **`staleness()` asserts a HARD ZERO at 400 km, and `Math.fround` is what makes that possible instead of a 5 cm tolerance** | PH-77 established the shape on the pad. The station is the worst case in the project: nothing about it moves, it is the only thing at 400 km, and "the station is gone" reads identically for a rebase, a refused slot and the visibility gate. A `BatchedMesh` keeps matrices in a float32 texture, so at 4e5 m the storage epsilon is 2^-23 x 4e5 = 0.048 m, and differencing the read-back against the f64 original would have forced a 5 cm band that then covers whatever it is next asked to. Rounding the expectation the way the GPU rounded the reality gives the exact zero back. Measured 0 on the ground and 0 in the hall. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-831 | **The hall mouth is cut by the SAME emitter as the corridor bulkheads, factored for a second caller rather than copied for one** | R56: `col_HallWall{A,G}` stood across the two 3 m spine mouths at full height while `HUB_PORTAL_DEG` cut a 4.40 m gap in the HULL at exactly those angles, so a proxy disagreed with the mesh it stood for and the walker stopped dead at local x 8.098644, 2.251356 m short of `col_JambHallFwdR`, with 93 blocked ticks. The fix is `frame(tag, axis, at, cross, flank, cz)`, which is the bulkhead loop's own body with the pierced wall's half width lifted to a parameter. ONE EMITTER IS THE POINT: the physics lane's caution is that `orbitdeck.js` was green for the wrong reason while holding 2.5 m width and headroom as private constants, and a second copy of `HATCH_HW` here would have let the hall's doorway and the bulkhead 1.65 m beyond it drift apart. They are one straight 2.10 x 2.30 m line because there is nowhere for them to disagree. Passing the corridor's flank instead of the chord's would have left 0.668 m of wall standing either side of the opening with nothing under it, so the emitter asserts the flank clears the hatch. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-832 | **`col_HallSill{Fwd,Aft}` are DERIVED from the two slabs they bridge, and P5 stopped measuring those slab edges because they stopped being the question** | R57: the hall's deck is a square inscribed in a round room, so it ended at 5.751 while the spine's began at 7.850 and the walker fell through the 2.099 m for 51 ticks. Both sill ends come off `HUB_RI * 1.42 * 0.5` and `HUB_RI - 0.25` rather than being typed, so resizing either slab takes the sill with it. THE MEASUREMENT HAD TO MOVE WITH THE FIX: P5's `floorGapM` was `spineMouthX - hallFloor.max[0]`, which is STILL 2.099 m and is no longer a hole, so promoting it to an assertion unchanged would have gone red on a station that works. It asks the whole deck set at 5 cm along the centreline now (`unflooredOnCentrelineM` 0.0000, `decksBridging` naming `col_HallSillFwd`) and hardcodes no proxy name. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-833 | **`tools/blender/check_station_deck.py`: the gap between `check-proxies` and `stationwalk.js` as a one-second loop, and it ships RED on purpose** | `check-proxies` compares declared against shipped both ways and has nothing to say about where a box IS; `validate_glb.py` counts triangles; `stationwalk.js` walks ONE route in a real browser and costs a Chromium boot. The hall passed all three while being a room a player falls out of in 92.7 per cent of directions. This sweeps 360 azimuths off the shipped bytes using the walker's own three samples at 0.15 / 0.90 / 1.65 m with no capsule radius. ITS FIRST VERSION PRINTED 0 OF 180 GREEN: the march started at r = 0, which is inside `col_HallCore`, so every azimuth found a wall on its first sample. The start radius is derived from the core's own box now and the docstring carries the story, because a gate that cannot fail is worse than no gate. Control against the pre-fix glb: R57 2.095 m, R59 93.3 per cent. Against the new one: R57 0.0000, R59 81.1. NOT wired into `npm run check` while R59 is open. | Accepted | 2026-08-02 |
| RN-491 | **A merge-to-one-material carries the per-part response it used to discard, as a vertex attribute, and it rides an EXISTING `onBeforeCompile` rather than buying one** | The merge that collapses an asset to one draw call per pass kept only COLOUR, so an authored fang at roughness 0.18 drew at the body's 0.95 and RN-455 had to spend colour on things that are really material properties. Colour surviving was the existence proof that per-vertex data gets through; `PartMaterial.ts` carries a second channel the same way: `aPartMat` = (authored roughness, authored metalness, bare). The rule is `effective = authored role value x family ORM channel`, so the family map keeps supplying the VARIATION and the role supplies the LEVEL, and `bare` additionally lifts a part out of the family's albedo, normal and AO. **The DW-10 ledger does not move (4 ShaderMaterial + 3 hooks): the file declares no material and installs no hook, and FurShader splices its GLSL into the one hook RN-463 already spent. Its own hook would have been 4 + 4, which is what RN-455 priced before FurShader existed.** The authoring channel is `of_lib.PALETTE` plus `of_lib.BARE_ROLES`, carried in the .glb as a glTF material `extras` entry, so the client needs no role table and no name parsing. **The player suit (fabric / glass visor / metal fittings) and the machines (painted steel / glass / rubber) inherit it unchanged; per-part FAMILIES, as opposed to one family plus an escape hatch, remain unbuilt and would be a real slot.** | **Accepted** | 2026-08-01 |
| RN-40 | **A greeble vocabulary keeps a coplanar gate at zero by making the proud height a property of the greeble TYPE, so a caller cannot pass the defect in** | The four clean machines are held at 0 by their ABSENCE from `check_coplanar`'s allowance table, and adding greebles is exactly how coplanar pairs are created; the catalogued cause is two parts dimensioned off one landmark where the landmark is a width copied rather than derived. `machine_form.LAYER` gives each of eighteen greeble types its own stand-off and no two share one, and `Face.part` buries every part's back at `plane - proud * 0.55`. Two parts can therefore only share an outer plane if they are the SAME type, which means the same role, which `check_coplanar` deliberately does not count because it is invisible by construction. The cost is that a type cannot be nudged for looks without moving every use of it, which is the point. Measured 0 before and 0 after across 2,292 added triangles on the assembler | **Accepted, shipped, measured** | 2026-08-01 |
| RN-59 | **A machine's triangle budget is set by its MULTIPLICITY times the four passes it is drawn in, not by its size, and on that arithmetic the machine set cannot be detailed until the shadow cascades stop drawing LOD0** | The assembler's raise cost 1.4% of a frame and was accepted, which reads as a precedent and is not one: portcost's own 79-building reference base contains 22 smelters and zero assemblers. The same +1,684 triangles on a smelter is 22 x 1684 x 4 = 148,192, or +22.4% of the 660,385-triangle frame that probe measured. Inverted, the entire affordable raise for a smelter at the rock lane's accepted 3.0% ceiling is 225 triangles, which is one greeble, so trimming the art does not rescue the conclusion. LOD1 and LOD2 already ship in every machine .glb and nothing selects them; a CSM cascade at section 2.1's measured 15.47 mm per shadow texel cannot resolve a 30 mm bolt head, so casting LOD0 into three cascades draws detail that is unresolvable by construction. Cascades drawing the shipped LODs make the same raise +0.65% instead of +22.4%, a factor of 34 | **Measured, escalated to Admin; the client change is NOT taken in this lane** | 2026-08-01 |
| RN-60 | **`panel` is re-authored rather than duplicated, because the player lane's `suitfab`/`suitplate` split already made it the industrial family and nothing else** | The brief asked for distinctly named new industrial families so three lanes extending texgen would not collide, which was correct when it was written. It has since stopped being: `Suit`, `SuitDark` and `Plate` moved to their own families, leaving `panel` holding Steel, SteelDark, SteelLight, Accent, Hazard and SuitAccent, every one of them painted industrial steel. A `paint` family beside it would have been the same surface authored twice, would have needed a client literal change to take effect at all (`MachineBatch` pins `panel` on the whole batch), and would have left the 0.032 roughness band that section 2.1 names sitting live on the rocket parts, the launch pad and the tools. Re-authoring reaches every one of them with no role move, no client edit and no VRAM growth beyond the new albedo | **Accepted, shipped, measured (Steel 0.032 to 0.184)** | 2026-08-01 |
| RN-41 | **Winding is not a rule an author can follow across six axis-and-sign cases; test the SIGNED VOLUME of the closed solid instead** | Every palette role except the double-sided few is backface culled, so a face wound the wrong way is invisible from outside and visible from inside, which reads as a hole in the machine. The in-plane basis is (Y, Z) on an X face and (X, Z) on a Y face, and those have opposite handedness against their own normals, so a corner list that is counter-clockwise seen from +X is clockwise seen from +Y. Six coin flips is six chances to ship an invisible panel on one side of one machine, and each would only be found by looking at that exact bearing. `machine_form.oriented` computes the signed volume once and reverses every face if it is negative. Related and from the same module: nothing in this project CUTS geometry, so a `sink()` that puts a box inside a solid body authors a recess nobody can see; a machine gets one by standing a COAMING proud of the surface, which is what `build_assembler._mouth` always did | **Accepted, shipped** | 2026-08-01 |
| RN-1 | Dual-camera scaled-space (near scene + scaled scene composited) | Proven KSP technique for seamless distance | **Accepted (Spike 1); NOT YET BUILT, scheduled Phase S** (2026-07-05, RETHINK §4-S1): M2.1 to M5.2 shipped the near/1:1 world; the scaled far-field over the 1:1 planet is the namesake seam, deferred to Phase S | 2026-06-14 |
| RN-2 | Logarithmic **or cascaded** depth buffer; near camera only | Single-frame coverage of cm→planetary scales. Spike 1: measure reversed-Z float first; cascaded passes preferred over `SV_Depth` log because Nanite can't take a custom per-pixel depth write | **Accepted** (Spike 1) | 2026-06-14 |
| RN-5 | Terrain = **classic streamed meshes (RealtimeMesh), NOT Nanite** | World-gen already does LOD (Nanite's headline feature redundant); Nanite-on-terrain conflicts with the cascaded/`SV_Depth` depth path. Nanite's payoff is later **factory** meshes, not terrain | **Accepted (Spike 1); SUPERSEDED by PMC in practice** (2026-07-05, Admin D-009): RealtimeMesh was never adopted; every shipped mesh path (chunks, voxel near-field, factory visuals) is `UProceduralMeshComponent`, which carried M2.1 to M5.2. Revisit RealtimeMesh/equivalent only if PMC becomes the perf bottleneck | 2026-06-14 |
| RN-3 | Factory items: GPU-instanced near, texture/decal flow far, none when unobserved | Only way 3D belts survive at scale | **Accepted; render-wall fit CONFIRMED vs factory-sim §6 stream (RC-8, §5), and now NUMERICALLY VALIDATED HEADLESSLY: a render-cost model over the emitted §6 stream quantifies the O(items)→O(lines) collapse (100k entities → 72 draw calls, 180k item-evals vs 12.18M belt items)** | 2026-06-15 |
| RN-4 | Lean on Nanite for factory mesh density (D-001) | Offloads LOD/triangle budget | Provisional; MOOT under the three.js pivot (DW-1/DW-11: `BatchedMesh`) | 2026-06-14 |
| RN-7 | **A layer that draws less than it was asked for must say so, and the way to know is to assert the RATIO of delivered to requested rather than a count** | The scatter's per-cell count was `Math.round(expected)`, which is exactly zero for every expected value under 0.5, and DW-19's finer terrain LOD walked it under that line without touching a line of scatter code. No count-based check could have caught it: the chunk-level `want` was right, the density constants were right, the pool was not exhausted, the props existed in the atlas and 159 of them were genuinely on screen. What was false was the RATIO. `deliveredFraction` is placed over requested, over the ground actually drawn on, so it is invariant to terrain, to density and to LOD depth, and it reads 0.0182 against the defect and 0.9971 against the fix. The same argument gave the two truncating caps counters instead of silence, and `chunksCapped` found a 16% shortfall within one run of being added. **The general form, which is RN-6's lesson pointed at a different layer: a stage in a pipeline should publish what it was ASKED for next to what it PRODUCED, because every other instrument in the chain reports the request** | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** (screen coverage 0.56% -> 28.87%, same seed, same camera, one binary) | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-15 | **A performance trade is only valid at the scale it was measured, and the failure mode is that nothing announces the scale changed** | `PropLibrary` turned per-instance frustum culling OFF with a recorded measurement (9,340 props, 8.2 ms of near-pass CPU with sorting against 11.1 ms without) and a correct conclusion: for 150-triangle props the walk costs more than the redraw. Raising the understorey to about 45,000 instances inverted it completely, because the scatter is a RING and roughly three quarters of it is behind the camera, so the saving is not a fifth of the triangles, it is three quarters of them. Measured in one binary with `?propcull=0` as the control: triangles 1,916,689 to 1,087,115, p50 22.8 to 11.3 ms, and screen coverage 0.5545 against 0.5563, which is the same picture. **This is the second time a DENSITY change has silently invalidated a constant nobody edited** (RN-7: DW-19's finer terrain LOD walked `Math.round(expected)` under 0.5 and deleted the ground cover). The general form: a comment that says "measured, this is faster" is a claim indexed by a workload, and the index belongs in the comment next to the number so the next lane can see whether it still holds | **Accepted, shipped, measured both ways** | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-16 | **A layer that PRODUCES instances must also REQUEST them, or it blinds the ratio that watches for the opposite failure** | The contact skirt places five understorey cards at the foot of every colliding prop. They are not a density draw, so the first version counted them in `propsPlaced` and not in `wantedProps`, and `probes/grass.js` read `deliveredFraction` 1.5146 against a check that requires 1.00 within 5%. Over-delivery looks harmless and is not: RN-7 built that ratio specifically because every other instrument in the chain reports the REQUEST, and a ratio that can legitimately read 1.5 can no longer be trusted to read 0.98 when the pool is quietly eating a quarter of the placements. The fix is to give the skirt an honest request, exactly `CONTACT_CARDS` per colliding prop placed, which restores 0.9997 and still catches truncation because cards refused by the per-chunk allocation are counted as asked for and not produced. **Worth keeping next to RN-7 rather than inside it: the ratio caught a defect in the code that was ADDED TO SATISFY the ratio's own design** | **Accepted, fixed, probe green** | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-17 | **A shared model gets a new term only if the term can be shown NOT to move the things it was not meant to move, and the check is a control band in the same capture** | Aerial perspective at playable range is a measured gap (near band against far band in ONE frame, so exposure, sun, tone curve and grade are equal by construction: saturation -0.149, the distant ground MORE saturated than the ground underfoot). A boundary-layer aerosol term is the physically right answer and was added to the shared atmosphere rather than as three's own `FogExp2`, correctly, because fog would have been free and would have been a SECOND authority on how distance looks, disagreeing with the terrain integral at every range and seaming exactly where a rock meets the ground behind it (DW-22). It still did not ship. Blue-biased it darkened instead of hazing; grey with a multiple-scattering floor hazed, and then the SKY control moved (saturation 0.494 to 0.410, mean red 81.9 to 97.0), because a 400 m scale height does not confine the term structurally the way the design claimed: near-horizon sky rays lie inside the layer. **The control is what made a clean revert possible instead of a plausible-looking regression in the one file the sky, the near terrain and the far scaled planet all read.** The negative result is the deliverable: the gap is quantified, the mechanism is understood, and the next attempt starts from a known-bad approach rather than from scratch | **Reverted; `Atmosphere.glsl.ts` byte-identical to main. Gap remains open** | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-30 | **Shadow contact is two terms that divide by RANGE, and neither of them is a shadow map** | RN-15 removed the understorey from the shadow pass for 12.3 ms of an 18.0 ms frame and removed every grass shadow with it. Putting it back in cascade 0 alone is not expressible in three r185: `WebGLShadowMap.renderObject` tests `object.layers.test( camera.layers )` against the VIEW camera, not the shadow camera, and `onBeforeShadow` fires after the `castShadow` gate. So the contact comes from (a) a base-contact gradient baked into the foliage geometry's existing per-vertex colour attribute, which costs zero draw calls, zero triangles and zero bytes and grounds the silhouette, and (b) a 0.45 m screen-space march toward the sun, which darkens the ground the card stands on. They divide from the cascades by RANGE the way the baked ORM map divides from SSAO by spatial frequency: a term bounded to half a metre of world distance physically cannot disagree with a cascade about a hill. Measured, one settled frame apart in one binary: near-band mean darkening 0.1012 with p99 0.6581 over 40.4% of pixels for (b), 0.0323 / 0.3705 / 17.9% for (a) | Accepted | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-31 | **A product capped by shrinking one of its factors has to be re-capped every time either factor moves; take the factor out of the product instead** | The understorey's height is card height x width jitter x height jitter x distance upscale. RN-15 found it too tall, retuned the height jitter 1.48 to 1.30, and it was still 1.34 m at the worst instance, because the retune left the product intact. RN-30 lowers the band AND demotes `DETAIL_FAR_GROW` to a horizontal-only upscale, so distance is no longer a factor in height at all: mean understorey height 0.4262 to 0.2811 m, tallest card 1.34 to 0.64 m, and neither number has to be re-derived when the other one is next tuned. The general form is the one this log keeps meeting from the other side (DW-33's "a constant that encodes an assumption is a scale assumption in disguise") | Accepted | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-32 | **Confine a shared model's new term BY PATH, not by height, and the confinement must be a property of the call graph rather than a tuning** | The first aerial-perspective attempt added a boundary-layer aerosol inside `ofAtmoScatter` and relied on a 400 m scale height to keep it off the sky. Near-horizon sky rays lie inside that layer for tens of kilometres, so the ground hazed correctly and the sky moved with it (saturation 0.494 to 0.410), and it was reverted whole. The seam that works already existed and had never been named: every ESCAPING ray in this codebase passes `tMax = 1.0e9` and every TERMINATING ray passes a real metre distance. `ofAtmoAerial` is therefore a separate entry point reachable only from the one call site that has a finite distance to geometry, so "the sky did not move" is structural and the probe measures exactly 0.0000 over 276,480 samples. Note what a plausible alternative would have got wrong: a `#define` on "is this the terrain material" fails, because the terrain's own sky-ambient ray is a terrain fragment issuing an escaping ray. DW-22 is unweakened: still one model, one shared uniform record, one GLSL string | Accepted | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-33 | **An optical depth over a finite path is analytic, never marched, because a quadrature rule weights its samples by the path length and a path can be 500 km long** | The aerosol column uses `L * H / (h1 - h0) * (exp(-h0/H) - exp(-h1/H))`, the closed form for a linear height profile, with the `dh -> 0` limit taken explicitly because a horizontal ray is both the commonest case and the one that zeroes the denominator. It self-limits by construction: a viewer 100 km up collects about H worth of dense air however long the ray is, so the same term that hazes 2 km of hillside costs the scaled far planet exactly sigma x H = 0.18 of optical depth. A midpoint or Simpson rule over the same segment multiplies its samples by the full 500 km and greys the planet out, and it would have done so only in the orbital frame, which is not the frame the term was being tuned in | Accepted | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-34 | **A band named "far" that is 30 m away is an instrument measuring the wrong question, and it fails by reporting that the thing under test does nothing** | `probes/groundart.js` compared a near band at frame rows 0.80 to 0.97 against a "far" band at 0.42 to 0.52. At the pinned camera the horizon is at about 0.28, so the far band is ground 30 to 80 m out, where no atmosphere on any planet changes anything. A correctly working aerosol term measured against it moved 0.3% of pixels and read as broken. A `horizon` band at 0.29 to 0.38 was ADDED and the old one left exactly where it was, so the RN-15 numbers stay comparable rather than being improved by moving the ruler: on the same three runs the horizon saturation drop goes 0.0278 to 0.2094 while the old band's stays negative, which is the right answer for both | Accepted | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-10 | **The post stack is NOT a fifth pass. It brackets the four and interposes at exactly one point: after the NEAR pass and before its `clearDepth()`** | ARCHITECTURE 3.1 composites by CLEAR ORDER, never by depth merge, so the depth attachment holds exactly one pass's depth at any instant. A composer that assumes one scene and one depth buffer (three's own `EffectComposer` among them) would read the VIEW MODEL's depth, the last pass to write, and compute ambient occlusion for the player's forearms against a world no longer in the buffer. The near 1:1 scene is also the CORRECT subject: a planet 600 km away in the far scaled pass has no contact shadow, and the arms are a separate depth range whose occluders are not in the same buffer. Consequence, stated because it is a choice and not an accident: AO never touches the sky, the far scaled planet or the view model, while bloom and the grade see all four passes, which is what makes the sun disc and the engine plume bloom. `EffectComposer` was also unusable without breaking the DW-10 seam, since it takes a concrete `WebGLRenderer` and `Renderer.ts` is the only file allowed to name one; the stack talks to a three-method `PostHost` instead | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** (probe asserts occlusion CONTRAST, not that the pass ran) | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-11 | **A post pass may not sample any attachment of the framebuffer it writes to, and the rule earns its own buffer** | The obvious three-pass AO folds the upsample into the multiply: read half-resolution AO plus full-resolution depth, multiply into scene colour. The depth texture is an ATTACHMENT of the scene framebuffer, so that pass is a feedback loop and WebGL says so 256 times a frame. The structural fix, and not a workaround: the depth-aware upsample resolves to its own full-resolution R8 buffer first, and the one pass that touches scene colour samples exactly one texture the scene framebuffer does not own. Cost of the rule is 1.4 MB and one draw call; the thing it buys is that the multiply is a one-tap shader with no depth uniforms at all, which is also why it cannot get the background test wrong. Generalises to any future pass that wants depth AND wants to write colour | **Accepted, shipped** (0 GL errors after; 256/frame before) | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-12 | **A ratio metric needs a floor on its denominator, and a colour grade needs the space its pivot was chosen in** | Two instrument bugs from one night, both of which PASSED. `darkening = 1 - with/without` divides by nothing on an already-black pixel and returns exactly 1.0, so the AO probe reported p99 and max darkening of 1.0000 and passed its own "the 99th percentile moves a lot" check on pixels ambient occlusion had never touched: standing rule 11 inside the instrument rather than inside the code. Percentiles are now taken over pixels above a luminance floor of 12/255 and the lit fraction is published beside them. Separately, a contrast of 1.06 about a pivot of 0.4135 was applied to the ACES output, where mid-grey is 0.18: every value below the pivot was pushed down, hardest at the dark end, and the sky's red channel went 37.9 to 7.9 of 255 while every whole-frame mean stayed unremarkable. The grade now runs after the sRGB encode, where mid-grey really is 0.5. **General form: a metric and a curve both have a domain, and neither announces when it is being evaluated outside it** | **Accepted, both fixed and re-measured** | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-13 | **A before/after pair must re-pin every quantity that a data-dependent wait can move, and the sun is one** | The first before/after pair from this lane showed a warm-to-cool shift across an entire hillside that looked exactly like a colour grade and was entirely SUN ANGLE. The build recipe places machines by trying camera pitches until a ghost is legal, so two runs of the same recipe consume different amounts of sim time, and the sun moves with sim time. Setting the time once at the top of a probe is not enough: it has to be set again after every data-dependent wait and immediately before the frame that is captured. The pair was not wrong by a little, it was wrong in a way that invented a result for the effect under test. Related and worth keeping next to it: I then read a green wash across the ground in the after image and was ALSO wrong, and only measuring the two captures region by region settled it (ground 1.5% darker, its green bias identical in the before). **WG-28 says structural validation cannot replace looking at the thing; this is the converse, and both are true** | **Accepted** (probe re-pins; screenshots retaken) | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-14 | **A baked occlusion map and a screen-space one divide by SPATIAL FREQUENCY, and the way to prove it is to shrink the radius, not to threshold a face** | The material lane's ORM map bakes occlusion INSIDE one surface (panel gaps, bolt recesses, centimetres); this stack computes occlusion BETWEEN separate pieces of geometry (a machine's foot on ground, metres). Multiplying two terms that describe the same thing double-darkens and reads as muddy. The separation is structural rather than tuned: `aoRadiusM` 0.9 m on a half-resolution buffer is physically incapable of resolving a panel gap. **The first attempt to check this was the wrong instrument and is the lesson.** It asserted that a machine face away from contact darkens less than 6%, measured 10.1%, and would have been "fixed" by moving the threshold: the band it sampled is the smelter's own RECESSED PANELS, which are decimetre-scale and are legitimately this term's business, and "how much does this band darken" is not a statement about spatial frequency at all. The check is now: re-run the entire measurement with the radius set to 5 cm, the scale a bolt recess lives at, and require it to produce nothing. If the signal does not exist at that scale, no tuning of the shipped radius can be competing with the baked map | **Accepted, shipped** | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-8 | **A face is lit by the sky it can SEE and by the ground beside it, not by a sky term evaluated along local up** | Task 109's black cut banks. `skyAmb` was traced along `up` for every fragment, so it was a constant, and a near-vertical face with `ndl` clamped to zero kept 2.5 to 5.9 per cent of flat ground's light and rendered black. Neither replacement term is tuned: `skyView = 0.5 + 0.5 * dot(n, up)` is the share of the dome the face sees, and the bounce term's radiance is literally the flat-ground branch the shader already computes at that point, using `vBiomeColor` which IS that ground's albedo there. Two properties follow and both are checked: a flat fragment has `skyView == 1` and is algebraically unchanged (measured 154.8 -> 155.4), and every term goes to zero together at night and under shadow. Isolated with a CIRCULAR pit so that bearing is the only free variable, which is what makes the 155-count azimuthal spread attributable to `dot(N, sunDir)` and nothing else | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** (cut face over sunlit floor 0.135 -> 0.313, tolerance 0.18 derived from clear-sky diffuse; headlamp lift 16.99 unchanged) | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-9 | **A batch key must carry every property the batch's MATERIAL expresses, and the moment a material gains a map, the surface family becomes one of those properties** | `NodeBatch` bucketed by `metalness > 0.5`, which was free while every role was a flat colour and stopped being free the instant the bucket got a normal map: Leaf, LeafDeep, LeafDry, LeafLight, Grass, Ice and Oil shared `matte` with Bark and Rock, and the texture pass had recorded a REASON for each of those seven being left bare ("sub-pixel blades at any real viewing distance", "a double-sided card whose normal map fights the flat-shaded silhouette"). A rock normal map on a foliage card is worse than no map. The key is now `(family, metalness)` and the recorded decision survives the batching. Two properties follow and both are asserted against the LIVE materials rather than the table: every `flat` material carries zero maps, and every mapped one carries all four with `aoMap.channel === 0` and `repeat === 1/tile_m`. The cost is exactly one extra batch, measured at +4 draw calls of a 150 budget, which is the main pass plus three shadow cascades and nothing else. **The general form: three copy-pasted `normalize()` functions and two independently-derived bucketing rules is how a shared decision drifts, so the role -> family decision lives in ONE module and is checked against the shipped manifest at runtime** | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** (normal map alone moves the ground 2.78 counts mean over 288k px against a sky control of exactly 0.000; UVs 294 copied / 0 synthesised across all three paths) | 2026-07-27 |
| RN-6 | **The near voxel mesh draws ONLY what the heightfield cannot express, and it imports the terrain's COLOUR but not its LIGHT** | Two surfaces exist by construction and are allowed to: /core's 1 m solidity shell and the smooth `surfaceRadius` the chunk draws (DW-26). Any face the near mesh puts on the derived surface is a second drawing of ground the chunk already drew, offset by up to half a cell diagonal, so it pokes through as cube corners. The rule makes the mesh's responsibility a property of the surface rather than of the edit sets, which is what "faces of an edit" got wrong: `levelArea` edits a scatter of cells and their faces are isolated cubes. Colour comes from `BiomePalette` so cut rock and the hillside are one substance; light stays `Headlamp`'s, because the terrain program ignores three's light list and shading the mesh with it silently disabled the headlamp | **Accepted, shipped, driven-verified** (924 exposed -> 20 drawn; pad-vs-ground 33.6% -> 7.6%; lamp lift 3.46x -> 11.5x) | 2026-07-26 |

## 4. Architecture & approach
- **Scaled space transition:** maintain two representations per body, a far proxy (low-poly + baked) rendered by the scaled camera, and near streamed terrain. As the observer enters a body's approach band, cross-fade proxy→terrain; floating-origin keeps near terrain at small coords. The handoff is the "no loading screen" moment.
- **Depth:** log **or cascaded** depth in the near camera only (RN-2); the scaled camera uses plain depth at its own scale; the two are composited, not depth-merged (near always over scaled). Spike 1 measures UE's reversed-Z float first, cascaded preferred over `SV_Depth` (Nanite-compat). See [spike1-rendering §3](../spikes/spike1-rendering.md).
- **Factory LOD ladder (conceptual, not built before Phase 1):** (0) full instanced meshes + animated items near; (1) instanced machines, items as scrolling material far; (2) machines as impostors, no items; (3) not rendered, sim is on-rails anyway ([core-engine](core-engine.md) SimProxy). LOD band ties to the same active/on-rails distance bands. **Nanite's intended home** (RN-5).
- **Atmosphere:** UE5 **Sky Atmosphere** component (Rayleigh/Mie, surface→space fade-to-black out of the box) driven by camera altitude + sun direction + `FAtmosphereProfile` from [world-gen](world-gen.md) (D-006). Custom analytic LUT is a Phase-1 fallback. Forge only; Cinder airless. See [spike1-rendering §4](../spikes/spike1-rendering.md).

## 5. Interfaces & dependencies

### Published this pass (RN-1200): `self.__ofMachineMat`, and one line that is now a CONTRACT

`state()` (enabled / flagPresent / mode / bareOn / bareFlagPresent / bare / baseRoughness /
baseMetalness / baked / roles) and `table()` (the per-ROLE rows `PartMaterial` actually
wrote, plus **`distinct`**, `injections` and `missing`). `distinct` is the discriminating
field and it tells the three states apart with one number: **0** under `?machinemat=0`,
exactly **1** under `?machinemat=flat`, and **17** across the 18 shipped machine roles under
the default. `table()` is also the ONLY reachable read of `partMatState()` on this path,
because `SpiderFlock.partMatStats` needs a flock; without it the anchor-miss failure mode
would have no read-out in a machine frame at all.

**`MachineBatch.makeMaterial`'s `metalness: 0.45, roughness: 0.55` are now an interface, not
an implementation detail.** `tools/blender/render_machines.py:326` regex-reads them as
numeric literals and raises rather than guess. They must stay literals in that constructor;
`MachineMat.assertMachineBase` throws at boot if they and `MachineMat`'s copy disagree.
Changing the machine base is therefore a two-file act with a third-party consumer, and it
needs an Admin-logged decision like any other published interface.

### Published this pass (RN-845): `window.__ofBodies`, the second body's driven surface

`report()` (present / reason / drawn / **refusedId** / texW / texH / bakeMs / oracleSamples /
uvResidual / **eyeM** / simSecs / per-body distanceM, angularDiamDeg, posM, texelM, relief
min-max, and an `optics` record READ BACK OFF THE LIVE UNIFORMS rather than recomputed),
`aim()` (yawDeg / pitchDeg exactly as `of.look` takes them, elevationDeg, angularDiamDeg,
distanceM, aboveHorizon), `planetshine()` (onGround and onBody, both as a fraction of the
sun's irradiance, plus a Rec.709 luma so a lighting lane does not re-derive one),
`facts()`, `airProfile(id)`, `setRelief` / `setDetail` / `setDebug` / `setTimeS`, and
`setFov` (which is `CameraRig.setFov`, present since W1 and never called by anything;
**a zoomed frame has a different shadow-LOD ladder, so no triangle, draw-call or LOD
number may be taken from one**).

Flags, all registered in `run.mjs`'s PAGE_PARAMS in the same commit: `?skybodies=0`
(structural control, nothing is constructed), `?skybodyrelief=`, `?skybodydetail=`,
`?skybodytex=`, `?skybodytime=`.

### Consumed from physics (PH-161), and this is the whole of it

`_of_body_state(bodyId, simTimeS)` -> 6 f64 in the PARENT's frame, and
`_of_body_facts(bodyId)` -> 4 f64 `[radiusM, muM3S2, soiRadiusM, orbitPeriodS]`. Both are
symbol-detected; a client on an older wasm reports what it is missing and draws nothing.
**No number about a body's position or size appears anywhere else in the render tree**, and
if one ever does it is a bug: that is the whole point of `render/CelestialEphemeris.ts`.

**Depends on (inbound), Spike-1 contracts now PINNED (code against these, not the prose):**
- **core-engine** ([spike1-core-engine.md §5](../spikes/spike1-core-engine.md)): `FUniverseCoord`+`FFrameId` & `ToEngine/FromEngine`/`FFloatingOrigin`; `FReferenceFrame`/`IFrameGraph` (`ToUniverse(SimTime)` for body/star positions); `ISimProxy.Position()/Velocity()`; `ISimClock.Alpha()/TickIndex()`. **Events: `OnOriginRebased(DeltaEngine, NewOrigin)`** (re-anchor near scene + atmosphere transform; scaled scene untouched) and **`OnSOIChange(FSOIChangeEvent)`** (re-target approach body). A `UniverseCoord` carries its frame, never a bare `FVector`.
- **world-gen** ([spike1-worldgen.md §4–§5](../spikes/spike1-worldgen.md)): `FTerrainChunk` (center as `FUniverseCoord`, chunk-local positions, skirts, `MaterialId`); `ITerrainProvider::OnChunkReady`/`OnChunkEvicted`/`UpdateStreaming`; `FBodyParams`/`FAtmosphereProfile` (we consume radius/atmosphere, D-006, hardcode nothing). Re-project chunk transforms on `OnOriginRebased` via `CenterUniverse.ToEngine`.
- **physics** (later): entity transforms (interpolated) for vehicles/parts.
- **factory-sim** ([spike3-factory-sim.md §6](../spikes/spike3-factory-sim.md)): the **factory→render stream, PINNED by factory-sim, CONFIRMED here (RC-8), now EMITTED + numerically validated headlessly (factory-sim FS-14: `EmitEntityStates`/`EmitBeltFlowStates`/`GetLineItems`)**. `FFactoryEntityState` (~32B/visible entity: `Id`,`TypeId`,`Position` as `UniverseCoord`,`FQuat16` orient, `VisualState`,`AnimPhase`,`Lod` hint,`Flags`), `FFactoryBeltFlowState` (per visible line: `ItemTypeDominant`,`FlowSpeedQuant`,`Density`,`Compressed`), and on-demand `GetLineItems(LineId)→{itemType,unitOffset}[]`, **the only O(items) call, pulled ONLY at RN-3 LOD-0**. Stream is **delta-only**, drained at render FPS, transforms interpolated via `ISimClock::Alpha()`. Belts emit `FFactoryBeltFlowState` only on flow-state *change* (a steady belt is silent). We evaluate items' world placement via the sim's baked `pathUnitToWorld` (sim never evaluates it; only we do, only for LOD-0 items). **See RC-8 resolution below + §5.4 factory-LOD-ladder mapping.**
**Provides to (outbound):** the rendered frame; camera/observer transform feedback for LOD/AOI; screenshot/observation hooks (useful to [networking](networking.md) AOI tuning).
**Open contract gaps flagged to Admin (Spike 1):**
- `FAtmosphereProfile` has Rayleigh fields but **no Mie params**, request world-gen add `MieScaleHeightM`/`MieScatteringScale`/`MieAnisotropy` (non-breaking; D-006-owned by world-gen). Placeholder Mie used meanwhile.
- UE5 Sky Atmosphere renders **only one atmosphere on screen at once**, fine now (only Forge has one), a constraint for the multi-atmospheric-body future.
**~~Still to negotiate later~~ → NEGOTIATED & CONFIRMED:** the factory→render stream schema (fields, update rate) was pinned by factory-sim Spike 3 §6 and is **confirmed sufficient for RN-3 here (RC-8, below)**, with one *non-breaking* field-add requested (`AABB`/bounds for culling; see RC-8 §4).

---

### RC-8 resolution, does the factory→render stream clear the render wall? **CONFIRMED (with one additive field request), and now NUMERICALLY VALIDATED HEADLESSLY.**
> *Owner: rendering · Closes reconciliation item [RC-8](../spikes/PHASE0-SUMMARY.md#4-consolidated-reconciliation-register) (the #1 open Phase-0 cross-risk). Co-validation of the integrated real-hardware case stays a Phase-1 joint task with factory-sim. Date: 2026-06-14; **headless numeric validation added 2026-06-15**.*

> **HEADLESS NUMERIC VALIDATION (2026-06-15, jointly with factory-sim).** The RC-8 verdict below was reasoned *on paper* from the §6 contract. It is now **backed by integers**, with no GUI and no GPU: factory-sim emits the §6 stream (`EmitEntityStates`/`EmitBeltFlowStates`/`GetLineItems`, additive + read-only) and a numeric render-cost model (`core/include/of/render_cost.h`, tested in `core/tests/test_render_cost.cpp`, the `render_cost_tests` ctest suite, 133 checks green) scores it against the RN-3 ladder. **The numbers confirm every load-bearing claim of this resolution:**
> - **The O(items)→O(lines) collapse is real and quantified.** A **100,000-entity** stress scene with a realistic LOD spread, Near0 1,500 machines / 300 lines · Mid1 8,000 / 2,000 · Far2 30,000 / 6,000 impostors · OnRails3 60,500 / 12,000 (not rendered), carries **12,180,000 discrete items on its belts**, yet per-frame item-render work is **180,000 item-evals = the 300 Near0 lines × 600 items/line** (the *only* band that calls `GetLineItems`). **>98% of items (12,000,000) are collapsed away** into LOD-1 flow-material / LOD-2 impostor / LOD-3 not-rendered and never hit `pathUnitToWorld`. Item-eval work scales with **Near0 LINES (hundreds), not items (millions)**, exactly the §6.2 mechanism.
> - **Draw calls scale with machine-TYPE variety (tens), not entity count.** The same 100k-entity scene issues **72 draw calls** (≈ 3 drawing bands × 20 machine TypeIds + belt-material draws), **NOT ~100,000**, instancing buckets per `TypeId`. A slice-scale scene (400 machines / 200 lines, all near) issues **16** (12 machine types + 4 item materials) vs 600 entities.
> - **Instances stay under the ceiling.** 219,500 live instances (39,500 drawn machines + 180,000 near item meshes), comfortably below the ~2 M instance ceiling (§5.2 budget).
> - **Monotonicity + LOD-3-zero proven.** Pushing a fixed population Near0→Mid1→Far2→OnRails3 strictly reduces cost; **LOD-3 (on-rails) contributes EXACTLY ZERO** draws, instances, and item-evals (the proxy is demoted, factory-sim §5).
>
> This retires the *paper* qualifier on RC-8: the stream contract demonstrably clears the wall for both a slice factory and a 100k-entity stress factory inside one per-frame budget. **What a cost model cannot measure remains the Phase-1 integrated co-validation residual (§5.3 / R5):** the real-hardware LOD-0 `pathUnitToWorld` instance-build cost, Nanite-on-small-meshes, LOD-band hysteresis, and camera-cut burst budgeting, the *render-side* hot spots, which this model flags but only a real renderer can time. (Source: factory-sim FS-14; `render_cost_tests` green in the 14-suite headless core.)

**1. LOD-band → stream mapping (RN-3 ladder, §5.4, against factory-sim §6.2).** Each band pulls strictly less from the stream as distance grows, the mechanism that decouples sim-scale (millions of items) from render-scale:

| RN-3 band | What we draw | What it pulls from the stream | Per-item cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LOD-0 (near)** | Full instanced machine meshes + **discrete animated item meshes** on belts; inserter/craft anim from `AnimPhase` | `FFactoryEntityState` per machine (one instance row) **+ `GetLineItems(LineId)`** to place item instances along the sim's baked `pathUnitToWorld` | **O(visible items)**, the *only* band that pays it |
| **LOD-1 (mid)** | Instanced machines; belt items as a **scrolling-flow material** (no discrete item meshes) | `FFactoryEntityState` + `FFactoryBeltFlowState`: `FlowSpeedQuant`→material scroll rate, `Density`→fill look, `Compressed`→cheaper path. **No `GetLineItems`, no per-item data.** | **O(visible lines)** |
| **LOD-2 (far)** | Machine **impostors** (billboard/octahedral); no items, no belt material | `FFactoryEntityState`: `TypeId`,`Position`,`VisualState` only (one tiny instance row); belts collapse into the impostor field | **~0** |
| **LOD-3 (on-rails)** | **Not rendered**, chunk is demoted to `ISimProxy` rate-model (factory-sim §5) | **nothing**, proxy emits no per-entity stream when on-rails | **0** |

The load-bearing guarantee we are confirming: **`GetLineItems` (the lone O(items) call) is pulled only at LOD-0, on demand, per line we *chose* to draw discretely.** The instant the camera pushes a line past LOD-0, its item cost collapses to one `FFactoryBeltFlowState` material param set (O(lines)), then to nothing. `Lod` in `FFactoryEntityState` is the sim's *hint*; **we own the final band decision** (screen-space size / distance), and the contract explicitly lets rendering override it, correct division of authority.

**2. Rough budget (assumptions stated).**
*Assumptions:* one commodity desktop GPU + UE5 instancing (HISM/Nanite-backed machine meshes per RN-4/RN-5); ~2 M instances/frame is a sane GPU-instance ceiling; target 60 FPS / ~16.6 ms; belt physics from factory-sim §2 (**256 sub-units/tile, 64-unit saturation spacing → 4 items/tile/lane × 2 lanes = up to 8 items/tile saturated**; lines capped at 100 tiles).

- **LOD-0 item ceiling (where `GetLineItems` O(items) starts to hurt).** A fully saturated 100-tile line = ~800 items (both lanes); a realistic visible near-field rarely shows whole 100-tile lines saturated. Budget the *discrete-item* draw, not the line count: aim to keep **LOD-0 discrete belt items in view ≲ ~50–100k item instances**. At that range the per-frame work is (a) the `GetLineItems` pulls, O(items) but a flat read of the sim's gap arrays, cheap relative to (b) building/refreshing ~50–100k instance transforms via `pathUnitToWorld`. **`pathUnitToWorld` evaluation, not `GetLineItems` itself, is the LOD-0 cost** (each visible item = one arc-length→transform eval + one instance write). So LOD-0 should cover roughly **a few hundred to ~1–2k machines + the belts physically near the player** (the foreground bay), which at 8 items/tile yields tens of thousands of item instances, comfortably inside the instance ceiling. Past that, LOD-1.
- **Where LOD-1+ must engage.** LOD-0 ends at the distance where a belt item subtends **≲ ~1–2 px** (discrete items no longer legible, the scrolling material is indistinguishable and far cheaper). For ~0.5 m items at 1080p/60° FOV that is on the order of **a few tens of meters**; we drive it on **screen-space size, not a hard metric distance**, so it auto-scales with zoom/FOV. LOD-1→LOD-2 at the distance a *machine* drops below ~a few px (impostor indistinguishable from mesh). LOD-2→LOD-3 is **not ours**, it coincides with core-engine's on-rails demote band (factory-sim §5 / FS-4), so "far enough to be on-rails" and "far enough to not render" are the *same* band by construction.
- **Draw-call / instance / bandwidth ballpark for a realistic base (thousands near, 100k total, most far/on-rails):**
  - *Draw calls:* machines instanced **per `TypeId`** → draw calls = O(distinct machine types in view) ≈ **tens, not thousands** (one instanced draw per type per LOD bucket). Belt LOD-1 material = one instanced/merged draw per material. This is the whole point of RN-3 + instancing: **draw calls scale with *type variety*, not entity count.**
  - *Instances:* LOD-0 ≲ ~1–2k machine instances + tens of thousands of item instances; LOD-1 machines = instanced rows (cheap); LOD-2 impostors = up to ~tens of thousands of 2-tri billboards (trivial). Total live instances comfortably **< the ~2 M ceiling**.
  - *Stream bandwidth:* `FFactoryEntityState` ~32B, **delta-only**. Machines don't move → `Position` is silent; only `AnimPhase`/`VisualState` (≤~3B) tick for *active* machines in view. A few thousand visible active entities × a few bytes × 60 Hz = **single-digit MB/s**, negligible. Belts emit `FFactoryBeltFlowState` only on flow-state change → near-silent in steady flow. The only heavy pull is `GetLineItems` at LOD-0, and it is **read-only, bounded by visible-item count, and never per-tick-pushed.**
  - **Conclusion:** a realistic factory (thousands near, 100k total with most far/on-rails) **clears the wall**: only the small foreground set ever hits the O(items) path, draw calls stay in the tens via instancing, and stream bandwidth is trivial. The 100k entities are mostly LOD-2 impostors (≈0) or LOD-3 on-rails (0). The contract's LOD-0-only `GetLineItems` rule is exactly what makes this hold.

**3. Verdict + residual risk.** **CONFIRMED, the contract plausibly clears the render wall.** The §6 stream gives us everything RN-3 needs and, critically, *lets us stop pulling per-item data the instant we zoom out*, the precise mechanism the render wall demands. The contract is sufficient **as-is** to build LOD-0/1/2/3; the one addition below is an *optimization for culling/band-selection*, not a correctness gap. **Residual render-wall risk → must be measured in the Phase-1 integrated co-validation (jointly with factory-sim):**
  - (a) **Instance build/refresh cost at LOD-0**, whether ~50–100k `pathUnitToWorld` evals + instance-transform writes/frame fit the frame budget on real hardware (this, not `GetLineItems` itself, is the suspect hot spot). Measure with a saturated near-field bay.
  - (b) **Nanite on thousands of small factory meshes** (RN-4/RN-5, rendering.md R1), Nanite's cost on many small static-ish machine meshes is still unmeasured; this is *the* open Nanite question (terrain already settled to classic).
  - (c) **LOD-band hysteresis / popping** at the LOD-0↔1 boundary (discrete-items → scrolling-material swap), must cross-fade or it pops, the factory analogue of R2.
  - (d) **Stream drain vs. camera-cut spikes**, a fast pan that promotes many lines to LOD-0 in one frame causes a burst of `GetLineItems` + instance builds; need amortized/budgeted promotion (spread builds across frames, like world-gen's per-tick mesh budget).

**4. Field requests to factory-sim (non-breaking; routed to Admin).** `FFactoryEntityState`/`FFactoryBeltFlowState` are **sufficient as-is for correctness**. One *additive* request would materially help our band-selection/culling and is cheap for the sim (it already stores `StaticDef.AABB`, §1.3 / `bounds`):
  - **`AABB Bounds` (or a packed bound-radius) on `FFactoryEntityState`**, lets us frustum-cull and compute screen-space size for LOD-band selection **without** a per-type asset-bounds lookup on the render side, and without evaluating geometry. Sim already carries `StaticDef.bounds`; surfacing it (or a 2-byte bound radius) in the stream row is non-breaking and removes a render-side indirection. *Nice-to-have, not a blocker.*
  - **(Already in contract, just affirming we rely on it):** the `Lod` hint field and rendering's right to override it; `Compressed` on the flow state (cheaper LOD-1 path); `FlowSpeedQuant`/`Density` for the scrolling material. **No other additions needed.** If the `AABB` add is declined, we fall back to a render-side per-`TypeId` bounds table, slightly more render bookkeeping, zero contract change.

---

## 6. Task backlog / roadmap
- **[RN-45, DONE 2026-07-28, built in this lane rather than requested] Authored `_LOD2` for the four `detail_cards.glb` props.** Blender was available here and the shipped glb already carried the same exporter version, so an unchanged rebuild was proved byte-identical first and the request was withdrawn. Shipped 4 / 4 / 4 / 6 triangles against 18 / 40 / 18 / 42, rebuilt rather than decimated. Measured one-binary against `?proplod2=0`: 1,087,719 to 972,049 triangles, -10.6% of the frame, coverage 0.5533 to 0.5438.
- **[RN-45, OPEN, needs world-gen] A per-chunk noise PHASE in the terrain chunk format.** Sub-metre terrain surface detail of any kind is unreachable from planet-centred metres: float32 at Forge's 6e5 m radius has a 0.0625 m ULP, which is 3 to 15 times the pixel footprint of the ground under the player, so any SCREEN DERIVATIVE of a field keyed on it quantises into concentric arcs (measured, `docs/screenshots/RN45_iso_bump.png`). The fix is a phase reduced modulo the octave period on the CPU in float64, carried alongside the integer cell index, so the shader adds a small local offset to a small local coordinate and never forms a 6e5 intermediate. That is a chunk-format change and therefore world-gen's. The macro COLOUR term does not need it and has shipped.
- **[RN-47, OPEN, this lane] Underwater polish beyond the correctness fix.** The arms are not attenuated (they are drawn after the post interpose point, so it needs a second interpose); the water plane has no lateral bound; a free camera is never tinted; there is no surface seen from beneath; no ripples, glint, refraction, foam or wet-sand darkening. Admin ruled items 2 to 5 of the water lane's list are polish and must not each buy a DW-10 slot; none was spent on the correctness fix either, so the approval is still available.
- **[Phase 1 / M3.1, BLOCKED ON USER (UI-only), 2026-06-15]** Get real **Megascans/Fab nature materials** into the survival slice (Valheim-grade photo grass/rock/dirt/bark/foliage), replacing the M3.0 authored install-free PBR materials. **Finding: Fab import is NOT scriptable** (see status header + `ue/CLAUDE.md`). **User action needed, Window → Fab → search term → "Add to Project" for each (~2 min total):** ① **Grass surface**, search *"Wild Grass"* (Surface) for the meadow ground · ② **Soil/dirt surface**, *"Forest Floor"* or *"Dry Dirt"* (Surface) to blend bare patches · ③ **Rock/cliff surface**, *"Granite Cliff"* or *"Rock Cliff"* (Surface) for the ore boulders · ④ **Gravel/ground**, *"Forest Ground"* or *"Gravel"* (Surface, optional) · ⑤ **Bark**, *"Pine Bark"* or *"Birch Bark"* (Surface) for the tree trunk · ⑥ **3D plant / grass clump**, *"Wild Grass"* (3D Plant) to replace the flat cone tufts · ⑦ **Wildflowers**, *"Wildflowers"* or *"Meadow Flowers"* (3D Plant) · ⑧ **Wood planks**, *"Wood Planks"* (Surface, optional) for tools/furnace. Filter to **Free** (Fab has a price=Free filter) + prefer **2K** quality to keep size down (each 4K Megascans surface ≈ 50–150 MB, flag size before committing). Fab imports under the folder chosen in the Add-to-Project dialog (commonly `/Game/Megascans` or `/Game/Fab`). **Once added, Admin runs a follow-up agent to wire them** into `M_Meadow_Textured` (ground grass/dirt blend), `M_OreRock` (rock surface, keep per-kind tint + crystal emissive), the tree bark, and replace the ~1800 flat dressing pieces with the 3D-plant clumps.

Floating origin solved **frame-aligned** (actor root rotates radial-up→world-+Z so the patch is horizontal + plain gravity works; chunks relative-pos `(centerUniverse−origin)×scale`).
