## 7. Open questions & risks
- **OPEN, AND IT IS THE HANDOVER RN-1252 EXISTS TO MAKE (2026-08-03) - the machine wall's remaining darkness is albedo times metalness, and that number belongs to the ASSET LANE, not to this domain.** Every lighting lever in this domain, measured on one box on one frame: the RN-64 ground bounce is already 29.1 per cent of the light (RN-1250), a physically correct ambient floor adds 2.1 per cent (RN-1252), the screen-space occlusion is worth 24.9 per cent and the contact shadow 2.8 per cent. Together they move a close-up machine wall from 9.0 to about 11.6 counts of 255. The residual is `of_lib.PALETTE`'s `Steel` at metalness 0.85 and `SteelDark` at linear 0.0685/0.0802/0.0953, which compose to an effective diffuse albedo of 0.013 to 0.061. **This lane must NOT fix that by editing `MachineMat.ts`'s table alone**: RN-1205 established that the client table and the authored `.glb` values are one authority, with the studio rig reading the client's file to decide whether to force, so changing one side re-opens exactly the divergence that pass closed. It is a two-file act and the second file is under `tools/blender/**`, which this lane was told not to touch.
- **OPEN, INSTRUMENT (RN-1250, 2026-08-03) - in the close-up machine frame the VEGETATION boxes are not repeatable and cannot serve as controls.** A same-flag null pair moved **7,403 pixels of 59,200 (12.5 per cent)** in a foreground ground box and **1,183 of 15,000 (7.9 per cent)** in a box containing distant trees, both symmetric darker/lighter, i.e. wind phase rather than light. The MACHINE box is bit-identical across the same pair (0 pixels moved, maxDelta 0.0), so in this scene the subject is the repeatable thing and the "control" is not. This is RN-1202's glove finding in a second place, and the general rule is the one that generalises: **publish a same-flag null pair for every box you intend to trust, not only for the subject.** A control box that moves 12 per cent under no change will absorb any effect smaller than that and report a pass.
- **OPEN, AND IT IS A REAL DOUBLE OCCLUSION (RN-1252, 2026-08-03) - a machine is occluded three times over and one of them is a tiling texture that has never seen its geometry.** `attachSurface(m, 'panel', ...)` binds `of_panel_orm.png` as `aoMap` (red channel mean 0.7224, floor 0.4196), `PostDefaults.aoStrength = 0.9` multiplies TOTAL radiance and says so in its own comment, and `csStrength = 0.72` does the same. On the terrain, where direct sun dominates, "holding a little back is the honest correction for occluding light that was never occludable" is exactly right. On a machine's camera-facing face, which this lane has now measured to be **100 per cent indirect**, a term that multiplies total radiance is occluding light that was already occluded, and the tiling AO map is doing it with a pattern authored for a flat panel rather than for the box it is wrapped around. `?ao=0` is worth 24.9 per cent on that face. **Deliberately NOT changed here**, because `aoStrength` and `csStrength` were calibrated by RN-208's `lookdev.js` on terrain frames with no machines in them, and retuning a calibrated constant without re-running its own instrument is the failure INSTRUMENTS.md opens with. The shape of the work is a machine-lit arm of `lookdev.js` first, then one pair per constant.
- **OPEN, AND IT IS THE REST OF RN-1203 (2026-08-03) - the machine batch still pins `panel` onto six families that are not `panel`, and the honest fix is five surfaces, not "one extra fetch".** `Rubber` belt decks and slats, `Rock`/`RockDark` on the smelter hearth, the foundation body and the launch pad's blast slab and flame trench, `Plate` on the station deck (99.7 per cent of all `OF_Plate` area), `Iron` and `Coal` on the station's torn structure and scorch, and most of `items_atlas.glb` all wear plate seams, rivet rows and a weld bead. `flat` is closed; the other five are not. **Do not close it by baring them**: bare removes the wrong map and leaves nothing, and those are large areas. The shape of the work is a family index baked per vertex beside `aRole`, plus the tiling surfaces bound as explicit uniforms rather than through `material.normalMap`, and it needs a DW-10 argument and a sampler budget before it is worth starting.
  - **CLOSED 2026-08-13 BY RN-1478, AND THE SHAPE PROPOSED ABOVE IS THE ONE THAT WAS REFUSED.** The sampler budget this paragraph asked for was computed and it kills the per-vertex-index design: `MAX_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS` measured **16** on the real D3D11 path, one machine program already spends **9** (5 surface samplers + PMREM env + three cascade shadow maps), and 3 more per extra family puts `beltCargo` at 18. What landed instead is one `BatchedMesh` PER FAMILY with the ordinary `attachSurface` call, so the per-program sampler count does not move at all and the cost is **+8 draw calls on the machine shot**. `leaf` is the one family that stays folded into `panel` and it is refused structurally rather than left undone: a card family is unit-UV and alpha-cut, and this path's UVs are metres, so a card sampled here would punch leaf-shaped holes through a solid item chunk (`Surfaces.isTilingFamily`, verified against the manifest's own `uv_space`).
- **OPEN, AND IT IS A LOOKDEV FINDING RATHER THAN A MATERIALS ONE (RN-1200, 2026-08-03) - a machine's camera-facing surface is lit almost entirely by ambient, at every hour this site can reach.** Swept in the channel-off arm across the whole reachable sun band at the spawn, the close-up machine box runs **6.89 / 9.64 / 11.07 / 12.24 mean counts of 255 at sun dot 0.20 / 0.45 / 0.65 / 0.80**: monotonic, and never above 12.24, while the GROUND in the same frames reaches 105. A player places a machine in FRONT of themselves and then walks up to it, so the face they see is the one turned away from a rising sun, and the site's azimuth is fixed. **This is why a large per-role material change moves the median by 9 per cent rather than by 30**: there is very little light on the surface for any material response to act on. It is the same shape as ART-DIRECTION.md's sequencing rule, pointed at the machines: the response curve and the ambient are upstream of the material.
- **OPEN, INSTRUMENT (RN-1202, 2026-08-03) - the first-person gloves are non-repeatable across page loads and a lucky null pair will tell you they are not.** In the close-up scene the glove box moved **7,131 pixels of 95,200 between two loads carrying the IDENTICAL flag**, and 12,072 and 14,857 under the other two arms, symmetric darker/lighter in all three. An earlier quiet-scene pair had shown 6 pixels for the null and 11,317 for the subject, which reads exactly like a real effect on the player's hands and is not one. **Any machine measurement must exclude that box and must publish a same-flag null pair rather than one, because a single null can land on the same animation phase by chance.**
- **PARTLY CLOSED, PARTLY ESCALATED (RN-850, 2026-08-03) - R17's Beach half is not a shader-constant problem and never was.** RN-843 left the default unchanged "pending an anisotropy instrument". The instrument exists (`probes/aniso.js`), validated 0.066 isotropic against 0.9993 corduroy, and the default stays where it is on a much stronger footing: Plains has a clean interior minimum AT the shipped 31.85 texels, and Beach has no minimum reachable from this knob because its corduroy is in `of_ground_relief` under biome 1's channel weights rather than in the finite difference. **CINDER'S HALF IS STILL LIVE and would yield to the support** (its difference-spectrum peak moves 9.4 px to 54 px across the sweep, which is the signature of a support stamping its own scale), but no single value serves both bodies, so a per-biome or per-body support is the shape of any real fix and that is a bigger decision than one constant. **A KNOWN GAP IN THE INSTRUMENT, stated because it bounds the conclusion:** the index is a CORDUROY detector, not a SCRIBBLE detector. A synthetic globally-isotropic scribble (nested contour bands, locally 1-D everywhere) reads 0.0204, indistinguishable from noise, and the structure-tensor coherence built to cover that case did not separate it either. The measured artefact at all three sites has an oblique preferred direction well above floor, so the instrument applies here, but that is a fact about these sites and not a guarantee about the next one.
- **OPEN, AND IT IS ANOTHER LANE'S BUT IT BLOCKS MINE (RN-846, 2026-08-03) - Cinder's night ground is a CONSTANT 15.93 counts and nothing in this domain accounts for it.** Measured at four sun elevations below the horizon: p05 12.93 and p50 15.93 at -0.35, -0.60 and -0.85 alike, so it does not vary with how far the sun is down and is therefore a constant term rather than a residue of grazing light. It survives `?lamp=0`, `?iblground=0` and `?bouncelit=0` UNCHANGED to the hundredth of a count, and `?horizonocc=0` moves it 0.71. It is not a tone-curve lift: pointed at the sky, the airless night sky is **0.00 exactly across 144,000 pixels, p05 through max**, so black maps to black and this is real light on the terrain. **Consequence, and it is why this is filed rather than fixed here: planetshine at full Forge is 1.837e-4 of sunlight, worth about 0.9 of a count, so it is 1/17 of a floor whose source is unknown. No night-lighting change on an airless body can be measured until this is found.** The suspect is a constant `ambient` term in `TerrainShader`; that file plus `TerrainMaterial.ts` and `TerrainArt.glsl.ts` are all dirty with another lane's edits tonight, so this lane did not open them. Whoever takes it gets the measurement for free: `probes/lunarnight.js` plus `?sundot=` at four values.
- **OPEN, R70 IS NOW VISIBLE AND HAS A RATE (RN-845, 2026-08-03) - the drawn moon and the frame-graph moon separate at 542.5 m/s.** `orbital.h` says plainly that `sim_world.h` installs Cinder as a STATIC frame offset while `transfer.h` solves against a Cinder that moves, and that reconciling them is a world decision. Admin ruled that Cinder orbits, so the renderer reads `of_body_state` every frame. The two therefore diverge, and the rate is not small: the orbit is 542.5 m/s (verified against the client, which reported z = -2305.6 m at simSecs 4.25, i.e. 542.5 x 4.25 to the metre), so after one hour of sim time at 1x the drawn moon is **1,953 km from where the frame graph has it, which is 9.8 Cinder radii**. Under warp it is much worse, because the day clock takes a warp credit and this one does not. Nothing is wrong in the renderer: it is drawing the ephemeris Admin chose. **But an autopilot that flies to the frame graph's Cinder will now visibly miss the one in the sky**, and that is the first time R70 has had a symptom anyone can see. Admin's to route.
- **OPEN (RN-849, 2026-08-03) - the bake is 591 ms of the boot thread and nothing amortises it.** One body, 262,144 oracle calls, measured; the ladder is 51.6 / 169.9 / 591.0 / 2,398.1 ms at 128 / 256 / 512 / 1024. It is paid once and it is not on any frame, so it is a boot regression rather than a frame one, but it is a seventh of a second and a third body would double it. The cheap fix is the oracle WORKER, which already exists and already has its own body handle (`WorkerProbe.ts`), so the bake could be posted rather than blocking; the disc would then need a low-resolution first texture and an upgrade, which is a real design and not a tweak. Named with the sizing attached rather than done in a hurry at the end of a lane.
- **OPEN, R59, AND IT IS THE FINDING OF THIS PASS (RN-833, 2026-08-02) - the hall's wall "ring" is twelve identically oriented boxes, and the walker falls out of the room in 81.1 per cent of azimuths.** The brief that sent me here stated the twelve segments "form an unbroken ring (4.536 m segments against a 4.341 m side, so they overlap at the corners)". They do not, and the shipped bytes say so. `walls.append(("col_HallWall%s" % ..., (0.30, HUB_RI * 0.56, CORR_CZ + 2.0), (c[0] * d, c[1] * d, ...)))` moves `loc` round the circle and never touches `size`, so all twelve are the SAME axis-aligned slab at twelve positions. Only A (0 deg) and G (180 deg) are tangential, and those are precisely the two I have just turned into doorways. D and J (90 / 270 deg) are 4.5 m RADIAL FINS pointing out of the room, reaching z = 10.518 past a hull whose outer radius is 8.40. The other eight are oblique. Measured at 180 azimuths off the shipped glb: 146 fall, worst unfloored run 10.2408 m at azimuth 88.0. TWO ROOT CAUSES AND THE SECOND IS THE HARD ONE: `of_lib.add_collision_box` never passes the `rot_z` that `MeshBuilder.box` supports, AND the runtime consumes every proxy as an axis-aligned bound anyway (`SpaceStation.learnStationProxies` reads `geometry.boundingBox` through the node matrix, and `Solid.boxes` are AABBs in the solid's frame), so a rotated chord could not survive the trip even if Blender authored one: a 30 deg chord arrives as a 2.42 x 4.08 m blob. THE COLLISION REPRESENTATION CANNOT EXPRESS THIS ROOM, so the fix is a rectilinear staircase and not a rotation. PRICED: holding the 0.276 m sagitta the twelve-gon was authored to needs 12 steps per quadrant, which is about 60 axis-aligned proxies for the hall alone against 63 for the entire station today, plus a `max_tris_collision` raise, plus it moves the input to `StationGravity.ts`'s per-deck volume derivation, which is another lane's. That is an Admin scheduling decision, so it is named here with the sizing attached rather than slipped into a render lane mid-task, and `check_station_deck.py` runs the measurement in a second.
- **OPEN, R60 (RN-833, 2026-08-02) - the four `col_GalleryFloor*` slabs already commit R59's sin in the opposite direction.** Each is a quadrant slab `(HUB_RI - MEZZ_RI, HUB_RI * 1.02, CEIL_PROXY_T)` placed on a diagonal, so its outer CORNER sits at radius 10.41 while the hall wall it is meant to be inside is at 8.10: there is standable proxy 2.3 m outside the drum at gallery height, with no mesh under it and no rail on the outboard side. Whoever closes R59 must decide the gallery in the same pass, because the two are approximating the same drum and a staircase wall with a square gallery poking through it is worse than either alone.
- **OPEN (RN-821, 2026-08-02) - the station has no far-scene presence at all, and that is a decision I declined to make alone.** The gate is the near camera's far plane, so from the ground there is nothing where the station is: no dot, no billboard. The record already buys a map marker and a selectable orbit line, so the station is FINDABLE, it is simply not visible in the sky. Adding a far-scene proxy is a fifth thing in a project that has four scenes, and it could not just join the far scene: at `FAR_SCALE = 1e-5` a 66.97 m station is 0.00067 units against `farCam.near = 0.01`, so it would need its own scale. Named rather than quietly added.
- **OPEN, AND THE BIGGEST SINGLE LEVER ON THE FOREST FLOOR (RN-311, 2026-08-01) - Forest shares `GROUND_DETAIL` verbatim with Plains and Hills.** Measured, not asserted: in a 694 m2 patch at the shipped densities the four Forest biome props place 27 instances and the ground-detail layer places 2,244, so 98.8 percent of what a standing player sees of a forest floor is a table that says "meadow". **The ask is a `FOREST_DETAIL` const in `web/src/assets/Registry.ts` beside `GROUND_DETAIL` (currently lines 214 to 225), referenced from `BIOME_PROPS[3]` (line 341), budget neutral on BOTH axes:** same 538,000 pre-scale sum and, at the shipped per-card triangle counts, the same ~61,900 triangles in the patch (+0.16 percent). GrassCardA 180,000 to 90,000, GrassCardB 105,000 to 30,000, GrassCardC 70,000 to 120,000, BroadleafForb 75,000 to 150,000, SedgeRosette 60,000 to 110,000, FlowerSprig 24,000 to 14,000, PebbleScatter 24,000 unchanged. **No new stem, no new asset, no new geometry, one `const` in one file.** The before / after / ask triple is `docs/screenshots/RN301_before_floor.png`, `RN301_after_floor.png`, `RN311_ask_floor.png`, all one seed, one lighting, one camera. Owned by whoever owns `Registry.ts`; this lane cannot land it under a `web/src` freeze and will not ship dead geometry to fake it.
- **OPEN (RN-311, 2026-08-01) - the grass card carries no lateral silhouette, and the leaf card's base is cut away.** A subagent decoded `assets/textures/dist/of_grass_a.png` and found ZERO transparent columns: all 256 columns are one opaque run reaching the bottom row, so the "bundle of 11 blades" is a bottom-anchored MAT with a serrated top edge. Consequence: **any u band of any width returns the same 0.6009 opaque fraction**, so `GRASS_BAND_HALF_U` controls only the variance of a per-blade HEIGHT crop (sd 0.120 at the shipped 0.045, collapsing to 0.024 at 0.25) and widening it buys no extra visible blade. **The 0.045 is correct and was left alone**; the defect is `texgen._grass_strips`, which authors 11 blades on a 0.0455 half-pitch with 0.058 to 0.082 half-widths so the roots overlap. Separately, `of_leaf_a.png`'s bottom row is only 22.7 percent opaque, so every Leaf/LeafDry strip (`Detail_GrassCardC`, `Detail_BroadleafForb`, the sprig heads, `Beach_DuneGrass`) is alpha-cut at its base and roots into thin air. **NOT TAKEN because regenerating either PNG moves a shared generated artifact whose sha256 every textured contract carries**, and because a card's albedo is look-development's under ART-DIRECTION.md's sequencing rule. The mask's SHAPE is arguably geometry and arguably this lane's; Admin should say which.
- **OPEN (RN-304, 2026-08-01) - `Detail_GrassCardA`'s IoU went the wrong way, 0.417 to 0.504.** Its width cv (0.088 to 0.100) and area cv (0.048 to 0.116) both improved, so the mass genuinely varies more with yaw; the overlap rose anyway because at `segs` 2 the new `twist` has exactly one interior level to act on and the plan filled out instead of changing shape. Card A is the single most instanced mesh in the game (1,080,000/km2 after the scale), so this is worth a targeted look: the cheap candidates are `segs` 3 on card A (+6 triangles, ~+41,000 in the 45 m radius, which is why it was not done blind) or a stronger `kink` at unchanged `segs`. `Detail_SedgeRosette` likewise barely moved (0.577 to 0.584).
- **OPEN (RN-309, 2026-08-01) - `bush_scrub` is fixed and does not draw.** No `Registry.ts` table names the stem (`ROADMAP-W11.md:415`; `water_pool.glb` and `oil_seep.glb` are in the same state). The IoU 0.745 to 0.580 fix, the 900 to 1000 file-total raise and the 1.00 x 1.00 to 1.14 x 0.86 footprint are therefore all real and all invisible. **Either give it a row or retire it**; it is currently paying BatchedMesh geometry slots at load for nothing, which is the same cost the RN-284 hand-off warned about for NEW stems.
- **OPEN (RN-305 to RN-309, 2026-08-01) - the mass primitive is blocky and both the bush and the mushroom caps are made of it.** `hc.blob` / `tc.canopy_mass` at `seg` 4 to 5 with two rings is close to an octahedron, and under ART-DIRECTION.md that reads as a defect at arm's length. Priced: `seg` 4 to 6 on the bush's nine masses is +72 triangles, which would take LOD0 from 270 to 342 against a 300 budget, so it is a contract raise and not a free change. The alternative that costs nothing is a third ring at the same `seg` (same +8 per mass). Deferred deliberately rather than guessed, because on `bush_scrub` it would buy a picture nobody can see (above) and on the mushroom caps it is 57 LOD0 instances.
- **CLOSED (RN-310, 2026-08-01) - the WG-13 rule was gated on ONE SIDE ONLY, and the rule itself is now retired.** Reid ruled: *"there should be no scenery trees. all trees should be minable."* There is one family, so there is nothing to separate; `assert_bare_trunk` is removed and no bare-trunk fraction is constrained anywhere. **The gate was right while the rule stood and it is recorded as such** (it caught the broadleaf pair 0.014 of height apart), and **the lesson survives its rule: a constraint that separates two families cannot be checked by measuring one of them.** What replaced it is the same measurement, printed and unasserted, plus a refusal on a canopy tree with NO foliage, which is a build defect rather than a design rule; negative control run (predicate inverted, refused by name on all three, reverted, glb byte-identical). **The consequence NOT taken: if every tree is minable, `props_canopy`'s three stop being scatter props and become harvest nodes. That is a Registry, world-gen and gameplay change and is Admin's to route.**
- **CLOSED (RN-271 to RN-278, 2026-08-01) - the canopy parasol repetition.** Fixed, and the scoping in the original note was wrong in an instructive way: the fix was not "2 to 3 authored variants" but breaking ROTATIONAL SYMMETRY, because the engine was already drawing every tree at hundreds of yaws and a solid of revolution looks identical at all of them. Mean pairwise silhouette IoU across yaw: pine 0.845 to 0.465, broadleaf 0.658 to 0.381, fir 0.822 to 0.617. **Authored variants were NOT shipped, and the reason is worth keeping: a new prop stem needs a row in `web/src/assets/Registry.ts`, and `PropLibrary.register` calls `addGeometry` for every stem it finds in a GLB whether or not any biome table references it, so an unreferenced stem costs BatchedMesh geometry slots and moves the `geometries` invariant other lanes measure, for zero visible benefit until the registry row lands.** If Admin wants a fourth and fifth canopy form, the ask is one `C('Canopy_<Name>', <density>)` line per stem in each biome table that should carry it (`Registry.ts:283-300`), and the builder work is then small.
- **SUPERSEDED by RN-310 (2026-08-01), kept per the never-delete rule.** **(was OPEN, RN-284, 2026-08-01) - the WG-13 rule is gated on ONE SIDE ONLY.** `build_props_canopy.assert_bare_trunk` refuses a SCENERY tree whose foliage hangs below its floor. Nothing stops the HARVEST trees drifting UP to meet it, and the broadleaf pair came within 0.014 of height of being indistinguishable during this pass with no check noticing. The clean fix is a floor AND a ceiling asserted from one table both build scripts read, or a `validate_glb` check driven by a contract field. `validate_glb.py` was not this pass's to edit.
- **OPEN (RN-274, 2026-08-01) - the canopy triangle raise is UNPRICED IN ENGINE.** props_canopy went 932 to 1996 file total, estimated at roughly +9 percent of Forest-site scene triangles (about +100,000, four draws on about 75 trees inside WG-17's 78 m radius). The arithmetic is in the commit; no frame was measured, because this pass ran without a browser. **The measurement owed is an A/B/A/B interleaved pair in ONE binary at the Forest site**, per INSTRUMENTS.md on timings while other lanes are building.
- **OPEN (RN-101 re-test, 2026-08-01) - the geometry-versus-texture split under the new target.** ART-DIRECTION.md asks for RN-101 to be re-tested now that leaf and grass cards have landed and measured essentially free at the Forest site (RN-183). **The geometric recommendation, stated without a frame measurement because none was possible this pass: keep GEOMETRY for the crown OUTLINE and move to CARDS for the crown INTERIOR.** RN-101 was right about the thing it measured, which is the silhouette: an alpha card's outline is its own rectangle until the alpha test cuts it, and RN-177 deliberately set coverage 0.665 ABOVE the 0.35 cutoff so far mips converge toward SOLID rather than eroding. That property is exactly what makes a card a poor silhouette and a good interior. Under the new target the two jobs separate: the crown EDGE is where "detail at every distance" lives and geometry is the only thing that can put a hole in a silhouette; the crown INTERIOR is where "no flat regions that resolve into nothing" lives and a card carries leaflet serration, midrib and translucency for zero triangles. Concretely: spend triangles on fewer, larger, better-shaped boughs and clumps at the crown's outer envelope, and spend cards on more and smaller foliage inside it, where overdraw is bounded because the clumps already occlude each other. **The cost re-measurement this recommendation is NOT allowed to assume is a fill-rate A/B at the Forest site with the interior card count raised, which RN-183 established the method for and which this pass could not run.**
- **OWED DEBT (RN-177, 2026-07-30) - the RGBA PNG writer exists twice.** texgen.py and groundtex.py each carry a write_png_rgba/read_png_rgba pair (texgen's was added deliberately without touching groundtex, whose lane was live in the same file). One should import from the other once both lanes are quiet; the duplication is annotated at both sites.
- **OWED DEBT (RN-179/RN-180, 2026-07-30) - two measured tolerances to watch.** (1) The decimated-LOD UV hull slack is 2e-2 for assets with `uv_authored_materials` (measured worst 1.2e-2 across the card/metre discontinuity, printed per run) against 1e-3 elsewhere; if the printed worst climbs toward the slack, look before widening. (2) `vramEstimateMB` is blind to ALL shared surface maps (RN-51/RN-78 precedent), now 7.83 MB across six families; the surfaces report carries the real number.
- **STATED LIMIT (RN-180, 2026-07-30) - the shell UV seam.** A closed sweep with per-vertex UVs cannot seam: one face column per shell interpolates backwards across the u repeats. Absorbed by the repeating card texture and invisible in every pair; recorded so nobody diagnoses it as a bug later.
- **OWED DEBT (RN-96 to RN-102, 2026-07-30) - Tree_Sway superseded; Tree_Fall status UNPROVEN.** In Admin's words, and the distinction is the point. `Tree_Sway` is superseded by the vertex wind (DW-38) and is deletable in an asset pass (contract `clips` change plus the full byte-identical control). `Tree_Fall` answers a DIFFERENT question: it is shaped exactly like the harvest felling animation, and DW-34 documents gameplay reading published impact frames from clips for swing timing. Before Tree_Fall enters any deletion list, someone must prove it unreferenced by the CLIP-NAME CONTRACT, not just unreferenced by the sway path. Do not delete it on RN-101's original framing, which called both clips dead and was wrong about the second.
- **OWED DEBT (RN-100, 2026-07-30) - the node batches are never frustum-culled, and now there is one more of them.** `NodeBatch` batches draw with `frustumCulled` false in main plus three cascades; the bark family split trunks out of `coarse:matte` into a new `bark:matte` batch, so an empty batch now costs 4 draw calls even at sites with no harvest node in range (measured 78 to 82 at the RN-15 camera). Bounded, stated, and it rides with whoever takes node-batch culling; a second family split would add another 4.
- **OWED DEBT (RN-102, 2026-07-30) - PropLibrary.ts is over the 400-line cap and was before this pass.** HEAD stood at 413 (one of SEVENTEEN repo-wide cap violations in `check-limits`); the flora pass trimmed its own additions to net +1 (414). The file wants the batch bookkeeping split from the load/registration path, and that split should not happen while multiple lanes are live in it.
- **R1 (terrain Nanite, RESOLVED for Spike 1):** terrain uses **classic streamed meshes, not Nanite** (RN-5), world-gen already LODs and Nanite blocks the depth path. **Nanite cost on thousands of small *factory* meshes is the surviving open Nanite question** → now scoped as a named RC-8 Phase-1 residual (§5, item b): measure in the integrated factory-render co-validation, not Spike-3 (which is sim-only).
- **OWED DEBT (RN-61, 2026-07-28) - node scale does not carry the harvest reach.** `NodeField.build` now gives trees a per-instance size of 0.86 to 1.14, but `art.radiusM` (harvest reach) and `art.hitUpM` (hit point) in `NodeArt.ts` are per-KIND constants and do not scale with `pl.scale`. So a big tree and a small tree are chopped from the same distance and struck at the same height. The range was capped at +/-14% specifically to keep this under the aim tolerance rather than to hide it. **Any lane widening node scale must scale those two with it**, or the art change becomes a gameplay bug. Same applies to outcrops, whose `pl.scale` comes from deposit richness and can already exceed this range.
- **R5 (render-wall LOD-0 hot spot, from RC-8 §5), now the SOLE surviving render-wall residual; the *contract* half is closed headlessly.** The numeric render-cost model (factory-sim FS-14, `render_cost_tests`) confirms the suspect cost is **not** factory-sim's `GetLineItems` (a cheap flat read whose work is bounded to the Near0 lines, 180k item-evals for 300 near lines in the 100k stress scene) but **our** per-frame `pathUnitToWorld` evals + instance-transform writes for those ~50–180k discrete LOD-0 belt items, plus burst spikes when a camera cut promotes many lines to LOD-0 at once. The model proves the *count* that reaches us is bounded (Near0 lines × items/line, O(lines)), what it can't time is the **per-item build cost on real hardware**. Mitigation direction unchanged: screen-space-driven LOD-0 band kept tight (foreground bay only), amortized/budgeted line-promotion (spread instance builds across frames), LOD-0↔1 cross-fade to kill the discrete-items→scrolling-material pop. **Measured in the Phase-1 integrated co-validation (jointly owned with factory-sim).**
- **R2 (cross-fade, mitigation DESIGNED, pass/fail set):** scaled↔near pop + lighting mismatch on fast approach. Addressed structurally in spike1-rendering §2 (proxy-as-backdrop + occlusion-first cross-fade + single shared sun + depth-aware horizon haze). Retired by validation **RV3/RV8** (frame-stepped orbital-speed descent: no silhouette jump, no terminator discontinuity, no band-edge flicker). **Primary residual visual risk until the build runs.**
- **R4 (new, depth × Nanite):** Nanite can't take a custom per-pixel `SV_Depth` write → log-depth and Nanite-on-terrain partly incompatible. Resolved by RN-5 (classic terrain) + measuring reversed-Z-float-first (RN-2). Flagged to Admin as a standing UE5 constraint.
- **R3 (Admin Q3):** 1st vs 3rd person affects camera rig and near-field detail budget. Rig (spike1-rendering §1) is perspective-agnostic, NearCam parents the pawn either way; decision still pending gameplay.

## 8. Subagent delegation log

- **2026-08-03 (RN-1250 to RN-1254)** - *One read-only subagent, spawned in parallel with my
  own setup of the isolated measuring tree rather than instead of it, and briefed with ten
  numbered questions that each demanded a file, a line and a literal, plus an explicit
  instruction to answer "not found" rather than to reason.* Sent to trace, term by term, what
  light reaches a `MachineBatch` fragment against what reaches a terrain fragment: which scene
  the batch is on, whether that scene is a `SkyIbl` target, who writes `environmentIntensity`,
  whether anything sets `envMapIntensity` or binds an `aoMap`, which UV channel that AO map
  uses and whether the geometry actually HAS it, the near scene's hemisphere and directional
  inventory with the single writer of intensity, the exact GLSL of the terrain's bounce beside
  the exact GLSL of `SkyAtmosphere`'s ground shell, every lighting URL flag in two trees
  against `run.mjs`'s whitelist, and the authored per-role palette. **It returned the two facts
  this pass turned on, and I had guessed wrong on both:** the batch is on `scenes.near` and
  therefore already had RN-64's bounce and full environment intensity, so the missing-bounce
  hypothesis I was handed was dead before I measured it (I measured it anyway, which is
  RN-1250); and the panel ORM's BLUE channel has a mean of 0.958, so the authored metalness
  0.85 arrives at the shader as 0.81 rather than being meaningfully modulated by the map. It
  also caught that `Surfaces.apply` divides `material.color` by the family's `albedo_mean`,
  which is why the vertex colour IS the effective albedo and the arithmetic in RN-1252 closes
  rather than being a rough order. **Folded into RN-1250, RN-1252 and the open items above.
  Nothing under `tools/` was run or modified, as briefed.**

- **2026-08-03 (RN-1200 to RN-1206)** - *One read-only subagent, spawned in parallel with
  my own reading of the client rather than instead of it, and briefed to answer five
  factual questions with file paths and line numbers rather than to form an opinion.*
  Sent to inventory which `.glb` reach a `MachineBatch`, which authored roles they carry,
  what `of_lib.PALETTE` gives each role, which of those roles are not `panel`, and where
  the studio rig lives. **Three of its five answers changed the design.** (1) Five pools,
  not one: the launch pad and the space station are `MachineBatch` instances too, so this
  was never only about the factory. (2) `BARE_ROLES` is `{"Fang"}`, spider-only, so NO
  machine role is authored bare, which is what makes `?machinemat=flat` a true identity
  and not merely a close one. (3) **`render_machines.py:326` PARSES `MachineBatch.ts`
  for its two material literals and raises `SystemExit` if they are missing**, which
  killed the one-authority refactor I had already written and turned it into RN-1204's
  boot assert. Without that question asked, the clean design would have hard-failed
  every machine render the asset lane takes, on the day this pass exists to unblock them.
  The lesson is the brief's, not the agent's: **ask what reads your file, not just what
  your file reads.**

- **2026-08-03 (RN-1000 to RN-1018)** - *One subagent for the whole pass, read-only,
  spawned in parallel with my own measurement rather than instead of it, and briefed to
  say "does not exist" explicitly rather than describe something adjacent.* Sent to
  inventory everything that decides how bright a finished pixel is, because designing a
  night exposure stage on a guess about what already exists is the expensive mistake.
  It returned file-and-line for the whole chain and **three facts that changed the
  design**: (1) `lift`, `contrast`, `saturation` and `vignette` are all applied AFTER the
  tonemap and after the sRGB encode, on display-referred values, so **`exposure` is the
  only scene-linear knob in the stack** and nothing else in the grade can substitute for
  it; (2) **no GPU luminance measurement exists and the bloom pyramid cannot be reused
  for one**, because the threshold and Karis weight are applied on level 0 only, so every
  level above it is a pyramid of "what is brighter than 0.75" rather than a mean, and an
  auto-exposure needs its own reduction; (3) **`loop.onPreRender` is the right hook and
  it takes no arguments**, so the render delta time an adaptation time constant needs is
  not published anywhere, which is a small plumbing change that has to happen first. It
  also confirmed `?lamp=` dead independently of the probe that found it, and named
  **RN-334 as the standing prior art**: one global exposure serves biome albedos spanning
  0.03 to 0.58, a 20x range, and that lane wrote down that no single value can be right
  for both ends. Adaptation is the answer to a question this domain already asked and
  parked.

- **2026-08-03 (RN-950 to RN-953)** - *One subagent, read-only, briefed with the premise
  under test QUOTED VERBATIM so that it could come back false.* Sent to measure the real sizes
  of `OF_Plate`'s consumers from the shipped `.glb` bytes: decode POSITION / TEXCOORD_0 /
  indices out of the BIN chunks, apply node transforms, weld at 1e-5 m, split into connected
  components, report per-component extent, area and UV repeats. It returned 207 components
  across the four files and **three corrections to my framing that I had not asked for and that
  changed the answer**: (1) a world AABB overstates a rotated part, so it reported the
  PCA-oriented extent instead; (2) a whole-part UV bounding box is inflated by island PLACEMENT
  rather than island SIZE, because these UVs are per-face planar projections with per-face
  offsets, so a 26 mm knuckle plate has a 3.7-repeat UV bbox and a 0.066-repeat largest island,
  and it reported both rather than picking one; (3) it verified the metre-UV contract itself
  instead of assuming it, 12,084 edges, ratio 1.0000 at p25 and p50 with a one-sided tail to
  1.20 at p90 that is planar-projection foreshortening and not a scale error. It also refused
  to let an area summary hide the shape, and the 99.72 per cent figure is what sent me to
  `MachineBatch.ts` and turned a refused shared-family change into a player-only one.
  **What I would brief differently:** it measured the bytes faithfully and had no way to know
  the client OVERRIDES the family for one of the four files, and I did not tell it to check.
  The lesson folded back into RN-953: a byte measurement of "who consumes a family" is
  incomplete without the client's own binding, and `MachineBatch` is the exception that makes
  it so.

- **2026-08-03 (RN-845 to RN-850)** - *One subagent, briefed narrowly, with an explicit
  do-not-touch list because I was live in six files under `web/src/render` for the whole
  pass.* It was sent at R17: build an instrument that separates a contour ARTEFACT from real
  detail by **anisotropy** rather than by contrast, because RN-843 shipped the attribution and
  then refused a new default on the grounds that its own metric could not tell "less artefact"
  from "less signal". The brief named the trap that would sink it before it measured anything
  (a ground plane at a grazing angle foreshortens in screen space, so ANY isotropic texture
  measures as anisotropic) and required it to validate the index on synthetic isotropic noise
  against synthetic corduroy before trusting it on a frame. It was told in terms that a null
  result is a complete and acceptable answer. **Mid-task I sent one amendment**, after Admin
  reported a sixth harness defect elsewhere: an instrument had classified pixels by `r - b > 45`
  and was then pointed at a change that halves `r - b`, reporting an area collapse on geometry
  that had not moved. The amendment made the same demand of this one: state what the mask and
  the normalisation depend on, prove neither depends on the swept quantity, and publish the RAW
  high-pass energy beside the normalised index, so that "flat and isotropic" and "detailed and
  isotropic" cannot be confused. **A support that wins on anisotropy while its raw energy
  collapses has not fixed the artefact, it has deleted the ground**, which is exactly what
  RN-843 refused to ship.
  **It came back with a null, which is what the brief asked for if the numbers said so, and with one finding worth more than the sweep: the instrument agrees with RN-843 about CINDER and disagrees about BEACH.** It also corrected its own brief twice and said so, which is the behaviour the delegation protocol asks for: RN-843's Cinder crater-floor site is unusable for any ground measurement (luma 1.0 of 255 across all 786,432 px at its maximum reachable sun, which is RN-844's warning arriving in practice), so it moved sites and said which; and it ADDED a third site, Forge Plains, which is the site that turned "no interior minimum anywhere" into "the three sites disagree and one of them already wants the shipped value". It reported the one thing its instrument cannot do (a globally isotropic scribble reads 0.02, indistinguishable from noise, and its second instrument did not cover the gap either) rather than leaving the limit for someone to discover.

- **2026-08-03 (RN-840 to RN-844)** - *Two subagents, both read-only against `web/src`, both briefed with an explicit do-not-edit list because I was editing seven files live and a concurrent write would have corrupted the pass.* (1) An **Explore** agent inventoried second-body rendering and returned the finding that shaped the rest of the night: **nothing draws a second celestial body anywhere in this client, and the client cannot even learn where Cinder is.** `sim_world.h` is not in the wasm build at all (`kCinderOrbitRadiusM = 1.2e7` exists in C++ only), and of 207 exports none returns a body position. It also established `FAR_SCALE = 1e-5` and the far camera's 1e5-unit far plane, which is what makes the angular-size arithmetic in section 7 possible. (2) A **general-purpose** agent diagnosed R17 with a five-arm controlled sweep and produced the whole attribution in RN-843, including the texture's real PNG header bytes and the correlation-versus-support table that named the cause. **It corrected its own brief twice and said so, unprompted**: it threw out two of its own rows after a mask gated on `luma >= 5` selected 10 per cent of a grazing frame and reported "no change" for something unmissable by eye (INSTRUMENTS.md, "a term measured only where it cannot work reports its own absence"), and it confirmed my `--sundot` correction against the claim written into its own brief. Both are the behaviour the delegation protocol asks for.

- **2026-08-02 (RN-821 to RN-833)** - *Two Explore subagents, read-only, run in parallel while I read the shipped glb myself.* One mapped how `col_*` proxies are authored in `build_space_station.py` and `station_form.py`, briefed to quote the corridor bulkhead jamb pattern literally as the thing to copy. It returned the emitter verbatim AND the finding that broke the brief's premise: the twelve hall wall boxes are never rotated, only A and G are tangential, D and J are radial fins. It also caught the budget before I hit it (`max_tris_collision` 700 against 57 boxes at 684, so +6 boxes overflows). The other mapped the render machinery: `Scenes` / `DepthPolicy` / `CameraRig`, including the 100 km near far plane that became RN-823's entire argument; the `emptyIndex` to `addLadder` to `indexRow` to `attachShadowLod` to `publishLadders` registration order; `run.mjs`'s screenshot timing with the `writeshot.mjs` escape hatch; and `stationwalk.js` P5 line by line. Both were briefed narrowly and both returned things I would not have found in the time, the sharpest being that `--out` fires after `settle()` and a walker parked in orbit never converges, which is why `stationdraw.js` grabs its own frame. I VERIFIED THE PREMISE-BREAKING CLAIM MYSELF against the shipped bytes before acting on it, which is the same discipline that then caught my own sweep printing 0 of 180 for the wrong reason.

- **2026-08-01 (RN-511 to RN-518)** - *One Explore subagent, read-only, run in parallel with the freeze build.* Asked for the complete list of terms that add radiance to a terrain fragment at low sun, every `ofAtmoAerial` call site with the distance it passes, the starlight gate's exact band, and every URL flag that touches any of them. It returned a correct and useful map and named `skyAmb`'s two-sample march as the strongest cyan suspect. **The map was right and the suspect was wrong**, which is worth recording: the wash turned out not to be a lighting term at all, and no amount of reading the lighting path could have found that. The measurement that settled it (one site, one sun, two frames) took one run. **Reading narrows a hypothesis space; only a pair eliminates one.**
- **2026-08-01 (RN-301 to RN-311)** - *One subagent, pure python, no Blender, for the understorey pass.* Brief: decode `assets/textures/dist/of_grass_a.png` and `of_leaf_a.png` without Pillow, walk every Grass-role blade in `build_detail_cards.py` and `build_props_plains.py` through `blade_uvs`'s own key expressions, and report what fraction of each strip survives `alpha_test` 0.35. **It falsified the hypothesis I was about to act on, which is the whole value of the delegation.** I believed `GRASS_BAND_HALF_U = 0.045` was wasting the mask and that widening it would multiply visible blades for free. It returned that the card has ZERO transparent columns, every column being one opaque run reaching the bottom row, so the mean survive fraction is pinned at the card's own 0.6009 at EVERY band width and widening buys nothing but a collapse in per-blade height variance (sd 0.120 to 0.024). Zero of 113 strips across both files land on a gap; the shipped 0.045 is correct and untouched. It also located the real defect upstream (`texgen._grass_strips` authors 11 blades on a 0.0455 half-pitch with 0.058 to 0.082 half-widths, so the roots overlap into a mat) and found a second one nobody had looked for: the LEAF card's bottom row is only 22.7 percent opaque, so every Leaf/LeafDry strip in the game is alpha-cut at its base. Both logged in section 7, neither taken, because both live in a shared generated artifact. | Done |
| Date | Subagent task | Status | Outcome |

Both halves are required and neither fails loudly on its own |
